# MeetingPipe Q6 archive (running)

> Running archive of Q6 items that reached done-done. Since the 2026-07-10 process change, a completed item moves here (its ToC row plus its Task-spec text) in the same commit that ships it, instead of sitting `done` in the active ToC until the quarter roll; the active backlog is [`meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md`](./meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md). This file freezes at the quarter roll, like [`q5-final.md`](./q5-final.md). Items moved here before the process change landed as a batch on 2026-07-10.

## Table of contents

| ID | Task | Category | Status | Priority | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VALID1 | On-device acceptance measurements | Validation | done | P1 | Closed 2026-07-16 after three quarters, all five bars settled. **DIAR1 DER = 8.99%** (3 meetings reviewed end to end; bimodal 0.3/13.6/49.0, and the 49% outlier is the real defect, now DIAR2), **DIAR1 latency PASS** (132 runs / 47.7 h, worst 5.3x real time), **UX4 PASS** (owner forced a capture-TCC failure), **A15 baseline set** (cold 39.8 s / warm 31.4 s / 8.4 s load, 7B), attribution baselined (3.2% unattributed post-roster); A16 + SUM1-APPLE quality already closed in Q5. It was never blocked on the Mac: the Q4 scaffolding had gone stale and could not see the data it was built to measure. One leg **waived, not measured** (SUM1-APPLE zero-egress) and split out as EGRESS1 (P4) rather than dressed up as a pass. |
| MIC10 | Re-lock the Teams mini-window mute control + VAD-contradiction staleness | Detection | done | P1 | Shipped 2026-07-14. The stale mute button was never the hub/pre-join window every earlier writeup blamed: a live AX dump proved it is the **backgrounded full meeting window of the same call**, which freezes its mute description when the call pops out into the floating compact panel and then reads `muted` forever. Both windows carry a `Leave` button, which is why the Leave anchor could never discriminate. Now anchored on the panel's `AXSystemDialog` subrole (locale-independent), so the frozen button is never read; plus a unanimity fusion (a cross-window disagreement reads blind under capture-first instead of letting the stale one win) and part 2's VAD-contradiction discredit as backstops. |
| MIC6 | Attribute-based (futureproof) native mute read | Detection | wont-do | P1 | Closed 2026-07-14 as unachievable, not unbuilt. Four of five parts had shipped; the fifth (read the mute button's stable state attribute instead of the localized title) was gated on a live AX dump, and the dump refuted it: **Teams exposes no `AXValue` and no `AXIdentifier` on any element in its entire tree.** The localized `AXDescription` is the only mute signal Microsoft offers, so the shipped `MuteLabels` catalogue + cross-locale fallback is the answer, not a stopgap. Both of MIC6's goals are met anyway: locale by that fallback, UI-redesign durability by MIC10's `AXSystemDialog` anchor (a structural signal, which is the durable thing MIC6 was hunting). Teams is the only native client the daemon has ever built AX handles for (386/386 in the whole event log), so the unmeasured Zoom/Webex leg is hypothetical; re-open with the same dump if either ever becomes a real client. |
| END6 | Device-idle end corroborator | Detection | done | P1 | Closed 2026-07-14. The correlated-pair gate (the load-bearing half) shipped earlier and is now **validated in production**: same-pid restart within 3 min of an end fell from 12/82 native ends (15%, incl. the 4-fragment screen-share incident) pre-gate to zero real chops post-gate. The remaining `kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere` leg is **refuted by measurement, not deferred**: a new probe (`daemon/scripts/end6-device-idle-probe.swift`) shows our own capture pins the default input **and** the default output RUNNING for the whole recording (`AVAudioEngine.inputNode` builds a `CADefaultDeviceAggregate` spanning both directions), so no device can read idle while we record and the signal is structurally blind, in either direction. It would not have helped anyway (post-gate the AX re-walk confirms native ends at a **median 0.17 s**, with zero backstop auto-stops) and would have hurt (a device-global cross-class signal instant-promotes a provisional end, re-opening the fragmentation hole the gate closed). The unconfounded read is the per-process one: DET2 owns it. Writeup: `docs/spikes/end6-device-idle-corroborator.md`. |
| END5 | Browser/PWA end-signal hardening | Detection | done | P1 | Minutes-long end-debounce floor (120 s) for tab-title-led browser ends, so a mid-call tab switch no longer chops the recording (a returning tab reverts; a browser quit still ends promptly cross-class; idle backstop is the ceiling). START-side corroboration: `googleMeet` now needs the Meet brand beside the 3-4-3 code, and a bare brand-token browser title (webex/teams/huddle) needs live process audio before it prompts. Unblocks MIC7's build (shared extension). |
| C6-FINISH | Real detection-trace corpus | Detection | done | P1 | 20 real redacted dogfood traces landed via `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py` (all `com.microsoft.teams2` native, the only client this user records): 13 ax-leave rewalk-confirmed ends (the dominant real ending), 4 shareable-debounce ends, 3 flicker-recover fragmentation ends. Derived from the real event log (verdict sequence + leading + confirmed_by + timing preserved; titles/pids redacted), each verified green through the real engine. Needed a `confirm_provisional_end` harness pseudo-event so the AX-re-walk ending (40/70 real meetings) can replay at all. The idle-stop backstop ending (~1/3 of sessions) is out of pure-engine scope, filed as C7. **[Corrected by C7, 2026-07-28: that skipped bucket is pre-END2 engine behaviour, not the idle backstop, which never emits a `lifecycle.ended` at all. See C7's row and entry.]** |
| CAL1 | Spike: EventKit calendar context | Detection | done | P1 | Closed 2026-07-17 NO-GO, measured. Owner ran the probe: 0/55 recorded meetings had a covering calendar event, but Calendar.app is ~empty (calendar events in window ~0), so it is a **false zero**, not a coverage measurement: the owner's Outlook/Teams calendar is not connected to macOS EventKit, so an EventKit read is structurally blind to their meetings. Owner chose to keep the calendar out of macOS, so CAL1's hints (pre-title / horizon / preflight) would never fire; ADR 0011 stands, now measured (revisit note added). Probe hardened to flag an empty store so a revisit can't re-trip the false zero; doc + probe kept, revisitable if the owner ever connects the calendar. |

| AI7 | Commitments loop | Intelligence | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-23. Both halves: the Facts rail row now wears the `Needs you` amber pill carrying the count of commitments overdue or due today, so an aging promise resurfaces without opening Facts; and a recurring series' restatements of one commitment group into a single row (`restated Nx`) that resolves every instance at once. Grouping is `mp actions --cluster` (new `action_clusters.py`, the third consumer of the AI3 on-device embedder), keyed on the **workflow** as the series key (AI6's rule, no fuzzy inference) and gated so two *named* owners never merge. Conservative thresholds per embedder family, since an over-merge silently closes something unfinished. Fail-open throughout: no embedder, no `mp`, or a clamped cold model fetch all degrade to DV1's ungrouped list. The Facts aggregation moved out of `FactsView` into `Facts.swift` + `LibraryRootView` so the badge and the rows can never disagree, and the subprocess respawns only when the open-action set gains an unseen key (resolving only removes keys, so the common interaction is free). `mp actions` now arms `entry.prepare(secrets=False)` and left the entry-contract exempt list. Followed up 2026-07-24: the DV3 People rail now shares these clusters rather than aggregating its own open actions, so the two rails count one commitment the same way. |

| LOCAL11 | Warm-server identity verification | Local models | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-23. Served identity is read from the listening server's **argv** (`lsof` -> `ps` -> parse `--model` / `--adapter-path`), because the spec's `/v1/models` route cannot answer it: mlx-lm 0.31.3 lists the whole HuggingFace cache there and echoes the caller's own `model` back from `/v1/chat/completions`. `LocalSummaryClient` verifies before reusing a warm server and replaces one it owns (this process, or a LOCAL10 marker naming that pid); it never kills the daemon's warm `mp serve-local`, which `LocalModelPreloader.refresh` now restarts from `ConfigStore.didPersist` instead. Marker gained `adapter_path`. Amended by LOCAL15 (same day): the adapter half of this check was built on the belief that `--adapter-path` serves the adapter, which it does not; the adapter is now settled per request and is no longer part of the reuse comparison. The model half stands. |
| LOCAL15 | Serve the LoRA adapter that `--adapter-path` silently drops | Local models | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-23, same day it was filed, because it blocked LOCAL9's A/B. `summarization.local_adapter_path` was a no-op: mlx-lm 0.31.3 accepts `--adapter-path` and drops it (`ModelProvider.load` resolves the model alias before keying `_adapter_map` by it, so the lookup always misses; upstream #1248, fix PR #1249 unmerged). Route (a) shipped: `LocalSummaryClient` sends the documented `adapters` field in every request, which does decide the loaded weights. Measured on the 7B: **5.9 s one-time reload** on the first adapter-carrying request, 0.24 s warm after (vs A15's 8.4 s base load), so the warm-start cost is one reload per server lifetime, not per request. A bad path now fails the run naming it instead of silently serving base. Two LOCAL11 beliefs corrected in the same commit: an adapter-only difference no longer respawns a server (the respawn never worked, the fresh server got the same ignored flag), and `run.json` records the adapter the request carried. `--adapter-path` stays on the spawn command so the argv readback keeps working and upstream's fix lands for free. |
| AI5 | Spike: meeting-type classification | Intelligence | done | P3 | Resolved 2026-07-23 **NO-GO**, measured. The 2026-07-11 prototype could not be judged (2 test meetings); the library has since filled to 193, so both legs ran for real (the `--llm` leg forced onto the local backend, since the owner's config is `anthropic` and the question is about on-device labels). Two independent labellers over the same 10-type taxonomy agree on **1 of 10 types** (`standup`, 94% Jaccard) and have **zero overlap on 4** (`client`, `brainstorm`, `retro`, `interview`); overall agreement 45.6%. Root cause is the input, not the classifier: `summary["title"]` is the *summarizer's invented* title, not the calendar's, the workflow tag has 3 distinct values library-wide, and `attendees` is diarization-derived (31 meetings report 1 attendee). The local LLM does not abstain, it relabels the garbage bucket (`other` 36% to 7% by moving 36 meetings into `one_on_one`, which it assigns 48 times but on only 5 of the 13 genuinely-2-person meetings). One real defect found and fixed: the `\bsync\b` to `one_on_one` rule produced 22 of 33 one_on_one labels with 20 of them at 4-12 attendees; removing it moves agreement only 45.6% to 47.7%, which is the proof that the taxonomy, not the regex, is the problem. No schema change landed (acceptance honored). Harness kept; verdict + revisit gate (a real calendar title, blocked by CAL1) in `docs/spikes/ai5-meeting-type-classification.md`. Unblocks AI9. |

The button collapses to zero width when there is nothing to show, so a workflow's first meeting looks unchanged; switching workflows from the chip re-reads the card. `mp prep <workflow>` is the shell twin over the same two sidecars. A projection over `<stem>.meta.json` + `<stem>.summary.json`, no new file, no engine call, no egress. Bounded to the last meeting on purpose (AI10 owns the unbounded-action-list problem). CAL1's calendar-aware timing stayed closed with CAL1. |
| AI8 | Quiet meeting analytics | Intelligence | done | P3 | Shipped 2026-07-24 (P3 promoted on demand, per the P3 = promote-on-demand rule; owner invoked it by id). A fifth INSIGHTS projection, **Meeting time**: hours per workflow over a picked range (7 / 30 / 90 days, all time), each row carrying the total, the meeting count, the average length, and your share of the talking. Hours and workflow tags come free off the rows `MeetingStore` already scanned; the talk split needs the diarized `<stem>.json`, which makes this the only projection that reads transcripts (the ~80 MB DV3 was built to avoid), so the view memoizes per stem for its lifetime rather than re-parsing on every store-revision bump. Two truthfulness rules are load-bearing and tested: a meeting with no identifiable owner voice reads `not measured`, never `you 0%` (the transcript cannot tell a silent owner from a failed identification), and `speaker_unknown` speech stays out of the ratio instead of counting as somebody else; the footer names the uncovered count, and names the missing `summarization.user_label` when that is why nothing can be measured. Share is seconds-weighted across the bucket, so a two-hour call does not count like a two-minute one. Deliberately not a dashboard (no bars, trends, targets or streaks), per the 2026-06-26 UX research that kept exactly one self-directed analytic in register. 37 new tests, 1578 daemon tests green. |
| UX24 | Saved smart folders | UX | done | P3 | Shipped 2026-07-23 (promoted on demand by the owner, per the P3 = promote-on-demand rule). A `SMART FOLDERS` rail group holds the user's saved searches: `SavedSearch` (a base scope + a persisted `MeetingFilter`, with pure `capture` / `apply`), `SavedSearchStore` (one JSON array at `~/.config/meeting-pipe/saved_searches.json`), and the filter bar's `folder.badge.plus` button, which appears alongside Clear once a filter is active. A folder behaves like a built-in: own row, own count (resolved through the UX16 FTS index so the rail number matches the list), own header, rename / update-to-current-view / delete on the row. Saving from a workflow scope folds the workflow into the chip rather than pinning its id, so deleting the workflow widens the folder instead of orphaning it. Filter-bar state moved from `LibraryListView` to `LibraryRootView` (it is peer state to `scope`, and saving needs both). 1468 daemon tests green. |
| ASR3 | Batch re-transcribe ratchet | Transcription | done | P3 | Shipped 2026-07-24 (P3 promoted on demand by the owner, per the P3 = promote-on-demand rule). Library multi-select **Re-transcribe with the current stack...** plus a single-meeting twin in the detail `...` menu: a `.retranscribe` job kind runs the same FluidAudio runner over the same audio and then `mp finalize` (new subcommand: run-all's stage 1 on its own, so speaker labels, voiceprint + roster match, `<stem>.embeddings.json`, and the glossary all re-derive) and stops. No summarize, no publish, no egress; the meeting keeps the summary it has and **Re-summarize all** is offered as an explicit follow-up. The load-bearing half is `TranscriptOverlayCarry`, a pure time-anchored re-anchor of both overlay sidecars: they key on segment index and diarization cluster, which a re-transcribe re-derives from scratch, so carrying them verbatim would have put a name on a different voice. What it cannot place is dropped and counted, never guessed, and the count is reported in the UI. 1641 daemon tests + 982 pipeline tests green (landed after a rebase onto AI8 + AI9, which collided on `MeetingLibraryService`'s injected collaborators; both sides were additive and both kept). |
| PIPE10 | Sleep-resilient summarize retry | Pipeline | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-24. `_create_message` samples both clocks around the SDK call, and when an `APITimeoutError` comes back with wall-clock elapsed exceeding monotonic by >= 60 s it reads that as "the Mac slept through the request" rather than a stalled connection, logs both numbers, and re-issues once. The tenacity predicate and the transient-retry classes are untouched, so a genuine stall (clocks agree) and a second timeout still fail exactly as before; the fix lives inside the retried function rather than around it precisely so that stays true. `diarize_cleanup` calls the same seam and inherits it. The identical hole in `engine.complete_text` and the `openai` provider is left open on purpose: `auto` already absorbs the class through `backend_fallback`, and promoting a helper for a path with no observed incident would be speculative. 4 new tests pin the three paths plus the once-only bound (verified failing with the divergence check disabled); 986 pipeline tests, ruff, and pyright green. |
| AI10 | Digest action list is unbounded | Intelligence | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-24. `mp digest` no longer rolls up every open action ever (970 on the 199-meeting library, a dump rather than a review). The list is scoped to the review window (raised in it, or already overdue, or falling due inside it), which drops the dormant backlog while keeping the oldest broken promise; then grouped by workflow (meeting title when untagged), ranked overdue-by-how-overdue then soonest-due then undated with confidence and recency as tiebreaks, and capped at 25 with 5 per group. What the caps hide is counted, never carried: an `Action scope` section names the shown/window/library totals and the per-group residue, the narration facts carry the same totals so a capped list is never narrated as the total, and the digest reader links to the Facts rail for the tail. One additive schema field (`ActionItem.group`, digest-only, absent from `SUMMARY_TOOL`) carries the grouping to both renderers, pinned by a new `summary-md-golden.json` case. On a 254-open synthetic library: 10 shown across 3 groups. |
| UX26 | Resolve action items from the Summary tab | UX | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-24. Every action item in the Summary reader carries a click target: an open circle that marks it done, a filled checkmark that reopens it, with the row staying exactly where it is, struck through and dimmed, so a finished commitment reads as finished instead of rendering identically to an open one. The write is one shared helper (`MeetingLibraryService.setActionResolved`) that the Facts rail's `Mark done` now calls too, so the index guard, the atomic `MeetingSummary.jsonObject()` round-trip that preserves fields the toggle does not edit, and the `action_resolved` / `action_reopened` events have a single implementation; a cluster resolve is one call per restatement, so the log now names every summary it changed instead of only the representative. No new publish code: DSN2's needs-republish badge and the Notion checked-to-do round-trip already covered it. 1661 daemon tests green (15 new), 996 pipeline tests, ruff, pyright, and truth fences green. Owner-owed: the eyeball on a real meeting. |
| C7 | Backstop-end corpus coverage | Detection | done | P3 | Closed 2026-07-28 as an accepted gap, and the premise it was filed on does not survive measurement. The skipped "lone ax-leave `.ended`, empty `confirmed_by`" bucket is **not** the idle backstop: it is pre-END2 engine behaviour, and all 23 such sessions ended on or before 2026-06-09 while END2 landed 2026-06-10, so replaying one would assert the behaviour END2 removed. The backstop cannot be in that bucket at all, since it stops the recording rather than the lifecycle and emits `coordinator.auto_stop_silence` then `lifecycle.idle`, never a `lifecycle.ended` for a trace to assert on. Nor is it ~1/3 of endings: the shipped `IdleStopBackstop` (END3, 2026-06-10) has auto-stopped **zero** recordings in 343,620 logged events. No harness change; the misattribution was corrected in `extract_detection_trace.py`, which now names the pre-END2 bucket in `--inventory`, and in the corpus README. |
| HYG3 | Dead-code removal, 2026-07-23 sweep | Hygiene | done | P3 | Shipped 2026-07-28, promoted on demand ("P3 here means promote on demand"). All eight items removed or made true: `_numbered` / `_todo` / `reap_orphan` / `icloud_desktop_sync_enabled` / `CaptureError.bufferConversionFailed` and the two never-loaded menu-bar SVGs are gone, and each greps to zero outside this archive. `MuteLabelsValidator` was **kept and relocated into the test target**, because the evidence for the branch was already in the tests: three of the four load the *bundled* `MuteLabels.toml` via `MuteLabelsLoader.loadDefault()`, so the validator is genuinely exercised against the shipped catalogue. The nightly-VM-farm claim is gone from both doc surfaces and replaced by the real guard, unit tests on every CI push, with the gap it cannot cover (a vendor renaming a button) named rather than papered over. `RecordingEventLog` stays in Core with the reason written down: both test targets use it and two SPM test targets cannot share a file. One deviation worth naming: `reap_orphan`'s two kill-path tests were **retargeted at `terminate_server`** instead of deleted, since that function is production-live and those were its only unit coverage of the SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL escalation. |
| UX30 | Workflow delete flow | UX | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-29. All five defects close at one seam: the new `WorkflowDeletion` owns the flow, so `run(id:store:dismiss:report:)` dismisses the host **exactly once** on success, hands a refusal or a failure its reason instead of closing as though it worked, and carries the confirmation copy, the two distinct refusal reasons, and the disabled-for-default explanation. `WorkflowEditor.deleteSelf` is now three lines over it, and UX32's rail-row Delete calls the same function rather than a second copy. **Destructive affordance: promoted, not softened.** `MPDangerButtonStyle` moved out of `BatchActionsPane`'s private scope into `Preferences/PreferencesControls.swift` as `.mpDanger`, and BatchActionsPane consumes the shared one so no parallel copy exists; the promotion also picked up the DSN24 disabled dimming, press scale, and reduce-motion the private copy lacked, which matters because both consumers disable themselves (the default workflow here, a running batch there) and a destructive button that reads live while it is inert is the same class of untruth this task exists to fix. **Belt chosen: the upsert-refuses-an-unknown-id arm**, shipped as `WorkflowStore.update`. An editor only ever edits something already persisted (even the New workflow sheet, whose stub is written on open), so its Save is an update by definition and `upsert`'s append branch was only ever reachable by accident; this also covers the race UX32 adds, a rail-row delete while an editor sheet is open on the same workflow. Alert copy now says the half it omitted: recorded meetings keep their sidecar metadata but drop out of the WORKFLOWS rail and do **not** land in Untagged either (name-based resolve against an absent-name test). One addition beyond the spec: a separate `onDelete` host hook, because the New workflow sheet's commit hook routes the rail to the id it just created and after a delete that id names nothing. 1793 daemon tests green (12 new), `swift build` clean, truth fences green. **Owner eyeball owed:** the control reading as destructive, confirming closing the sheet, and Return afterwards not bringing the workflow back. |
| UX31 | One name per summarization engine | UX | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-29. Every backend now carries one name in each register, out of a single pure type (`SummarizationEngine`, keyed off the raw backend id): the bare name for the two pickers, `Name (where it runs)` for the provenance line / Re-summarize menu / workflow inspector, and one present-tense phrase for the busy chip and Ask's footer. Eight sites read it and none keeps a literal; the spec listed seven, and the eighth was the Summary tab's failed-summary recovery button, which said "Re-summarize with Local" while the header menu said "Local (on-device)" for the same run. The reported defect is gone (header and `...` menu both say `Anthropic (cloud)`), and the coverage half with it: `claude_cli` and `openai` are named everywhere, so an OpenAI meeting renders an engine row instead of dropping it and Ask no longer prints a raw config id (an id this build cannot name now hides the line, matching what the provenance row always did). Verified end to end before adding them to the **workflow** picker, which the spec had left conditional: pinning either works with no pipeline change (`apply_overrides` validates against `config.BACKENDS`, which has held both since PROV1, and `summarize._build_client` dispatches both), and `effective_backend` classes both as cloud so a regulated / NDA run still forces local. `WorkflowBackend` gained the two matching cases. 1816 daemon tests green (18 new), `swift build` clean, CI5 truth fences green, no pipeline change; those numbers are after rebasing onto UX35 and UX30, which landed while this ran. |
| UX34 | Fixed-size detection prompt: cap the Last time card | UX | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-29. The "Last time" disclosure had no ceiling, so a full 3+3 card grew the prompt from 64pt to ~260pt and covered the top of the meeting window it hovers above. The card now lives in an `NSScrollView` whose viewport is clamped to `PrepCardView.maxCardHeight`, with the overflow scrolling instead of the panel growing: a full card holds the panel at 187pt. The cap is **derived from the card's own row metrics**, not a round number, at title + a full `PrepCard.maxPoints` recap + the OPEN ACTIONS eyebrow + half an action row (123pt). The half row is the affordance: macOS overlay scrollers are invisible at rest, so a viewport ending on a row boundary would read as the end of the card. A pure `PrepDisclosure.decide` owns the arithmetic for both the toggle and the build site. Collapsed state, top-edge anchoring, reduce-motion, and the 600x64 pill are untouched. 1821 daemon tests green (5 new); that number is after rebasing onto UX35, UX30 and UX31, which landed while this ran. Owner-owed: the eyeball on a real full card. |
| UX32 | Workflow row quick actions | UX | done | P2 | Shipped 2026-07-29, on top of UX30 the same day. The `WORKFLOWS` rail row now carries its own **Edit workflow…** and **Delete workflow…**, on right-click and on a trailing swipe, so removing a duplicate is one gesture instead of the five-step walk through the editor sheet that WF9's cleanup pays per row. **Both entry points are calls, not copies:** `LibraryRootView.deleteWorkflowFromRail` confirms with `WorkflowDeletion`'s own strings (through a new `LibraryDialogs.confirmWorkflowDelete`, the AppKit idiom the Library's Move-to-Trash already uses) and then runs `WorkflowDeletion.run`, the same function `WorkflowEditor.deleteSelf` runs; a source fence asserts the set of files calling `run(` is exactly those two, so a third surface growing its own delete path fails the suite. The rail's `dismiss` is not a no-op: it clears a scope still pointed at the deleted workflow, which would otherwise render an empty list explaining nothing. New pure `WorkflowRowActions` holds what the row offers and whether Delete is live, pre-flighted with `WorkflowDeletion.refusalReason` verbatim rather than a second is-this-the-default read, and shared by both surfaces so one cannot go live while the other is disabled. Full swipe is off (a destructive action must not fire on momentum), and the swipe Delete is tinted `.mpDanger` rather than system red. **One judgement call:** a swipe action has no caption slot, so on the default workflow the disabled swipe Delete carries its reason in the accessibility label only, while the context menu shows the sentence as a caption row under the item (the UX29 shape). 1839 daemon tests green (10 new), `swift build` clean, truth fences green, dash guard green (measured after rebasing onto UX31 / UX34 / UX36). **The swipe half shipped on analysis, not observation:** `.noNativeListSelection()` was read and judged harmless to row actions (it only sets `selectionHighlightStyle = .none` on the backing `NSTableView`, a highlight-drawing property), but no swipe was ever performed, because rendering cannot be reached from a build or a unit test. **Owner eyeball owed:** that swipe actions render and fire at all, plus the right-click path on a duplicate "Client work" row. Owner's call on 2026-07-29 was to keep this `done` and open a fresh session if the swipe turns out not to work. |
| UX47 | Prep card wraps its lines, admits its scroll, and stays inside its panel | UX | done | P2 | **Shipped 2026-07-30, same day as filed: 1886 daemon tests green (4 new), truth fences green.** The document is constraint-hosted with width pinned to the clip, which is the whole bug fix; the promoted probe is now the regression fence, measuring alignment rects (constraints position those, and a label's frame sits ~2pt wider, which tripped the fence's first draft at 590 vs 588 before it measured the right rect). Points and actions wrap via `NSTextField(wrappingLabelWithString:)` at a measured `layout(for:width:)` whose self-calibrating row gap keeps every short-card height byte-identical, so the UX34 clamp arithmetic and its tests stood untouched; the action ring pins to the first baseline so a wrapped action does not float its ring between lines. The fade is a gradient mask on the clip (the panel is vibrant material, so an overlay had no color to paint), driven by pure `PrepDisclosure.fadeVisible` at the 2pt-slop bottom rule, torn down with the scroll at all three teardown sites; the scroller flashes once on expand. **Owner confirmed the eyeball 2026-07-30 ("okay, looks good"), so the row is closed.** **Follow-up the same day, after the owner rebuilt: the fade mask did not scroll with the card.** The wrap landed but the card read empty below its second bullet with the eyebrow and actions unreachable. A second probe cleared the arithmetic first (205pt computed, lowest label at 191, slack exactly the 12pt pad plus rounding), then found `grad.frame = CGRect(origin: .zero, ...)`: a `CALayer` mask is positioned in its layer's coordinate space and an `NSClipView`'s bounds *origin* is what moves when its document scrolls, so the mask stayed over the first screenful and everything past it had no coverage and composited transparent. The numbers predict the screenshot exactly (scrolled 82 into 205 with a 123 viewport: visible only in bounds 82-123, eyebrow at 142 and action at 159 masked to nothing, fade band 97-123 explaining the dissolved second line). Fix is `clip.bounds` whole; gradient stops lifted into pure `fadeLocations(viewport:fadeHeight:)` so the band stays 26pt rather than a viewport proportion, clamped into [0,1]. The new source fence initially failed against correct code because the comment beside it quotes the banned literal, so it strips comments now; mutation-verified both ways, the second fence in this task to be wrong about its own subject. 1888 tests green. **Owner-owed:** the next real prompt: bullets read as complete sentences, the fade reads as "continues", scrolling reveals the eyebrow and actions, the date sits top right. **Provenance, from the filing:** owner's live prompt screenshot, 2026-07-30 (bullets sliced mid-glyph at the panel edge, no wrap, scroll invisible); a layout probe separated one bug from two design calls: as UX34's "frame-sized, not constrained" scroll document, the card's width is dictated by content under the layout engine, so it laid out **879pt wide in a 600pt panel** (long bullets at maxX 773/867/823 against a 588 inset; the right-aligned date at maxX 867, off-panel since UX34 landed; the configured tail-ellipsis never fired because nothing compressed). The design calls: CAL2's recorded single-line-truncation trade, overruled by the owner's eyeball ("I was thinking text will be line breaked"); and UX34's half-row-slice scroll cue, which the owner read as broken, not inviting. Design run 2026-07-30 offered wrap+fade / two-line clamp / honest ellipsis; **owner picked wrap+fade**. Scope: pin the document width to the clip (fix shared by any option), wrap points and actions at a measured width with the one-arithmetic-pass invariant kept (self-calibrating row gap keeps short-card heights byte-identical), keep the 123pt viewport, fade the bottom edge while content continues below (gradient mask so the vibrant material shows through), flash the scroller on expand. Two-line clamp is the pre-agreed one-constant retreat if wrapped scrolling annoys. CAL3 owns the card's content; this is its form. |
| UX36 | Summary reading measure: centered column, honest padding, wrapping metadata | UX | done | P2 | **Closed 2026-07-30: owner confirmed the eyeball ("UX36 - good to close") after the fourth pass put both reading tabs on the shared 720 measure.** Shipped 2026-07-29, 1829 daemon tests green (8 new), no pipeline change, dash guard and truth fences clean. All four cap sites in the three summary surfaces now read `.frame(maxWidth: readingMeasure).padding(contentPadding).frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .top)`: gutter outside the cap, block centered. That alone **widens the real text column from 600 to 640** without touching the constant, which matters because 600 was below the 640-720 reading column `design/README.md` asks for, so the app had drifted under its own design system. `SummaryLayout` gained `contentPadding` and `noticeMeasure = 460`, the latter absorbing the two stray literals (which are in the failed state, not the "BYO/preview area" the spec guessed); the acceptance grep comes back empty. **Band pick, recorded: `ViewThatFits` with a two-line fallback, not trailing truncation.** Truncation must sacrifice either the owner (the most load-bearing fact on an action, and a clipped name can mean two people) or the anchors (the row's only route back to the recording); in an already-scrolling pane a second line is free, so height is the cheaper currency than width, and the split at owner/due/confidence over anchors converges on the shape the bullet rows already use. `MeetingRow`'s deliberate removal of `ViewThatFits` does not apply: that was NSTableView caching row heights inside a `List`, and these rows sit in a `LazyVStack`. **Not in the spec:** the band's guard read `action.evidence` while `EvidenceChips` draws nothing without an `onCite`, so a static surface (digest reader, A16 compare pane) with anchors and no chips opened an empty indented band; a new `shownEvidence` closes it. The inline editor's `contentPadding: 16` became the shared 20 (its squeeze rationale was about the old inside-the-cap geometry, and 4pt a side of drift across the edit crossfade is what the shared constant exists to prevent). The A16 compare pane's headings moved onto the measure too, since centering the columns they label would otherwise strand them a whole margin away. **Deliberately not done:** the optional 640 to 680 nudge, which the spec marks owner-eyeball-gated and a headless session cannot judge; a test pins the measure inside 640-720 so it stays safe to apply. **Second pass 2026-07-29, after the owner's eyeball came back negative ("still pretty much ugly, this time less readable"), mocked at true scale and approved before any code.** The eyeball caught the relationship, not the measure: the body centred while the header sat at the pane edge and the tabs spread the full pane, so the first 200pt offered three left edges and none was the prose's. Underneath it was a real bug, `tabButton` drew the active underline as a bare `Rectangle` **sibling** in a `VStack`, and a `Rectangle` is a greedy shape, so it took every point offered, the stack inherited that, and three greedy buttons divided the pane while the trailing `Spacer` got nothing; the strip read left-aligned in source and shipped spread across 1230pt with an underline several times wider than its word. Now an `.overlay(alignment: .bottom)` on the label, and a `daemon/CLAUDE.md` "Don't", since only the render was wrong. **Owner decision, recorded: header + tab strip are chrome, not content**, banded on `bgRaised` across the pane with the existing Divider as its edge. Putting them on the reading column was traced and rejected because `TranscriptTab`/`AudioTab` are full-width and uncapped, so a measured header aligns on Summary and misaligns on the other two; the two alternatives offered (one ~1000pt column for the whole pane, which would have narrowed this spec's own no-max-on-the-detail-panel rejection, and left-parking everything, which returns the dead space this task was filed for) were declined. **The pre-registered nudge was taken, 640 to 680**, inside the 640-720 `design/README.md` band, which the first pass's range test allowed without an edit; 720 was rejected for prose (near 100 characters a line) even though the inspector card is 720, because a label/value grid is not prose. 1877 daemon tests green, 2 new fences. **Third pass 2026-07-29, after the owner rebuilt and marked both remaining gutters on a screenshot.** Two findings. The band was **invisible**: `bgRaised` over `bg` in dark is an 11-of-255 step, unfindable in a screenshot even knowing where to look, and a band nobody sees licenses nothing, so it now recesses to `bgSunk` with a `borderStrong` hairline (the direction mac toolbars take, and it frees `bgRaised` to mean content). And the **axis jumped between tabs**, which was my error in proposing the band: I traced whether a measured header would fight the transcript, but never whether the two tab bodies would fight each other, and they did (summary centred at 275, transcript full-width at 16, so changing tab slid the body 259pt under a fixed header). Owner's counter beat my proposed inner page card: a 940 page inside a 1372 pane only relocates the gutters, so **cap the column instead**. That reverses this spec's own first-pass rejection of a detail max, correctly: the rejection assumed the other tabs used the width, and the transcript was running past 1300pt unmeasured, which is not use. `LibraryLayout.detailMaxWidth = 1040` as the column's `max:`; split-view columns always fill, so slack goes to the meeting list where titles truncate today (~460 to ~740 on the owner's window) and the detail pane carries **zero** gutters there. With the pane bounded, all five cap sites drop their centring and every tab's content shares one left edge; UX33's 720 card is now nearly redundant inside it. Verified there is exactly **one** three-panel UI (one `NavigationSplitView`), so the cap has one home; Preferences is a separate fixed two-panel split; all five detail contents suit a cap, including `DigestReaderView`, which is prose despite its comment claiming it wants full width. 1882 tests green, 3 new (2 arithmetic invariants, 1 source fence). **A fence that could not fail, caught by mutation:** the left-align fence first used a 320-char window that the intervening comment overran, so it ran zero assertions and passed against a mutated file; it now anchors on the next placement frame and asserts `checked > 0`. **Fourth pass 2026-07-29: the transcript joins the measure, shared at 720 (option A of the reading-width run, owner-picked).** The third pass's left seam closed and the owner's next look found the right one: summary text ended at 700, transcript ran to ~1024, a ~300pt right-edge jump per tab change. Three options were mocked at the shipped cap (one measure; screenplay gutter, rejected for re-breaking the left axis; reference rail, rejected for dashboard drift) and A won: `TranscriptTab`'s reading zone (cast strip + captions + rows) rides the identical `SummaryLayout.readingMeasure` stack with the internal horizontal 16s stripped so the stack's gutter is the only inset, while the transport, empty state, and Audio stay full-bleed (instruments and states, not reading content). Measure 680 to 720 by owner pick, the top of the design band; it now equals the inspector card's 720, a deliberate convergence recorded in the inspector test whose strictly-greater assertion asked "if these converge, say why" (relaxed to greater-or-equal, keeping the half that matters: a grid never narrower than prose). 1882 tests green; the left-align fence now scans `TranscriptTab.swift` too. **Owner-owed:** rebuild and read one meeting across both tabs, right edge at 740 on each, cast chips starting on the rows' text edge, 720 prose comfortable; the single knob left is `detailMaxWidth` if the trailing margin bothers the eye, at the cost of Audio's waveform width. |
| UX35 | HUD width parity between recording and off-the-record | UX | done | P2 | **Closed 2026-07-30: owner confirmed the eyeball ("UX35 good to close"); the 96pt pill reads right in both states.** Shipped 2026-07-29, 1786 daemon tests green (5 new), no pipeline change. One shared `HUDPanelGeometry.pillWidth = 96` for both states, and `setOffTheRecord` no longer resizes at all; the degraded 232pt card is untouched and is now the only resize the HUD performs. The width is **measured, not eyeballed**: the dot row (dot 8 + gap 5 + the state label at 10pt medium) lays out at 64pt for "Recording" and 83pt for "Off the record", so the filed ~89pt was an over-estimate and 96 is 83 plus the same 12pt of side margin 76 gave "Recording", rounded onto the 4pt grid. The decision moved onto a pure `HUDPanelGeometry` in the house decide style, so "off-the-record never resizes the panel" is a unit test rather than a code-reading exercise, and the constant is the single place UX46 widens for its third control (that row goes 68pt -> 102pt, so it will need to). It also closes a latent bug nobody filed: `clearSystemAudioDegraded` resized to the recording-only 76 unconditionally, so a successful system-audio retry during an open span shrank the pill under a label it could no longer fit. **Owner-owed:** the eyeball the spec reserved, which is load-bearing here because the spec asked for a width picked by eye against the real label and this one came off a measurement instead. Start a recording, toggle off the record and back, confirm the pill does not move in either direction, both labels fit, and the workflow name still truncates gracefully. Needs one `./scripts/rebuild.sh`. |

## Task specs

**[DONE] C7 (P3): backstop-end corpus coverage.** Band origin: carried from Q4 with a changed scope. C6-FINISH (below) landed 20 real redacted dogfood traces (`teams_native_*` in the detection corpus) covering the three pure-engine endings real sessions actually produce, derived by `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py`. The one real ending the corpus structurally cannot cover is the idle-stop backstop: a lone ax-leave `.ended` with empty `confirmed_by` (~1/3 of real sessions) is force-ended by `IdleStopBackstop`, not `PromotionEngine`, and the replay harness (`DetectionCorpusTests`) only drives the pure engine. Scope: either extend the harness with a backstop-level end driver so a real backstop session replays, or accept the gap explicitly and close. Not correctness (the backstop path works in production); this is test-coverage completeness, so it pairs with T3.

**Closed 2026-07-28 as the spec's second option, an accepted gap, and the premise it was filed on does not survive measurement.** Owner-promoted for this batch (the P3 band's promote-on-demand rule). Three findings, each measured against the real 343,620-event log (`events.jsonl` plus its rotation) rather than argued:

1. **The skipped bucket is pre-END2 history, not the backstop.** `--inventory` reports 100 armed sessions, 73 reproducible, 27 skipped, of which 23 are the "lone ax-leave `.ended`, empty `confirmed_by`" shape. Every one of those 23 ended **on or before 2026-06-09**, and END2 landed **2026-06-10** (`0726ae8a`). They are the pre-END2 engine promoting a lone ax-leave on the debounce alone, which is precisely what END2 removed: `tick()` now returns nil for a `requiresCorroboration` lead with no cross-class corroborator, pinned by `PromotionEngineTests.test_lone_ax_leave_invalid_does_not_promote_to_ended_after_debounce`. The extractor is right to skip them, and a corpus trace built from one would assert the defect END2 fixed.
2. **The idle backstop cannot be in that bucket, or in any bucket.** It force-stops the recording, not the lifecycle: `IdleStopBackstop.onAutoStop` reaches `MeetingSessionController.handleIdleAutoStop`, which stops the recorder and disengages. The log shows `coordinator.auto_stop_silence`, then `lifecycle.idle`, then `coordinator.recording_stopped`, and never a `lifecycle.ended`. `sessionize()` closes a session on `lifecycle.ended`, so a backstop-ended meeting is not even a session in the inventory. It was never the thing being skipped.
3. **It is not ~1/3 of endings either; it is zero.** In the whole log: 2 `auto_stop_silence` (2026-05-22, 2026-06-03) and 3 `force_stop reason=mic_only_silence` (2026-06-08 to 2026-06-10), all of them the retired `SilenceDetector` / `MicOnlySilenceBackstop`, since the shipped `IdleStopBackstop` landed 2026-06-10 (`50220154`) and the `auto_stop_silence` action name predates it (TECH-C2, 2026-05-11). Since END3 the shipped backstop has fired 3 nudges and 1 stand-down (`auto_stop_silence_skipped`, lifecycle still live) and **zero** auto-stops. The "~1/3" was the size of the skip bucket, correctly counted and wrongly attributed, and it also reached project memory as "~1/3 of real ends are idle-backstop", which is wrong the same way.

**Why option (a) was not honestly available**, since "accept the gap" must not become a way of avoiding work. There is no real backstop session to replay, and the log cannot yield one even for the two pre-END3 auto-stops: the backstop's second input, `hasSystemAudio` (derived from `latestSystemLevelDb`, sampled at ~1 Hz in `armSystemLevelMirror`), is never logged, so the idle streak's start time is unrecoverable. Worked example, 2026-06-03: the last logged verdict transition sits 302 s before the auto-stop while the nudge lands 210 s before it, so the streak start has to be guessed at an unlogged system-audio drop. And 2026-05-22 logs two nudges 123 s apart with a `hot` verdict between them, which today's `IdleStopBackstop` cannot reproduce at all (a `hot(rmsAboveOpenThreshold)` clears `didNotify`), confirming those two sessions characterize the retired unit rather than the shipped one. What remained was a synthetic trace re-asserting what `IdleStopBackstopTests` already asserts, behind a new trace schema and loader: coverage theatre, so it was not built.

**What changed.** The false attribution lived in code, in the tool that produced this task, so it was corrected at the source rather than only in prose. `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py` now splits the pre-END2 bucket into its own skip reason (`pre-END2 lone ax_leave_button_invalid end; the engine no longer promotes this`), so an `--inventory` run cannot be read as a coverage gap again, and its docstring records both why replaying one would be wrong and why the backstop never appears there. The corpus `README.md`'s "Coverage reality" section carries the same correction, and C6-FINISH's row and ship note point at it. No harness change, no fixture added, no behaviour touched: `INDEX.json` still lists all 29 traces and they still replay green.

**Coverage, the T3 pairing.** T3's baseline is unmoved by design (no test added, no source line newly exercised), so its table needed no line. The backstop is not an untested surface in the first place: `IdleStopBackstopTests` covers both horizons, the sticky auto-stop, the END7 clock-fed re-ingestion, `safeNotifySeconds` across the whole configurable range, the VAD and system-audio resets, the MIC5 confidently-unmuted floor, and reset / keepAlive; `SilenceAutoStopPolicyTests` covers the stand-down, which is the one real post-END3 backstop event in the log. The honest statement of the residual gap: **no end-to-end replay of a backstop-ended meeting exists, and none can be derived until the daemon logs the backstop's inputs**. What would change that is a one-line-per-streak event carrying the streak start and the `hasSystemAudio` mirror, at which point a real trace becomes derivable; that is a detection-instrumentation task, not a corpus one, and it is not worth filing while the shipped backstop's real-world fire count is zero.

**[DONE] ASR3 (P3): batch re-transcribe ratchet.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Transcripts are frozen at capture-time ASR quality: glossary normalization does not touch old transcripts, roster names learned later never reach them, and Reprocess/Regenerate re-run summarize only; STOR1 chose FLAC over AAC precisely so later reprocessing stays possible. A Library multi-select "Re-transcribe with current stack" (confirmation, progress, overlays and corrections preserved via the existing sidecar patterns, re-summarize offered after). Acceptance: an old meeting re-transcribed picks up the current glossary and roster and its overlays survive.

**Shipped 2026-07-24.** Promoted on demand by the owner (the priority-band note allows P3 work when a task explicitly promotes it, and the 2026-07-12 assessment band records "P3 here means promote on demand"). Four new files: `pipeline/src/mp/finalize.py`, `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/TranscriptOverlayCarry.swift`, and a test each.

**The re-transcribe is transcribe + finalize, not a full `run-all`, and that is the one real deviation from how the spec reads.** The spec's "re-summarize offered after" already implied the re-transcribe itself does not summarize, and the reasons to hold that line are concrete: a batch over an old library would otherwise pay for a cloud summary per meeting that nobody asked for, and would rewrite every Notion page as a side effect of an ASR fix. Running finalize alone also means the meeting keeps its existing summary throughout, so it is never summary-less mid-run. That needed the finalize stage callable on its own, hence `mp finalize <wav>`: `run-all`'s three finalize helpers (`existing_daemon_transcript`, `finalize_streamed_transcript`, `apply_glossary`) were promoted from module-private to public and re-used rather than copied, per the pipeline rule that a module-private copy of a sibling's helper is the worst of the three options. The command arms `entry.prepare(secrets=False)`, so it is local by construction.

The job goes through `SinkDispatcher`'s existing queue as a new `ProcessingJobKind.retranscribe` rather than spawning beside it, so a 20-meeting batch cannot put a second transcription on the Neural Engine while a live recording's own job is running, and the menu-bar processing badge counts it like any other work. `PipelineJobDispatcher` suppresses the done/error routing for that kind: a re-transcribe published nothing, so "meeting published" would be false and would fire once per meeting in a batch, and the Library surface that asked for it already shows its own progress and result. Two failure paths, deliberately different: a failed **transcription** writes no failure sidecar, because nothing was written and marking a healthy meeting failed would be a lie; a failed **finalize** does, because at that point `<stem>.json` is the raw un-relabelled FluidAudio sidecar and the row should offer Reprocess.

**"Overlays survive" could not be taken literally, and that is the finding worth keeping.** `TranscriptCorrectionStore` and the per-segment half of `SpeakerLabelStore` key on the segment's array index; the cluster half keys on the diarization label. A re-transcribe re-derives all three. So the sidecars as they stand describe a transcript that no longer exists, and keeping them would land a name on a different voice or an edit on a different sentence, which is worse than losing them. `TranscriptOverlayCarry` (pure, 17 tests) re-anchors on time instead, the one thing both transcripts agree about because they describe the same audio: a new segment inherits an old one's overrides when it covers at least half the old span, and a new cluster inherits a name when it owns a **strict** majority of that cluster's speaking time. Both mappings are injective (one segment has one speaker and one edited text), collisions resolve to the larger overlap with a deterministic label tiebreak, and everything that fails to anchor is dropped and counted. The strictness on the cluster side is the interesting one: a cluster the new diarization split exactly in half has no majority owner, and naming one half anyway would be a coin flip wearing a deterministic tiebreak. Leaving it unnamed is a prompt to name it; naming it wrong asserts a person who was not in the room. Two smaller rules fell out: a carried correction re-snapshots `original_text` against the new transcript so the sidecar keeps meaning "this is what the pipeline produced", and a correction the new transcript already satisfies (because the glossary now fixes it) is **retired** rather than carried, matching `upsert`'s existing no-op rule. Carried / retired / dropped counts surface in both UIs, so a lost edit is something the owner is told about rather than discovers later.

De-CLI: the multi-select affordance the spec asked for shipped as a "Transcript" card in `BatchActionsPane` (confirmation alert naming what is replaced, the shared progress strip, a result line, and the **Re-summarize all** follow-up reusing `regenerateMeeting`), plus a single-meeting **Re-transcribe...** in the detail `...` menu, because wanting to re-transcribe exactly one meeting is the common case and the pane only appears above a multi-selection.

Docs corrected in the same commit rather than only added to: README claimed transcript files "are written once and not modified" (a troubleshooting answer that is now wrong) and that re-normalizing an old transcript with the glossary "is an explicit regenerate" (it never was; regenerate only re-runs summarize). Both now point at Re-transcribe.

**[DONE] UX24 (P3): saved smart folders.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. PRODUCT.md already names the rail "the Library's smart-folder rail" and UX16 shipped a real FTS5 query engine with filter chips, but scopes were a fixed enum; let a query + chip combination be saved as a named rail scope. Acceptance: a saved search persists across restarts and behaves like a built-in scope.

**Shipped 2026-07-23.** Promoted on demand by the owner (the 2026-07-12 assessment band records "P3 here means promote on demand, per the AI5/I8 precedent", and the priority-band note allows P3 work when a task explicitly promotes it). Three new files. `SavedSearch.swift` is the pure value: an id, a name, a `Base` (the saveable subset of `LibraryScope`, its raw values being case names rather than display labels so a rail row can be reworded without orphaning a folder), a `MeetingFilter`, and a rail `order`; plus `capture` (folds the view on screen into a folder) and `apply` (base scope narrows, then the chips + query, the same order the live rail + filter bar use). `SavedSearchStore.swift` persists the set as one JSON array at `~/.config/meeting-pipe/saved_searches.json`, deliberately the `ConsentStore` single-file idiom rather than `WorkflowStore`'s file-per-item TOML (a folder is daemon UI state, the pipeline never reads it, and any edit rewrites the whole set), writing atomically off the main queue and loaded by the Coordinator rather than in `init` so a headless model never touches the real file. `SavedSearchViews.swift` holds the pure criteria summary (the row tooltip / VoiceOver hint / save-sheet preview) and the one-field naming sheet.

Three decisions worth recording. **The base scope is saved, not just the chips.** The spec said "a query + chip combination", but the chips cannot express `Needs you`, `NDA only`, or `Untagged`, so saving from one of those rails would have silently widened the folder relative to what the user was looking at. Saving the base too costs one enum field. **A workflow scope folds into the workflow chip** (base becomes `.allMeetings`) rather than pinning `Workflow.ID`, so deleting a workflow later widens the folder instead of leaving an unresolvable scope; the chip cannot already disagree with the scope, because `MeetingFacets` are built from the scoped meetings. **`LibraryScope.saved` returns `false` from `includes`**, like the `.facts` / `.ask` / `.digests` projections, because a folder is a base scope plus a filter whose free-text arm needs the FTS index that a pure predicate cannot reach; the two call sites that need it (`LibraryListView.recomputeDerived`, `ScopeCounts.build`) resolve through the store and call `SavedSearch.apply`. `ScopeCounts.build` gained an `ftsMatches` resolver (defaulted to nil, so every existing caller and test drives it unchanged) specifically so a folder's rail count matches what selecting it shows.

One refactor rode along: the filter-bar state moved from `LibraryListView`'s `@State` to `LibraryRootView`, which already owns `scope`. They are peer state and saving a folder needs both; the alternative was passing the filter up through a callback purely to work around its location. Behaviour is unchanged (it still persists across scope switches). De-CLI: the capability is entirely in-app, no CLI surface. Verified: 1468 daemon tests green (35 new across `SavedSearchTests` + `SavedSearchStoreTests`, including the acceptance case of a second store over the same path reading back what the first wrote), `swift build` clean, truth fences + em-dash + design-token guards clean. Docs synced: README (rail group + a saving-a-search paragraph), ARCHITECTURE (Library UI files, storage list, on-disk table, Smart folders glossary entry), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` quick index, and the `design/ui_kits/macos_app` rail + filter-bar recreation. CONVENTIONS deliberately untouched: no event category, no config key, no sidecar, and no cross-tree contract (the file is daemon-only, so it belongs in ARCHITECTURE's disk table beside `auto_consent.json`, not in the Swift-to-Python schema section). Owner-owed: the standard rendered eyeball on the menu-bar app (not screenshotable here), specifically that the rail group reads right at the owner's workflow count and that the save button is discoverable where it sits.

**[DONE] UX23 (P2): shipped-surface floor.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Rough edges on recently shipped surfaces the owner had not yet e2e-tested, each violating a written floor or the quiet-register promise. (a) The prompt panel was mouse-only: `PromptRecordButton` was a bare `NSControl` with no `acceptsFirstResponder`/`keyDown`, and the close/chevron controls had no accessibility labels, contradicting PRODUCT.md's keyboard + VoiceOver floor. (b) Off-the-record (MIC14) was undiscoverable: no Preferences hotkey row (the other three had one), no HUD affordance. (c) Search could not distinguish "index still building" from "SQLite failed to open" (`SearchIndexer.matchingStems` returned nil for both and degraded silently forever). (d) `DigestsView`'s Generate discarded its `Result`, so a failed `mp digest` rendered nothing. (e) The status-bar permission warning did not name consequences. Acceptance: prompt panel keyboard-operable with labels; the off-record hotkey visible and editable; QuickFind shows an indexing hint and doctor reports index health; a failed digest shows why; the permission warning names the consequence.

**Shipped 2026-07-22; (b)-(e) landed, (a) reverted and deferred to UX27 (P4).** (b) A fourth "Off the record" row in Preferences ▸ General ▸ Hotkeys binds the existing `off_the_record_hotkey`, and the recording HUD gained a mic-slash toggle beside Stop (one-click, plus a right-click "Go / Back on the record" menu, VoiceOver-labelled, coral while a span is open) routed through the same `session.toggleOffTheRecord()` the hotkey uses. (c) `SearchIndexer` publishes a `building`/`ready`/`degraded` `Health` (degraded is terminal: SQLite/FTS5 could not open); a shared `searchHint` one-liner shows in the Library filter bar and Quick Find while building or degraded, a new `search.index` doctor probe reports index reachability + count, and `SearchIndex` gained a `busy_timeout` so the probe's second connection cannot false-fail against a live reindex. (d) `DigestsView.generate` now captures the `Result` into an inline warning-triangle error row (the AskView.errorState vocabulary). (e) `StatusBarModel` replaced the single "Permissions need attention" row with per-permission consequence rows ("Microphone off: recordings will be silent", "Screen Recording off: the call audio will not be recorded (mic only)"); Accessibility kept its existing dedicated degraded row. (a) The prompt-panel + recording-HUD keyboard/Tab/focus-ring operability was built (key-capable `NSPanel` subclasses, per-control `keyDown` + focus rings + `nextKeyView` tab order, and a click-to-focus that forced `makeKey` + `NSApp.activate` on empty-area clicks) and reverted: the floating panels never reliably became the key window in the accessory menu-bar app, so no focus ring appeared and Tab did nothing in owner testing, and the owner did not need it. Deferred to UX27 (P4), which records the finding and the reverted approach; the VoiceOver labels added to the close/chevron/off-record controls were kept. Verified: 1416 daemon tests green (StatusBarModel consequences, SearchIndexer degraded-init + hint, the `search.index` probe), `swift build` debug + release clean, ruff + truth-fences clean. Docs synced (README permission-warning copy + off-record row/HUD toggle + doctor probe list; ARCHITECTURE per-permission warnings + doctor probe + search-index health). Owner-owed nothing beyond the standard rendered-eyeball on the menu-bar surfaces.

**[DONE] UX21 (P2): first-run traps.** (a) Startup fires all four TCC dialogs before the OnboardingGate branch (`App.swift:148` runs `coordinator.start()` first; `Coordinator.swift:367` calls `requestPermissionsAtStartup()` ungated), so a fresh install gets four unframed system dialogs stacked over the onboarding window and the framed permissions step arrives pre-answered; gate the burst on onboarding completion and have completion trigger prewarm + refreshAll. (b) A local/NDA workflow never prefetches the MLX model: `ConfigRefreshCoordinator.swift:30-35` keys only on the global backend, and the onboarding "Client work (NDA)" preset creates backend .local with no cache check, so the first NDA meeting records fine and then summarize fails with a terminal-command remedy (a de-CLI violation in an error path); on workflow save and the preset, when the resolved backend is local and the model is uncached, show an inline "model not downloaded (~4.3 GB), Download now" affordance reusing ModelDownloadSupervisor, and teach doctor's local-stack check to also run when any workflow carries backend=local or nda_mode. (c) Onboarding's Accessibility row gives no relaunch hint even though the product's own model requires one (the Preferences footnote documents it). Acceptance: a fresh install shows zero TCC dialogs before onboarding's permissions step; saving a local-backend workflow with no cached model offers the download inline and the failed-row remedy is in-app; the AX row explains the relaunch.

**[DONE] UX22 (P2): setup completion surfaces.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. There are four setup surfaces (terminal installer, README, Preferences, onboarding) and no in-app answer to "am I done?". Scope: a menu-bar "Finish setup" row, styled like the existing permissions-attention row, listing unmet items (key missing, database_id empty, permission missing, model uncached), each deep-linking to its fix, computed from what doctor already knows; an onboarding publish-target step (Keychain key status + the existing NotionDatabaseList picker + a small test publish), closing the last terminal/TOML dependency between install and first value; reuse that picker in Preferences Integrations (the raw Database ID field stays as the fallback); rewrite install.sh's completion epilogue as a numbered mirror of what first launch actually does, and symlink the mp launcher into `~/.local/bin` so README's bare `mp` examples work as written. Acceptance: clean-Mac first value requires no hand-edited TOML and no terminal beyond install.sh; the checklist row disappears when everything is green; `mp doctor` runs from a fresh shell. Shipped with two owner-approved deviations: the onboarding "small test publish" is a read-only "Verify connection" (a `GET /v1/databases/{id}`, the same probe `mp doctor` runs) so no page is created and the daemon stays read-only (writes remain the pipeline's job); and `mp` is symlinked into `~/.local/bin` without editing the shell profile, so bare `mp` works once `~/.local/bin` is on PATH rather than being forced there. Both follow the owner's UI-first steer (the CLI is a debugging / owner-dev escape hatch, not a user surface and not removed): every user-facing capability has an in-app home, so the checklist's model item triggers the in-app download and `SETUP.md`'s doctor step leads with Preferences -> Run doctor.

**Shipped 2026-07-22, owner-eyeballed.**

**(a) The burst is gated, not deferred.** `Coordinator.start()` now calls `requestPermissionsAtStartup()` only when `OnboardingGate.isCompleted`, so a fresh install never stacks the four unframed dialogs over the Welcome window; the framed permissions step is the sole prompt surface. A new `Coordinator.onboardingDidComplete()` runs in the burst's place on completion or skip (wired through a new `OnboardingDependencies.onFinish`): `SystemAudioCapture.prewarm()` so the first recording never re-prompts Screen Recording, plus `PermissionsCenter.refreshAll()` + a menu-bar refresh. It is prewarm + refresh, not a re-fired burst, because the framed step already requested each permission one at a time.

**(b) A deliberate download, not a silent 4 GB pull.** The spec's remedy is a *manual* "Download now", kept manual on purpose: silently prefetching ~4.3 GB because someone picked an NDA preset would fight the quiet/deliberate personality, so the daemon's existing eager (global-backend-keyed) prefetch was left untouched and a new `LocalModelPreflight` seam on `ConfigRefreshCoordinator` surfaces the affordance instead. A workflow whose backend resolves to local (an NDA flag or a `.local` pin, matching `Workflow.effectiveBackend`) shows an inline "model not downloaded (~size), Download now" row in the workflow editor's Backend section and under the NDA onboarding preset; the button routes through the daemon's one long-lived `ModelDownloadSupervisor`, so the pull survives the sheet closing and its progress shows in the menu bar. The size label is computed from the repo id (`ModelDownloadSupervisor.downloadSizeLabel`, mirroring doctor's `_estimate_model_gb`), so the 3B preset reads "~1.9 GB" honestly rather than the spec's literal "~4.3 GB". The failed-row remedy for a missing local model is now an in-app "Download model" button (mirroring the PIPE6 pattern): `Meeting.failureSuggestsModelDownload` matches the marker phrase "not in the local HuggingFace cache" in the pipeline's failure reason, a cross-language coupling pinned by comments on both sides (there is no shared constant across the boundary, the same shape as the AppleIntelligence language marker). `mp doctor`'s `check_local_stack` now runs whenever any workflow forces local/NDA, not just when the global backend does, via a new `workflows.local_backend_workflow_names` (a shared `_load_workflow_docs` helper the existing `nda_workflow_names` was refactored onto, behaviour-preserved).

**(c) The AX relaunch hint** was added under the onboarding Accessibility row, matching the Preferences footnote language ("After granting, quit and reopen MeetingPipe for it to take effect").

**Verified.** Full suites green (daemon `swift build` clean + 1398 tests including new size-estimate / preflight / marker tests; pipeline ruff clean, pyright 0 errors, 899 pytest including new `workflows` + `doctor` tests; CI5 truth fences OK). Docs synced in the same commit: README's first-run permissions description (framed onboarding on a fresh install vs. the ordered re-check on later launches) and the failed-state section (model-download remedy + inline affordance), and `docs/SETUP.md` Step 4 (the Welcome window's per-permission framed flow). Owner eyeballed all three surfaces on a real fresh install (onboarding reset) and a local workflow with the model cache moved aside: the framed permissions step with no stacked burst, the AX relaunch hint, the editor/preset "Download now" affordance, and the failed-row "Download model" remedy.

**[DONE] DOC10 (P2): SETUP.md + persona amendment.** Owner priority: easy setup for non-programmers with one exhaustive guide; today no such path exists (README's install is dev-register: git clone, Homebrew, uv, hand-run `security add-generic-password`) and the written persona actively steers sessions away from building it (PRODUCT.md's "read CLI flags faster than landing pages"; the CLAUDE.md out-of-scope list catches onboarding under selling). Scope: write `docs/SETUP.md`, the single exhaustive clean-Mac walkthrough (real prerequisites: Xcode CLT, Homebrew, network + download sizes, about 630 MB ASR models and about 4.3 GB for the local backend; the four TCC grants including the Accessibility relaunch; keys; the Notion integration + database; first recording to first published summary); add the missing prerequisites to README Requirements and a swift/CLT check to install.sh with a friendly die(); amend PRODUCT.md and the project CLAUDE.md so setup ease and the guide are written in scope while licensing/telemetry/landing-page work stays deferred. Coordinate with DIST1: the guide documents the from-source path now and gains a short drag-install section when the notarized bundle lands. Acceptance: a non-programmer can go clean-Mac-to-first-published-meeting from SETUP.md alone; install.sh fails helpfully without CLT; the persona docs no longer contradict the priority.

**Shipped 2026-07-20.**

**The guide assumes nothing.** `docs/SETUP.md` starts before the Terminal: how to check the Mac is Apple Silicon and on macOS 14+ (both hard requirements with no workaround, and both previously discoverable only by failing), then Xcode CLT, then Homebrew including the "Next steps" PATH commands whose omission is the single most common way a Homebrew install looks finished and is not. Every command is given in full to paste. The four permissions get a table saying what each one buys **and what breaks without it**, since a first-run user has no way to judge a dialog otherwise, and Screen Recording gets an explicit sentence that only audio is captured, because the permission name reasonably alarms people. The Accessibility relaunch is called out as its own move, because the permission reads as granted while doing nothing until the daemon restarts. Notion gets the full path including the •••-to-Connections step, which is the one that produces a 404 later when skipped, and the database-ID-from-the-URL rule. It ends with a troubleshooting section written from the failures that actually happen rather than a generic FAQ.

**The download numbers are measured, not repeated.** The spec (and `install.sh`'s own message) said about 630 MB of ASR models. On disk it is **475 MB** (`parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` 461 MB + `speaker-diarization` 13 MB), so the guide says 475 MB and about 1.5 GB for a whole first install, with the 4.3 GB local model flagged as separate and optional. Correcting `install.sh`'s message to match was deliberately left alone: its number describes the fetch, the guide's describes the result, and DOC9's lesson is that two numbers must not silently disagree, so if a future measurement shows them genuinely different that is a real finding rather than a typo to paper over.

**`install.sh` now fails early on both hard prerequisites.** A missing Swift toolchain produced `xcrun: error: invalid active developer path` from `swift build`, which sends a first-time user to the wrong search results; it now dies with the `xcode-select --install` command and the `-s` repoint for the post-upgrade case. The check is `xcode-select -p` **and** `command -v swift`, because the former can point at a directory that no longer exists. Added in the same pass, unasked but the same class: Intel Macs previously built fine and then failed at the first recording, since FluidAudio has no Intel fallback, so the arch check now refuses up front with the reason.

**The persona amendment, which is the half that keeps this from regressing.** Two sentences were actively steering work away from setup. PRODUCT.md's "read CLI flags faster than landing pages" was true as description and read as licence, so it is replaced with an explicit statement that reading CLI flags is a tolerance and getting set up is not the job, plus a note that a guide is not marketing. The project `CLAUDE.md`'s out-of-scope list caught setup under "onboarding flows"; that phrase is removed and the distinction written down, an in-app onboarding *flow* is selling machinery, a person getting a working install is not. Both now name `docs/SETUP.md` as a maintained surface, and it joins the orientation table with an edit trigger, so a change to install steps, prerequisites, permissions, or the first-run path updates it in the same commit. A seventh design principle ("Setup is a surface, and its failures are the loudest ones") states the rule positively as principle 5 applied to the half hour before anything works.

**Acceptance, honestly.** The first two legs are verifiable and verified: `install.sh` fails helpfully (both guards exercised in isolation) and the persona docs no longer contradict the priority. The first leg, "a non-programmer can go clean-Mac-to-first-published-meeting from SETUP.md alone", cannot be proved from here: it wants a real clean Mac and a real non-programmer. Every command, path, menu label, and UI string in the guide was checked against the code (`Open Library…`, `Diagnostics…`, `Quit MeetingPipe`, the `mp doctor` path, the uninstall flags), so the claims are true; whether the *sequence* is followable is an owner walk, best combined with DIST1's owed clean-Mac test.

**[DONE] DOC9 (P2): docs truth sweep.** The post-DOC7 drift this review verified, fixed in one pass: README's backstop troubleshooting numbers (90 s nudge / 5 min auto-stop vs the shipped 480 s nudge and `mic_only_silence_seconds` default 900, configurable 60-1800); the three-Keychain-items claims in CONVENTIONS and README vs the four managed keys (OPENAI_API_KEY joined via PROV1; the backup manifest names four); `config.example.toml` missing the `[output]`/`[obsidian]`/`[filesystem]`/`[lan]` sections and `detection.default_prompt_action` that README documents, plus two stale comments; "Republish all" claiming Notion-only in README and the BatchActionsPane card title while the code fans out via `mp publish`; the Preferences "Whisper" sublabel (ASR is FluidAudio/Parakeet); the CONVENTIONS event-category table missing five emitted categories (daemon, recording, Swift-side pipeline, detail, prefetch) and the non-snake `toolbar.action`; ARCHITECTURE's stale detail-pane tab list and pre-INSIGHTS rail description plus dead `RawFilesTab.swift` (delete it); the where-files-live table's missing rows (markers, embeddings, voiceprint, roster, glossary, transcript_corrections, empty-marker); pipeline/CLAUDE.md's VCR-cassette claim (the real convention is httpx.MockTransport; drop the dead pytest-vcr/vcrpy dev deps). Acceptance: each listed surface matches code; suites + ruff/pyright green after the dep removal.

**Shipped 2026-07-20. Two of the listed items were already closed** when the work started, which is worth recording rather than quietly skipping: the event-category table was brought current by CI5 on 2026-07-14 (and `toolbar.action` renamed then), and `transcript_corrections` had gained its files-table row with PIPE9. The rest were live and are fixed.

**The numbers were the worst of it.** README claimed a 90-second nudge and a 5-minute auto-stop. Neither has ever been true on the shipped code: the horizon is `detection.mic_only_silence_seconds`, default **900 s**, adjustable 60-1800 on the Preferences ▸ Prompt slider, and the nudge is `min(480, autoStop / 2)`, so **450 s** at the default. The README now states the rule rather than a frozen pair of numbers, names the slider, and mentions the native-lifecycle stand-down (a quiet-but-live call is re-nudged, not stopped). The adjacent "an extra 5 minutes of wav file" in the Accessibility bullet was corrected to 15, and `SilenceAutoStopPolicy`'s doc comment still called this "the 5-minute backstop (TECH-C2)".

**The Keychain count is now derived, not asserted.** Seven surfaces said three items; the managed set has been four since PROV1 added `OPENAI_API_KEY`, and both trees already agreed (`KeychainSecrets.managedKeys`, `MANAGED_SECRET_KEYS`), which is why the backup manifest correctly named four while the docs describing that manifest said three. Each fixed surface now points at the constant, so the next key added edits two constants and no prose. The README's restore commands list all four with a line on which ones a given backend actually needs.

**`config.example.toml` is a complete reference again, and CI5's allowlist is empty.** The file never showed `[output]`, `[obsidian]`, `[filesystem]`, `[lan]`, or `detection.default_prompt_action`, so a user who copied it (which is what the header tells them to do) could not learn that four sinks exist. All eleven keys are documented with their real defaults, checked against the pydantic models rather than transcribed from the README. With HYG2's three entries already gone, **both CI5 allowlists are now empty**, which is the intended steady state: the ratchet has nothing left to tolerate, and the fence is the standing guard against the next instance.

**Dead code and a dead dependency.** `RawFilesTab.swift` (271 lines) and its test were kept on disk when DSN2 dropped the Raw-files tab; nothing referenced either but each other, so both are deleted. `pytest-vcr` and `vcrpy` were dev deps with no import anywhere and no `tests/cassettes/` directory that ever existed; the real convention (`httpx.MockTransport`, four test files) is now written down in both places that used to describe cassettes, and `uv sync` drops three packages.

**Two beyond the list, same class.** `pipeline/CLAUDE.md` said the pipeline writes two event categories; it writes three (`prefetch` joined with the model pre-fetch). And four whisper-era references outlived the FluidAudio migration in a way that would mislead a reader about what runs (`State.swift`'s "two concurrent whisper.cpp runs", `TranscriptTab`'s "whisperx/mlx-whisper", ARCHITECTURE's "two whisper invocations"). `SegmentBuilder`'s two mentions were left alone: they document a deliberate parity target (matching Whisper paragraph granularity across the migration), which is provenance, not drift.

**[DONE] HYG2 (P2): dead-knob wire-or-delete.** Three controls render, persist, and drive nothing, the exact class END4(b) fixed for the end-debounce knob. (a) `transcription.language`: ConfigStore round-trips it and Preferences offers the picker, but the sole transcribe call hardcodes `languageHint: nil` (`SinkDispatcher.swift:89`) while `FluidAudioRunner.resolveLanguage` would honor it, and README claims the key was removed; recommendation: wire it (real value for the en/uk owner) and fix the "Whisper" sublabel wording (DOC9 has the copy). (b) `recording.sample_rate`: parsed, round-tripped, offered as 16/24/48 kHz, read by no capture or post-process code (16 kHz is a deliberate constant); recommendation: delete the picker and key.
