(c) `detection.debounce_start_sec`: live 1-30 s slider, documented, consumed by nothing (`PromotionEngine.handleIdle` promotes instantly; only the end knob is wired); wire it into the promotion start path or delete the key, slider, and glossary sentence. Acceptance: every remaining Preferences control demonstrably changes behavior; config docs match; no key is read into Config/ConfigStore without a consumer.

**Shipped 2026-07-20, two deletes and one wire.**

**(a) `transcription.language`, wired.** `Coordinator` reads `configStore?.transcriptionLanguage ?? config.transcription.language` once at init and passes it to `SinkDispatcher`, copying the shape END4(b) used for the end-debounce and the idle backstop use for their horizons; the value is immutable on the dispatcher, so the off-main `runDaemonTranscription` can read it without touching a `@Published`. The key was also absent from `Config.swift` entirely (ConfigStore-only), so it is now a first-class Config key and a hand-run daemon honours it.

**The default moved from `"en"` to `"auto"`, and that was the load-bearing decision.** Before the wiring the runner received `languageHint: nil`, which `resolveLanguage` treats as auto-detect. Wiring the key while ConfigStore still defaulted to `"en"` would have pinned every existing config, none of which set the key (it was never in `config.example.toml`), to English on the next launch: a silent regression aimed squarely at the en/uk owner the task was filed for. `"auto"` preserves today's behaviour exactly for an untouched config and matches both the picker's leading option and `summary_language`'s default.

**(b) `recording.sample_rate`, deleted** from `Config.swift`, `ConfigStore.swift` (property, read, write-back), the Preferences picker, `pipeline/src/mp/config.py`, and `config.example.toml`. Capture is a deliberate 16 kHz constant (what Parakeet TDT consumes) written as literals in `MeetingRecorder`; the picker offered 24 and 48 kHz and nothing honoured either. `ConfigStoreTests.test_writeBack_is_pure` was entirely about this key, so it was re-pointed at `manual_hotkey` rather than deleted, keeping the purity coverage.

**(c) `detection.debounce_start_sec`, deleted, not wired.** The spec left this open; deleting is the right call on the evidence. `PromotionEngine` promotes `.idle -> .starting` on the first PRIMARY `.live` **by design**, so the prompt panel appears promptly, and `.starting -> .inMeeting` happens only through `confirmRecording()` (the recorder actually arming). The real start gate is corroboration and scoring (`MeetingSourceScorer`, `MicInUseTier`, `requiresCorroboration`), not elapsed time. Wiring a start debounce would have needed a `startedAt` on the `.starting` phase plus a tick path, and would have bought nothing but a delayed prompt and a lost head on every meeting. The glossary entry is rewritten to say so rather than describing a knob that never existed in behaviour.

**One defect found in the writing, same class as the three.** A test asserting `debounce_end_sec = 7` failed: TOML separates integer from float literals, TOMLKit does not coerce, and every seconds knob in `config.example.toml` is written as an integer. So a user hand-editing `debounce_end_sec`, `prompt_timeout_sec`, or `reprompt_cooldown_sec` in the shipped example file had the value silently discarded on load, and then overwritten with the default on the next Preferences save. Only `mic_only_silence_seconds` already read both forms. Both files now read through a small `seconds(key, fallback)` helper, pinned by tests on both the int and float paths. This is the same "the user changes a setting and nothing happens" failure HYG2 exists to close, reached through the config file instead of the UI.

**The fence caught a regression in its own extractor.** That helper broke `truth_fences.configstore_properties()`, which only understood `self.<prop> = <alias>?["<key>"]`, and the mapping matters for the one key whose TOML name and Swift property disagree (`mic_only_silence_seconds` -> `micOnlySilenceSec`, kept for back-compat) since `_camel` cannot bridge it. Result: a live knob reported as dead. The extractor now also resolves `self.<prop> = <helper>("<key>", …)` by reading the helper's own body for the table alias, so the section is still derived from source rather than hardcoded.

**Docs.** `config.example.toml` (the `language` knob documented, both deleted keys replaced with a line saying why there is no such knob), `README.md` (config reference gains `transcription.language`; the "the old `transcription.*` ASR keys were removed" claim corrected, since the section has two live knobs), `ARCHITECTURE.md` glossary ("Debounce (start / end)" -> "Debounce (end)" with the no-start-twin rationale), `CONVENTIONS.md` (the allowlist note no longer names HYG2), `design/ui_kits/macos_app/PreferencesWindow.jsx` (both controls removed from the mockup, Whisper sublabel replaced). The Preferences "Whisper" sublabel DOC9 owns the copy for is fixed here, since the picker it labels is the knob being wired.

**[DONE] VALID1 (P1): on-device acceptance measurements.** Carried from Q4 DONE-partial, closed 2026-07-16. Full results + the command that regenerates each number in [`docs/validation/valid1-acceptance-runbook.md`](../validation/valid1-acceptance-runbook.md).

**Why it sat open for three quarters: not the missing Mac. The Q4 scaffolding had gone stale.** It was written when ASR + diarization lived in Python, so `valid1_check.py --timings` only ever paired `pipeline.stage_*` events; both moved to Swift/FluidAudio and now log under `transcription.*` in `events.jsonl`, which the helper never read. The runbook's A15 recipe had the same rot (it said to read `stage summarize` after `mp summarize`, but only `run-all` emits stage events). Two bars were unmeasurable through the very tool built to measure them while the data sat in the log. Two bars were also unsatisfiable as written: A15's "cold-start within 10%" had no baseline to regress against, and DIAR1's "DER within the target" had no target defined anywhere in the repo.

Results:
- **DIAR1 DER: 8.99%** over 39.7 min, three meetings the owner reviewed end to end (`--der --reviewed 20260715-163053 20260716-103024 20260716-113206`). Derived from the owner's own in-app speaker corrections: a reassigned line is a line the diarizer got wrong, so the ground truth accrues from normal use instead of a labelling chore. `--reviewed` is what makes it a DER and not a bound: only the owner can say a line was looked at and left alone, and no file can infer it. This is the project's DER (mislabelled / total speech), not the academic NIST DER. **The mean flatters it**: 0.3% / 13.6% / 49.0% is bimodal, and the 49% meeting is the real defect, split out as **DIAR2 (P1)**. VALID1's job was to produce the number; DIAR2's is to move it.
- **DIAR1 latency: PASS.** 132 real transcription runs over 47.7 h, 0 failures; real-time factor min 5x / median 91x / max 130x. The original "no segment over 10 s" predates FluidAudio (whole-file, not per-segment), so "faster than real time" is the meaningful restatement, and it passes with 5x of headroom in the worst case.
- **UX4: PASS (2026-07-15).** The owner forced a real capture failure by declining the capture TCC prompt mid-recording; one `recording.degraded` landed and `--ux4` exited 0. It had never fired in the entire event history, which was a true "the SCStream has never failed", not a measurement gap.
- **A15 cold-start: baseline set.** Cold 39.8 s, warm 31.4 s, model load 8.4 s (Qwen2.5-7B-4bit). The "within 10%" bar had no prior number to regress against; now it does.
- **Attribution coverage: baselined.** 3.2% of speech unattributed post-roster (79 meetings / 21.2 h) against 25.9% pre-roster.
- **A16 + SUM1-APPLE quality:** already closed by the Q5 engine-comparison run; only the ToC row still claimed them.
- **SUM1-APPLE zero-egress: WAIVED, not measured.** Owner decision 2026-07-16: the on-device-by-construction argument (Foundation Model on-device, no outbound HTTP in the daemon, `egress_guard` clamps under NDA) accepted in place of watching the wire. Recorded as a waiver rather than a pass, and split out as **EGRESS1 (P4)**.

Named deviation, the **step-ratio leg split out**: the knob is not plumbed (`FluidAudioRunner.ensureDiarizer` builds a bare `DiarizerManager()`), so picking an operating point is a Swift tuning change, not a validation. The latency measurement decides it anyway: the published case for step 0.2 was "~2x faster", and at a median 91x real time there is no speed pressure, so the accuracy-favouring 0.1 is free. DIAR2 owns the plumbing.

Traps documented so nobody re-walks them: **the free-ground-truth shortcut for DER is circular** (the stereo channel looks like an oracle for me-vs-them since FluidAudio diarizes a mono `(L+R)/2` downmix, but `diarize.label_me_speaker` already picks "me" *from* the channel, so grading it against the channel measures the channel against itself; it is blind to remote-vs-remote regardless), and **the Swift test suite writes into the real `events.jsonl`** (its fake engines emit `transcription.engine_failed`, so an unfiltered read reported 243 failures that were all `FakeRunner.Boom` against 132 real runs; `--diar` filters on `engine`). Side effects: the owner's labelling surfaced the speaker-naming bugs fixed in `f2945c3` + `5b2efa0`, and the 14B/7B config drift was corrected to the LOCAL6 standard.

**[DONE] C6-FINISH (P1): real detection-trace corpus.** Shipped 2026-07-13 (carried from Q4 DONE-partial: the redaction note + `scripts/redact_detection_trace.py` were already in). The corpus grew from 9 synthetic seeds to 9 seeds + **20 real redacted dogfood traces**, derived from the daemon event log by a new owner-dev diagnostic `scripts/extract_detection_trace.py` (de-CLI exempt, like `redact_detection_trace.py`). The extractor segments `events.jsonl` into meetings and, for each ending it can faithfully reproduce, emits a distilled `promotion` trace whose replay through the pure `PromotionEngine` reproduces the exact verdict sequence the shipped engine logged live (leading signal, `confirmed_by`, flicker count, and relative timing preserved; titles dropped, pid 1234, so redaction-clean by construction, and every file still passed `redact_detection_trace.py --check`). Honest scope: the owner's entire recorded history is `com.microsoft.teams2` native (no other client is ever recorded), so all 20 are Teams and the cross-app seeds stay synthetic; the 20 cover the three pure-engine endings real sessions produce, 13 `rewalk_end` (ax-leave led, confirmed by the AX re-walk, the dominant ending, spanning 8.7 s-3175 s), 4 `debounce_end` (shareable-window-gone promoted on the debounce), and 3 `flicker_rewalk_end` (a share re-render flickers the end signals before the real leave, the END6/END8 fragmentation path). Two enabling changes shipped with it: (1) `DetectionCorpusTests` gained a `confirm_provisional_end` pseudo-event (mirroring `tick`) that fires `PromotionEngine.confirmProvisionalEnd`, without which the AX-re-walk ending, 40 of 70 real meetings, could not replay at all (a real prior corpus blind spot); (2) `redact_detection_trace.py`'s validator now accepts the `tick` / `confirm_provisional_end` control pseudo-events (it rejected `tick` before, so no tick-bearing trace, including several seeds, could ever pass the mandatory gate). Verified: 1284 daemon tests green, the full 29-trace corpus replays green, ruff clean, all 20 pass the redaction gate. The one real ending out of scope, a lone ax-leave `.ended` with empty `confirmed_by` (the idle-stop backstop, ~1/3 of sessions, promoted by `IdleStopBackstop` not `PromotionEngine`), is filed as **C7** rather than faked. **[Corrected by C7 on 2026-07-28, and left in place as the record of what was believed: that bucket is pre-END2 engine behaviour (all 23 sessions ended on or before 2026-06-09; END2 landed 2026-06-10), and the idle backstop is not in it, since it stops the recording rather than the lifecycle and never publishes a `lifecycle.ended`. Skipping the bucket was still the right call, for a different reason. See C7's entry above.]**

**[DONE] LOCAL12 (P1, spike): MLX-Swift trigger evaluation (the I7 gate).** Shipped 2026-07-12. Verdict doc `docs/spikes/local12-mlx-swift-trigger-evaluation.md` + owner-run harness `docs/spikes/local12_bench.py`. **Verdict: NO-GO now**, keep the DIST1 embedded-Python runtime as the end state (not a transitional artifact). A measured A/B is not runnable in the coding harness, and that is itself the finding: there is zero MLX-Swift code / `mlx-swift` SPM dependency in the repo, so "benchmark MLX-Swift" means first building the generation + tokenizer + HF-loading + JSON-schema-constrained-decoding stack DIST2 already judged materially less mature than `mlx_lm`, the object under evaluation, not a precondition (plus a new SPM dep needs owner approval, the 7B isn't cached, and quality is hand-graded by design). So rather than a hollow GO, the spike operationalizes I7's amended trigger: `local12_bench.py` drives the real `LocalSummaryClient` path (pipeline system prompt + LOCAL3 map-reduce), measures per-run / p50 latency + peak server RSS (an `lsof` + `ps` sampler keyed on the listening port, so it measures the mlx-lm server it spawns and an external MLX-Swift server identically) + writes the summary for dogfood SHIP_GATE grading, and is keyed on host:port so the same tool A/Bs mlx-lm today (`--manage`) and MLX-Swift later (`--no-manage --port`). The trigger fires only when a candidate clears four bars (quality SHIP_GATE, latency within margin, peak RSS not materially worse, holds the `MeetingSummary` schema without a higher repair rate). Spot-checked the LOC accounting the decision weighs (total src 13,942 LOC; publishers 2,042; the ~8% / ~1.1k Swift-mirror upkeep pinned by the chunking / json-extract / summary-md golden fixtures + the meta-contract). DEP1-style throwaway (not in the `mp` package / CI); `--help` + ruff verified; the measured run is owner-owed like DEP1's script and MIC7's probe. I7's row + spec updated to point at the verdict; I7 stays deferred (P3). Owner-owed remainder: capture the baseline (the 7B needs the ~4.3 GB fetch), then run the candidate half once an MLX-Swift server exists.

**[DONE] DOC8 (P1): mute-story truth.** Shipped 2026-07-12. Owner chose to keep capture-first as the default (ADR 0016 + the MIC9 amendment stand; flipping to redact-by-default would re-introduce the silent-speech-loss the amendment fixed), so this was a docs / copy sweep with no behaviour change. Every surface that asserted pre-ADR-0016 live mic zeroing now describes the real model: the mic is recorded in full, `honor_app_mute` only makes MicGate *observe* mute (feeding the per-workflow `redactMutedSpans` timeline and the idle backstop), and only the regulated / NDA gate zeroes the mic live. Rewritten: the Preferences toggle "Pause mic when muted" → "Track in-app mute" with a sublabel pointing at per-workflow "Redact muted spans" and regulated mode (plus its group footer), the `config.example.toml` `honor_app_mute` comment, ARCHITECTURE's verdict-fusion intro, the "Mute gating" section body + heading, the recording sequence-diagram note (and the omitted `captureMode` start arg), and the glossary "Mic gate" entry, plus three stale code doc comments the review had not listed (`Config.swift`, `ConfigStore.swift`, and the `ConfigStoreTests` assertion message). README's ADR 0016 bullet and the Workflows "Redact muted spans" copy were already correct and left untouched. Acceptance met: a search for the old "pause / zero mic while muted" claim across Preferences copy, config comments, and ARCHITECTURE now returns nothing. 1272 daemon tests green.

**[DONE] REC6 (P1): orphan recovery cadence + visibility.** Shipped 2026-07-12. The orphan sweep was launch-only with a 24 h staleness cutoff, so the stop path's "recovered on the next launch" promise was false for a launchd daemon with multi-day uptime, and a failed-merge stem was invisible to the doctor (`OrphanScan` counted `.mic.wav` as "a wav exists" because `pathExtension == "wav"`). Fixes: (a) the sweep runs from `Coordinator.reapStorage()` (launch + after every job completion, the established cleanup slot; the explicit launch call was folded in), so a merge that failed while the daemon keeps running recovers on the next job without a relaunch; the currently-recording stem (`libraryModel.liveRecordingStem`) is excluded so an in-flight recording is never merged mid-write. (b) `OrphanRecordingRecovery.scanOrphanStems` exempts a stem carrying a `.recovery.json` or `.recordfail.json` marker from the 24 h cutoff (a deliberate marker means a real interrupted recording, not stale test debris). (c) `OrphanScan.detect` excludes `.mic.wav`/`.system.wav` from the "a wav exists" check, so an unmerged orphan surfaces as an `mp doctor` finding ("stem(s) with sidecars but no wav"). (d) `.recordfail.json` is cleared once a re-merge succeeds. (e) the stop-path notification now reads "recovered automatically". Acceptance met: recovery without a relaunch, doctor visibility, a true stop-path notice. Scoped to the doctor-finding visibility floor the spec allows ("or at minimum a doctor finding"); the richer retryable-Library-row is unneeded now that the sweep auto-recovers on every job completion. Verified: 1272 daemon tests green (4 new: live-stem exclusion + `.recovery.json` / `.recordfail.json` TTL exemptions + the OrphanScan predicate). ARCHITECTURE + CONVENTIONS sidecar docs updated off the "next launch" wording.

**[DONE] END5 (P1): browser/PWA end-signal hardening.** Shipped 2026-07-12. Promoted P3 to P1 and re-scoped in the 2026-07-12 assessment (base spec in [`q4-final.md`](./q4-final.md)); both halves shipped. **End side:** a plain browser meeting exposes no end signal but the active tab title, which flips on any tab switch, so with the short configured end-debounce a >5 s mid-call tab switch promoted to `.ended` and chopped the recording. Cross-class corroboration is impossible for a browser (only a full browser quit fires the cross-class `workspaceAppTerminated`), so the fix is the spec's other option: a per-signal `endDebounceFloor` on `PrimarySignalKind` (120 s for `.browserTabTitle`, 0 elsewhere) that `PromotionEngine.tick` applies as `max(configuredDebounce, floor)`. A returning tab reverts to `.inMeeting` (recording never stops), a genuine sustained leave promotes once the title stays gone past the floor, a browser quit still ends promptly on the cross-class fast path, and the idle-silence backstop remains the ceiling. **Start side (the brand-token corroborator):** `MeetingTitlePatterns.googleMeet` now requires the "Meet" brand alongside the 3-4-3 code (a bare slug like "one-more-day" is a valid 3-4-3 and false-prompted); and `MeetingSourceScanner` admits a plain-browser candidate on a bare brand-token title (a page whose title merely contains "webex" / "microsoft teams" / "huddle") only when live process audio corroborates it, since a real browser call holds the mic while a doc about those products does not. A strong Meet code / `meet.google.com` title still stands alone (`BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.strongTitleMatchers`). Verified: 1268 daemon tests green (6 new PromotionEngine / LifecycleAdapter tests + the `google_meet_browser_clean_leave` corpus trace updated to the floor). ARCHITECTURE.md's Debounce glossary corrected (it had claimed a `meeting_apps.toml` per-app debounce override that never existed). Unblocks MIC7's deferred build (shared extension artifact).

**[DONE] HYG1 (P3): post-review hygiene sweep.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Four independent items. (a) Retired `ai2_spike.py` (a self-described throwaway harness whose packing logic already ships in `rag.py`): deleted the module + its tests, the `mp ai2-spike` dispatch arm + USAGE block, the ARCHITECTURE subcommand row, and the README runnable-harness claim + example commands; the two docstrings that named the file (`rag.py`, `embed_index.py`) and the `ask.py` comment were repointed, and the historical spike report (`docs/spikes/ai2-embedding-rag-latency.md`) gained a one-line note that its harness was retired (git history preserves it). (b) A new `ExecutableResolver` (env override + optional PATH walk + ordered fallbacks, injectable for tests) replaces the two hardcoded discovery lists; `MeetingRecorder.findFFmpeg` and `PipelineLauncher.findMP`'s uv lookup are now thin callers, and the uv fallback gains `~/.local/bin/uv` + `~/.cargo/bin/uv` (uv's standalone-installer defaults the Homebrew-only pair missed). (c) The HUD's 10 Hz `meterTicker` now pauses when the panel is occluded (a `didChangeOcclusionStateNotification` observer plus the pure `shouldMeterTick(occlusionVisible:hasProvider:)` gate) and resumes when it shows, so an off-screen HUD stops waking the CPU to update an invisible meter. (d) `prefetch_model`'s 2 s progress poll now calls a new `_incremental_bytes` that caches finished blobs' sizes and only re-stats new or `*.incomplete` files, instead of `rglob`+`stat`-ing the whole HF cache tree every tick; the three one-shot `_bytes_on_disk` callers are unchanged. Verified: daemon 1134 green (5 `ExecutableResolverTests` + a `shouldMeterTick` test), pipeline 667 green (4 new `test_prefetch_model` incremental-accounting tests including the `.incomplete`-rename no-double-count case; ruff + pyright clean). Acceptance's ai2-spike-gone-with-docs-and-tests-agreeing, one-resolver-owns-discovery, and meter-idle-when-not-visible are met; the HUD occlusion pause is the correct `occlusionState` idiom (an always-on-top all-spaces floating panel is rarely fully occluded, so the CPU saving is modest), and its visual eyeball is owner-owed like other HUD changes.

**[DONE] PERF7 (P3): event-log rotation / size cap.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Rename rotation, chosen over ring-buffer trim for concurrency safety: atomic renames mean a writer that loses the race just appends to a fresh file, where an in-place newest-half rewrite could clobber a concurrent append (`pipeline_events.jsonl` has multiple concurrent writers). A file reaching `MEETINGPIPE_LOG_MAX_BYTES` (default 5 MiB) rotates `foo.ext` -> `foo.1.ext` (the index goes before the extension so a rotated file stays valid JSONL / a tail-able log), shifting older generations up and dropping past 3 backups, so each log family self-bounds at ~4x the cap. Swift: `Log.rotateIfNeeded` at the single `appendData` choke point covers `events.jsonl` + `main.log`/`daemon.log`/`recorder.log`, and `PipelineLauncher.runMP` rotates `pipeline.log` before opening its per-run handle. Python: `events.rotate_if_needed` in `emit` (for `pipeline_events.jsonl`) and reused by `orchestrate._configure_logging` before it opens `pipeline.log` (both sides rotate at open time, so a daemon-spawned `mp` never has a stale open handle to a renamed file). Readers span the boundary via `Log.logGenerations` / `events.log_generations` (base plus generations, oldest first): `mp logs`, `mp analyze-detection` (its `pair_sessions` sorts by `ts`, so cross-file order is moot and a session split across a rotation still pairs), and the two stdlib-only scripts (`dogfood_report.py`, `valid1_check.py`) which get a local mirror of the generation glob rather than importing the package (they advertise stdlib-only). `launchd.{out,err}.log` are launchd-owned and outside the app's write paths, so out of scope. Docs corrected in passing: ARCHITECTURE + README listed `detector.log` / `recording.log`, which are never written (only the `main`/`daemon`/`recorder` categories reach `Log.writeLine`). First-ever tests over the log write path on both sides (4 Swift `LoggerTests` incl. generation-bounding, 7 `test_events.py` incl. a session paired across a rotation boundary). Verified: daemon 1138 green, pipeline 680 green (ruff + pyright clean). No detection or behaviour change.

**[DONE] CI3 (P3): parity fixtures for the unpinned Swift-Python mirrors.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Two golden fixtures under `daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/Fixtures/` (`json-extract-golden.json`, `summary-md-golden.json`), each read by both suites off the one file (Swift via `Bundle.module`, Python via the repo-root-relative path, the chunking-golden / CI2 precedent); Python is the source of truth (fixtures generated from the real functions). `AppleIntelligenceSummarizerTests` (Swift) and `test_ci3_parity.py` (Python) assert their function reproduces each case, so a divergence in either mirror breaks one suite. The fixture-building surfaced a genuine divergence, not just a naming pin: Python's `render_summary_md` renders a resolved action item as `[x]`, while Swift's `renderMarkdown` always emitted `[ ]` and never read `resolved`; fixed Swift to honor it (a real change to the Apple-Intelligence markdown path, though freshly-generated summaries carry `resolved: false`, so live output is unchanged in practice). Two stale mirror doc-comments in `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.swift` corrected in passing (they named `summarize_local._largest_balanced_json_object` and `summarize._render_summary_md`, both promoted away by PROV1/PIPE7 to `json_extract.largest_balanced_json_object` / `markdown.render_summary_md`). `pipeline/CLAUDE.md`'s shared-helpers note records the new parity contract. The third `largestJSONObject` copy in `daemon/scripts/ai-summarize.swift` (a throwaway harness outside the SPM target) is out of CI3's named scope and left as-is. Verified: daemon 1140 green (2 golden tests), pipeline 690 green (10 golden params; ruff + pyright clean).

**[DONE] UX20 (P3): in-app diagnostics viewer + the hidden doctor probes.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Two de-CLI residuals closed. (a) A new read-only `DiagnosticsWindow` (`DiagnosticsWindow` + `DiagnosticsView` + `DiagnosticsLog`), opened from the status-bar menu ("Diagnostics…"), reads `events.jsonl` + `pipeline_events.jsonl` across PERF7's rotated generations (`Log.logGenerations`) and applies the `mp logs` since/category/action filters in-process (no terminal, no follow: a Reload button re-reads). The Swift JSONL reader was written from scratch (none existed); it mirrors `logs_cmd`'s `k=v` detail formatting and caps the rendered set at 2000 newest-first so a large log cannot stall the window. (b) The Preferences doctor sheet folds the daemon's own probes in: `DoctorRunner` runs `DoctorCommand.daemonSelfCheckProbes()` (AX trust, Screen Recording, Microphone, per-app AX reachability, events-writable, orphan scan; the `pipeline.*` probes are excluded because spawning `mp doctor` already exercises them) inline before spawning `mp doctor`, so the two halves show in one sheet under `== Daemon self-check ==` / `== mp doctor ==` headers, and the sheet title changed from "mp doctor" to "Doctor". `mp logs` itself is unchanged (acceptance). Reader logic pinned by 5 `DiagnosticsLogTests` (parse, filter/sort, cross-generation read, probe-subset). Verified: daemon 1145 green, `swift build` clean. Visual eyeball owner-owed (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable in the delegation harness).

**[DONE] UX17 (P3): playback speed + skip-silence.** Shipped 2026-07-11. An `AVAudioUnitTimePitch` unit is spliced between the player node and the main mixer in the shared `AudioPlaybackController` graph (`playerNode -> timePitch -> mainMixerNode`), so a `@Published playbackRate` (1x / 1.25 / 1.5 / 2x, via `PlaybackRateMenu` in both the Transcript strip and the Audio controls) applies live with no reschedule and stays pitch-corrected. The existing `computedCurrentTime()` is source-frame based (it reads the player node's own sampleTime, which a downstream time-stretch pulls faster), so the transcript highlight and play-head track correctly at every rate through the existing `currentTime` `onChange` path, unchanged. Skip-silence is an optional per-controller toggle (in the Audio tab) that hops silent spans derived from the waveform peaks cache: a new pure `SilenceScanner.spans(peaks:)` (max-abs below 2% full scale for >=1.5 s), computed off-main on load, and a one-seek-per-gap hop in the 15 Hz tick (landing at the span end moves the next tick out of the gap; the transcript follows via `currentTime`). The rate is shared across tabs (one `@StateObject`), so it survives a tab switch, meeting the acceptance. Logic pinned by 7 `SilenceScannerTests` (span detection, min-duration floor, threshold split, trailing run) plus the rate-label/options. README's Transcript/Audio tab descriptions updated (the old text said the only speed-looking control was zoom). Verified: daemon 1152 green, `swift build` clean. Audible/visual eyeball owner-owed (the menu-bar app is not runnable in this harness).

**[DONE] DEP1 (P3, spike): native AVFoundation merge/transcode to replace ffmpeg.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Verdict GO for both scoped operations, measured by a runnable throwaway harness (`daemon/scripts/dep1-native-audio-spike.swift`, run locally against synthesized mic/system fixtures) and written up in `docs/spikes/dep1-avfoundation-vs-ffmpeg.md`. Transcode: native `AVAudioFile` FLAC is frame-exact (55994/55994), duration-verified (the 0.05 s bar `AudioTranscoder` already uses), lossless, and on the test tone smaller than ffmpeg (24% vs 28% of the WAV); clean GO, lowest-risk to port first. Merge: native mic-L / system-R 16 kHz stereo with channel separation intact; GO on feasibility, not sample-identical to ffmpeg (different resampler, so frame-parity + channel-correctness is the achievable bar), with a length-policy decision to make (the prototype pads to the longer input; ffmpeg `amerge` truncated to the shorter, which also contradicts the `MeetingRecorder` "pads to the longer input" comment). Two porting gotchas found and documented in the harness: FLAC needs `AVEncoderBitDepthHintKey` in the settings (else `forWriting` throws an empty nil-error), and reads must be position-bounded (read-past-EOF throws an empty nil-error on this SDK rather than returning 0 frames). Two spec premises corrected: the "hardcoded path lists in three files" is stale (HYG1 consolidated ffmpeg discovery into one `ExecutableResolver` + `MeetingRecorder.findFFmpeg` the same day the spec was written), and a GO does not delete the binary because `MuteRedactor` is a third ffmpeg spawn site outside DEP1's scope, so DIST1 sizing cannot assume ffmpeg disappears from a merge+transcode port alone. No production switch inside the spike (acceptance); the production port is DIST1 prerequisite work, and DIST1's row now records this verdict as its gate.

**[DONE] SEC14 (P3): security hygiene tail.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Four verified findings, none urgent on a single-user Mac, all cheap: (a) `TranscriptTab.saveCorrection` logged verbatim `original_text` / `edited_text` into `events.jsonl`; it now logs `original_len` / `edited_len` by default and the full text only under `UISettings.verboseLogging` (the stale UISettings doc comment that claimed such a gate existed is now accurate). (b) `raw/` transcripts and summaries landed 0644 while `originals/` and the logs are deliberately 0600; the Swift `TranscriptSidecar.write` and the pipeline's transcript rewrites (`orchestrate`, three sites) + summary write (`summarize`) now set 0600 at write, and a launch-time `MeetingStore.tightenArtifactPermissions` self-heals pre-existing files (mirrors the SEC11 `Log.tightenLogPermissions` sweep, called once from `Coordinator.start`). (c) the three daemon ffmpeg spawns (`MeetingRecorder`, `MuteRedactor`, `AudioTranscoder`) inherited the daemon env including the four managed API tokens; each now sets `proc.environment = Secrets.scrubbedEnvironment()`, which strips every `KeychainSecrets.managedKeys` entry (SEC13 owns the pipeline side). (d) `PipelineLauncher.runMP` created `pipeline.log` 0644 and relied on the next launch tightening it; it is now created 0600 directly. Verified: daemon 1128 green (new tests: `Secrets.scrubbedEnvironment` drops every token, `MeetingStore.tightenArtifactPermissions` self-heals 0644 to 0600 and leaves the recording alone, `TranscriptSidecar.write` lands 0600), pipeline 674 green (the summary write asserted 0600). Acceptance's new-artifacts-land-0600, ffmpeg-env-carries-no-tokens, and correction-events-carry-no-verbatim-sentences-by-default are all met.

**[DONE] PERF6 (P3): gate discovery-scan AX walks behind a cheap precondition.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. The 2026-06-30 idle-energy finding put MeetingPipe's ~8-13% idle CPU in `MeetingSourceScanner`'s three recursive AX tree walks (`findCallingControlsToolbar` + `findAllLeaveButtons` + `findAllMuteButtons`), re-run per candidate on every discovery pass even for a backgrounded app with no plausible meeting; PERF5's backoff covered only the in-meeting pollers, never discovery. Fix: `collectSignals` now computes the two cheap preconditions first (the process-audio probe, moved ahead of the walks; the meeting-titled-window `titleMatch`, already there) and gates the three walks behind a new pure predicate `MeetingSourceScanner.shouldWalkControlAX(titleMatch:processAudioActive:bundleID:)` = `titleMatch || processAudioActive`. An idle app skips the walk and keeps the default (false) control signals, which the scorer already treats identically to "walked and found nothing" (it drops any candidate with `distinctSignalCount == 0`), so detection is unchanged for real meetings while the idle walk disappears. Webex (`com.cisco.webexmeetingsapp` / `com.cisco.spark`) is carved out (always walks): it is deliberately excluded from the process-audio probe (Cisco holds the mic open post-call for ultrasound device discovery), so it has no audio leg and must not gate on title alone; the carve-out keeps Webex at today's behaviour, and Teams (the profiled hot path) is a native app that gets the win. The Webex bundle IDs are consolidated into one `webexBundleIDs` set shared by the audio exclusion and the predicate. Verified: daemon 1125 green, incl. 4 new `MeetingSourceScannerTests` pinning the predicate (idle-skips, title-admits, audio-admits, Webex-always-walks); `DetectionCorpusTests` (a downstream `PromotionEngine`/`MicGate` smoke check) still green. Acceptance's `DetectionCorpusTests`-green is met; the "a real meeting still scores" dogfood / `mp analyze-detection` live confirmation is owner-owed (not exercisable in this harness), consistent with how detection changes ship here.

**[DONE] PROV1 (P2): pluggable cloud providers, claude CLI first, then OpenAI API.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. (1) Seam: a new `services.TextClient` protocol formalizes the free-form `complete` shape `engine` previously duck-typed, beside the existing `SummaryClient`, so a provider is one module implementing both; Anthropic's two-class split (`AnthropicSummaryClient` + `AnthropicTextClient`) was left untouched as legacy (wrap over rewrite). (2) `claude_cli` (`provider_claude_cli.py`): spawns `claude -p --output-format json --allowed-tools ""` with the prompt on STDIN (so a long transcript never hits ARG_MAX), parses the `{is_error, result}` envelope, and validates the result through the same pydantic `MeetingSummary` path with a one-shot repair retry and the shared balanced-JSON recovery. No API key: it rides the user's Claude Code login. `mp doctor` preflights binary presence + `--version` (a local probe, egress-free, so it runs even under regulated). (3) `openai` (`provider_openai.py`): an OpenAI-compatible chat-completions API over raw httpx (not the SDK), JSON mode + schema-in-prompt for the structured call, key `OPENAI_API_KEY` in the Keychain (SEC8, a fourth managed key), model id in `summarization.openai_model`; doctor probes an unbilled `GET /models`. (4) Fail-closed, the load-bearing edge: `config.effective_backend` classes every non-local backend cloud (a new `CLI_BACKENDS` set documents the CLI ones), so regulated/NDA force local and never select, hence never spawn, a cloud provider; `claude_cli._run` additionally refuses to spawn while `egress_guard.is_armed()`, defense in depth (SEC10). `auto` is unchanged (anthropic-then-local, never a CLI provider). The balanced-JSON scan was promoted out of `summarize_local` into `json_extract.largest_balanced_json_object` (shared by both providers and the local path, the "promote, don't copy" rule), with a back-compat alias so `test_summarize_local` is untouched; `workflow.py`'s hardcoded backend set now imports `config.BACKENDS` (the duplication the review flagged). Swift: the Preferences ▸ Pipeline picker gains Claude CLI + OpenAI with per-backend footers, and a new OpenAI key field in Integrations (SecretsStore + `KeychainSecrets.managedKeys` + the App env mirror) so the openai backend is fully UI-driven (de-CLI). Docs: README's "Messages API directly, not Claude Code" rationale amended with a note on ADR 0007 recording the why; ARCHITECTURE (the two provider modules + json_extract), CONVENTIONS (the sidecar `workflow_backend` enum), config.example.toml, and pipeline/CLAUDE.md updated. The codex-CLI follow-on (scope item 4, "only if claude_cli proves stable") was deliberately not built. Verified: pipeline 674 green (ruff + pyright clean) incl. effective_backend forcing local for claude_cli/openai under regulated+NDA, the CLI-never-spawned-when-armed tripwire test, claude_cli summarize end-to-end with no API key, and the openai egress-guard-blocks-when-armed test (the same clamp anthropic gets); daemon 1115 green incl. the OpenAI key round-trip. Acceptance's claude_cli-with-no-key, doctor-preflights-honestly, regulated/NDA-never-spawn-a-CLI, openai-passes-the-egress-tests, and picker-gains-entries are all met; the visual eyeball of the new Preferences entries (menu-bar app not screenshotable here) is owner-owed.

**[DONE] PERF5 (P3): 4 Hz engine-tick gating.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. `MeetingLifecycleCoordinator` armed a `tickInterval` (0.25 s = 4 Hz) `Timer` in `engage` and ran it for the whole meeting, even though `PromotionEngine.tick` only advances an `.endingProvisional` toward `.ended` on the debounce and is a no-op in every other phase, so it woke `engineQueue` four times a second for the entire `.starting` / `.inMeeting` span to do nothing. Fix: `PromotionEngine` exposes `hasPendingEndDeadline` (true only in `.endingProvisional`), and a new `reconcileTick(generation:)` arms or disarms the timer to match. It runs on `engineQueue` after every engine mutation (`ingest`, `tick`, `confirmRecording`, `confirmProvisionalEnd`), samples the pending state on that queue (the only one that mutates the engine), then hops to main to touch the `Timer` and the main-only `cancelTick`, guarded by the AUD-29 session generation so a superseded session's late reconcile is dropped; `engage` no longer arms unconditionally and `disengage` still cancels directly. The arm/disarm is idempotent, so overlapping mutations converge. Net: the tick fires only while a provisional end is pending (seconds per meeting, not its whole length). The poll-backoff remainder for the three 1 Hz pollers had already shipped; this closes the 4 Hz leg. Verified: daemon 1114 green, incl. two new coordinator tests asserting the tick arms on a provisional end and disarms on both the promote-to-`.ended` and the flicker-revert-to-`.inMeeting` paths; no behaviour change (the manual `coordinator.tick()` drive and every existing lifecycle assertion are unchanged). PERF6 (the discovery-scan AX-walk gate) stays a separate, unrelated task.

**[DONE] PIPE5 (P2): Notion sink property enrichment.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. `NotionRestPublisher.upsert` now probes the target database once per publish (`GET /v1/databases/{id}` via `_probe_db_properties`, fail-soft to `{}` on any error), and `_optional_properties` fills only the columns the database defines, matched by exact name AND Notion type: `Workflow` (select) and `Source` (select) from the meeting's `<stem>.meta.json` (`workflow_name` / `source_display_name`, loaded beside the notion sidecar so both the `publish()` and the `publish_router.fanout` paths resolve the same meta file), `Attendees` (multi-select) and `Open actions` (number) from the summary (`attendees`, count of unresolved `actions`). A property the DB lacks is skipped silently, a name that exists with the wrong type is skipped with one log line, empty select/multi-select values are skipped so a republish never wipes a hand-set field, and `Open actions` writes its count including 0 (the value the property exists to filter on). Select/multi-select option names are comma-sanitised (Notion rejects commas). `Duration` from the original scope was dropped deliberately: it is in no sidecar the publisher reads (the meta schema carries no duration), so filling it would couple the sink to the transcript-JSON schema for one "e.g." property; the four filled properties are the ones with clean sidecar sources. Both the create and the update paths enrich, so republish keeps the columns in sync. The regulated no-op is untouched because the zero-egress skip short-circuits before any publisher is built, so enrichment never runs under a clamped mode. Verified: pipeline 658 green (ruff + pyright clean) incl. create-path and update-path enrichment, a bare Name/Date/Status DB writing exactly three properties (the byte-identical guarantee), a wrong-type property skipped, and a probe-failure fallback; the two existing call-sequence tests were updated for the leading schema probe. README's Notion schema section documents the optional set. Acceptance's fills-on-publish-and-republish, bare-DB-byte-identical, and regulated-no-op-unchanged are all met.

**[DONE] PIPE4 (P2): backend-aware long-meeting routing.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. The 80k-char guard was backend-blind, dead-ending a long LOCAL meeting in a paste bundle even though summarizing it on-device is free. Two changes. (1) `orchestrate._check_too_long` is now cloud-only: a new `_will_summarize_locally(cfg)` returns True for `local` / `apple_intelligence`, and for `auto` only when no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in the env (mirroring `backend_fallback.run_with_local_fallback`; `entry.prepare` loaded secrets before the guard, so the env is authoritative). The owner chose to also map-reduce keyless `auto`. When it returns True the guard writes no bundle; `anthropic` and keyed `auto` still bundle, so the cloud bill is unchanged. (2) `LocalSummaryClient.summarize` map-reduces a transcript over `map_reduce_above_chars` (threaded from `summarization.skip_above_chars` at both construction sites, so one knob drives the cloud bundle and the local routing): it windows via `chunking.chunked_windows` at a Cyrillic-safe ~1500-char budget (AI2's ~4K-token safe ceiling; 16K OOM'd the Mac), summarizes each window through the existing schema-retry + language-repair loops, then reduces the partials in batches of 4 until one remains (generalizing `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.generate`'s LOCAL3 batched reduce out of Swift; new `_render_for_reduce` / `_reduce_instructions` / `_batched` helpers). The whole map-reduce runs under the client's single lock on one warm server, resetting the idle timer per call, and the final merged summary still passes the language-mismatch repair. Below the threshold the single-pass path is byte-unchanged. The paste bundle stays the cloud-over-threshold behaviour and the failure fallback; the daemon watchdog stays the hard backstop. `chunking.py` is stdlib-only, so its new top-level import in `summarize_local` respects the lazy-heavy-import contract. Verified: pipeline 653 green (ruff + pyright clean) incl. a fake-server map-reduce test (multi-window + a reduce call; a short transcript stays single-pass), `_will_summarize_locally` by backend (incl. keyless-auto + regulated), `_check_too_long` cloud-bundles-but-not-local, and a run-all-reaches-summarize test. The Preferences ▸ Pipeline "Long meetings" footer now says cloud-only. Acceptance's >80k-local-produces-a-summary and anthropic-path-unchanged are met; memory-stays-bounded-on-a-real-Mac is owner-owed (VALID1 precedent), the windows being explicitly sized to the AI2 budget for exactly that.

**[DONE] WF8 (P2): post-hoc workflow reassignment.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. "Change workflow..." in the Library detail `...` menu (a nested list of the user's workflows, the current one disabled) reassigns a misrouted or manually-recorded meeting. The rewrite is a pure `MeetingMetaSidecar.reassigned(existing:to:)`: it drops every `workflow_*` key and rebuilds the block from the new workflow through the single `build` (so the omission rules stay in one place and a stale `workflow_notion_database_id` / `workflow_backend` from the old cloud workflow cannot survive a move into NDA), while preserving the source, title, and top-level `regulated_mode`. `MeetingLibraryService.reassignWorkflow` writes it atomically, so `MeetingStore`'s directory watcher refreshes the row chip and scope counts on its own (scope resolves by workflow name), with no extra poke. NDA transitions are the sharp edge and confirm explicitly via an NSAlert (into NDA re-clamps from now on but cannot un-egress past publishes; out of NDA is a deliberate downgrade). On success a follow-up NSAlert offers Regenerate (re-summarize with the new context prompt, then republish) and Republish (re-send the existing summary to the new sinks / DB), plus "Not now"; neither fires without the user choosing it, and the Python `apply_overrides` already reads whatever the meta says at process start, so no pipeline change was needed. The meta contract gained the rewrite case: a fourth committed fixture (`workflow-reassigned-to-nda`, a cloud-app source with an NDA workflow block) asserted by `MetaContractFixtureTests` (build reproduces it, stale keys gone) and `test_workflow_overlay.py` (overlay clamps it to local + filesystem). Verified: daemon 1112 green (new `MetaContractFixtureTests` reassign case + a `MeetingLibraryServiceTests` round-trip), pipeline 648 green (ruff + pyright clean). Acceptance's reassigned-republishes-to-new-sinks, scope-counts-update, meta-contract-gains-the-rewrite-case, and no-automatic-run are met; the visual eyeball (menu-bar app not screenshotable here) is owner-owed.

**[DONE] FEAT3-MANAGE (P2, after FEAT3-UNDO): roster management view.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Preferences ▸ Pipeline gains a "People" group beside "Voice profile" (the owner's chosen home over a new section or a Library rail row): each enrolled voice is a row with its name, sample count, and Rename… / Remove buttons; an empty roster shows a quiet pointer to the transcript naming affordance. Reads go through a new read-only `RosterProfile` (roster.json to name + sample count), the same display-only exception `VoiceprintProfile` makes for voiceprint.json; every mutation still spawns `mp roster`, so the matching + k-means logic keeps a single owner. Rename needed a new primitive: `RosterStore.rename(old, new)` (keeps samples + centroids, refuses to merge into a different existing name, an empty or absent target is a no-op) behind a new `mp roster rename --old --new` (mirroring `forget`'s `--name`-only shape, not `enroll`'s embedding-bound one), spawned via a new `PipelineLauncher.rosterRename`; Remove reuses `mp roster forget`. Rename confirms via an NSAlert text prompt, Remove via an NSAlert warning that states the consequence (the voice stops being auto-named; past transcripts are unchanged). "Last-matched meeting" was dropped per the spec's "where cheap": no field records it, and computing it is a library scan. Swift emits `coordinator`/`roster_{rename,remove}_{requested,done,failed}`; the CLI emits `pipeline`/`roster_renamed`. Verified: pipeline 647 green (6 new rename tests, ruff + pyright clean), daemon 1110 green (5 new `RosterProfileTests`). Acceptance's forget-from-UI-un-enrolls, list-reflects-roster.json, and CLI-parity are met; the visual eyeball (menu-bar app not screenshotable here) is owner-owed, as the spec notes.

**[DONE] STOR3 (P2): backup surfacing in-app, the STOR2 follow-up.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Closes the CLI-only gap STOR2 shipped with (its spec predated the de-CLI rule). Preferences ▸ Storage gains a "Backup" group: a destination picked once via `NSOpenPanel` and remembered in `UISettings.backupDestinationPath` (UI-only, the pipeline never reads it, per that store's documented purpose), and a "Back up now" button that spawns `mp backup <dir>`, with an indeterminate spinner while it runs and any failure shown inline in `.mpDanger`. Deviation (owner-flagged in the plan): rather than a third bespoke `Process` spawner alongside `PipelineLauncher.runMP` and `DoctorRunner`, a new `PipelineLauncher.backup` funnels through the shared `runMP`, since backup needs only a result, not `DoctorRunner`'s live-output streaming; `mp backup` has no clean failure exit code, so a failure surfaces as `runMP`'s `LaunchError.nonZeroExit(code, stderrTail)`. A read-only `LastBackup` reader (mirroring `VoiceprintProfile`'s direct-read display pattern and `doctor.py`'s `check_last_backup` wording) shows "Last backup N days ago", refreshed after a successful run; it tolerates Python's microsecond ISO-8601 fraction. Restore deliberately stays CLI + runbook (a new-Mac restore runs before the app is configured); `mp backup` itself is unchanged. Verified: daemon builds clean, `LastBackupTests` (7 cases incl. the microsecond-fraction parse) green, full daemon suite 1105 green. Acceptance's backup-from-Preferences, age-line-updates, and failure-surfaces-in-UI are met; the visual eyeball (menu-bar app not screenshotable here) is owner-owed, as the spec notes.

**[DONE] AI4-FINISH (P2): digest surfacing, schedule toggle + Digests view.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Closes the CLI-only gap AI4 shipped with. (a) A new `DigestSchedulerService` writes `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.meetingpipe.digest.plist` (a `StartCalendarInterval` running `mp digest`, with no RunAtLoad/KeepAlive since it is a scheduled one-shot, not a resident daemon) and `launchctl bootstrap`/`bootout`s it; a "Scheduled weekly digest" toggle + weekday + time in Preferences → Pipeline install/rewrite/remove it on change (schedule state in `UISettings`, mirroring the launch-at-login binding). The `mp` path is resolved via `PipelineLauncher.findMP` so the agent points at the installed venv `mp`. (b) A read-only `Digests` row under the INSIGHTS rail group (`LibraryScope.digests`, the FactsView/AskView host precedent): `DigestsView` lists the `digests/` sibling's `digest-<date>.summary.json`, renders the selected one with the shared `SummaryRenderedView`, and offers Generate now (a new `PipelineLauncher.digest` -> `mp digest`), reveal-in-Finder, and move-to-Trash. No new egress (the digest already routes through the engine + clamp; the Generate-now call goes through `runMP`, so regulated still forces local). Deviation: "Generate now" lives in the Digests view (which holds the library model) rather than the Preferences control, so the launcher need not be threaded into Preferences; the Preferences control owns the schedule. Verified: daemon 1098 green (`DigestSchedulerServiceTests` over the plist mapping, incl. the ISO-Sunday-to-launchd-0 case). Acceptance's toggle-installs-agent, disabling-removes-it, and digests-render-in-Library are met; the visual eyeball (menu-bar app not screenshotable) is owner-owed, as the spec notes.

**[DONE] FEAT3-SEGMENT (P2): per-segment speaker reassignment.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Rides FEAT3-UNDO's `SpeakerLabelStore` overlay via its `segments` map (index to label); resolution is per-segment override, else raw speaker, then mapped through the cluster-name table (so a segment reassigned to a named cluster shows the name).

(a) Right-click a transcript segment, "Reassign to..." lists the meeting's other speakers (label-only: no embedding is folded into any roster centroid, so centroids are byte-identical after a reassignment; enrollment stays the explicit "Name this speaker" path), plus "Reset to original label" on a reassigned segment. (b) Multi-select: a plain left-click still seeks; Cmd-click toggles a segment and Shift-click extends a contiguous run (in display order), and a right-click on a selected row reassigns the whole batch in one overlay write. `SegmentSelection` is the pure, unit-tested model. (c) Regenerate/republish are left to the detail menu (offered, not auto-fired).

Design decision (owner-approved, resolving a tension the FEAT3-UNDO overlay-only choice left open): the acceptance's "a subsequent republish reflects it" cannot hold if the overlay stays display-only, so the overlay became a Swift-to-Python contract. New `mp.speaker_overlay` reads `<stem>.speaker_labels.json` and, in `summarize`, re-renders `<stem>.json` through it before summarizing, so a regenerate reflects BOTH reassignments and FEAT3-UNDO namings in the summary body + attendees. The Swift and Python resolutions are byte-identical by construction (documented as a contract in CONVENTIONS + pipeline/CLAUDE.md). Scope note: the reassignment target list is the speakers present in the meeting (covers the diarization-merge case); assigning to a roster person not in the meeting, or a brand-new name, stays the "Name this speaker" path. Verified: pipeline 641 green (ruff + pyright clean), daemon 1094 green (`SegmentSelectionTests` + store batch/chain tests + service + `test_speaker_overlay`). Transcript reassignment UI (incl. modifier-click selection) visual eyeball owner-owed.

**[DONE] FEAT3-UNDO (P2): reversible speaker naming.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. Design decision (owner-approved): overlay-only. Rather than rewriting `<stem>.json` in place (the one-way door), in-app naming now enrolls the voiceprint via `mp roster enroll --no-relabel` (a new flag that folds the embedding into the roster but leaves the transcript untouched) and records the assigned name in a new Swift `SpeakerLabelStore` (`<stem>.speaker_labels.json`, paralleling `TranscriptCorrectionStore`), resolved at display time in `TranscriptLoader.load`. Because the diarization label is never overwritten, an undo always restores it.

(a) The overlay store carries a `labels` map (cluster to name, FEAT3-UNDO) and a forward-compatible `segments` map (index to label, which FEAT3-SEGMENT rides); resolution is segment-override then cluster-name then raw label. (b) A named speaker's transcript context menu offers "Rename..." and "Undo naming"; Undo drops the overlay entry AND un-enrolls the voiceprint (`mp roster forget --name`, a new `PipelineLauncher.rosterForget`) so the voice is not auto-named in later meetings, with the revert applied first and always so the display reverts even if the un-enroll subprocess fails. (c) The naming sheet states plainly that it enrolls a persistent voiceprint affecting future meetings and that it is reversible from the same menu.

Scope note: the CLI `mp roster enroll` keeps its in-place relabel (CLI users have no overlay); the divergence is intentional and was the approved trade for the overlay model. An auto-named speaker (already in the roster at finalize, so its name is baked into `<stem>.json` by the pipeline, not the overlay) is out of the transcript menu's reach in v1; un-enrolling it to stop future auto-naming is FEAT3-MANAGE's roster view. Verified: pipeline 633 green (ruff + pyright clean), daemon 1086 green (new `SpeakerLabelStoreTests` + service naming/undo tests + a Python `--no-relabel` test). Pure local roster + overlay ops, no new engine egress. Transcript naming UI visual eyeball owner-owed.

**[DONE] PIPE6 (P2): re-summarize a recording on a different backend, and recover from a summarize failure.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. As built:

(a) One-shot backend override. `config.with_backend_override` + `config.extract_backend_flag` (validated against `config.BACKENDS`) back a new `mp summarize <transcript> --backend <anthropic|local|auto|apple_intelligence>` and `mp run-all <wav> --backend ...`. The override is applied to `cfg.summarization.backend` AFTER `entry.prepare`, so it wins over the workflow's persisted backend but still flows through `effective_backend`'s regulated/NDA clamp (a `--backend anthropic` on a regulated meeting is still forced local; tested). run-all also applies it before the apple-handoff check so an override to local on an Apple-pinned meeting summarizes locally instead of handing off. The workflow TOML is never rewritten (that stays WF8's job).

(b) Library affordance. The detail `...` menu gains "Re-summarize with... (Local / Anthropic)", and `MeetingLibraryService.regenerateMeeting(stem:backend:)` threads the one-shot backend into `PipelineLauncher.summarize(transcriptMD:backend:)` (a new `PipelineDriver` requirement with a forwarding default so existing fakes are unchanged), then republishes. The `cloudSecretPolicy` gotcha is fixed: it takes a `backendOverride` that wins over the meeting's sidecar backend, so "Re-summarize with Anthropic" on a meeting whose `workflow_backend` is `apple_intelligence` keeps `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` instead of being stripped; regulated still strips both (tested).

(c) Failure recovery. `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer` maps the Foundation Models unsupported-language/locale rejection to a typed `AppleIntelligenceError.unsupportedLanguage` (deterministic), stamping a shared marker into the reason. `Meeting.failureSuggestsLocalReSummarize` recognizes it from `<stem>.error.json` (no schema change; producer and consumer share the marker constant), and the failed-state view leads with "Re-summarize with Local" (which succeeds) instead of a doomed same-backend retry, keeping the plain retry underneath.

Scope decisions: (c) surfaces the one-click local re-run (the spec's "at minimum" option) rather than silently auto-falling Apple to local, so an explicitly-chosen on-device backend is never silently swapped. Apple Intelligence is offered as a failure to recover *from*, not a menu *target* (`mp summarize --backend apple_intelligence` cannot run in Python, it is daemon-only, and stays reachable via a workflow's configured backend), so the "Re-summarize with..." menu lists the two backends Python runs. Verified: pipeline 632 green (ruff + pyright clean), daemon 1077 green. Pure re-run over existing text; no new egress beyond the backend the user explicitly picks. Library affordance + failed-row recovery visual eyeball owner-owed.

**[DONE] LANG1 (P2): summary language fidelity, verify the generated sections and cover the cloud path.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. As built: the LOCAL7 language helpers were promoted out of `summarize_local.py` into a new shared `mp.summary_language` module (public `language_signature`, `expected_summary_language`, `divergent_sections`, `language_reinforcement`, `language_correction_message`). (a) Detection is now per-section over title / summary / decisions / questions / **actions[].task**, replacing the single merged bag that both omitted the action items and let an English majority mask a drifted section. (b) `summarize.summarize()` runs a backend-agnostic post-hoc check after any client answers: if a section diverges it re-asks the same client once with a reinforced directive, keeping the repair only when clean, so the Anthropic path is verified for the first time (it verified nothing before) and a failed or still-divergent repair never costs the already-valid summary. (c) `expected_summary_language` under `auto` now treats a confidently all-Latin transcript (zero Cyrillic, enough letters) as an `en` target, so a Russian action block in an English meeting is an unambiguous divergence; a mixed-script or too-short transcript stays unverified so a code-switched meeting is never force-flattened. (d) `config.example.toml` + README recommend a fixed `summary_language` as the safer setting. LOCAL7's in-client reinforce-and-replay is unchanged in mechanism but now shares the per-section detector, so it catches action-only drift too. New `test_summary_language.py` plus cloud-repair tests in `test_summarize.py` and an action-only-drift test in `test_summarize_local.py`; full pipeline suite 628 green. Pure local verification over text the backend already produced, no new egress.

**[DONE] ARCH4 (P1): a test seam for the daemon spine.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. All four parts landed; `swift test` goes from 1057 to 1071, and the release build is clean.

(a) `SessionHost` (in `SessionHost.swift`) is the surface `MeetingSessionController` sees; its field is now `unowned let coordinator: any SessionHost` and `Coordinator+SessionHost.swift` is the production conformance. Two deviations from the spec, both load-bearing. First, a pure Extract Interface would have vended the concrete subsystems, and then no fake could be built, because `StatusBarController`, `Notifier`, `MeetingRecorder`, `RecordingHUDWindow`, `MeetingPromptWindow`, and `PipelineJobDispatcher` need AppKit, AVFoundation, UserNotifications, or a process spawn. Those six are protocol-typed (`SessionStatusPresenting`, `SessionNotifying`, `SessionRecording`, `SessionHUDPresenting`, `SessionPromptPresenting`, `SessionJobDispatching`); the other eight members the controller reads (`DetectionStateMachine`, `MicGate`, `MeetingLifecycleCoordinator`, `IdleStopBackstop`, `ConsentStore`, `WorkflowStore`, `ConfigStore?`, `MuteLabels`) are already constructible in a test and stay concrete, so the fake uses the real ones and the assertions land on state the controller actually drove. Second, the spec scoped the protocol to "the members read through `coordinator.`", but `beginRecording` called the global `AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for: .audio)` directly, and that single line is what made the permission-denied branch, the one deciding whether a meeting records at all, unreachable. It is now `host.micAuthorizationStatus`. Note also that a protocol requirement cannot be witnessed by a stored property of a different type, so the six are exposed under role names (`statusUI`, `notifications`, `audioRecorder`, `hud`, `prompt`, `jobs`) and Coordinator keeps its own `statusBar` / `notifier` / ... names for the rest of the daemon.

(b) `FakeSessionHost` plus six spies, and `MeetingSessionControllerBranchTests`: 14 tests over the spec's named branches (permission denied, and `.notDetermined` too; dry-run; the second-press in-flight guard, and that it does not latch after a failed start; the workflow override is honoured and cleared; the prompt timeout reaches `promptWindow.present`) plus start-failure routing, cooldown arming, auto-consent, the re-prompt cooldown, and the `never two .recording` invariant ARCHITECTURE.md declares. The coverage was mutation-checked rather than assumed: relaxing the permission test to `micStatus == .denied` fails 2 of the 14, and neutering `guard !recordingStartInFlight` fails 1; the suite is green again on restore.

(c) `LibraryWindowModel` now holds the `MeetingLibraryService` (injected from `Coordinator.init`) and calls it directly; the twelve one-line `library.foo(stem:)` forwarders in `Coordinator+MenuActions` are deleted, along with the twelve identical `NSError(domain: "LibraryWindowModel")` "Coordinator unavailable" literals, replaced by one `Unwired.libraryUnavailable`. Two passthroughs survive on Coordinator (`recentCorrectableMeetings`, `failedMeetingCount`): their caller is `StatusBarController`, which holds a Coordinator and not a service, so deleting them would have moved the middle man rather than removed it. Forcing the `lazy var library` inside `init` is safe: its `enqueue` closure reads the still-nil IUO `jobDispatcher` at call time, not at construction, and the comment on the property now says so.

(d) `MeetingLibraryService.LibraryError: Error, LocalizedError, Equatable` with nine named cases replaces the eleven `NSError(domain:code:)` sites (the spec said 13+; there were 11, and none were Swift `throw`s, they were all `.failure(...)` / `completion(.failure(...))`). The codes it replaces were meaningless: `publishFromPaste` used code 2 for an empty paste and code 1 for a missing transcript, while `keepCandidate` used code 2 for a missing file, so no caller could ever have branched on `.code`. User-facing strings are unchanged, moved verbatim into `errorDescription`. `CONVENTIONS.md` now bans `NSError(domain:)` outright. Acceptance verified: `swift test` constructs the controller against the fake and pins every named branch; the existing 1057 tests stayed green throughout; no `NSError(domain:` remains under the Library service (the one grep hit is the doc comment explaining what it replaced).

**[DONE] TYPE1 (P2): static type checking in pipeline CI.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. pyright (basic mode) is a dev dependency and its own CI step, configured in `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.pyright]` over `src/` only; `tests/` stays out of scope (its fakes are deliberately partial) and ratcheting to strict remains a later decision. `src/` is at 0 errors.

The 26 findings were nearly all real, not annotation noise. The headline ones: `diarize.resolve_speaker_labels`'s untyped `roster` param (named in the spec; now `RosterStore`, imported under `TYPE_CHECKING` because `roster` imports `cosine_similarity` back out of `diarize`); `engine.AnthropicTextClient.complete` filtering content blocks with `getattr(b, "type", None) == "text"`, which defeats the discriminated-union narrowing that a plain `b.type == "text"` gets, so `.text` was being read off ten block types that do not have it; `egress_guard`'s `_orig_sync` / `_orig_async` typed as `None` and then called (the wrappers now read the global at call time, which is what lets the tests stub them, and raise rather than call None); `apply_speaker_labels` passing a possibly-None speaker as a dict key; and `_duration_sec` narrowing `seg.get("end")` on a value it then re-fetched. Two small consolidations fell out: `summarize()` now returns a `SummaryOutput` TypedDict instead of `dict[str, Path | str]` (which is what let `orchestrate` drop a pile of defensive `Path(...)` / `str(... or "")` re-coercions at the call site), and `schemas.Confidence` names the `Literal["low","medium","high"]` that `digest._clamp_confidence` coerces onto.

Two things the spec did not anticipate. (1) pyright reads its interpreter from config, not argv[0], so a bare `.venv/bin/pyright` (how CI invokes ruff and pytest) reported *every* third-party import as unresolved; `venvPath` / `venv` in `[tool.pyright]` fixes it. This was caught by building a CI-identical light venv in a scratch dir rather than trusting the local run, where `uv run` had been exporting `VIRTUAL_ENV` and masking it. (2) CI deliberately never installs `mlx_lm` / `mlx-embeddings` (darwin/arm64 wheels only) and therefore not `huggingface_hub` either, so three lazy imports are unresolvable there. They carry a per-site `# pyright: ignore[reportMissingImports]` rather than a global `reportMissingImports = "none"`, so a genuine typo in any other import still fails the build. Acceptance verified end to end in the CI-identical venv: clean exits 0, an injected `render_summary_md(int)` exits 1.

One behaviour note: tightening `orchestrate`'s run-sidecar write to `s["backend"]` broke `test_orchestrate_tolerates_summarize_without_meta`, which pins that a summarize result missing those keys must not cost the sidecar. The tolerant `.get` was restored (a TypedDict is a static promise Python does not enforce, and that write is best-effort); the test's own docstring, which justified the tolerance by citing the byo and long-meeting paths, was corrected, since those short-circuit before the sidecar write and never reach it.

**[DONE] LOCAL10 (P2): local-engine server lifecycle, no orphans.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. As built: `mp.local_server` owns a `~/Library/Logs/MeetingPipe/mlx-server.json` marker (pid, owner pid, port, model, spawn time, `0600`), written by `LocalSummaryClient._spawn` and cleared by `_terminate_proc` on every clean path. `mp doctor`'s new `check_local_server` reports an orphan without killing it; Swift's `LocalServerReaper` kills one from two triggers: `Coordinator.reapStorage()` at launch (the OriginalsReaper slot the spec named) and, better, `PipelineLauncher`'s termination handler immediately after the watchdog kills a job, which is the case that creates the orphan in the first place. The schema is documented in CONVENTIONS.md as a Python-to-Swift contract, and both sides gate on *process identity* (`ps -o command=` contains `mlx_lm.server`) rather than bare liveness, so a recycled pid is never signalled and a recycled owner pid cannot shield an orphan.

Two corrections to the spec's own premises, both verified in code before building anything. First, "the server is reused warm across runs and must survive its parent `mp` exiting" is not what `_spawn` does: `summarize()`, `engine._complete_local`, `_AutoFallbackClient`, and `diarize_cleanup` all call `close()`, which terminates the server, so a `_spawn`ed server never outlives a *clean* `mp`. The real warm-reuse path is `mp serve-local`, which the daemon spawns directly and owns through a child handle, and which therefore can never be orphaned. `setsid` buys signal isolation, not warm reuse, and the orphan window is exactly "the parent was killed before its `finally` ran". Second, and following from that, spec part (c) is a **NO-GO**: `serve-local` `execvp`s into `mlx_lm.server`, so no idle timer can survive the exec; keeping one would require a supervisor process (the layer `serve-local`'s design comment explicitly rejects) which additionally could not observe the HTTP traffic it would have to call idle. The marker plus a reaper owned by the daemon, the one process that outlives every `mp` and is the one doing the killing, is the right shape. Acceptance met: a watchdog SIGKILL now reaps the server within seconds rather than "beyond the idle window", the next launch reaps anything missed and emits `pipeline`/`local_server_reaped`, warm `serve-local` reuse is untouched (it writes no marker), and doctor reports honestly, including clearing a stale marker whose server already died.

**[DONE] PIPE7 (P2): orchestrate decomposition + one auto-fallback implementation.** Shipped 2026-07-10. Original scope in the ToC row above. (a) `_run_all_inner` lost its five near-identical skip-result literals to a `_skip_result(t, reason, **extra)` helper and five named checks returning dict-or-None (`_check_no_speech`, `_check_suspect_transcript`, `_check_byo`, `_check_too_long`, `_check_apple_handoff`), plus a `_mark_empty` for the duplicated best-effort marker write. `test_orchestrate` passes unmodified, as the spec predicted. The apple hand-off check stays sequenced after `diarize_cleanup` (the daemon summarizes the transcript the sentinel points at, so cleanup must have already rewritten it) and its docstring now says why.

(b) `backend_fallback.run_with_local_fallback` is the single ladder; `summarize._AutoFallbackClient` and `engine.complete_text` both call it and return `(result, backend)`. `diarize_cleanup._select_cleanup_backend` keeps its cheaper key-presence rule, documented in one line, because `run_all` already treats a cleanup failure as non-fatal. **One deliberate semantic change:** engine's `auto` path used to catch bare `Exception`, so a `BadRequestError` or a bug in our own call site silently re-ran on the local model and reported success. The shared ladder catches only the unreachable-or-unwilling-cloud set (connection, timeout, auth, permission, 429, 5xx); a caller bug now propagates. `test_engine.py`'s `test_auto_falls_back_to_local_on_anthropic_failure` pinned the old behaviour with a bare `RuntimeError` and was rewritten to a real transport error, with `test_auto_does_not_fall_back_on_a_caller_bug` added to pin the tightening. New `test_backend_fallback.py` pins the policy once.

(c) `markdown.render_summary_md`, `config.parse_local_endpoint`, and `workflow.read_meta` are public now, and `publish_lan`'s four other private imports from `publish_fs` (`_file_url`, `_load_sidecar`, `_now_iso`, `_stem_from_summary`) were promoted too, since they are the same smell the review named. **The `publish_fs` copy was not verbatim.** Its comment claimed "same rendering as summarize._render_summary_md ... drift would surface as a test diff", but no test compared them and it had drifted: it rendered an action item's due date as `(due X)` where the canonical renderer writes `- due X`, and used one space before the confidence suffix where the canonical one writes two (a Markdown hard break). Deleting the copy therefore changes what the **filesystem and LAN sinks** write into `<stem>.summary.md`; both now match the summarizer, `publish-from-paste`, `digest`, and Obsidian. The sinks' idempotency hash covers the summary text, so the first publish after this change rewrites once and is stable thereafter. No doc pinned the old format.

**[DONE] DOC7 (P1): documentation truth sweep.** The review verified the steering docs mislead the sessions that depend on them. Fix in one pass: (a) ARCHITECTURE.md daemon section: Coordinator is 633 lines, not ~1500; document MeetingSessionController (TECH-ARCH2) and the Coordination/ quartet (DetectionStateMachine, SinkDispatcher, PipelineJobDispatcher, ConfigRefreshCoordinator); (b) ARCHITECTURE.md pipeline section: add the ~17 undocumented modules, at minimum `engine.py` and `egress_guard.py` (the security spine), the AI band (ask, digest, actions, embed_index, rag), publish_lan, voiceprint, roster, glossary, markers, backup/restore; (c) CONVENTIONS.md and daemon/CLAUDE.md (twice: the exemplar list and the where-things-live Recording row) still cite the deleted `SilenceDetector`; replace with live exemplars (`MicGate.decide`, `AudioRetention.decide`), commit acd50d2 cleaned ARCHITECTURE.md only; (d) root CLAUDE.md's "torch / whisperx / mlx don't download" predates the FluidAudio migration; name the actual heavy deps (mlx-lm, mlx-embeddings, soundfile/numpy); (e) README: the BYO paragraph still instructs hand-running `mp publish-from-paste` although the in-app paste box shipped (TECH-UX3); `publish-notion` is documented as the manual step though the daemon moved to the `mp publish` fanout; the two `launchctl kickstart` troubleshooting steps predate menu-bar Quit auto-relaunch; (f) the three stale code comments saying Republish "spawns `mp publish-notion`" (SummaryTab, LibraryWindow, MeetingLibraryService); (g) add `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md` stating the island rules (strict concurrency stays on, no AppKit imports, pure `decide()` pattern, TOMLKit the only dep); (h) add to pipeline/CLAUDE.md: a new subcommand updates ARCHITECTURE.md's table in the same commit. The backlog-process docs (CONVENTIONS' backlog section, this file's Marking a task done) were already rewritten with the archive-on-done flow on 2026-07-10; DOC7 owns the rest. Acceptance: every named drift fixed; a grep for SilenceDetector over the doc set hits only archives and ADR provenance; a session following daemon/CLAUDE.md's index finds only files that exist. Shipped 2026-07-10. Every named drift fixed, each verified against the code first rather than taken on the review's word. Two corrections to the spec's own claims: (b) undercounted, 24 pipeline modules were missing, not 17 (entry.py and publish_cmd.py were added by SEC13 and PIPE1 in the same session); (g) asked for a "no AppKit imports" rule, but `Lifecycle/Signals/WorkspaceSignal.swift` imports AppKit deliberately, for `NSWorkspace`'s app-termination notification and `NSRunningApplication` KVO, which have no Foundation equivalent. The new `MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md` states the rule as it actually is (no UI, and AppKit only where Foundation cannot reach, currently one file) rather than a rule the code already breaks. Also fixed beyond the spec: DOC7 (f)'s three stale `mp publish-notion` comments were corrected in PIPE1's commit, since PIPE1 touched the same call path. Acceptance verified: a grep for SilenceDetector over the live doc set hits only the ARCHITECTURE.md line that says it was superseded, and a script walk of every file named in daemon/CLAUDE.md, MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md, pipeline/CLAUDE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, and CONVENTIONS.md resolves all 154 to files that exist.

**[DONE] PIPE1 (P1): honest publish-failure contract (AUD-14).** Shipped 2026-07-10; the original spec text is the `**PIPE1` entry in [`q4-final.md`](./q4-final.md) (carried from Q4 unchanged). As built: `publish_router.all_sinks_failed` distinguishes "every sink failed" from "no sink ran" (a regulated run whose only sink was Notion is a clean success, not a failure), and `EXIT_PUBLISH_FAILED = 3` is returned by `run-all`, `mp publish`, and `mp publish-from-paste`. `orchestrate` emits `run_failed` with `stage=publish` instead of `run_completed`. Deviation on "resolve the page URL from per-sink sidecars": per-sink sidecars cannot express a run's outcome, because a publisher that raises never writes one and the previous run's `<stem>.notion.json` survives, indistinguishable from a fresh one. `fanout` therefore writes a run-scoped `<stem>.publish.json` (state + landed page URL + per-sink detail, rewritten every run) and Swift reads it through `PublishResult.load`; this also fixes AUD-30's nil-success on a non-Notion workflow and the page-less-sink-ahead-of-Notion bug. `SinkDispatcher.stage(for:)` maps exit 3 to a new `PipelineFailureSidecar.Stage.publish`, whose `retriesFromSummary` drives the stage-aware `retryMeeting`: a publish failure republishes the summary already on disk instead of paying the summarizer twice. Scope added beyond the spec, both load-bearing for the acceptance criteria: `mp publish-from-paste` inherits the same exit contract (the daemon's in-app paste box read its zero exit as "published"), and `republishMeeting` now writes a stage=publish failure sidecar on failure, without which a failed republish left the row looking done and Retry was never offered.

**[DONE] SEC13 (P1): make zero-egress literal at the process boundary.** The guard is honest at the sink layer (armed at all ten sink-capable entry points, verified) but not literal as documented. Two material gaps: (1) it patches in-process httpx only, while `LocalSummaryClient._spawn` launches `mlx_lm.server` with an unscrubbed env and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` appears nowhere in the repo, so a regulated/NDA run with an uncached model downloads it from huggingface.co from inside the child, and huggingface_hub / mlx_embeddings use the requests stack outside the patch; (2) the daemon's SEC5 token strip is knowingly undone by `load_secrets()` refilling from the Keychain, collapsing defense-in-depth to the single httpx layer, with the refilled tokens inherited by the server child.
