Scope: (a) when the guard is armed, spawn local children with a scrubbed env (drop `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `NOTION_TOKEN` / `HF_TOKEN`) plus `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` and `HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1`; an uncached model under regulated then fails closed with an actionable message pointing at `mp prefetch-model` (fail-closed beats silent egress); (b) `load_secrets()` skips the cloud-token Keychain refill when `egress_guard.is_armed()` (every entry point arms before loading, so the ordering already holds); (c) route `publish_notion.py`'s hand-rolled regulated skip through the `effective_sinks` chokepoint so the invariant keeps a single owner (the TECH-ARCH1 promise); (d) extract the `apply_workflow_overrides` + `arm_for_config` + `load_secrets` triple, copy-pasted at 7+ entry points, into one shared entry helper so a future entry point cannot forget the clamp; (e) correct the `config.py` "no transcript can leave the machine" wording: the LAN sink ships summaries and transcripts to a mount by design, so the honest claim is "no cloud egress". Acceptance: under regulated/NDA the mlx server env carries no cloud tokens and no HF fetch occurs (asserted via a fake spawn in tests); an uncached-model regulated run fails with the prefetch message; all entry points route through the shared helper; the existing egress tests stay green. Shipped 2026-07-10. Deviation on (c): `publish_notion`'s skip routes through the new `config.zero_egress(cfg)` predicate rather than `effective_sinks`, because `mp publish-notion` names its sink explicitly and gating it on the configured sink list would silently no-op the command for an obsidian-only install. Same single-owner outcome (the predicate now backs `effective_backend`, `effective_sinks`, `arm_for_config`, `workflow.apply_overrides`, and the publish_notion skip), no behaviour change. `arm()` scrubbing `os.environ` process-wide, rather than only building a clean child env, was added on top of the spec: mlx_embeddings reaches huggingface_hub in-process over `requests`, outside the httpx patch, so the env is the only lever that covers it.

**MIC13 (P1): originals/ retention, reaper, delete cascade.** Carried from Q4 unchanged; the full spec lives in [`q4-final.md`](./q4-final.md) (search the id). Ship note in the ToC row above.

**STOR1 (P1): audio library storage governance.** [DONE] Stereo WAV is ~0.7 GB per hour and `raw/` grows unbounded; MIC13 covers only the `originals/` recovery copies and PERF7 only the event logs. Scope: a per-workflow audio retention policy (keep forever / transcode to FLAC or AAC N days after a terminal published-or-skipped state / drop audio keeping transcript + summary), a reaper sweep sharing MIC13's mechanism (one reaper, two scopes), a Preferences Storage section (library size, per-policy breakdown, HuggingFace model-cache size with an evict-unused-models control, waveform-cache size), and disk numbers in `mp doctor`. Consumers must survive a transcode: playback and the waveform peaks re-derive from the new file (the cache is keyed on size + mtime, so it self-invalidates) and the Library's audio affordances follow the extension. Defaults are conservative: every workflow starts on keep-forever until the owner opts it in, and a policy never touches a non-terminal meeting (`Needs you` members are exempt). Acceptance: a meeting past its window is transcoded or reaped on the next sweep with playback and waveform intact; delete cascades stay correct against MIC13; Preferences and doctor report real sizes; keep-forever workflows are byte-unchanged.

**SEC12 (P1): iCloud/cloud-sync egress check on the library path.** [DONE] The default `~/Documents/Meetings/` sits inside iCloud's Desktop & Documents scope when that setting is on, so every WAV and transcript silently uploads to Apple, an OS-level hole in the zero-egress claim; ADR 0016 already mandates iCloud/Time-Machine exclusion for `originals/`, but the main library has no check. Scope: detect a cloud-synced library root (iCloud Desktop & Documents state, plus Dropbox / Google Drive / OneDrive path patterns), WARN in `mp doctor` and in a Preferences Storage status line naming the provider, and hard-FAIL the doctor check under `regulated_mode` or when any NDA workflow exists; offer an assisted move to a non-synced root (guided, never performed without explicit confirmation) or a per-folder exclusion where the provider supports one. README documents the caveat. Acceptance: a synced library path is flagged with the provider named; regulated + synced is a FAIL, not a note; the assisted move yields a working library at the new root with config updated.

**ASR1 (P1): post-ASR glossary / custom vocabulary.** [DONE] Recurring proper nouns, client and product names, and en/uk code-switched terms are mangled by ASR every meeting, and the damage propagates into summaries, embeddings, ask, and roster display. Scope: a deterministic substitution layer applied at transcript finalize in the pipeline, before summarize/embed ever see the text: a global glossary plus an optional per-workflow table (TOML under `~/.config/meeting-pipe/`, the workflows precedent), case-insensitive whole-word matching that is Unicode-aware (Cyrillic word boundaries), longest-match-first, with applied-substitution counts logged to pipeline events. Transcripts stay write-once: the glossary applies to new meetings at finalize; re-applying to an old meeting is an explicit regenerate, never a mutation. An optional bounded-edit-distance fuzzy stage for names ships behind a flag, off by default (a wrong substitution is worse than a missed one). Any new `uk` entries are verified with the user (the locale note in the delegation section). Acceptance: a glossary term is normalized consistently across transcript, summary, and the embedding index; per-workflow entries apply only to that workflow's meetings; zero egress impact (a pure local transform).

**FEAT8 (P1): in-meeting moment flag.** [DONE] The one in-meeting capability the competitor set has that meeting-pipe lacks, and it fits the register: one global hotkey (configurable, chosen in-task alongside `manual_hotkey`) during a recording stamps a timestamp marker; the HUD acknowledges with a single quiet blink (opacity-only, reduced-motion honoured), no persistent chrome. Markers are written at stop as `<stem>.markers.json` (schema_version stamped, documented in CONVENTIONS, the DOC6 precedent); the transcript tab renders each marker as an anchor chip at its timestamp (click = seek, the existing play-head path); summarize receives the flagged excerpts (the segments spanning each marker) as user-flagged moments with an instruction to reflect them, so capture is LLM-free and deterministic while emphasis is model-side. BYO and long-meeting paste bundles include the marker list. No new egress class: flagged excerpts travel to whichever backend already summarizes the transcript. Acceptance: a hotkey press mid-recording lands a marker in the sidecar; the chip seeks correctly; a flagged moment demonstrably surfaces in the summary; works under regulated/NDA; visual eyeball owner-owed.

**STOR2 (P1): backup + restore manifest.** [DONE] The compounding assets are irreplaceable and scattered: the library (`raw/` + sidecars + `digests/`), `~/.config/meeting-pipe/` (config, workflows, voiceprint, roster, glossary), the corrections corpus in `~/Library/Application Support/MeetingPipe/`, and the Keychain items; caches are rebuildable and excluded. Scope: `mp backup <dir>` writes a dated archive of the non-rebuildable roots plus a manifest naming the Keychain items to re-create (secret values are never exported); `mp restore <archive>` unpacks to the configured roots (per ADR 0003 a manual copy also works, and the runbook says so); `mp doctor` reports last-backup age when a backup marker exists; a README migration runbook (new Mac: install, restore, re-add the Keychain items, done). Acceptance: a backup restored into a scratch HOME yields a working library, workflows, roster, and corrections state; the manifest names every Keychain item; doctor reports backup age; the runbook is verified by walking it.

**AI6 (P2): recurring-series continuity.** [DONE] Summaries of recurring meetings re-extract known context and lose the thread between sessions. Scope: at summarize time, inject the same workflow's previous meeting as a bounded context block (its decisions plus its still-open actions, post-AI1 resolved state), with instructions to mark carried-over items as carried over rather than re-extracting them as new discoveries. The workflow is the series key (explicit, already resolved per meeting); no fuzzy series inference. Prompt-only in v1, no schema change; the block is bounded (~1K tokens, latest prior meeting only) and assembled from disk, so cloud and local backends get it identically and the egress profile is unchanged. The first meeting in a workflow is a clean no-op. Acceptance: the second meeting in a workflow produces a summary that acknowledges the carried-over open action instead of re-listing it as new (dogfoodable via `mp dogfood`); regulated/NDA behaviour unchanged.

**[DONE] AI7 (P2): commitments loop.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Open actions resurface only if the owner opens Facts or reads a digest, so commitments rot silently between digests; and recurring meetings restate the same commitments, so Facts and digests accumulate near-duplicates and resolving one leaves its clones open. Scope: an amber overdue count on the Facts rail row reusing the needsYou badge mechanics (AI1's aging already computes overdue from ISO due dates); embedding-cluster dedupe of action items across a workflow's meetings (the on-device embedding index already exists) with resolve-the-cluster semantics so marking one instance done resolves its restatements. Local-only per the AI/DV rule. Acceptance: an overdue action shows an amber count without opening Facts; a restated standup commitment groups with its prior instances and resolves once.

**Shipped 2026-07-23, both acceptance criteria met.** *Badge.* `ScopeCounts` gained `factsOverdue` (overlaid via `with(factsOverdue:)` rather than folded into `build`, so a changed count never re-runs the O(meetings x scopes) bucketing) and `.facts` now takes the same filled amber `warning600` capsule `.needsYou` wears. It counts **clusters, not instances** (a promise restated five times is one thing that is late) and uses the cue the row already paints amber, i.e. overdue *or due today*, deliberately a wider set than `mp actions --overdue`, so the badge and the amber rows can never disagree; the row stays bare at zero, since this is an attention cue and not an inventory. Both attention pills gained a VoiceOver label (`ScopeAttentionLabel`), because a bare number read aloud says nothing.

*Dedupe.* New `pipeline/src/mp/action_clusters.py` embeds each open action's task with the AI3 on-device embedder (`embed_index.default_embedder`, MLX multilingual-e5-small in production, the shipped hashing fallback on CI) and merges near-duplicates greedily against a running re-normalized centroid. Three decisions the spec left open: the **workflow is the series key** (AI6's explicit rule, no fuzzy series inference), so two series never merge and an untagged meeting's action is always a singleton; **two named owners never merge** (a hard gate beside the similarity score, since e5 alone would fold "Sam sends the deck" into "Alex sends the deck"), while an unattributed action stays compatible with anything; and the **threshold follows the embedder family** (0.90 semantic / 0.72 hashing, tunable with `--cluster-threshold`) because e5 similarities sit high and compressed while the hashing fallback is lexical on a much wider scale, and no single constant serves both. Both defaults sit conservative: an under-merge leaves a duplicate row, an over-merge silently resolves a commitment the owner never finished. The whole path is fail-open: MLX absent, a cold model fetch clamped by the egress guard, or a missing `mp` all degrade to singleton clusters rather than losing actions.

*Surface.* `mp actions` gained `--cluster` / `--cluster-threshold` / `--out`, carries the meeting's `workflow` on every row, and under `--cluster` prints one line per commitment tagged `restated Nx` (the newest restatement represents the series). In the app, the Facts open-actions section became a list of commitments: one row per cluster, a `restated Nx` label whose tooltip names the meetings behind it, and a mark-done that resolves every instance across every summary file at once (grouped per file so two instances in one summary both land).

*Structure.* The aggregation moved out of `FactsView` into `Facts.swift` (`OpenActionFact`, `DecisionFact`, `ActionCluster`, `FactsLoader`, `ActionClusterBuilder`, `FactsResolver`) owned by `LibraryRootView`, with `FactsView` reduced to a pure renderer over a `FactsSnapshot`. That was load-bearing, not tidying: the badge has to be right whether or not Facts is the active scope, and a second aggregation would have drifted from the one the rows show. Cluster ids key on `(stem, task)` rather than the array index, because the Swift and Python decoders skip a malformed action differently and their indices can drift. The subprocess spawns only when the open-action key set gains something the last pass never saw, so resolving a commitment (which strictly *removes* keys) never respawns an embedding-model load; a failure marks the keys seen too, so an unavailable pipeline degrades once instead of retrying on every revision bump. A cluster's deadline is the representative's `due`, falling back to the earliest any instance carried, because a series that restates a promise without repeating the date has not dropped the date.

*Posture.* `mp actions` now calls `entry.prepare(secrets=False)` and left `ENTRY_CONTRACT_EXEMPT`: with `--cluster` it can load an embedding model, so it is no longer a pure read of files on disk and a regulated Mac should clamp the cold fetch rather than rely on the flag going unused. No new config key, no new dependency, no new sidecar (the cluster payload is a temp file the daemon deletes, the `mp ask --out` precedent).

Verified: pipeline 951 pytest green (14 new in `test_action_clusters.py`), ruff + pyright clean; daemon 1449 `swift test` green (16 new in `FactsClusterTests`), debug + release builds clean, CI5 truth fences clean. Docs synced: README (rail description + `mp actions --cluster`), ARCHITECTURE (subcommand table, module list, Library UI map), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (Library UI index), and `design/ui_kits/macos_app/SummaryLibrary.jsx` (`SLInsightRow` carried a now-false "no count" comment; it gained the amber overdue pill, and the missing Digests row was added in passing). CONVENTIONS needed nothing: no schema, category, or pattern changed, and the two new event actions ride the existing `coordinator` category, which is ungoverned by design. Owner-owed: the usual rendered eyeball, plus a real-corpus sanity check that 0.90 is the right floor for the owner's meetings (`mp actions --open --cluster --cluster-threshold 0.85` to compare).

**LOCAL7 (P2): local summaries honor summary_language on non-English meetings.** [DONE] The engine comparison found the local backends ignore `summary_language = auto` on Ukrainian: the 7B drifts uk to en, the 14B uk to ru; only Anthropic preserves Ukrainian. Strengthen the local language directive (`summarize_local` plus the Apple Intelligence instructions) and add a cheap detected-language-mismatch retry so a uk transcript yields a uk (or the configured-language) summary. Shipped: `summarize_local.py` reinforces the resolved target right before the transcript and, after the fact, detects the summary's language (a tiny script + uk/ru-signature detector) and replays once when it disagrees; the target is threaded from `summary_language` and, for `auto`, from a Cyrillic read of the transcript, with non-verifiable Latin codes (de, es, ...) left untouched so no bogus retry fires. `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.languageDirective` was strengthened to the same field-by-field, non-negotiable form. On-device MLX efficacy on real Ukrainian meetings is owner-owed dogfood (the fix is unit-tested structurally).

**LOCAL8 (P2): Apple Intelligence, experimental gate + viability fixes.** [DONE] The engine comparison showed the macOS 26 Apple Intelligence backend is native/free/zero-egress but not usable as a daily summarizer today: its 4096-token context forces heavy chunking (minutes/meeting), it echoes raw dialogue on short meetings, ignores the bullet cap (~11 vs 5), mislabels Ukrainian, and 1/5 failed. Keep the code but mark it experimental / not-recommended in Preferences (so it is not silently chosen), and track the fixes that make it viable: LOCAL3 (reduce + Cyrillic window, shipped in Q5), LOCAL7 (language), bullet-cap adherence, the short-meeting transcript-echo. Revisit-when: those land OR Apple widens the system-model context. Owner kept it (not dropped) as the only native zero-egress path and the I7 trigger. Shipped: the Preferences backend picker labels it `Apple (experimental)` and the footer states plainly it is experimental / not recommended for daily use with the caveats; the instruction/reduce prompts now cap summary bullets at 5 and forbid echoing raw dialogue; LOCAL3 and LOCAL7 already landed, so the only open revisit condition is Apple widening the system-model context. On-device efficacy stays owner-owed dogfood (SUM1-APPLE precedent).

**UX18 (P2): in-app paste completion for BYO / long-meeting bundles.** [CLOSED 2026-07-10, not implemented] The 2026-07-10 architecture review found the premise stale: `SummaryTab.swift` already renders an in-app "Paste your summary" editor with inline validation errors and a "Save & publish" action wired through `publishFromPaste` (TECH-UX3, its comment reads "no terminal command"), covering both BYO and long-meeting rows; the egress guard and workflow overlay ride the same publish-from-paste path the spec asked for. The remaining delta against the spec below (a sheet presentation instead of the inline pane) was judged not worth a task. Original spec for the trail: the paste-pending detail pane gains "Paste summary...", opening a sheet (raw markdown box, parsed through publish-from-paste's existing parser into the typed model, with inline validation errors); on confirm it publishes through the PIPE2 fanout with the workflow overlay applied and the egress guard armed, exactly like the CLI path, which stays supported. DSN22's "Reveal bundle" verb remains the manual escape hatch.

**[DONE] MIC15 (P1): capture-time input-device identity + dead-mic warning.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above; the trigger was meeting `20260707-150057`, a Teams-in-Safari call that recorded the owner's voice as 4m30s of noise floor (a Bluetooth headset idle in A2DP was the system default input) while system audio was clean, with no warning. Root cause is structural: the daemon captures `AVAudioEngine.inputNode`, which macOS binds to the System-Settings default input, and there is no macOS API to read the meeting client's own chosen device, so the fix is to record what we captured and catch the mismatch, not to follow the meeting's device. Three layers, all shipped. **(a) Device identity.** New pure `InputDeviceResolver` + `InputDeviceIdentity` in `MeetingPipeCore/Infra/` (CoreAudio, the island's expected subject matter), injectable string/uint32/float64 read seams over `AudioObjectGetPropertyData` mirroring `HALSystemMuteProbe`'s default seams, so the mapping + transport bucketing (built-in/bluetooth/usb/aggregate/other/unknown) are unit-tested without hardware. `MeetingRecorder.start()` resolves it right after the input format read, logs it always (`recorder`/`input_device_resolved` with name/uid/transport/sample-rate), and stashes it for `stop()`; `MeetingSessionController` persists the name as the top-level `mic_device_name` meta key and the Audio tab shows "Recorded with ...". **(b) The dead-mic warning (the must-have).** A whole-recording coverage aggregate (total + un-muted seconds, loudest un-muted per-buffer RMS) accumulated allocation-free on the render thread from the RMS the gate already computes, snapshotted at stop; a pure `MicCoverageWarning.evaluate` (the mirror of `RemoteAudioWarning`) fires `micRecordedNothing` when system audio was present the whole call, the recording is >60s, an un-muted span >=30s exists (this is the muted-whole-meeting exclusion, mode-independent because the coverage tally runs before the capture-mode switch), and the loudest un-muted RMS never crossed a -65 dBFS floor. On fire: a `notifyMicRecordedNothing` Notifier alert ("Your mic recorded almost nothing. Check System Settings, Sound, Input"), a `recorder`/`mic_recorded_nothing` event, and the top-level `mic_silent` meta key that drives a "Mic silent" `.warning` pill on the Library row (reusing the NDA/regulated flag pipeline, no new sidecar type). **(c) Start-time mismatch nudge.** END6's `kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere` read was never implemented (it is confounded on the end side because we hold the device); MIC15 uses it at start, before `recorder.start` arms the engine, enumerating input devices and warning (`notifyInputDeviceMismatch` + `input_device_mismatch_warned`) when the about-to-record default input sits idle while another input is active. Contract work: `MeetingMetaSidecar.schemaVersion` 1->2, the two informational keys added under the skip-empty guard (a device name alone never springs a sidecar into existence), the four CI2 golden fixtures regenerated and the Python + Swift version assertions bumped; `mp.workflow.apply_overrides` needs no change (fail-open on the informational keys), pinned by a Swift-only `test_mic_keys_present_only_when_set`. Deviation from the plan worth noting: the coverage tally lives in the recorder render thread (mode-independent, reuses the per-buffer RMS) rather than a parallel verdict-stream tally in the controller. Verified: daemon 1169 green (new `InputDeviceIdentityTests`, `MicCoverageWarningTests`, extended `MetaContractFixtureTests`), pipeline ruff + `test_workflow_overlay` green. Owner-owed: the live notification firing on a real Mac and layer (c)'s real mismatched-device runtime (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here), plus a judgment call on whether the -65 dBFS floor / 30 s / 60 s thresholds want tuning against real recordings.

**[DONE] MIC14 (P2): off-the-record manual redaction spans.** Shipped 2026-07-11. Original scope in the ToC row above. Mute redaction only protected app-muted spans; the human cases (a side conversation, "this next part stays out") had no affordance. A fourth global hotkey (`off_the_record_hotkey`, default `ctrl+option+o`, threaded through `Config`/`ConfigStore`/`Coordinator` with the same collision-dedup guard as the flag-moment hotkey) toggles an off-the-record span, `MeetingSessionController.toggleOffTheRecord` (a no-op when idle, like flag-moment). The span rides the existing mute-timeline machinery: `MuteTimeline.Span` gained a `source` field (`mute` | `manual`), the file `version` bumped 1->2 with a tolerant decoder (an absent `source` in a v1 file reads as `mute`), source-aware coalescing so a manual span never merges with an abutting auto span, and the write hardened to atomic since the file is now privacy-load-bearing even in default capture-first. Two invariants held. (1) A manual span is explicit intent, so `MuteRedactor` splits the timeline by source and always redacts manual spans, exempt from the MIC12 per-span speech-bearing withhold (which exists only to guard against a wrong AUTO oracle); and the stop-time write was broadened so a manual span produces a manual-only timeline even under the default `.captureFirst` (where auto mute spans stay un-redacted). (2) Under the regulated/NDA real-time gate, `MicGateWriter.apply` gained a `forceMuted` override so the render thread zero-fills the span live (no off-record audio at rest), driven by a `manualOffRecord` flag added to the recorder's render-thread `gateAccess` lock and set via `setManualOffTheRecord` on the main thread. The HUD shows a persistent, truthful "Off the record" state (retitles + retints the "Recording" label and widens the pill via the degraded-banner resize idiom) so it cannot be forgotten; an open span auto-closes at stop (`muteTimeline.finalize`). The sharpest edge, an orphan leak, is closed: a default capture-first recording that went off-record but crashed before the manual-only timeline was written would have auto-published the off-record audio, so a `<stem>.offrecord` start-marker (written the moment the toggle first turns on, before any off-record audio exists on disk, removed at a clean stop) makes `OrphanRecordingRecovery.shouldQuarantine` treat such a recording exactly like a redaction-opt-in orphan whose timeline was lost. New `SessionHost`/`FakeSessionHost` seam methods (`setManualOffTheRecord`, `setOffTheRecord`) and `recorder`-category events (`off_the_record_started`/`_stopped`/`_ignored`). Verified: daemon 1189 green (source round-trip + coalescing + backward-compat decode in `MuteTimelineTests`, the mirror "manual span over a live mic IS redacted" ffmpeg test in `MuteRedactorTests`, `forceMuted` in `MicGateWriterTests`, the off-record-marker quarantine in `OrphanRecordingRecoveryTests`, the toggle drive + no-op-when-idle in the branch tests, `testOffTheRecordDefaultParses`). CONVENTIONS (sidecar section, the `source` field + `.offrecord` marker + the CI2 version-exemption note), ARCHITECTURE (file table + HotkeyManager), README, and config.example.toml updated. Owner-owed: the HUD "Off the record" look + hotkey feel on a real Mac (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here).

**[DONE] UX16 (P2): one search story, merged on FTS5.** Shipped 2026-07-12. Original scope in the ToC row above; the decision (merge, not label-and-cross-teach) was recorded 2026-07-05, sequenced after DSN27 (shipped). Before UX16 there were two search surfaces (the Library filter bar's free-text field and the menu-bar Quick Find panel) over one in-memory corpus (`Meeting.searchableText`: title + summary, no transcripts). UX16 merges them onto a single SQLite FTS5 index over transcripts + summaries + titles. Three new files: `SearchQuery` (pure, unit-tested: turns free text into a safe `token*` prefix MATCH expression, Unicode/Cyrillic-aware via the `unicode61` tokenizer), `SearchIndex` (a thin `SQLite3` FTS5 wrapper, one connection on a serial queue, `user_version` invalidation, WAL), and `SearchIndexer` (keeps the index in sync with `MeetingStore`). The system `SQLite3` module is used directly, so **no package dependency** was added (verified the SDK exposes `SQLite3.modulemap` + FTS5 is compiled into the Apple system SQLite; `SearchIndexTests` proves it end-to-end at runtime, including a Cyrillic query). The index is a rebuildable cache under `~/Library/Caches/MeetingPipe/search/` (ADR 0003: an index over files, not a database of record; the `WaveformPeaksLoader` precedent), built incrementally off `MeetingStore`'s existing `revision` bump + a per-stem file-mtime signature, with the transcript body read OFF the scan + main paths (preserving the no-transcript-reparse scan perf). The merge: `MeetingFilterEngine.apply` (the filter bar) and `QuickFindRanker.rank` (Quick Find) each gained an `ftsMatches` parameter (defaulted, so every existing test drives them unchanged) and UNION the FTS candidate stems with the in-memory match, so a title/summary hit works immediately while the index catches up and a transcript-only hit is added on top (Quick Find surfaces it below the field matches with a transcript baseline). The list re-derives when a background index build completes (a mirrored `searchIndexRevision` in the memoization key), and a deleted meeting never surfaces because the FTS matches are intersected with the scoped in-memory meetings, so no explicit de-index hook was wired (a retention-`drop` meeting keeps its meta + summary and stays correctly searchable, which is why de-indexing is NOT tied to the waveform-cache purge). Quick Find was rebound from Cmd+Shift+F to **Cmd+K** (owner's call). The retired-`TECH-A3` doc pointers were repaired (`ARCHITECTURE.md` glossary + a new *Search index* entry, and the six code comments), and README + `daemon/CLAUDE.md` updated. Verified: daemon 1204 green (`SearchQueryTests`, `SearchIndexTests` incl. reopen-persistence + Cyrillic + reindex-replaces, the FTS-merge cases in `MeetingFilterTests`, the transcript-baseline case in `QuickFindRankerTests`). Owner-owed: the live feel of Cmd+K + search-as-you-type over the real library (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here), and confirming the background index build is imperceptible at the owner's library size.

**[DONE] WF7 (P3): per-workflow summary sections.** Shipped 2026-07-12, promoted on demand. Workflows could season extraction (`context_prompt`) but not change the output shape; WF7 lets a workflow add named sections on top of the standard summary. **The data model (tolerant, both sides).** New pydantic `ExtraSection {name, content: list[str]}` and an optional `MeetingSummary.extra_sections` (default empty, so every legacy `<stem>.summary.json` and every non-configuring workflow round-trips unchanged), mirrored by a tolerant Swift `MeetingSummary.ExtraSection`. **Definition -> sidecar -> overlay.** A `WorkflowExtraSection {id, name, instruction}` on the Swift `Workflow` (mirroring `WorkflowMatchingRule`), persisted as `[[extra_sections]]` in the workflow TOML (`WorkflowStore` encode/decode, omitted when empty like `[retention]`), and stamped into `<stem>.meta.json` as `workflow_extra_sections` (omitted when the workflow defines none; `schema_version` 2 -> 3, the four CI2 golden fixtures + the Swift `MetaContractFixtureTests` version test + the Python `test_workflow_overlay` version assertion bumped, plus a Swift present-only-when-set test in the MIC15 idiom). `apply_overrides` reads it into a new `summarization.extra_sections` (`ExtraSectionSpec {name, instruction}`), filtering half-filled rows. **Production (the two real backends).** The generic-field approach keeps the `SummaryClient` signature fixed: `extra_sections` is a new optional array on `SUMMARY_TOOL` (not `required`, so a backend that fills nothing still validates), and `_load_system_prompt` injects an `{extra_sections_directive}` that lists the requested sections by name+instruction (or tells the model to leave the field empty). Anthropic fills it via the tool schema; the local MLX backend rides the same shared `SUMMARY_TOOL` + system prompt through `_augment_with_schema`, so both produce it with no per-backend code. **Rendering (every publisher + read-only editor).** `markdown.render_summary_md` (the shared renderer -> fs/lan sinks + digest), `publish_notion._build_blocks` (an `_h2` + bullets per section), and the `publish_obsidian` template (a new `{extra_sections}` placeholder) render each non-empty section after the standard ones; on the Swift side `SummaryRenderedView` renders them read-only in the Library, and `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.renderMarkdown` mirrors `render_summary_md` for the CI3 golden parity (a new `extra_sections` case added to `summary-md-golden.json`, asserted by both suites). The correction editor renders them read-only but `makeCorrectedSummary` + `MeetingSummary.jsonObject()` carry them through, so an edit never drops them. **BYO paste round-trip.** `publish_from_paste` collects any H2 section that is not one of the standard ones (transcript excluded) into `extra_sections`, preserving the original-case name, so a hand-written summary with a `## Billable follow-ups` block survives. Verified: 722 pipeline green (13 new `test_wf7_extra_sections` + the CI3 parity case + the overlay/version bumps; ruff + pyright clean) and 1220 daemon green (WorkflowStore round-trip + omit-when-empty, MeetingSummary decode + jsonObject preservation, the CI2 present-only-when-set test, the CI3 case). Deviation worth noting: the spec implied every backend produces the sections, but Apple Intelligence is the experimental backend (LOCAL8) and its `schemaDirective` is a hand-written prompt string; wiring it to *generate* extra sections is deferred (it renders them if present, for parity, but does not request them), so a workflow with extra sections on the Apple backend produces none until that follow-up. Editing extra sections in the correction editor is out of scope by the spec ("read-only in the editor at first"). Owner-owed: the workflow-editor UI + the Library read-only render on a real Mac (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here).

**[DONE] FEAT9 (P3): merge fragmented recordings.** Shipped 2026-07-12, promoted on demand (the owner ran `/tech-task FEAT9`). A dropped-and-rejoined call leaves two stems, two summaries, two pages; FEAT9 rejoins them into one meeting. **Pipeline.** New `mp merge-meetings <primary.wav> <fragment.wav>...` (`merge_meetings.py`): the first arg is the primary (its stem, page, and sidecars survive), the rest are the later fragments in chronological order. A pure `merge_transcripts(fragments, durations)` concatenates the already-final transcripts onto one timeline, offsetting each fragment's segment timestamps by the prior fragments' *audio* duration (not their transcript end, which trails into silence) and inserting a `{"kind":"gap"}` marker segment at each boundary; `render_markdown` now renders a gap segment as a `---` + italic divider note and excludes it from the speaker counts, so the summarizer sees an explicit break and never mistakes the marker for an attendee. Audio is concatenated with `soundfile` block-streaming (memory-safe for a multi-GB merge) into a `.merging.tmp`, its duration verified against the sum of the inputs before `os.replace` onto the primary (the REC1 verified-outcome rule: nothing destroyed until the merged artifact is proven good), then `summarize` + `publish_router.fanout` re-summarize and republish under the primary stem (the upsert path + the exit-3 all-sinks-failed contract, inherited for free). A `merged_from` provenance key on the transcript is an idempotency guard: a retry after a publish-stage failure re-summarizes/republishes instead of concatenating the audio a second time. `entry.prepare(cfg, primary)` arms the egress guard on the primary's workflow, which the daemon guardrail guarantees every fragment shares. **Deviation from the spec, flagged:** the spec named ffmpeg for the concat, but the pipeline has never shelled ffmpeg; `soundfile` (already a dep) gives the same verified-outcome guard with fewer moving parts, so the concat is `soundfile` block-streaming. **Daemon (de-CLI: UI ships in the same task).** A "Merge into one meeting..." card in the existing multi-select `BatchActionsPane`, gated by a new pure `MeetingMergeEligibility.decide` (>=2 selected, all `.done`, all with audio, all the same workflow, all the same zero-egress posture, else the card explains why it can't and offers no button); the primary is the earliest by start time. It spawns via a new `PipelineDriver.mergeMeetings` / `PipelineLauncher.mergeMeetings` (the shared `runMP`, anchored on the primary so `cloudSecretPolicy` matches the pipeline's arming), and `MeetingLibraryService.mergeMeetings` soft-deletes each fragment after a clean run (their content now lives under the primary's stem and page, the note surfaced on the pane's completion). `Meeting` gained a `workflowID` (lifted from `<stem>.meta.json["workflow_id"]`) so the same-workflow gate compares stable identity, not the mutable display name. **Verified:** 10 `test_merge_meetings.py` (pure offset/gap logic, the gap render, the format-mismatch + short-output guards, the orchestration with faked concat, the idempotency guard, and a `soundfile`-gated real-audio end-to-end that concatenates two real WAVs) + 1215 daemon (`MeetingMergeEligibilityTests` + 3 service merge tests) green; pipeline ruff + pyright clean, full pipeline 707 + the real-audio test. The real-audio test earned its place: it caught a bug the fakes missed (`soundfile` cannot infer the output format from the `.merging.tmp` extension), fixed by passing `format=` explicitly. ARCHITECTURE subcommand table + README batch-actions section updated. Owner-owed: an end-to-end run on a real dropped call (real subprocess spawn + continuous-playback confirmation in the Audio tab + one Notion page) and the Library click-through eyeball, since the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here.

**[DONE] DET4 (P1): adapter registry as data + coverage fence + user overlay.** Shipped 2026-07-12, first of the detection-coverage band (`/tech-task DET1, DET3, DET4, DET5`). The whitelist (`meeting_apps.toml`) and the adapter/recognizer layer had drifted silently, and nothing caught it. **The registry.** A new `MeetingAppRegistry` (MeetingPipe target) is the single source of truth for the four discovery bundle lists: it parses the bundled `meeting_apps.toml` and, layered on top, an optional `~/.config/meeting-pipe/meeting_apps.toml` user overlay (unioned, read at launch like `config.toml`), exposing `nativeBundles` / `browserBundles` / `browserURLFragments` / `micPlausibleBundles`. `MeetingSourceScanner` now reads its sets from `MeetingAppRegistry.shared` (its private `loadMeetingApps` + `MeetingApps` struct deleted, `import TOMLKit` dropped), and the `Coordinator` builds the `BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter` over `MeetingAppRegistry.shared.browserBundles`, so a browser discovery enumerates always has a matching lifecycle adapter (and an overlay browser gets full coverage on relaunch). **Drift fixed.** (a) `com.cisco.spark` (the unified Webex App) was in the Webex adapter + `appNameByBundle` but absent from `[native]`, so it was never enumerated: added to the TOML, and given a recognizer branch (the scanner's `isActiveMeetingWindow` Webex case now covers both Webex bundles and routes through the shared `MeetingTitlePatterns.webex`, so spark titles match instead of falling through to `default -> false`). (b) Brave/Vivaldi/Opera/Kagi/Chrome-Canary were in `[browser.bundles]` but not in the browser adapter's hardcoded 5-bundle set, so they were discovered then dropped into a `no_adapter_for_context` no-op (discovered, never prompted): the adapter's set is now `BrowserMeetingLifecycleAdapter.defaultBrowserBundleIDs`, the canonical 10-bundle constant the fence pins the TOML to, and `NoOpMuteConfig.browserBundleIDs` (a third copy) was collapsed onto it. (c) `com.skype.skype` and `com.google.meet` were dead rows (Skype is EOL; there is no real native `com.google.meet` bundle, Meet is browser-only): removed from the TOML along with their now-unreachable `isActiveMeetingWindow` branches. **The `mic_plausible` tier (reconciling DET4 with DET1).** DET4's "add FaceTime/Discord/WhatsApp/Telegram rows" and DET1's "these are the catch-all's job" are the same tension's two ends: none of the four expose Teams/Zoom-style AX call controls or a clean meeting-title pattern, so making them whitelist natives would trip the very fence DET4 adds. They ship instead as a new `[mic_plausible]` TOML table (adapterless by design), which DET1's mic-in-use tier reads to name a catch-all prompt; the coverage fence deliberately exempts it. **The fence.** `MeetingAppRegistryFenceTests` (8 tests) asserts: every `[native]` bundle has a `NativeLifecycleConfig` (new `NativeLifecycleConfig.all`) + an `appNameByBundle` mute/leave predicate + an `isActiveMeetingWindow` recognizer branch (via a representative-title map, so a native added without a branch fails the test); `[browser.bundles]` equals `defaultBrowserBundleIDs`; `mic_plausible` is non-empty and disjoint from the whitelist; the overlay unions over (not replaces) the defaults; and the specific pre-DET4 drift (spark discoverable, dead rows gone) stays fixed. It goes red on the next vendor rename or additive edit. **Owner-approved deviations (surfaced in the plan before implementation).** (1) "Leave/mute needles as data" is scoped to the *registry* (bundle lists + the adapter/recognizer mapping the fence covers), not a TOML externalization of the AX predicates: `matchesLeave` / `matchesMute` / `callingControlsToolbarNeedles` carry locale word-boundary logic and Teams-specific exact-match rules (`isTeamsCallLeaveLabel`) that are not clean data, and the acceptance (fence red on drift; spark prompts; overlay works) does not require it. (2) The four consumer apps are the fence-exempt `mic_plausible` tier, not whitelist natives, per the reconciliation above. **Verified:** `swift build` clean; the fence (8), `MeetingSourceScannerTests`, `MeetingSourceScorerTests`, and the lifecycle-adapter suites green (70 in the filtered run). ARCHITECTURE (detection subsystem list + on-disk overlay row), README (troubleshooting: teach an app via the overlay), and `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (detection quick-index) updated. Owner-owed: a live `com.cisco.spark` call and an overlay-added app confirmed prompting on a real Mac (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here).

**[DONE] DET5 (P1): start-detection evidence hardening.** Shipped 2026-07-12, second of the detection-coverage band. Native start detection was single-gated on exact window-title text (the control-AX walk ran only on `titleMatch` or the dead `processAudioActive` leg), so a Zoom build that renamed its window silently lost start detection; and the trustworthy-browser-title exemption applied only to a lone candidate, so an idle Teams chat window could suppress a real Meet call in a contested scan. **What shipped.** (1) *Walk-gate decouple.* `shouldWalkControlAX` gained `isFrontmost` and `isLoneNative` (computed once per scan in `enumerateCandidates`): a lone native, or the frontmost native, now runs the control-AX walk even without a title match, so a renamed-window call is still evidenced. PERF6's cost gate still bites for an idle backgrounded app in a multi-native scan; Webex still always walks. (2) *Shared matcher table.* The scanner's `isActiveMeetingWindow` now routes slack + webex through the shared `MeetingTitlePatterns` (and `MeetingTitlePatterns.slackHuddle` was made word-boundary to match the scanner, so `team-huddles` no longer matches on either side); webex keeps a scanner-side idle-launcher guard (`"webex"`/`"cisco webex"` rejected) because the start-side recognizer must be stricter than the end-side adapter, which tolerates a bare "Webex" reading live. A `MeetingAppRegistryFenceTests` parity test asserts the scanner recognizer and the adapter matcher both accept a representative live title per vendor, so they cannot diverge. (3) *Contested browser exemption.* `MeetingSourceScorer.pickBest`'s contested branch now lets a trustworthy-title browser win unless a CONFIDENT native ties-or-beats it, fixing the filed idle-Teams-suppresses-Meet regression. (4) *`candidate_dropped` telemetry.* The scanner returns `droppedCandidates` (running apps with a signal that did not win) and `MeetingDiscoveryWatcher` emits a throttled `detector`/`candidate_dropped` event (signature keyed on the drop set + reason so a reason-flip re-logs) for DET3's auditor. **The adversarial-review save (why the scorer is shaped this way).** Two `/tech-task`-internal review workflows ran over the uncommitted diff. Round 1 confirmed 3 real defects, two of them high: the DET5-draft **localized bare-verb leave needles** (uk `вийти`/`покинути`, which also mean *sign out* / *leave channel*) fabricated a native `leaveButton` on an idle app that the new walk-gate now walks, which then (a) false-prompted a lone idle app and, worse, (b) blocked the contested-browser exemption and **silently suppressed a real title-only browser meeting** (a missed recording, the worst failure class). Fixes: the localized needles were **reverted entirely** (locale extension deferred until there is a MuteLabels-style live-AX validator for leave/toolbar labels, the same bar MuteLabels itself holds), and a new `isConfidentNativeMeeting` (calling-controls toolbar, which non-meeting shells never carry, OR >=2 distinct signals) became the bar for both the lone-native prompt and the contested rival check, so a single stale-prone control (a lingering Leave button, a hub mute toggle) can neither prompt nor suppress. Round 2 confirmed those closures and caught a **fix-induced regression**: the raised bar dropped a real Webex call that exposes only its Leave button, because Webex is structurally excluded from the audio probe (so it can never reach a second signal from audio) and its toolbar label is an unreliable English guess. Fix: a `MeetingSourceCandidate.hasAudioLeg` flag (false for the `webexBundleIDs` set) keeps DET4's single-corroborator bar for the audio-excluded natives in the *lone* branch only (DET5's walk-gate change never applied to Webex, which always walked, so DET5 must not change its detection); the *contested* rival check stays strict for every native, which actually improves Webex-vs-browser over DET4. **Owner-approved deviations / deferrals.** Locale leave/toolbar needles deferred (above). The stretch item (reset per-bundle cooldown/latch on a meeting-window title-hash change across a discovery gap, the `SkippedMeetingLatch` back-to-back blind spot) was **not implemented** and is not part of the acceptance; it stays a future refinement. **Verified:** `swift build` clean; full daemon suite 1243 green, including the new walk-gate cases, the contested-exemption + confident-native + Webex-lenient-bar scorer tests, the recognizer/matcher parity test, and the throttle. Docs checked: the changes are internal detection logic (no module/contract/subcommand rename), and the new `candidate_dropped` / (DET3's) `mic_busy_*` events are documented where DET3's `mp analyze-detection` consumes them, so ARCHITECTURE/CONVENTIONS/README/CLAUDE needed no DET5-specific edit. Acceptance met: a lone renamed-window Zoom walks and prompts (its toolbar+leave+mute clear the confident bar); a title-matched browser wins a contested scan against an evidence-free native; drop events are emitted for `mp analyze-detection`. Owner-owed: a live renamed-window call and a non-whitelist browser/localized call confirmed on a real Mac (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here).

**[DONE] DET3 (P1): detection-miss auditor.** Shipped 2026-07-12, third of the detection-coverage band. Silent start-detection false negatives were invisible: nothing recorded "the mic was busy and nothing fired," so a whitelist gap or a recognizer rot was only found when the owner noticed a missed meeting. DET3 makes them visible. **Swift (the signal).** A new pure `MicBusySpanTracker` (state machine: `update(busy:at:frontmostBundle:frontmostName:) -> Transition?`, idempotent so the poll can re-sample without duplicate events, 6 unit tests) is fed by `MeetingDiscoveryWatcher` from two sources: the existing `AVCaptureDevice.isInUseByAnotherApplication` KVO, and the backstop poll (the KVO has been flaky across macOS versions, so the poll is the reliability net). On a rising/falling edge it emits `detector.mic_busy_started` / `mic_busy_ended` with a `duration_sec` and a best-effort `bundle_id` / `display_name` = the frontmost app at grab time (the API only says "another app holds the mic," not which, so the frontmost app is the plausible holder, the same attribution DET1 will use). An open span is closed at `stop()` so its duration isn't lost at shutdown. **Python (the audit).** `mp analyze-detection` became a dual-end tool: the original meeting-end miss report is untouched, and a new dimension pairs the mic-busy spans (`pair_mic_spans`) and correlates each against the recording sessions, `prompt_shown`, `candidate_dropped` (DET5), and `discovery_shadow_pick` (`classify_mic_spans` + `collect_correlation_markers`, all pure). A span that overlaps a recording or has a prompt in its window (+/- a 10 s grace for the join->grab->prompt skew) is *handled*; otherwise it is a miss, and the reason is picked by precedence, most-actionable first: a `candidate_dropped` in-window means a known app was seen but scored below threshold (**recognizer rot / a scoring gap**, the DET4/DET5 staleness canary the spec asked for); a `discovery_shadow_pick` with no prompt means it was **discovered but suppressed** (cooldown / skip-latch / consent); neither means it was **never a candidate** (an unlisted app, or Accessibility / Screen Recording denied). A new "Mic-busy misses" report section, a `mic_busy` block in `--json`, and `--min-miss-sec` (default 30 s) exclude brief dictation / voice-memo blips (the false-positive class the spec flagged, and the same guard DET1 will lean on before its thresholds are trusted). The report skeleton was fixed so a missed meeting (mic-busy spans but *no* recording session) still renders; the old early-return keyed on sessions existing would have hidden exactly the misses DET3 exists for. **Deferrals.** The optional quiet weekly-digest line was **not built** (it is out of the acceptance and would couple to the AI4 digest scheduler); the C6-FINISH corpus feed is a downstream consumer of these events, not part of DET3. **Verified:** the pure Swift tracker (6) + full daemon suite 1249 green; the Python auditor 28 `test_analyze_detection` green (pairing, drop-of-unclosed-tail, the four reason classes, the handled-by-recording / handled-by-prompt cases, the min-miss-sec filter, and `main` end-to-end in both markdown and `--json`); ruff + pyright clean. Docs: the ARCHITECTURE subcommand row (analyze-detection is now dual-end), the README `mp analyze-detection` usage block, and `daemon/CLAUDE.md`'s detection quick-index (`MicBusySpanTracker`) updated; the new `detector` actions follow the CONVENTIONS "no central action registry, update `mp analyze-detection` if it cares" rule (done). Acceptance met: a deliberately-missed unlisted-app call appears in the report and `--json` with its app attribution and a "no candidate" reason; a clean week (every real meeting recorded, blips sub-threshold) reports zero mic-busy misses. Owner-owed: eyeball a real week's report on the owner's Mac once mic-busy spans have accrued (the events are only emitted live).

**[DONE] DET1 (P1): mic-in-use catch-all detection tier.** Shipped 2026-07-12, last of the detection-coverage band (`/tech-task DET1, DET3, DET4, DET5`). Detection was whitelist-only, so a WebRTC call on a domain absent from `meeting_apps.toml` (the owner's lemon.io interview), FaceTime, Discord, WhatsApp/Telegram never prompted. **The tier.** A new pure `MicInUseTier.decide` returns a synthesized `AppSource` when a sustained mic-busy dwell (`defaultDwellSec` 30 s, deliberately long + provisional pending DET3 measurement of the dictation/voice-memo false-positive class) coincides with no whitelist winner and a plausible holder. `MeetingDiscoveryWatcher` reuses DET3's `MicBusySpanTracker` for the dwell, evaluates the tier once per span after each scan (with the fresh no-winner outcome), and routes the source through the session's normal `handleMeetingStarted`, so the skip-latch, reprompt cooldown, and auto-consent all apply and the prompt (its de-CLI UI affordance) is the existing `MeetingPromptWindow`. Permission-light by design: it engages no AX and no Screen Recording, so it also gives the permission-degraded matrix a working tier instead of silence. **The plausible set is browsers + `mic_plausible` only** (DET4's adapterless tier), NOT native whitelist apps: discovery owns natives, so DET1 catches only what the whitelist structurally cannot see, which keeps the register quiet (a random app, or dictation into a non-meeting app whose frontmost is that app, is never named) without a brittle denylist. **The adversarial-review saga (five rounds, why the code is shaped this way).** A `/tech-task`-internal review workflow plus follow-up verifications found and closed five real defects, all medium, several in the "records forever / false-prompt storm" class this tier is most dangerous in: (1) *Re-prompt storm* - a mid-call `isInUseByAnotherApplication` flap (Bluetooth/HAL route renegotiation) churned the mic-busy span, and DET1's skip-latch is never refreshed (no discovery winner), so a dismissed call re-prompted; fixed by a **release debounce** on `MicBusySpanTracker` (the mic must read idle for `releaseDebounceSec`, default 5 s, before a span closes; a rising sample cancels the pending close), so a flap keeps the same span and the once-per-span latch holds. (2) *Whitelist pre-empt* - DET1 fired during a native (Zoom) pre-join that holds the mic before its window surfaces, set `.prompting`, and blocked the real winner's `engageLifecycle` (losing Leave-button end-detection / mis-attributing); fixed by **excluding natives** from the plausible set. (3) *Never-stops* - a DET1 recording engages no lifecycle and can starve the idle backstop of a MicGate verdict, so it ran forever; fixed by a **mic-release LEVEL check** (`onMicBusyBundle`, reporting the currently-held bundle every poll) that stops a DET1-tagged recording once its app no longer holds the mic - a level (not a one-shot edge), so a recording that started *after* its span closed (a late prompt answer, a slow recorder bring-up) is still stopped. (4) *Stale-tag mis-tag* - the tag that marks a recording as DET1 lingered after a skip and mis-tagged a later whitelist recording of the same bundle (which the level check could then false-stop mid-call); (5) *Read-after-await race* - the tag was read after the recorder's async start, so a concurrent `handleMeetingStarted` could untag a DET1 recording (records forever again). Both (4) and (5) are closed by making the tag a **one-shot flag** (`micTierPromptSource`) consumed by every `handleMeetingStarted`, **armed only after the suppression guards** (so an early-return can't leave a stale tag), and **captured synchronously in `beginRecording` before the await** (so the async start can't race it). Two `MeetingSessionControllerBranchTests` pin the tag path (a DET1 recording stops on mic release; a whitelist recording after a skipped DET1 prompt is NOT mis-tagged and NOT stopped) - the second fails without fix (4). **Known low residual (documented, deferred to DET2):** the holder is attributed by the frontmost app at mic-grab time, so if a browser is frontmost the instant a native grabs the mic and the native's window does not surface for the whole dwell, DET1 can name the browser; inherent to frontmost-attribution, and DET2's CoreAudio process-tap attribution is the real fix. **Verified:** `swift build` clean; full daemon suite 1262 green, incl. `MicInUseTierTests` (dwell / no-winner / plausible-gate / native-excluded / attribution), the `MicBusySpanTracker` debounce tests, and the two branch tests. Docs: ARCHITECTURE (the two new detection files + the tier), README (the catch-all backstop in the detection-troubleshooting note), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (detection quick-index). The new `detector.mic_in_use_prompt` / `coordinator.mic_in_use_stop` events follow the CONVENTIONS "no central action registry" rule; DET1's prompt is a normal `prompt_shown`, so DET3's auditor already sees a DET1-caught call as handled. Acceptance met: a browser call on an unlisted domain prompts within the dwell; music (no mic hold) and dictation-into-a-non-meeting-app do not; the prompt names the app; whitelist behaviour is unchanged (DET1 only fires on a no-winner scan). Owner-owed: confirm on a real Mac that a live lemon.io-class call and a FaceTime call prompt, and that skip/record/mic-release behave, once dwell data accrues (thresholds are provisional).

**[DONE] END8 (P2): window-gone false-end hardening.** Shipped 2026-07-12. A sustained off-screen native meeting window (minimized, other Space, fullscreen elsewhere) promoted a false end after the debounce: `ShareableContentSignal` enumerates with `onScreenWindowsOnly: true` so an off-screen window reads as gone (leading `.shareableContentWindow`, `requiresCorroboration == false`), and although `rescueProvisionalEnd` re-walked AX and found the still-healthy Leave button, `PromotionEngine.handleEndingProvisional` reverted a provisional only on `case .live where event.kind == leading`; the rescue's event is `.axLeaveButton`, a different kind, so the revert never matched and the debounce promoted to `.ended`. **Fix (reuses END6 machinery, no new engine method):** the revert guard broadened from same-kind to same-`EndEvidenceClass` (`event.kind.evidenceClass == leading.evidenceClass`). `axLeaveButton` and `shareableContentWindow` already share `.callWindowOrControl` (END6/AUD-10), so a healthy Leave now reverts a window-gone lead, and symmetrically a returning window reverts an ax-leave lead; a cross-class `.live` (process-audio) still cannot revert (the conservative choice, so an unrelated signal cannot resurrect a genuinely ended meeting). Preserved: the existing ax-leave revert, and the "lone window-gone still promotes when there is genuinely no button" debounce path (the `.ended`/`tick` path, untouched). Two new `PromotionEngineTests` pin the window-gone-led revert and the cross-class non-revert; full daemon suite 1274 green. Docs: the ARCHITECTURE "Debounce (start / end)" glossary entry gained the native window-gone revert clause beside END5's browser one; no new event/category/sidecar, so CONVENTIONS/README/CLAUDE needed no edit. Acceptance met: a window-gone-led provisional reverts on the AX rescue's healthy Leave (minimizing longer than the debounce does not stop the recording), and a real leave still confirms via `confirmProvisionalEnd` at the current latency. Owner-owed: a live minimize-mid-call eyeball on a real Mac (the menu-bar app is not screenshotable here).

**[DONE] REC7 (P2): bounded, crash-consistent stop path.** Shipped 2026-07-12. `stop()` bounded the SCStream awaits at 5 s each (the 2026-06-05 lesson) but not the writer drain (`SerialBufferWriter.finish()` was an unbounded `queue.sync`) or the ffmpeg merge, so a wedged disk write made `.stopping` inescapable; and the merge wrote the final WAV at its canonical path with no temp+rename, so a hard interruption left a truncated final that orphan recovery then refused to touch (`!fileExists(final)`) while intact intermediates sat beside it. **What shipped.** (1) `SerialBufferWriter.finish(timeout:)` (a semaphore sentinel; on timeout the drain is ABANDONED, and because the writer closure strong-captures the `AVAudioFile` the abandoned queue keeps it valid, so nil-ing the recorder's ref is memory-safe); `stop()` bounds both writers (10 s) and, on a wedge, skips the merge and keeps the intermediates + a recordfail breadcrumb exactly like a merge failure. (2) A shared `produceFinal` (used by both `stop()` and `recoverOrphan`) merges/converts to a `<stem>.merging.wav` temp bounded by `runWithTimeout` (300 s), atomically promotes the temp onto the canonical final only once verified, and on any failure/timeout keeps the intermediates + writes the recordfail; ffmpeg therefore never writes `<stem>.wav` directly, so a killed or timed-out merge can never leave a truncated final. The old `PostProcessOutcome` enum + `mergeViaFFmpeg`/`convertMonoMixdownViaFFmpeg` collapsed into `produceFinal` + `mergeToTempViaFFmpeg`/`convertToTempViaFFmpeg` + `promoteMerged`. (3) The failure-path bundle: FEAT8 markers are flushed *before* the merge-outcome branch in `MeetingSessionController` (so a failed merge no longer discards flagged moments; the sidecar sits beside where the orphan sweep will land the final) and `MarkerFile.write` is now `.atomic`; the MIC15 coverage snapshot is read *after* the tap is removed (closing an unguarded cross-thread read of the render-thread coverage tally); `handleSystemAudioLost` now `await dying.stop()`s the dead SCStream before dropping it (ARC never called `stopCapture()`, leaking the tap); and a confirmed lifecycle `.ended` that lands during the async `recorder.start()` window is latched (`endObservedDuringStart`, MainActor-serialized so the set-then-read is not raced) and consumed right after the start commits, so the fresh recording is stopped at once instead of running against a dead meeting until the idle backstop (new `coordinator`/`recording_ended_during_start` event). **Verified:** 1277 daemon green, incl. `finish(timeout:)` true / false-on-wedge tests, the temp-cleanup + `mergingTempURL` naming assertions on the real-ffmpeg REC1 recoverOrphan tests, and the existing "no partial final on a failed merge" guarantee now flowing through the shared temp+promote path. New events (`recorder`/`writer_drain_timed_out`, `recorder`/`merge_timed_out`, `coordinator`/`recording_ended_during_start`) use existing categories, so CONVENTIONS/ARCHITECTURE/README/CLAUDE needed no edit (the `.merging.wav` temp is transient, like `.flac.writing` / `.merging.tmp`, so it is not a files-table row). Acceptance met: a simulated wedged writer returns from `finish(timeout:)` within budget (unit-tested), leaving intermediates + recordfail; a failed merge leaves no final at the canonical path (unit-tested via recoverOrphan). Owner-owed: a live wedged-writer / killed-merge eyeball on a real Mac is not exercisable here, but the guarantees are unit-pinned.

**[DONE] MIC16 (P2): mute-backstop AX poll off main.** Shipped 2026-07-13. The 1 Hz mute-state backstop (`MeetingAXWindowWatcher`) re-resolved and read the live mute button via a fresh cross-process AX tree walk (`readMuteStates` -> `findMeetingWindowMuteButtons`) synchronously on the main thread every tick for the whole recording, so a wedged meeting client's slow AX call stalled the run loop exactly when the panic force-stop hotkey (a main-thread Carbon handler) needed to fire. **Fix:** `poll()` splits into an off-main dispatch shell plus a main-thread `consume(_:)` continuation. With an injected `walkQueue` (production passes the shared `MeetingSessionController.axWalkQueue` that `armLifecycleLeaveButton` / `rescueProvisionalEnd` already use), the resolver (the AX walk) runs on that serial queue and only the resulting `[MuteLabels.State]` readings hop back to main for fusion + the mute state machine; all watcher state stays main-only. A `generation` token bumped in `stop()` is captured at dispatch and re-checked on delivery, so an AX walk still in flight when the recording stops is dropped instead of re-arming a torn-down poll. The `walkQueue` seam defaults to nil, which keeps the resolve synchronous on the caller, so the ~20 existing `ManualScheduler`-driven watcher tests step the poll without a run loop, untouched. **Verified:** `swift build` clean; full daemon suite 1282 green, incl. two new tests: a deliberately slow (semaphore-gated) resolver on a real queue proves `poll()` returns to the caller before the walk finishes (the hotkey path is never blocked), and a `stop()` during an in-flight walk proves the late delivery is dropped (no emit, no re-arm). No new event/category/sidecar and no user-visible surface change, so README/ARCHITECTURE/CONVENTIONS needed no edit; only the watcher's own "Threading: main-queue only" class doc was corrected to describe the off-main resolve. Acceptance met: no cross-process AX call runs on the main thread during a recording (the walk is on `axWalkQueue`); watcher tests green; a slow resolver in tests does not block the caller. Owner-owed: a live wedged-client eyeball (force-stop still instant while a meeting app hangs) on a real Mac, not exercisable here.

**[DONE] ASR2 (P2): per-job diarizer reset.** Shipped 2026-07-13. `FluidAudioRunner` caches the FluidAudio `AsrManager` + `DiarizerManager` for the daemon session lifetime (deliberate, to avoid CoreML recompilation), but the `DiarizerManager`'s `SpeakerManager` also accumulated a speaker-clustering database across every meeting in that session, so a second meeting's speaker ids continued the first's counter (`speaker_3`, `speaker_4`, ...) instead of restarting, and label assignment could drift with daemon uptime. **Fix (one line, the "call FluidAudio's reset, if exposed" path):** `runDiarization` calls `diarizer.speakerManager.reset()` right after `ensureDiarizer()`, before `performCompleteDiarization`. Confirmed against the FluidAudio source that `SpeakerManager.reset(keepIfPermanent:)` (default false) does exactly `speakerDatabase.removeAll(); nextSpeakerId = 1; highestSpeakerId = 0` and nothing else, so the clustering DB + id counter reset to a fresh-launch state while the loaded CoreML models and the `EmbeddingExtractor` stay resident (no recompilation, no re-download). We enroll no permanent speakers (`initializeKnownSpeakers` / `enrollSpeaker` are never called), so the full reset is correct. Safe because transcription jobs are strictly serialized (`SinkDispatcher.startNextJobIfNeeded` guards `activeJob == nil`), so the reset can never wipe a concurrent job's mid-flight clustering. Both acceptance clauses follow by construction: `nextSpeakerId = 1` makes back-to-back jobs produce the same labels a fresh launch produces on the same fixtures, and clearing only the in-memory dictionary means no per-job recompilation. **Verified:** `swift build` clean (the `speakerManager.reset()` call compiles against the real API); full daemon suite 1282 green (no regression). FluidAudioRunner's diarization path needs the CoreML models + ANE, so it is not unit-testable in CI, and faking the concrete `DiarizerManager` for a one-line reset would be over-engineering; the correctness argument is the read of the library source above. The stale `FluidAudioRunner` doc comment that said the persisted embedding is "unaffected by the diarizer's speaker database accumulating across meetings in one daemon session" was corrected (the DB no longer accumulates). No new event/schema/subcommand, so README/ARCHITECTURE/CONVENTIONS needed no edit. Owner-owed: a live dogfood eyeball (two back-to-back meetings; the second starts at speaker_1) confirms it end-to-end, not exercisable here.

**[DONE] PIPE8 (P2): pipeline posture + honesty bundle.** Shipped 2026-07-13. Five small fixes in one pass. (a) `mp serve-local` had bypassed the entry contract (`Config.load()` then a raw `os.execvp`): it now calls `entry.prepare(secrets=False)`, refuses an armed-and-uncached model with the `mp prefetch-model` pointer (mirroring the lazy `_spawn`), and execs via `os.execvpe(cmd[0], cmd, egress_guard.child_env())`; the daemon's `LocalModelPreloader` spawns it with `freshEnvironment(stripAnthropicKey: true, stripNotionToken: true)`, so a local model server inherits neither cloud token. (b) A new `test_entry_contract.py` walks every `from .<module> import main` dispatch target in `__main__.py` and asserts the module calls `entry.prepare` or sits on a named read-only/diagnostic allowlist (doctor / logs / prefetch-model / corrections / analyze-detection / roster / actions), so the next forgotten arm is a red build; SEC13 was enforced only by convention before. (c) One shared `storage.atomic_write_text(path, text)` (sibling tmp -> chmod 0600 -> `os.replace`) replaces the plain `write_text` + `chmod` pairs in `orchestrate._finalize_streamed_transcript` + `_apply_glossary` and `summarize`, so a crash mid-rewrite leaves the prior file intact rather than a truncated one. Named public (not the spec's `_atomic_write_text`) because it is shared across three modules, per the pipeline "an underscore name belongs to its module" rule. (d) `mp digest --publish` now checks `all_sinks_failed(pub)` and returns `EXIT_PUBLISH_FAILED`, joining every other fanout caller (a zero-sinks digest is still a success). (e) FEAT9 follow-up: `merge_meetings` now commits the transcript carrying `merged_from` (the retry guard's only key) atomically BEFORE the audio `os.replace`, so a crash between the two commits leaves a transcript that already records the merge and a retry takes the re-publish-only branch instead of double-concatenating the fragments; the reverse residual (transcript merged, audio not yet swapped) re-publishes over the correct transcript, fragment audio recoverable from the kept originals. Tests: +2 serve-local (armed refusal, token strip), +3 tripwire, +2 storage, +1 digest exit-3, +1 merge crash-window. Docs synced: CONVENTIONS entry-contract section, pipeline/CLAUDE.md exit-3 list + the new atomic helper, ARCHITECTURE storage.py row. 740 pipeline + 1282 daemon tests green.

**[DONE] PIPE9 (P2): transcript corrections reach the pipeline.** Shipped 2026-07-13. In-app transcript text edits (`TranscriptCorrectionStore`, the Transcript tab's "Edit text") were resolved only at display time in Swift and had zero Python readers, so `mp summarize` regenerate and the `mp ask` embedding index ran on the raw ASR while the Library showed corrected text. Fix: a new `mp.transcript_corrections` module mirroring the `speaker_overlay` precedent (reader keyed by the segment's `<stem>.json` array index, the same `enumerate` convention Swift's `TranscriptSegment.index` uses; pure `apply_corrections`), plus a composed `overlaid_markdown` that applies the speaker-label overlay THEN the text corrections and re-renders. `summarize` and `embed_index.chunk_library` now re-read the transcript through that composed view, and `embed_index.library_fingerprint` folds `*.transcript_corrections.json` + `*.speaker_labels.json` in so editing a correction (which rewrites only the sidecar, not `<stem>.md`) invalidates the cached index. `speaker_overlay.overlaid_markdown` was retired into the composed resolver (its only production caller was `summarize`). Swift's `TranscriptCorrectionStore.write` now stamps `schema_version: 1` (the reader stays fail-open). A golden parity fixture (`Fixtures/transcript-corrections-golden.json`) is read by both `test_transcript_corrections.py` and `TranscriptCorrectionStoreTests.test_golden_parity_with_python`, so the resolution cannot drift. `mp digest` is covered transitively (it reads `<stem>.summary.json`, regenerated from corrected text; it does not read raw transcripts). Docs synced: CONVENTIONS sidecar contract, pipeline/CLAUDE.md overlay section, ARCHITECTURE module list + files-live table, README Transcript-tab note. Tests: new `test_transcript_corrections.py` (12) + 2 `embed_index` (index fold, fingerprint) + 2 Swift (schema stamp, golden parity). 753 pipeline + 1284 daemon tests green.

**[DONE] MIC10 (P1): re-lock the Teams mini-window mute control + VAD-contradiction staleness.** Shipped 2026-07-14 (part 2 on 2026-07-11). The task that finally killed the recurring mute data loss, by discovering that every previous diagnosis of it was wrong.

**Part 2 (2026-07-11): the VAD-contradiction discredit.** A pure `VADContradictionTracker` (clock-injected, the `RMSGateProbe` idiom) in `MeetingAXWindowWatcher`'s poll loop detects a *confidently-wrong* app-mute: the watcher reads `.muted` while `MicGate.currentVadActive` (a new thread-safe accessor over the fused HAL VAD) reports sustained OS voice for a ~4 s dwell. It suppresses the stale `.muted` so `MicGate.decide` falls through to the live voice and calls `onStaleMuteContradiction`; the host mode-gates the actual `clearAxMute` to capture-first only, never under the regulated/NDA gate where a wrong un-mute leaks at-rest-free audio on a genuine muted side-conversation. New `micgate`/`mute_state_cleared_vad_contradiction` event.

**Part 1, stage 1 (2026-07-14): the fusion, found by mining the real event log.** The filed spec said the Leave-anchored walk "falls back to all-windows in the new mini-window topology", so the fix was a re-scope and the prerequisite was a live AX dump. `events.jsonl` said otherwise: the 2026-06-08 data-loss session (MIC9's incident) started its poller with `found_leave: true`, so the anchor did **not** fall back, and the walk still returned two mute buttons, `["unmuted", "muted"]`, which the MUTED-biased fusion resolved to `.muted` at poll 1 and latched for the whole ~800 s call. Worse, `findMeetingWindowMuteButtons` keeps at most **one button per window** (the per-window DFS short-circuits), so `states.count > 1` is *always* cross-window, which makes the MUTED bias's stated justification ("privacy over capture on a genuine in-window multi-button disagreement", 2026-05-29) structurally unreachable: its only reachable effect was letting a stale window out-vote the live one. It did exactly that 25 times in the log, every one with `previous: "unmuted"` (a correctly-unmuted live call flipped mid-sentence; four times in the 2026-06-17 session alone), while part 2's discredit had fired **0 times**. Fix: `MeetingAXWindowWatcher.fuse` is now unanimity-based with an explicit `DisagreementPolicy`. Unanimous readings are trusted exactly as before (a genuine mute still mutes, so this is not a blanket unmute); a disagreement resolves `.blind` under capture-first (the stale button cannot latch `.muted`, and the existing blind-clear drops any stale latch) and `.mutedWins` under the regulated gate (byte-identical to the old behaviour, where a wrong un-mute is unrecoverable). Same `activeCaptureMode?.capturesLosslessly` mode gate part 2 established, so the watcher stays mode-agnostic. New `micgate`/`mute_state_window_disagreement` event, emitted once per streak, not at 1 Hz.

**Part 1, stage 2 (2026-07-14): the re-anchor, off the owner's live AX dump.** The owner ran `daemon/scripts/ax-dump-meeting.swift` during a real Teams call, muted and then unmuted, and the two dumps settled it. Three windows exist during a backgrounded call, and **two** carry both a mute button and a `Leave` button:

    win[0] "Meeting compact view | ..."  subrole=AXSystemDialog    desc="Unmute mic" -> "Mute mic"
    win[1] "Calendar | ..."              subrole=AXStandardWindow  (no mute button)
    win[2] "Meeting with ... | ..."      subrole=AXStandardWindow  desc="Unmute mic" (frozen)

Toggling the real mic flips **only** win[0]. win[2], the full meeting window the call popped out of, freezes its mute description and reads `muted` (`desc="Unmute mic"` means "click to unmute", i.e. currently muted) for the rest of the call. So the stale control is **not the hub / pre-join window** that MIC9, MIC10 and the 2026-06-03 writeup all blamed: it is the backgrounded full window of the very same call. That is why every Leave-based scoping attempt kept it (it carries a `Leave` button too), and why the MUTED bias then zeroed a live mic for a whole meeting. Fix: `MeetingAXHandleBuilder.scopeToLiveControl` anchors on the floating call panel, identified by the `AXSystemDialog` subrole, a locale-independent AX constant (Teams exposes no `AXValue` and no `AXIdentifier` anywhere in its tree, so a subrole is the durable structural signal actually on offer, which also settles MIC6 negatively). Panel wins outright when present; absent a panel it falls back to the Leave anchor, then to every window. `build()` now sources the primary `AXMuteButtonProbe`'s cached element from the same scoped walk instead of a first-match-across-windows scan, because that probe subscribes to its element for the whole meeting and was choosing the live control by luck of window order. New `found_call_panel` attribute on `ax_handles_built`.

**Part 1, stage 3 (2026-07-14): the inverse case measured, and the rule's safety proven rather than assumed.** "The panel wins whenever it exists" is only sound if a panel can never itself be the stale one. The obvious way that could fail: if Teams freezes the *occluded* window (which the mechanism above implies), then with Teams FOCUSED and the meeting window in front, the panel would be the occluded one and would freeze, and the anchor would prefer a frozen panel over the live main window, inverting the bug. The owner captured the inverse dump pair (Teams focused, toggling in the meeting window; taken with a delayed `sleep 10` so that focusing Terminal to launch the script would not itself background Teams and re-spawn the panel, the observer effect this measurement had to dodge). Result: **the panel does not exist at all when Teams is focused.** The window count drops 3 to 2, no `AXSystemDialog` window is in the tree, and the full meeting window resumes tracking (`desc="Unmute mic"` -> `"Mute mic"` on toggle) while keeping its `Leave` button, so the fallback anchor is intact. Teams **destroys** the panel when the meeting window becomes visible rather than leaving it stale, so a panel that is not the live control cannot exist and the rule is safe in both directions. This also closes the regulated/NDA exposure the stage-2 note left open (a panel frozen at `unmuted` capturing a genuinely muted side-conversation): it is unreachable. Confirms the freeze mechanism too: the frozen value is whatever the window last painted, which is why the two windows agreed on roughly half the `buttons_found: 2` polls in the log and contradicted each other on the rest.

**Verified:** daemon 1298 green (14 `MeetingAXWindowWatcherTests` incl. a replay of the real 06-08 trace and the 06-17 mid-call-flip signature, with the regulated-gate case still fusing to `.muted` as the negative control; 6 new `scopeToLiveControl` tests modelled on the dumps' exact topologies). Docs: ARCHITECTURE's "Lockon" glossary entry rewritten (the anchor plus the three backstops), MIC6's spec updated with the negative result so no future session re-runs the dump. **Residual, non-blocking:** confirm from `events.jsonl` after a few real meetings that `buttons_found` is now 1 on every poll (both dumps predict exactly that: 2 mute-bearing windows backgrounded, of which only the panel is now read, and 1 focused) and that `mute_state_window_disagreement` never fires (filed on the watch list). The ~4 s contradiction dwell and a possible `clearAxMute` escalation under the regulated gate remain owner-tuning items; the re-anchor makes both largely moot, since the regulated gate now reads only the live control.

**[WONT-DO] MIC6 (P1): attribute-based futureproof mute read.** Closed 2026-07-14. Carried from Q4 DONE-partial. The four shipped parts (cross-locale category-precedence fallback, `AXMuteButtonProbe` element re-arm after N `.unknown`, decoupled `MeetingAXWindowWatcher` blind-clear, verified `uk` labels) are in place and tested and stay. The owed fifth was part 1's headline: read the mute button's stable state attribute (AXValue / pressed-state / mark-char) instead of scraping the localized title, so the read survives UI redesigns and locale, gated on an owner-run live AX dump to pick the per-app attribute + polarity.

**The dump ran on 2026-07-14 (owner, during a live Teams call, in service of MIC10) and refuted the premise.** `daemon/scripts/ax-dump-meeting.swift` prints `value=` and `id=` for every pressable control precisely so this attribute could be chosen. Across the entire Teams tree, all three windows: **zero elements carry an `AXValue` or an `AXIdentifier`.** The mute control is `role=AXButton desc="Unmute mic"` and nothing else. There is no stable state attribute to migrate to, so the task is unachievable rather than unbuilt, and the already-shipped `MuteLabels` catalogue plus its cross-locale category-precedence fallback is not a stopgap: it is the answer for this client.

**Both of MIC6's goals are met by other means, so nothing is lost by closing it.** Locale independence is that cross-locale fallback (it matches any locale in the catalogue, so the walk finds the button regardless of system locale). UI-redesign durability is MIC10's re-anchor: the live control is now identified by the `AXSystemDialog` subrole of the floating call panel, a locale-independent AX constant, which is exactly the kind of durable structural signal MIC6 was hunting, just not the kind it predicted.
