And a Teams re-label now degrades *safely* rather than wrongly: an unrecognised label reads `.unknown`, the blind-clear drops any stale latch, and `MicGate.decide` falls through to HAL VAD + RMS with capture-first keeping the full mic.

**Scope note on the unmeasured clients.** The dump only covered Teams (the other three targets were not running). This is not a real gap: `ax_handles_built` is 386/386 `com.microsoft.teams2` across the entire event log, so Zoom, Webex and Slack have never once been AX-handled by this daemon, and dogfood is Teams-only by construction. Re-open MIC6 with the same two-dump procedure (muted, then unmuted, so the diff labels the live control) if one of them ever becomes a real meeting client. No code is owed. **Successor gap, deliberately not folded in here:** nothing surfaces a *blind* mute oracle to the user, so a future Teams re-label would silently and safely stop mute redaction working with no visible sign (`mute_state_cleared_blind` has fired 16 times in the real log and nothing reads it). That is a visibility task, not an attribute task, and is filed separately if the owner wants it.

**[DONE] END6 (P1): device-idle end corroborator.** Closed 2026-07-14. Carried from Q4/Q5 as DONE-partial: the correlated-pair gate had shipped, and the remainder (land `kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere` as "the genuinely independent corroborator", plus a screen-share dogfood trace) was owner-owed pending live-Mac validation. Both halves are now settled, and no engine code was written, deliberately.

**The device-idle leg is refuted by measurement, not deferred.** The blocking question turned out not to need a live meeting: it is not "what does Teams do to the flag", it is "what does *our own recorder* do to it", which is answerable with our own capture alone. New owner-dev probe `daemon/scripts/end6-device-idle-probe.swift` (read-only; snapshots every audio device at baseline, while an `AVAudioEngine` input tap runs in the exact shape `MeetingRecorder` uses, and after stop) was run on the owner's Mac. Result: baseline all-idle; while our tap runs, the default input reads **RUNNING** (the confound the backlog predicted, now measured) **and so does the default output**, because `AVAudioEngine.inputNode` instantiates a `CADefaultDeviceAggregate` (`in:1 out:2`) spanning both directions. That second finding is new and it kills the obvious rescue ("read the speaker side instead, a call client renders remote audio and stops when the call ends"). While the daemon records, no device on the machine carries any information about the meeting client, in either direction. The signal is not noisy, it is structurally blind, so the leg cannot exist. Mic TCC is irrelevant to the reading (the flag tracks a running IOProc, not real samples), so an unauthorized engine pins it identically.

**It would also not have helped, and would have hurt.** Over the real event log since the gate landed (2026-06-28), across 25 native endings: 22 of 23 provisional ends were confirmed by the AX re-walk at a **median 0.17 s** (max 0.38 s), 1 promoted on the debounce (window-gone lead, 6.0 s), and there were **zero** idle-stop backstop auto-stops and **zero** force-stops. A 1 Hz polled HAL boolean cannot improve on a fifth of a second. And a signal in a new `EndEvidenceClass` instant-promotes a provisional end through `handleEndingProvisional`'s cross-class fast path with no debounce, so a device-GLOBAL read (shared with every other app's audio) would have been the one signal in the system able to instantly confirm a *false* ax-leave: the exact fragmentation hole the gate closed.

**The gate is validated in production (the owner-owed dogfood trace, settled from the log).** Fragmentation signature, an `.ended` followed by a same-pid `.starting` within 3 min: pre-gate 12 of 82 native ends (15%), including the documented 4-fragment screen-share incident; post-gate 1 of 22, and that one chopped nothing (2026-07-02: the end was correctly re-walk-confirmed, the recording stopped, the pipeline finished; 78 s later Teams re-rendered a call-shaped window and the daemon prompted, the prompt timed out to skip and recorded nothing, so it is a start-side false prompt, DET-band, not an end-side chop). **Post-gate, zero recordings were chopped.**

**Closing leaves no hole.** Every genuinely independent end corroborator is already owned elsewhere: the per-process read (`kAudioProcessPropertyIsRunningInput`) is the unconfounded one because it asks about the client's process rather than the shared device, and that is **DET2** (probe-gated, owner-owed at the time; **closed NO-GO 2026-07-20**, the read cannot be revived on this macOS even with a process tap, so this corroborator does not exist and nothing is waiting on it); the calendar end horizon is **CAL1** (**closed NO-GO 2026-07-17**, the owner's calendar never reaches EventKit, so this one does not exist either); and an AX-free end path already exists and works (window-gone comes from ScreenCaptureKit, not AX, and led 12 historical ends on the debounce), with the idle-stop backstop as the ceiling. Docs: the design-time claim that seeded this leg (`docs/architecture/signal-fusion-and-mic-gating.md` PART A classified the device-scope read CORROBORATING, assuming the daemon observes the device rather than being a client of it) is corrected in place to REJECTED with the measurement, so the next reader does not re-propose the dead signal from the same source; `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s signal list no longer names a non-existent input-device signal; `InputDeviceIdentity.shouldWarnMismatch` (MIC15's start-time read, the one legitimate use) now carries the never-reuse-this-as-an-end-signal note. Full trace and decision in `docs/spikes/end6-device-idle-corroborator.md`.

**[DONE] CAL1 (P1): spike EventKit calendar context (re-evaluated ADR 0011).** Carried from Q4; promoted P2 to P1 2026-07-12 (assessment). Closed 2026-07-17 NO-GO, measured. The spike shipped its analysis doc (`docs/spikes/cal1-eventkit-calendar-context.md`) and an owner-run read-only probe (`daemon/scripts/cal1-calendar-probe.swift`) that measured the one headline question: does the owner's calendar carry their meetings?

**The measurement is a false zero, and that is the finding.** The owner ran the probe: 0 of 55 recorded meetings had a covering EventKit event, and 0 had a resembling title. But the calendar-events-in-window count was ~0 and Calendar.app is essentially empty, so EventKit had nothing to match against. The zero does not measure calendar-boundness (the meetings are almost certainly scheduled, being Teams invites); it measures that the owner's work calendar (Outlook / Teams) is not connected to macOS EventKit, so an EventKit read is structurally blind to their meetings. The probe's original READ line would have reported this as "meetings are NOT calendar-bound, close CAL1", a confidently-wrong verdict on a void measurement; that defect is fixed in the same close (the probe now detects an empty event store and prints a distinct false-zero READ that tells the owner to connect the calendar and re-run, never a spurious close), so the kept doc + probe stay honest for a revisit.

**Disposition (owner-chosen): close.** The owner keeps scheduling in Outlook / Teams and leaves macOS Calendar.app empty by choice, so CAL1's hints (pre-title, expected-end horizon, preflight) would never fire regardless of whether the meetings are calendar-bound in the source system; the alternative (connect the calendar to macOS, then re-measure) was offered and declined. ADR 0011's disposition (no calendar signal, no new Calendar TCC prompt) stands, now backed by a measurement instead of an assumption, with the operative reason refined for this user: not "the meetings are unscheduled" but "the scheduling does not reach EventKit" (a revisit note was added to ADR 0011). The doc and the now-hardened probe are kept so the decision is revisitable if the owner ever connects their calendar to macOS. Synergy closed with it: the FEAT3-ROSTER / DV3 attendee seed needs a covering event to read an attendee list from, and with none there is nothing to seed (DV3 rides owner enrollment alone); CAL2's calendar-aware timing + title/attendee seeding, which sequenced behind this spike, is likewise closed, while CAL2's workflow-keyed "Last time" affordance needs no calendar and is unaffected.

**[DONE] CONC4 (P2): strict concurrency on the executable target.** Shipped 2026-07-18. `-strict-concurrency=targeted` now compiles the `MeetingPipe` executable too, not just the Core island; the executable builds warning-clean, the full 1324-test daemon suite stays green, and there is no behaviour change.

**The measured shape (2026-07-10, confirmed 2026-07-18).** Flipping the flag produces 20 unique warnings (the raw build prints ~400, the same 20 repeated per compilation unit). 2 were in Core and shipped in the 2026-07-10 measurement pass (`MeetingLifecycleVerdict` / `MeetingLifecycleContext` / its `Kind` / `EndingReason` made `Sendable`, matching `MicGateVerdict`). The remaining 18, across 5 executable files, were NOT the mechanical Sendable annotations the original spec predicted, which is why the first pass stopped rather than guessing.

**How the 18 closed, file by file.**
- `MeetingRecorder` (7): `@preconcurrency import AVFoundation` for the two off-main `engine.start()` captures plus the AVFAudio hint (Apple has not audited AVFAudio for Sendable); `SystemAudioCapture` marked `@unchecked Sendable` (cross-thread by design, and `stop()` was already bounded off-main); the `recovered` / `degraded` callback hops rewritten to `Task { @MainActor in self?.x }` (weak self reached on the main actor, no non-Sendable value crossing the boundary); the recovery-task handle boxed in a tiny main-confined `RecoveryTaskBox: @unchecked Sendable` so `stop()`'s `MainActor.run` drain captures the box rather than the whole non-Sendable recorder (the hop stays, so the race it serialized against the in-flight recovery Task's defer-clear is preserved).
- `DoctorRunner` (2): the `appender` closure typed `@Sendable (Data) -> Void` at its declaration (it only reaches a `@MainActor` DoctorRunner through `weak self`).
- `CorrectionsTab` (5): the reload no longer sends a `Task<[String: Any]?, Never>` across the actor boundary; a new `CorrectionStore.readData` returns Sendable `Data` off-main and the non-Sendable `[String: Any]` is parsed back on the main actor, so revert still re-serializes the exact original bytes.
- `MeetingSessionController` (2) + `SinkDispatcher` (2): `@unchecked Sendable` with a documented main-queue invariant (see below).

**The decision that mattered: `@unchecked Sendable`, not `@MainActor`, for the two spine types.** The original spec's lean was "the correct fix is `@MainActor` on the type, not `@unchecked Sendable`". Implemented and measured, that call does not survive this codebase. Making `MeetingSessionController` + `SinkDispatcher` (and the cascade they force: `Coordinator`, `PipelineJobDispatcher`, plus the `App` / `StatusBarController` / `LibraryWindowModel` entry points) `@MainActor` compiled, but a full recompile then surfaced 22 NEW warnings, not the 4 it cleared: Timer closures touching now-isolated state (`SinkDispatcher`'s stall timer, `StatusBarController`'s permission poll + model-download closures), the three delegate-protocol conformances plus `SessionHost` crossing into main-actor code, and the AX `@Sendable` closures in the session controller's re-walk. Clearing those 22 means making the delegate protocols `@MainActor` (cascading into `Notifier` / `MeetingPromptWindow` / `RecordingHUDWindow`) and `@preconcurrency`-importing `ApplicationServices` / `MeetingPipeCore`, i.e. the whole-app `-strict-concurrency=complete` migration this task explicitly scopes out. So the two types keep a documented `@unchecked Sendable` recording the real invariant (every stored property touched only on the main queue; `SinkDispatcher`'s one off-main stretch reads only the immutable transcription runner), which is honest for main-queue-confined types, while the other 16 warnings got real fixes. The durable win is the flag being ON: a new Task that captures non-Sendable spine state is now a compiler warning rather than a comment.

**Docs.** `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md` (the island rule: both targets are now checked, and the `@unchecked Sendable` rationale), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a Patterns-to-match bullet so an executable-Swift session knows the flag is live), and `Package.swift` (the flag carries a CONC4 comment). No user-visible surface changed, so README / ARCHITECTURE / CONVENTIONS were checked and left untouched.

**[DONE] DET2 (P2): CoreAudio process-tap attribution (DET1 phase 2).** Filed from the 2026-07-12 assessment review, spiked 2026-07-12, closed 2026-07-20 NO-GO by measurement. Analysis doc `docs/spikes/det2-process-tap-attribution.md` + owner-run read-only probe `daemon/scripts/det2-process-tap-probe.swift`.

**The question.** `ProcessAudioSignal` was fully built and unit-tested but dead in production: `kAudioHardwarePropertyTranslatePIDToProcessObject` returns object 0, so `kAudioProcessPropertyIsRunningInput` never reads. The standing explanation blamed the capture model (ScreenCaptureKit, no audio-tap authorization, no Core Audio process tap), which implied a fix: adopt a tap and the HAL process-object list populates. DET2 refused to build on that and measured instead, because the wrong guess is either dead code or a large blind build. The probe measures three mechanisms: (A) the Screen Recording grant alone, (B) a bare private process tap held live, (C) a full private aggregate device around that tap.

**The measurement: none of them.** The owner ran the probe on a real Mac against a live Teams call (PID 667), with the probe's grant line reading `granted`. A, B, and C all returned `OSStatus 0` (`noErr`) and object 0. B and C were genuinely exercised rather than skipped: the probe prints a distinct construction-failure line when the tap or aggregate cannot be created, and neither appeared, so both were built successfully and the translate still reported no process object. The HAL answers the query and says there is nothing there; it is not refusing it.

**Corroborated at production scale, which is the stronger instrument.** Mechanism A did not actually need the probe. The signal ran live in the daemon before TECH-END1 disabled it, and `~/Library/Logs/MeetingPipe/events.1.jsonl` (2026-05-18 to 2026-07-13) holds the record: **13,419** `process_audio_unresolved` (all `com.microsoft.teams2`), 64 `process_audio_object_unresolved` every one carrying `osstatus: 0`, and **0** `process_audio_is_running_input` across both log generations, while `permission_granted kind=screenRecording` first fired at `2026-05-18T13:23:54Z`, the head of that same window. So mechanism A was measured from inside a genuinely authorized app, at production scale, for eight weeks, with the identical signature the probe reproduced. (13,419 supersedes the 13,407 mid-flight count quoted in the older comments; the log kept accruing until the signal was switched off.)

**Disposition: close the process-audio leg.** `usesProcessAudio` stays `false` on all four native adapters. No production detection code was ever written, so there was nothing to revert and no regression risk in the close. DET1's frontmost attribution remains the catch-all; the AX re-walk plus the idle-stop backstop remain the end-detection ceiling (END6 already validated that gate in production); MIC8 remains the stronger futureproofing bet. The `requiresCorroboration = true` rail the spike designed for the end leg was never needed, since nothing was flipped. The two non-runtime acceptance clauses (the scorer treats it as independent, prompts name the attributed app) were already satisfied by existing code and stay dormant behind the dead read.

**Closing leaves the docs honest.** The claim that would have re-opened this ("kept so the signal can be revived if we adopt a process tap") appeared at two code sites and is now corrected in place at both (`ProcessAudioSignal`, `NativeLifecycleConfig.usesProcessAudio`): adopting a tap is precisely what was measured, so the hypothesis is refuted, not untried. `LifecycleAdapterTests.test_process_audio_signal_is_disabled_for_every_provider` keeps its assertions and gains the measurement in its comment, so it now pins a permanent state rather than a provisional one. END6's close note, which forward-referenced DET2 as the one unconfounded end corroborator still coming, is updated so no reader waits on a leg that is closed. Doc and probe are kept (the CAL1 precedent) for a revisit if a future macOS changes process-object authorization.

**A methodological gotcha worth the ink.** The first probe run reported `screen-recording grant: NOT granted` and printed the same "close the leg" verdict, which would have been the right answer for the wrong reason. It was launched as `swift daemon/scripts/det2-process-tap-probe.swift` from a TCC-*disclaimed* parent (an agent harness spawns its shell through a `disclaimer` helper that deliberately severs responsible-process inheritance), and the executing process is `swift-frontend`, which holds no grant of its own. Granting Screen Recording to MeetingPipe.app does nothing for it. Re-running from an ordinary granted terminal produced `granted` and the real numbers. The probe header now carries this, and the rule generalizes to every owner-run probe in `daemon/scripts/`: read the tool's own precondition line before trusting its verdict, because a void measurement that happens to agree with the expected answer is the easiest kind to bank by mistake.

**[DONE] CI4 (P2): pin the remaining cross-tree contracts.** Three must-agree Swift-Python surfaces have no shared fixture, unlike the CI2/CI3 set (meta, chunking, json-extract, summary-markdown): `<stem>.publish.json` (the PIPE1 outcome contract; Python tests pin Python-authored expectations, Swift tests pin Swift-authored string literals, so coordinated drift passes both suites), the `mp prefetch-model` JSONL progress stream (`ModelDownloadSupervisor` switches on event strings pinned by neither side), and the speaker-overlay resolution (documented "must stay byte-identical to Swift"); the speaker-labels sidecar is also the one cross-language sidecar with no `schema_version`. Scope: golden fixtures generated from the Python writers and parsed by Swift tests for all three, plus the version stamp (the reader is already fail-open). Acceptance: a deliberate writer change breaks the other tree's test; CI green otherwise.

**Shipped 2026-07-20.** Three fixtures under `daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/Fixtures/`: `publish-result-golden.json`, `prefetch-progress-golden.json`, `speaker-overlay-golden.json`, read by `CI4ContractFixtureTests` (Swift) and `test_ci4_contracts.py` (Python).

**The one design call that was not in the spec: the fixtures are generated, not hand-authored.** The existing five (CI2/CI3/ARCH4/PIPE9) are maintained by hand: change the writer, run the test, paste the failing actual in. That process is exactly the drift hole CI4 exists to close, since a hand-authored fixture is just a third set of hand-authored expectations. `scripts/gen_contract_fixtures.py` instead calls `publish_router._write_publish_sidecar` and `speaker_overlay.read_overlay` / `apply_overlay` directly, and AST-walks every `_emit(...)` call site in `prefetch_model.py` so a renamed field or a new event appears in the fixture rather than being silently absent from it. `test_ci4_contracts.py` regenerates in-memory and diffs the committed files, so forgetting to regenerate is a red Python build.

**Two defects the fixtures surfaced, both real.**

- **The two overlay readers were fail-open in different ways.** Swift's `SpeakerLabelStore.read` filters an empty cluster key, an empty or non-string name, and a non-integer segment key; Python's `read_overlay` checked only that `labels` and `segments` were dicts and kept everything inside. So a hand-edited or truncated sidecar carrying `{"labels": {"THEM-A": ""}}` resolved that speaker to the empty string in a regenerated summary while the Library showed `THEM-A`, and `{"THEM-A": 42}` put an integer where a name goes. A zero-padded segment key (`"01"`) was worse: Swift keys segments by `Int` so it matched, Python looks up `str(i)` so it did not, and the same sidecar produced two different transcripts. Python now filters exactly as Swift does and canonicalizes segment keys. Both sides stay fail-open; they are now fail-open the same way, which is the property that actually matters.
- **`publish_state`'s vocabulary was unpinned.** `PublishResult.state` is an opaque string Swift passes through, but six sites compare it against the bare literals `"full"` / `"partial"` / `"none"` (`MeetingRow`'s badge and its retry-vs-publish action, `AudioRetention.isSettled`, `LibraryScope.needsYou`, `MeetingDetailView+Header`). A fourth value, or a rename, would leave every one of them taking the else-branch: a publish that landed nowhere renders as a clean success and the retention sweep treats it as settled, which is deletable audio. The fixture now carries the vocabulary, derived by exercising every `publish_state` branch rather than transcribed, and the Swift suite asserts both the spelling and the `isSettled` semantics per value.

**`<stem>.speaker_labels.json` now stamps `schema_version: 1`**, the one cross-language sidecar CI2 missed. Both readers stay fail-open on it (an unknown value is ignored, not rejected), matching `TranscriptCorrectionStore`; it exists so a future shape change is diagnosable from the file rather than inferred. `ModelDownloadSupervisor.handleEvent` went from `private` to internal so the fixture can drive it.

**Acceptance verified by deliberate drift, both directions.** Renaming `page_url` in the Python writer fails at generation time with a message naming the Swift reader that would break (the generator cannot invent an expectation for a key that no longer exists). Changing a *value* or a resolution rule (`publish_state` returning `"ok"`, `apply_overlay` upper-casing a name) regenerates cleanly and then fails the **Swift** suite, which is precisely the drift that used to pass both trees. Both were run and reverted.

**Docs.** `CONVENTIONS.md` gained a "Cross-tree golden fixtures" index (all eight, both trees, with the regeneration command) plus a "Model prefetch progress stream" section, and its publish-result, speaker-labels, and `schema_version` notes were amended. Root / `daemon/` / `pipeline/` `CLAUDE.md` quick indexes point at the new section. `README.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` were checked and needed nothing: no user-visible surface changed, and the new script falls under `scripts/`'s existing "dev tools".

**[DONE] T3 (P2): shipped-untested tests + coverage harness.** The load-bearing untested residue: `SearchIndexer`'s reconcile/signature diff (UX16, shipped with zero test references), `StatusBarController` (731 lines, the always-visible surface, zero references), `Notifier`'s response-identifier-to-delegate routing (a consent surface), and `__main__.py`'s dispatch (the pipeline front door); and no coverage measurement exists in either tree (pytest-cov absent from dev extras, no `--enable-code-coverage` anywhere). Scope: pin reconcile (first-scan build, mtime-change reindex, dead-stem delete, no-change no-bump); extract a pure `StatusBarModel` derivation (title/icon/menu state from AppState + processing count + NDA + permissions + download state) and a pure Notifier routing map per the repo's `decide()` idiom, table-test both; a parametrized dispatch test over every registered command name; add pytest-cov to dev extras + `scripts/coverage.sh` (pytest --cov=mp; swift test --enable-code-coverage + llvm-cov report) and record a baseline here. Optional: `SystemAudioCapture.pcmBuffer(from:)` conversion. Acceptance: new tests green in both suites; `scripts/coverage.sh` prints per-module numbers for both trees; the baseline is written down.

**Shipped 2026-07-20.**

**The three extractions, and the defect each one found.** All follow the `MicGate.decide` / `AudioRetention.decide` idiom: explicit inputs in, a value out, the AppKit / SQLite / UserNotifications host reduced to forwarding and effects.

- **`SearchIndexReconciler.decide(indexed:live:)`** (10 tests). The UX16 reconcile was `private`, on a background queue, wrapped around a concrete `SearchIndex` (a serial queue over a raw SQLite handle) with two filesystem reads inline, so it was not reachable from a test at all. Now the diff is a value and the host keeps the transcript read. No latent defect here, but the no-change-no-bump case is now pinned, and it is the one that keeps a rescan from re-deriving the whole Library list for nothing.
- **`StatusBarModel.derive(_:)`** (24 tests). Found the menu header reading `MeetingPipe: Idle` during a stop while the title read `Stopping…`, because `setStopping()` called `rebuildMenu(state: .idle)`; `stateLabel`'s `.stopping` case was written, correct, and unreachable. Deriving the header from the real state makes the author's own case live. `StatusBarController` shed 136 lines.
- **`NotificationRouter.route(...)`** (15 tests). Found a misroute in a consent surface. `notifyMicOnlyRecording`'s `.granted` case says the permission is fine and no system audio reached the recorder, go read `recorder.log`; it deliberately sets no category and no action button, so a plain tap is the only interaction. It was posted as `perm-stop-<file>`, which matched the generic `perm-` rule, so that tap opened the Screen Recording pane, the one place that cannot help someone whose permission is already granted. It now has its own id namespace and routes nowhere. The suite also asserts the property nothing enforced: exhaustively over every id namespace times every action times both tap kinds, no response ever fires two routes.

**The `__main__` dispatch test** (76 assertions) AST-extracts the router's `if cmd == ...` / `if cmd in {...}` branches and pairs each with its lazy `from .<module> import main` line, so all 34 accepted spellings across 22 modules are covered and a newly registered subcommand is picked up automatically rather than needing a new test. It pins what a lazy-import router cannot tell you at startup (every name reaches a real callable `main(argv)`), plus the dash-and-snake_case alias convention, that no command name collides with the pre-dispatch help/version handling, that every command appears in `USAGE` (the only discovery surface), and that an unknown subcommand exits 2, not 1.

**Coverage harness.** `pytest-cov` added to the dev extras; `scripts/coverage.sh [both|python|swift]` runs pytest with `--cov=mp` and `swift test --enable-code-coverage` piped through `llvm-cov`, ignoring `.build/` and the test targets so the SPM checkouts do not dominate the table. It restates the line-coverage number separately because `llvm-cov report` has four columns labelled `Cover` (region / function / line / branch) and reading the wrong one is easy. Two gotchas cost time and are commented in the script: the profdata lives beside the build products rather than where `--show-codecov-path` points, and the naive binary glob matches the `.dSYM` bundle nested under the same `.xctest/Contents/MacOS/` path, which llvm-cov rejects as "not a valid object file".

**Baseline (2026-07-20, measured, not estimated).**

| Tree | Line coverage |
|---|---|
| Pipeline (`src/mp`) | **84%** (5376/6393 lines, 894 tests) |
| Daemon (`Sources/`) | **30.4%** (12908/42446 lines, 1389 tests) |

Per-file, for the surfaces this task targeted:

| File | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `SearchIndexReconciler.swift` | n/a (did not exist) | 100% (21/21) |
| `NotificationRouter.swift` | n/a (did not exist) | 100% (65/65) |
| `StatusBarModel.swift` | n/a (did not exist) | 95.2% (179/188) |
| `SearchIndexer.swift` | 0% | 0% (73 lines, now pure I/O + Combine wiring) |
| `Notifier.swift` | 0% | 0% (305 lines, now `UNUserNotificationCenter` construction + delegate effects) |
| `StatusBarController.swift` | 0% (731 lines) | 0% (595 lines, now `NSMenuItem` / `NSImage` construction) |

The three hosts staying at 0% is the intended shape, not a shortfall: what is left in them is AppKit and UserNotifications object construction, which needs a real `NSStatusItem` and a real notification center. The decisions they used to hide are now at 95-100%. The daemon's 30.4% is dominated by SwiftUI view bodies and the AVFoundation / ScreenCaptureKit / AX layers, which is the honest number to watch rather than one massaged by excluding them.

**Not done, deliberately.** No coverage threshold is enforced and CI does not run `coverage.sh`. The task asked to make coverage measurable and record a baseline; turning that into a ratchet is a separate decision with its own cost (a floor that fails a PR for touching a view file), and it was not asked for. The optional `SystemAudioCapture.pcmBuffer(from:)` leg was skipped: it is a format-conversion path whose inputs are `AVAudioPCMBuffer`s, so a test would need real audio buffers and would be an integration test, not the table test the rest of this task ships.

**Docs.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the menu-bar, notifier, and search-index entries now name their pure halves), `CONVENTIONS.md` (the three new `decide()` examples in "Testability via clock and decision injection"), root / `daemon/` / `pipeline/` `CLAUDE.md` (the coverage command in each build-and-test block, and the daemon's pure-logic bullet), `.gitignore` (coverage output). `README.md` was checked and left alone: the notification misroute is a bug fix to an undocumented deep link, and no documented flow changed.

**[DONE] CI5 (P2): truth fences.** This review found the same failure class END4(b) fixed once, three more times (a documented, rendered knob consumed by nothing) and the event-category table five categories behind the emitters despite DOC7. Scope: a CI step that extracts keys from `config.example.toml` + the README config reference and diffs them against the actual readers (`Config.swift` / `ConfigStore.swift` / `config.py`), and one that greps `Log.event` / `events.emit` category literals and diffs against the CONVENTIONS table (rejecting non-snake actions like `toolbar.action`). Sequence after HYG2 and DOC9 make the current state clean. Noted but not gated here: an arm64 pipeline CI leg; the Swift-warning fence lands with CONC4 (its own acceptance). Acceptance: reintroducing a dead knob or an undocumented event category fails CI.

**Shipped 2026-07-20 as a ratchet.** The spec sequenced this after HYG2 and DOC9 "make the current state clean", and neither has landed, so the fence would have gone red on arrival. The owner chose the ratchet instead: build both fences now, put every known-drift item in an allowlist tagged with the task that clears it, and let anything NOT listed fail from day one. That satisfies the stated acceptance verbatim (reintroducing a dead knob or an undocumented category fails CI) without waiting, and it inverts the dependency: HYG2 and DOC9 are now "delete these allowlist lines", and **a stale allowlist entry fails too**, so neither task can be declared done while its drift survives. The lists cannot outlive what they describe.

**One check beyond the spec, and it is the important one.** The spec's design (extract documented keys, diff against the readers) cannot detect the failure class it was filed for. A dead knob **is** parsed, **is** persisted, and **is** rendered as a live Preferences control; that is exactly why it looks alive and why END4(b) shipped one. A documented-versus-parsed diff sees no drift at all. So the fence carries a third check: for each parsed key, does anything anywhere ever read the value. It finds exactly HYG2's three (`recording.sample_rate`, `detection.debounce_start_sec`, `transcription.language`) and nothing else.

Getting it to zero false positives took three passes, and the conservatism is deliberate, because a false positive here sends someone to delete a live setting:

- Only reads through a **config receiver** count (`cfg.x`, `configStore?.x`, `config.detection.x`). A bare `.sampleRate` matches every `AVAudioFormat` in the recorder and faked a consumer for `recording.sample_rate`.
- **Assignments do not count.** `config.sampleRate = 48000` in `SystemAudioCapture` is a write to a ScreenCaptureKit object, not a read of ours.
- The receiver pattern needs `_?`: publishers hold the config as `self._cfg`, and `\b` will not match inside `_cfg` because `_` is a word character. Missing this reported `notion.include_full_transcript` as dead when `publish_notion.py:76` reads it.
- `config.py` is **not** excluded from the search, even though it defines the models: the receiver rule already skips its parse lines, while its derived predicates are real consumers. `zero_egress` reading `cfg.modes.workflow_nda_mode` is the only thing keeping that key alive.
- The Preferences UI **is** excluded. Rendering a control for a knob is what makes a dead one convincing, not evidence it drives anything.

**Three findings the spec did not have.**

- The event-category count was **4, not 5**. `recording` was documented on 2026-07-14 (commit db9fb3e97), after the 2026-07-12 assessment wrote the spec. The live set was `daemon`, `detail`, `prefetch`, and a Swift-side `pipeline`.
- The **config-key drift is larger than the four keys named**. Ten keys the pipeline parses are absent from `config.example.toml`, not four: the whole `[obsidian]` section is six keys, and `[lan]` is two. The example file is what every doc points at as the reference, so a third completeness axis was added (a key in the README but not in the example file) and DOC9's allowlist covers all ten.
- `summarization.local_adapter_path` is **not** drift. It is commented out in `config.example.toml`, which the first extractor read as absent. A commented key still shows the user the knob exists, so commented keys now count as documented.

**Free drift fixed inline** (facts, not decisions, so no reason to allowlist them): the four categories are documented in the CONVENTIONS table with a note on why `pipeline` legitimately appears in both rows (`LocalServerReaper` emits one row about the pipeline's process), and `detail` / `toolbar.action` is renamed `toolbar_action`. Nothing consumed the old action name, so the rename is a pure convention fix.

**Deliberately not built.** The fence is a CI job, not a pre-commit hook: the whole-state scan takes ~2.7 s, too slow for every commit, matching the DSN3 token guards which are also CI-only. It is whole-state rather than diff-based on purpose, because both failure classes go wrong *by omission* (a knob stops being read, a table stops being updated) and a diff guard sees no added line to flag. The two items the spec explicitly did not gate here (an arm64 pipeline CI leg, the Swift-warning fence) stay out; CONC4 already shipped the latter.

**Verified by reintroducing each drift class**: a new documented+parsed-but-unconsumed knob, an emitted category absent from the table, and a dotted action literal. All three fail with an actionable message; all three were reverted. 1389 daemon + 894 pipeline green.

**Docs.** `CONVENTIONS.md` (a new "Truth fences (CI5)" section, the categories table now lists all 19 + 3 with the `pipeline`-in-both-rows note, and "Adding a new action" names the snake_case fence), root / `daemon/` / `pipeline/` `CLAUDE.md` (the command, plus a "don't add a config key without a consumer" rule on each side). `README.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` checked and unchanged: no user-visible surface moved, and the new script falls under `scripts/`'s existing "dev tools".

**[DONE] STOR4 (P2): scheduled automatic backups.** Shipped 2026-07-23. Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Original scope in the ToC row above. The corpus is irreplaceable (the 2026-07-05 review's own framing), yet `mp backup` ran only when the owner clicked "Back up now" or remembered the CLI, and doctor merely reported how stale that was: observing the staleness is not fixing it.

**The agent.** A new `BackupSchedulerService` installs `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.meetingpipe.backup.plist`, a `StartCalendarInterval` running `mp backup <destination>`, and `launchctl bootstrap`/`bootout`s it, driven by an "Automatic backup" toggle in Preferences ▸ Storage beside STOR3's existing destination picker + "Back up now" button. The destination is baked into the plist's `ProgramArguments`, so changing it in Preferences rewrites the agent rather than silently keeping the old folder; with no destination the agent is removed however the toggle reads, since an agent invoked without a directory would fail every night into a log nobody reads. The STOR3 last-backup age line is the run's visible outcome, unchanged and already in the right place.

**Deviation 1 (shared mechanism, not a copy).** The spec said "copy the AI4 launch-agent mechanism exactly". Copied literally that is ~60 lines of plist writing plus the launchctl dance duplicated, and this repo has a documented precedent for what that costs: `publish_fs`'s module-private copy of `_render_summary_md` drifted from the original until PIPE7 deleted it. So the mechanism was **lifted** into a shared `LaunchAgentScheduler` (plist body, install/remove, `launchctl`), with `DigestSchedulerService` and `BackupSchedulerService` reduced to what is actually theirs: label, `mp` subcommand, events. Two real callers, no speculative generality. The digest's public API and its `digest_schedule_installed` / `_removed` events are unchanged.

**Deviation 2 (daily, not weekly).** The digest is weekly; a backup defaults to **daily**. A weekly cadence on an irreplaceable corpus means up to seven days of new meetings exist on exactly one disk, which is the risk STOR4 was filed against. The frequency is a picker (Daily / Weekly); daily omits launchd's `Weekday` key entirely, which is how launchd expresses "every day", and the Day picker greys out. Defaults: daily at 21:00, late enough that the day's meetings are in, early enough that the Mac is usually awake. A Mac asleep at that hour backs up on wake.

**Deviation 3 (an unmounted-drive refusal in `mp backup`).** Not in the spec, and a real hazard that unattended scheduling *creates*: `mkdir(parents=True)` cannot tell "the drive is unplugged" from "the folder is new", so a scheduled backup to `/Volumes/Backups` with the drive away would create that path on the startup disk, write gigabytes into it, stamp the marker, and report success. Doctor and Preferences would then show a *fresh* backup that is on the wrong disk, and the real drive would mount at `/Volumes/Backups 1` next time it was plugged in. That defeats this task's own acceptance ("last-backup age stays fresh") by making the age line lie. `backup.check_destination` refuses exactly that signature (a missing volume root under `/Volumes`, nothing else) before anything is created or stamped, exiting 1 with a message naming the fix; a mounted volume and every path elsewhere behave as before.

**Verified:** `LaunchAgentSchedulerTests` (8: the shared mapping, incl. the nil-weekday daily shape and that a daily plist still round-trips through `PropertyListSerialization`, since an unserializable shape is a silent no-fire) + `BackupSchedulerServiceTests` (4: daily drops the weekday whatever the picker holds, weekly pins it, the frequency round-trips through its persisted string, distinct label) + a trimmed `DigestSchedulerServiceTests`; full daemon suite **1427 green**. Python: 4 new `test_backup_restore` cases (unmounted refused, mounted allowed, non-`/Volumes` never refused, and a refusal leaves no marker, since a fresh-looking marker is the actual damage); full suite **911 green**, ruff + pyright clean, CI5 truth fences OK (the new `backup_schedule_installed` / `_removed` actions are snake_case under the documented `coordinator` category).

**Docs.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the daemon file list gains `LaunchAgentScheduler` + `BackupSchedulerService` and the digest entry shrinks to its specifics; the on-disk table gains the `com.meetingpipe.backup.plist` row naming its log files), `README.md` (an "Automatic backup" paragraph in the backup runbook, plus the unmounted-drive behaviour), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a "Scheduled `mp` jobs" quick-index row saying a new scheduled job adds a thin service, not a second plist writer). Checked and unchanged: `CONVENTIONS.md` (no schema or event-category change; `coordinator` is already documented and there is no central action registry), `docs/SETUP.md` (no install step, prerequisite, permission, or first-run path moved), `design/` (only existing `SettingsToggleRow` / `SettingsMenuPicker` / `SettingsRow` primitives used, and the JSX kit does not recreate the Storage pane), root `CLAUDE.md`.

**Acceptance:** toggling on installs the agent and toggling off removes it (unit-pinned mapping; the install path is the same one AI4 has been running in production). Owner-owed, as with every menu-bar surface here: eyeball the Storage section, and confirm one scheduled backup lands unattended and the age line goes fresh, which needs a real overnight fire on the owner's Mac.

---

**[DONE] LOCAL11 (P2): warm-server identity verification.** Shipped 2026-07-23. Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Original scope in the ToC row above. The filed problem was real and reproduced: `_is_healthy` checked HTTP 200 on `/v1/models` and nothing else, so a warm `mlx_lm.server` kept serving whatever it was started with after a `local_model` / `local_adapter_path` flip, `_identify_backend` recorded the *configured* model in `<stem>.run.json`, and a LOCAL9 adapter A/B could not distinguish "the adapter did not help" from "the adapter never served".

**One deviation from the filed scope, forced by measurement.** The spec said "compare the `/v1/models` payload against the configured identity". That endpoint cannot answer the question. mlx-lm 0.31.3's `handle_models_request` scans the HuggingFace cache and lists *every* downloaded MLX model, appending `--model` only when it is a filesystem path, so a repo-id server is indistinguishable from any other cached repo; `/v1/chat/completions` is no better, echoing back the `model` the caller itself sent (`self.requested_model`). So identity is read from the **server's own argv**: `lsof` on the port, `ps` on that pid, parse the `--model` / `--adapter-path` flags `build_server_command` emitted. That is strictly better than the filed route in the way that matters: it covers a server *we did not spawn*, including the daemon's warm `mp serve-local` (which `execvpe`s, so its argv is the server's) and one a previous `mp` left behind. The LOCAL10 marker gained `adapter_path` and serves as the fallback when `lsof` is unavailable. The `lsof`-on-the-listening-port shape is the one LOCAL12's bench already uses.

**As built.** `local_server.served_identity(port)` returns a `ServedIdentity` (pid, model, adapter, source `argv`/`marker`) with a pure `parse_identity` underneath, reading each flag value to end-of-string-or-next-` --` so an adapter path containing a space (`~/Library/Application Support/...`) is not truncated. `LocalSummaryClient._reuse_is_safe` runs it before reusing a warm server, and `terminate_server` (extracted out of `reap_orphan`, which now calls it) does the killing. Three rules, each unit-pinned: **unknown is not mismatch** (an unparseable `ps` line, nothing listening, or a non-mlx process on the port all reuse, because respawning on ignorance costs a multi-GB reload); **only kill what you spawned** (this process, or a marker naming that exact pid; never the daemon's warm server, which would race its supervisor); **never launder the mismatch** (when the run proceeds anyway, `model` / `adapter_path` report what *served*).

The daemon owns the other half, because Python rightly refuses to kill what it did not spawn: `LocalModelPreloader` gained an `Identity` + a pure `decide(running:desired:)` and a `refresh(...)` wired to `ConfigStore.didPersist` (already debounced 500 ms), so a model or adapter change restarts the warm server and an unrelated save is a no-op. The restart is a delayed main-queue hop, not a `waitUntilExit()`: blocking main on a subprocess is the freeze class the audio and AX paths already paid for, and if the rebind loses the port race the failure mode is a cold first summary, never wrong weights, since `LocalSummaryClient` then spawns its own correctly-identified server. `ConfigStore.summarizationLocalAdapterPath` is a read-only computed read off `rawDocument`, following `outputSinks` rather than becoming a persisted knob with no Preferences control (which is what the CI5 dead-knob fence exists to catch).

Attribution was fixed on the `auto` ladder too, which the spec did not name: `_AutoFallbackClient` was reporting `cfg.summarization.local_model` after a cloud-to-local fallback, the same mis-attribution one rung further in. It now reads the served pair off the client before the `with` block closes it, through a deliberately tolerant `_served_local_identity` (attribution metadata read *after* a summary already succeeded must never cost the summary, the same rule `orchestrate` applies to a failed sidecar write). `write_run_sidecar` gained `adapter_path`, always present and `""` for every backend but a local one serving an adapter, so a reader never has to distinguish "no adapter" from "written by an older `mp`".

**UI affordance:** none built, none needed. The doctor probe surfaces verbatim in Preferences → Integrations → Run doctor… (`DoctorRunner` spawns `mp doctor` and streams its whole output into the sheet); the respawn and the daemon restart are automatic.

**Verified:** 937 pytest, 1433 swift test, ruff, pyright, CI5 truth fences, all green. 24 new Python tests (13 over the identity layer, 5 over client reuse/respawn, 5 over the doctor probe, 1 over the sidecar) and 6 new Swift tests over the restart decision. Docs: CONVENTIONS (marker schema gains `adapter_path`; a new "served identity is read from argv" section with the three rules and the mlx-lm finding), ARCHITECTURE (`local_server.py` entry rewritten; `LocalModelPreloader.swift` added to the daemon file list), README (a "changed the model and summaries did not change" troubleshooting entry), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (pipeline-subprocess index row).

**Known limitation, stated rather than papered over:** argv is the *spawn contract*, not a readback of the loaded weights. It catches a server started for a different model or adapter; it cannot catch a server that failed to apply an adapter it was correctly handed. That distinction is load-bearing here, because mlx-lm 0.31.3 appears to drop `--adapter-path` on its own: `ModelProvider.load` looks up `self._adapter_map` with the *already-mapped* model path while the map is keyed `"default_model"`, so `load_default()` resolves the adapter to `None`. If that reading holds, LOCAL9's `--adapter-path` seam serves the base model regardless of anything meeting-pipe does, and the working route would be the per-request `adapters` body field. Left unfixed deliberately: it is an upstream defect in LOCAL9's serving seam, not LOCAL11's identity question, and changing how the adapter is requested wants the owner's call plus a measured A/B. Worth filing before the LOCAL9 remainder is attempted, or that A/B will measure the base model against itself.

---

**[DONE] LOCAL15 (P2): serve the LoRA adapter that `--adapter-path` silently drops.** Shipped 2026-07-23, hours after being filed, because it blocked LOCAL9's owner-owed A/B. Route (a) chosen as the spec expected: `LocalSummaryClient._chat_completion` sends `adapters` in every request body when one is configured, omits the key entirely when it is empty, and `build_server_command` keeps `--adapter-path` so LOCAL11's argv readback still resolves and upstream's fix lands for free if #1249 ever merges.

**Verified against a real `mlx_lm.server`, not only in unit tests.** The acceptance bar both directions: a run carrying a real (throwaway, 2-iteration) LoRA returned a summary through the client's own request path, and `local_adapter_path` pointed at a directory that does not exist stopped the run with `LocalSummaryError` naming the path, instead of quietly producing a base-model summary. One correction to the filing session's evidence: the server does send a body on that 404 (`{"error": "The adapter path does not exist: ..."}`); the earlier probe raised before reading it. The client still appends its own diagnosis, since the server's message covers only a missing path, not a directory that exists without loadable weights.

**Measured reload cost** (the spec asked for the number whatever it turned out to be), 7B on the M1 Pro: first request carrying `adapters` **5.89 s** (the model key flips and the weights reload), warm adapter requests **0.24 s**, switch back to base 1.94 s. So it is one reload per server lifetime, not per request, and it sits under A15's 8.4 s base load. Not material enough to teach `mp serve-local` to warm the adapter-carrying key; if that changes, that is the follow-on the spec named.

**Two LOCAL11 beliefs corrected in the same commit,** both consequences of the same fact. LOCAL11 respawned a server we own when only the adapter differed, to stop a LOCAL9 A/B comparing base against base; that respawn never delivered it, because the fresh server was handed the same ignored `--adapter-path`. An adapter-only difference is now not a reuse mismatch (respawning costs a multi-GB reload and changes nothing), and `LocalSummaryClient.adapter_path`, which `<stem>.run.json` records, returns the configured path rather than the argv readback, deliberately unlike `model`. That asymmetry is real: since the request field overrides whatever the server was started with, our configured value is what served in both directions, including a warm adapter-carrying server answering our adapter-free requests from the base model. `test_adapter_flip_alone_replaces_a_server_we_own` was rewritten to `..._does_not_replace_a_server` with the reasoning in its docstring, and the CONVENTIONS served-identity section carries the rule.

**Verified:** 940 pytest (3 new: the `adapters` field present when configured, absent when not, and a bad path failing loudly with the path named), ruff, pyright, CI5 truth fences, all green. Docs in the same commit: `config.example.toml`, README's "Improving local quality", the CONVENTIONS served-identity section, and LOCAL9's precondition note updated to record that both of its blockers are now resolved.


Band origin: filed 2026-07-23 (found while shipping LOCAL11, commit 65ba629; verified three ways in the filing session before filing). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

**LOCAL15 (P2, new): `summarization.local_adapter_path` is a no-op, because mlx-lm drops the `--adapter-path` flag LOCAL9's serving seam passes.** The base model serves regardless of the setting, silently, with no warning on either side. Correctness in character (a documented, README-advertised config knob does nothing), banded P2 rather than P1 only because the blast radius as of filing is zero: the knob is opt-in with an empty default and no adapter has ever been trained on this Mac. The owner may re-band.

## Context

`build_server_command` (pipeline/src/mp/summarize_local.py:154) appends `--adapter-path <dir>` when `summarization.local_adapter_path` is set. Both spawn paths use it: the lazy `LocalSummaryClient._spawn` and the warm `mp serve-local` (`summarize_local.main`, which `execvpe`s the same argv). `mp dogfood --adapter` is on the same seam and equally affected. The flag never reaches the model loader.

Verified three ways on 2026-07-23, so the pickup session does not need to re-derive any of it.

**(1) Code read**, mlx-lm 0.31.3 as installed in `pipeline/.venv`. `ModelProvider.__init__` keys its maps by the alias: `self._adapter_map["default_model"] = self.cli_args.adapter_path`. `ModelProvider.load` then does `model_path = self._model_map.get(model_path, model_path)` followed by `adapter_path = self._adapter_map.get(model_path, adapter_path)`, so the adapter lookup is keyed by the **already-reassigned** real model path while the map is keyed `"default_model"`. The lookup misses and the adapter falls back to `None`. The sibling line immediately below it, `draft_model_path = self._draft_model_map.get(draft_model_path, draft_model_path)`, keys off its original argument, so the adapter line is inconsistent with its own neighbours: a defect, not a design choice.

**(2) Empirical**, a real `ModelProvider` constructed with `_load` stubbed so the resolved arguments are observable without loading weights. `load_default()`, which is the path the server takes at startup, resolves to `adapter_path=None`: the adapter is dropped. A chat-completion request carrying the repo id as `model` and no adapter field also resolves to `None`. A request carrying the `adapters` body field resolves to the real path: the adapter is served.

**(3) Upstream, reported and unfixed.** Issue [ml-explore/mlx-lm#1248](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/issues/1248), "mlx_lm.server --adapter-path silently ignored at startup", open since 2026-05-06 with the same root cause and a repro showing a bogus adapter path starting cleanly instead of raising. [PR #1249](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/pull/1249) carries the one-line fix (bind `alias = model_path` before the reassignment, look up `_adapter_map` by the alias), open and unmerged since 2026-05-06, last touched 2026-06-04. Duplicate fix [PR #1365](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/pull/1365) was closed unmerged on 2026-06-07. `main` on GitHub still carries the buggy line, and 0.31.3 is the latest PyPI release, so waiting for upstream or bumping the pin is not a plan.

**Why this is urgent rather than merely true.** LOCAL9 is `partial` with the owner-owed remainder "run `mp train-adapter --source runs --adapter-path <dir>`, then `mp dogfood --adapter <dir> <held-out transcript>`, hand-grade the scorecard, and set `summarization.local_adapter_path` only if it wins". If the adapter never serves, that A/B compares the base model against itself and returns a false negative, and an honest-looking "the adapter did not help" would wrongly close LOCAL9 after the owner spent real training and grading time. Resolve this before that A/B is run.

**Why LOCAL11 did not catch it, and structurally cannot.** LOCAL11 (shipped 2026-07-23, archived in [q6-final.md](../q6-final.md)) verifies what a warm server is serving by reading the listening process's argv. Argv is the **spawn contract**, not a readback of the loaded weights: it catches a server started for a different model or adapter, but a server handed `--adapter-path` and silently ignoring it looks correct from the outside. LOCAL11's archive entry records this under "Known limitation". This task is that blind spot.

## Scope

Decide between three routes and implement one. The evidence favours (a), but the decision is the task.

**(a) Send the adapter per request.** Add `"adapters": <path>` to the payload in `LocalSummaryClient._chat_completion` (pipeline/src/mp/summarize_local.py) when `self._adapter_path` is set. This is a documented public field, not an internal detail: `mlx_lm/SERVER.md` specifies `"adapters": (Optional) A string path to low-rank adapters`. It is also the only route the empirical test proved works on the installed version.

Two facts checked during filing so the pickup session does not re-check them. An **absolute** path is fine despite SERVER.md's "the path must be relative to the directory the server was started in" note: `mlx_lm/tuner/utils.load_adapters` does `Path(adapter_path)` then `.exists()`, so an absolute path resolves correctly, which matters because `local_adapter_path` holds a user-chosen absolute path. And that same function raises `FileNotFoundError` on a missing path, so route (a) additionally buys the fail-loud behaviour issue #1248 argues the CLI flag should always have had: a typo'd `local_adapter_path` currently fails silently and serves base.

**(b) Vendor the upstream one-line patch.** Rejected as the default because patching a dependency's source in a repo with a deliberately small dependency surface is a maintenance liability that survives every `uv sync`, and it needs re-verifying on every mlx-lm bump.

**(c) Pin and wait for upstream.** Weakest: the fix has been open and unmerged for roughly two and a half months and one duplicate was closed without merge.

**Cost to measure if (a) is chosen.** The server loads the base model at startup through `load_default()`, giving `model_key = (repo, None, None)`. The first request carrying `adapters` flips the key to `(repo, adapter, None)` and forces one model reload. The key is stable afterwards, so this is a one-time cost per server lifetime, not per request, but it partially defeats the warm-start benefit `mp serve-local` exists to provide, for adapter users specifically. Measure against the warm-start budget A15 baselined (cold 39.8 s, warm 31.4 s, 8.4 s load, 7B) and record the number; if it is material, the follow-on is teaching `mp serve-local` to warm the adapter-carrying key rather than the base one.

**Keep `--adapter-path` on the spawn command even if (a) lands.** Two reasons, and this is load-bearing rather than tidiness. LOCAL11's identity check reads the served adapter out of argv, so dropping the flag would make an adapter-serving server report `adapter_path=None` and read as a mismatch against a configured adapter on every run, inverting the check it was built for. And the flag becomes correct for free if upstream ever merges #1249. If the pickup session concludes the flag must go, LOCAL11's `served_identity` in pipeline/src/mp/local_server.py has to change in the same commit, along with the served-identity section of CONVENTIONS.md.

## Explicitly not

Not fixing mlx-lm upstream: filing or shepherding a PR against ml-explore/mlx-lm is out of scope, and #1249 already exists. Not changing `mp train-adapter`: the training side is unaffected, since it shells out to `mlx_lm.lora` and never touches the server. Not running the LOCAL9 A/B: that stays LOCAL9's owner-owed remainder, and this task is its precondition, not its replacement. Not revisiting whether an adapter is worth adopting: an honest negative from a *valid* A/B remains a fine terminal outcome for LOCAL9.

Observed but deliberately unfiled: mlx-lm's `/v1/models` cannot name the loaded model (it lists the whole HuggingFace cache), which LOCAL11 already worked around and documented in CONVENTIONS.md; no task is needed unless upstream changes that endpoint's contract.

## Acceptance

The decisive bar needs no trained adapter and no grading, so it is checkable on any Mac the moment the change lands: with `summarization.local_adapter_path` pointed at a directory that does not exist, a local summarize run **fails loudly** with the adapter path named, instead of silently producing a base-model summary. That single check distinguishes "the adapter is served" from the 2026-07-23 behaviour, and it is the reason it belongs in acceptance rather than the A/B.

Unit-pinned, in `pipeline/tests/test_summarize_local.py` against the existing fake-server fixture: the chat-completion payload carries `adapters` set to the configured path when one is configured, and omits the key entirely when `local_adapter_path` is empty (an empty string must not be sent as a path). A test that fails if the code regresses to relying on `--adapter-path` alone, so the next session cannot quietly undo this.

If `--adapter-path` is kept on the spawn command, per the scope note above, the existing LOCAL11 identity tests in `pipeline/tests/test_local_server.py` and `pipeline/tests/test_summarize_local.py` stay green unchanged; if it is removed, `served_identity` and the CONVENTIONS.md served-identity section change in the same commit and their tests are updated to match.

Green: `uv run --extra dev pytest -q`, `ruff check src tests`, `pyright`, `python3 scripts/truth_fences.py both`, plus `swift build` and `swift test` if the daemon side is touched at all.

Docs in the same commit: `config.example.toml`'s `local_adapter_path` comment and README's "Improving local quality" section describe the real serving route, and LOCAL9's spec note about preconditions to its A/B is updated to record that this one is resolved. The measured reload cost from the scope section is recorded in the ship note, whatever it turns out to be.

**[DONE] AI5 (P3, spike): meeting-type classification.** Band origin: intelligence layer (AI band). Prototype on-device meeting-type labels over the existing library (a short local call, or a heuristic over attendees / cadence / title) and show the results to the owner before committing a permanent `MeetingSummary` field. Uncommon among competitors (only MeetGeek auto-detects). Acceptance: a throwaway classifier labels the library by type; the spike returns a recommendation on whether the labels are useful enough to wire into grouping/digests and earn a schema field; no schema change lands until that decision.

**Progress (2026-07-11): prototype shipped, usefulness judgment owner-owed.** `mp classify-meetings` (`classify.py`) discovers every `<stem>.summary.json` and labels it against a fixed 10-type taxonomy. Default is a zero-egress heuristic over the title, workflow name, and attendee count (first-match keyword rules, then attendee-count fallbacks); `--llm` adds a per-meeting label from `engine.complete_text` beside it, routed through `effective_backend` so a regulated/NDA config stays on-device (the AI/DV standing rule), plus a `--json` payload. It arms the egress guard via `entry.prepare` (secrets only under `--llm`). CLI-only is intentional (a spike harness, de-CLI exempt like dogfood / analyze-detection); registered in `__main__.py` + USAGE + the ARCHITECTURE subcommand table. The pure heuristic, discovery, LLM-snapping, and report are pinned by 8 `test_classify.py` tests; a live heuristic run on this Mac confirmed the end-to-end flow, but the library was only 2 test meetings (both landed `client`), too thin to judge.

**Resolved 2026-07-23: NO-GO on a permanent `MeetingSummary.meeting_type` field.** The blocker was never the classifier, it was the corpus: the library has since filled to **193 summarized meetings**, so the spike's headline question was finally answerable. Both legs ran for real. The `--llm` leg was forced onto the local backend (the owner's config is `backend = "anthropic"`, so a plain `--llm` would have shipped 193 real meeting titles to the cloud; the spike asks about *on-device* labels, so `effective_backend` was made to return `local` and the run went to the already-warm Qwen2.5-7B mlx server, zero egress).

Method: two independent labellers, completely different failure modes, same 193 meetings, same fixed taxonomy. Neither is ground truth and hand-labelling 193 meetings was not on offer, so **agreement is the measurement**: where both land on a bucket the bucket is real, where they diverge the taxonomy is not separable from the available signal.

Result: overall agreement **88/193 = 45.6%**, and that headline flatters it because the mass sits in one easy bucket. Per-label Jaccard: `standup` **94.1%** (50 vs 49, 48 shared) and nothing else clears 41%. `one_on_one` **11.0%**, `other` 6.5%, and `client` / `brainstorm` / `retro` / `interview` all **0.0%**, four types with not one meeting both labellers agree on; `retro` and `interview` the heuristic never assigns at all. **1 of 10 types is reliable, 4 of 10 have zero overlap.**

Four reasons the signal is not there, all upstream of the classifier: (1) `summary["title"]` is the *summarizer's invented* title, so the heuristic keyword-matches model prose and the `--llm` leg asks a small local model to re-classify a large cloud model's paraphrase, neither ever sees the real meeting name; (2) the workflow tag has 3 distinct values library-wide (`Personal` 95, `General` 52, `Client work` 1, plus 45 with none) and `Personal` alone spreads across 7 of the 10 types; (3) `attendees` is `len(summary["attendees"])`, diarization-derived rather than a roster, with 31 meetings reporting 1 attendee and only 13 reporting exactly 2; (4) the LLM does not abstain, it relabels the garbage bucket, its `other` is 6.7% against the heuristic's 35.8% purely by moving 36 of those meetings into `one_on_one`, which it assigns 48 times while calling only 5 of the 13 genuinely-2-person meetings a one-on-one.

One real defect found and fixed: the heuristic's `one_on_one` rule matched `\bsync\b`, which produced **22 of the 33** `one_on_one` labels, and **20 of 33** `one_on_one` meetings had 4 to 12 attendees ("Team Sync: Releases, 5.11 Issues & Compliance" at 12). `sync` names a cadence, not a headcount; removed and pinned by a test. Removing it is honest but does not rescue the verdict, and that is the load-bearing point: agreement moves only 45.6% to **47.7%** because those 22 meetings fall straight back into `other` (35.8% to 43.0%). The problem is the available signal, not one bad regex.

Incidental, left unfiled: roughly 8 rows in the `other` bucket are not meetings at all (test recordings, a voicemail, "No Substantive Meeting Content"). A type field would be labelling junk.

The specific thing not to build: a field that is 94% right on a quarter of the library, coin-flip on another quarter, and silently wrong on the rest, presented in the UI with one confidence everywhere; grouping the Library by it would make the low-agreement types look like categories rather than guesses. The one bucket that does separate (`standup`) is already reachable by typing "standup" into the UX16 full-text search, which searches the real text instead of a derived guess. **Revisit gate is a better input, not a better classifier**: a genuine meeting title, which means the calendar, which CAL1 measured as structurally blind on this Mac. A larger local model is not the unlock, the 7B was not wrong through weakness, it was guessing because the input does not contain the answer.

**Verified:** 938 pytest green, ruff, pyright, CI5 truth fences. Artifacts: `docs/spikes/ai5-meeting-type-classification.md` (verdict), `docs/spikes/ai5_agreement.py` (the reducer, so the measurement re-runs in one command), `docs/spikes/ai5-classification-results.json` (aggregates only, deliberately no meeting titles, the raw dump is the owner's real meeting names and does not belong in git). Harness kept as owner-dev (de-CLI exempt, spike class). No schema change landed, acceptance honored. **Unblocks AI9**, whose gate was "the AI5 owner verdict": AI9 is not blocked by this, and should not take a dependency on `meeting_type`, its WF8 reassignment pairs are real owner corrections and a strictly better-grounded label.

**[DONE] UX25 (P3): quick-ask handoff.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Quick Find and Ask are separate stories; detect a question shape in Cmd+K (trailing question mark, or an "Ask about ..." row under the results) and hand off prefilled-and-running via the existing `meetingpipe://ask` plumbing. Acceptance: typing a question in Quick Find reaches a cited answer in two keystrokes.

**Shipped 2026-07-24.** P3, promoted by the owner asking for it by id. Both halves of the filed parenthetical are built rather than picked between, because they answer different failures: the trailing question mark decides *ranking*, and the row decides *reachability*. Every non-empty query now carries an `.ask` row (`QuickFindAskRow`: bubble glyph, "Ask about your meetings", the query as its subtitle, a trailing `⌘↩` hint). It leads the list when the query is question-shaped and trails the results when it is not, so `⌘K` -> type a question -> `↩` is the whole path to a cited answer, which is the acceptance. `⌘↩` asks from anywhere in the panel regardless of selection, so the row is never stranded below 50 results.

**Where the shape is decided.** A new pure `QuickFindList` (`items(query:matches:)` + `isQuestionShaped`) owns the row order, split from `QuickFindRanker` so the placement rule is unit-testable without the panel; `QuickFindItem` is the `.meeting` / `.ask` sum the model now publishes instead of a bare `[Match]`, with namespaced ids so a meeting whose stem is literally `ask` cannot collide with the row in `ForEach`. **A leading question word ("how", "what") is deliberately not a signal**, against the obvious temptation to add one: it opens as many meeting titles as it does questions, and a wrong lead turns Return into the wrong action, while the trailing mark is a deliberate keystroke the user typed. Nothing is unreachable without it, only unranked, because the row and `⌘↩` are always there.

**One handoff path, not two.** The Quick Find row calls `Coordinator.handleAutomation(.ask(question:), source: "quick_find")`, the same router `meetingpipe://ask` and the App Intent go through, rather than setting `libraryModel.pendingAskQuestion` itself; the AI3 `AskView.consumePendingQuestion` prefill-and-run seam is reused untouched. `handleAutomation` gained a defaulted `source:` so the `automation` / `command` event distinguishes an in-app caller from a real deeplink instead of inflating the URL scheme's usage trail (no new category or action, so CI5 is unaffected).

**Two behaviours changed beyond the row.** A query with zero meeting matches is now an Ask-only list under a quiet "No meetings matched." caption instead of a dead-end empty pane. And `refresh()` resets the highlight to the top on every open: the panel outlives its window, so a selection left three rows down last time would silently steer the first Return of the next open, which the two-keystroke acceptance cannot tolerate.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean, 1441 daemon tests green (8 new over `QuickFindList`), CI5 truth fences clean. Docs synced: README (the Quick Find handoff under the filter bar, plus the Ask rail's entry points), ARCHITECTURE (a new Quick Find surface bullet, since the panel had no entry in the surfaces list, and the Automation bullet's in-app caller), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a Quick Find quick-index row). CONVENTIONS needed nothing (no schema, category, or action changed). No UI affordance is owed: the task is the affordance. Owner-owed: the standard rendered eyeball on the panel, not exercisable here.

**[DONE] DV3 (P2): People rail.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. The DV pivot's re-open condition is now structurally met: FEAT3-ROSTER and FEAT3-MANAGE shipped, so named, stable, cross-meeting speaker identities exist. Scope: a Facts-style INSIGHTS projection ("People"): one row per enrolled person showing their meetings, the open actions they own (the summaries already carry owners), and last-seen; rows link back to meetings (the Quick Find hook), rename flows through the existing roster rename. Read-only projection over existing sidecars, no schema change; local-only per the AI/DV rule. Gate: needs a minimally populated roster, which is owner-owed enrollment volume; build after real usage proves a handful of stable identities. Acceptance: selecting a person lists their meetings and open actions; a roster rename reflects everywhere; the empty-roster state points at how to enroll.

**Shipped 2026-07-24.** The gate cleared before a line was written: the owner's roster held 4 enrolled people, and a dry run of the derivation over the real 193-summary library gave Heorhii 27 meetings / 66 open actions, Ivan 18 / 24, Maharsh 6 / 3, Rana 3 / 4. Not a rail that would render empty on day one.

**Where identity comes from.** The roster is the spine, not the transcript. A raw diarization label is not a person (`MeetingCast` documents why), and only an enrolled voice carries a name across meetings, so the rail has one row per `roster.json` entry and none for an unenrolled owner string. That is a real exclusion in this library, and deliberately so: the top action owners include `1`, `2`, `3`, `speaker_0`, and `THEM-A`. An identity you cannot rename or merge is not an identity, and a rail full of them would be a rail nobody could maintain.

**Where membership comes from.** Two sources, unioned, because each alone under-reports. (a) The summary's `attendees`, resolved through `<stem>.speaker_labels.json` so an in-app naming counts even when the summary predates it (and a name the overlay introduced counts even when the summary never listed it, the `MeetingCast` "New person" case). (b) Ownership of one of the meeting's open actions, because the summarizer names owners the diarizer never labelled: Heorhii is an attendee in 27 summaries but an action owner in 66, so attendance alone would have hidden meetings whose actions the rail was already showing. Matching is trimmed + case-insensitive exact. Nothing looser: first-token or substring matching would fold two different Ivans into one row, which is the wrong-name failure the roster's own two-gate accept rule exists to avoid.

**Why it does not read transcripts.** The projection re-derives on every `MeetingStore.revision` bump. The library's `<stem>.json` transcripts total ~80 MB against ~1 MB of `<stem>.summary.json`, and the summary's `attendees` is the same speaker set the transcript produced, so the cheap source is also the correct one. `<stem>.embeddings.json` was considered and rejected: it exists for 27 of 175 meetings.

**The one deviation from "read-only", and why it was necessary.** The spec framed this as a read-only projection whose rename "flows through the existing roster rename", with the acceptance line "a roster rename reflects everywhere". Those two do not compose: `mp roster rename` renames the entry in `roster.json` and nothing else, so summaries and transcripts keep whatever name they were written with, and a rename would have emptied the renamed person's history in the very rail that lists it. Shipping the rail without addressing that would have made a pre-existing FEAT3-MANAGE defect newly visible while leaving it broken. So the rename **carries**: after the CLI call lands, the new name is written into the reversible `<stem>.speaker_labels.json` overlay of every meeting the person appears in. Three shapes are covered, because a person reaches a meeting three ways: the pipeline baked the roster name into `<stem>.json` at finalize (no overlay entry, so map the raw label), the name came from an in-app naming (rewrite the overlay value), or a regenerate did both. `<stem>.json` is never rewritten (FEAT3-UNDO's invariant) and identity mappings are pruned, so renaming back collapses what the carry added rather than leaving "A displays as B" behind. A failed carry is not a failed rename: the roster entry has already moved, so the per-meeting failure is logged and the caller is told the rename succeeded.

**One rename path, two surfaces.** `RosterRename` owns the prompt, the CLI call, the carry, and the events; the People rail and Preferences ▸ Pipeline ▸ People both call it. Preferences previously had its own copy of the prompt and the rename, which is exactly how the two would have drifted.

**Files.** New: `PeopleRail.swift` (pure derivation + the pure overlay edit + the last-seen wording), `PeopleView.swift` (the rail, its off-main aggregation, and the rename glue), `RosterRename.swift` (the shared rename operation). Touched: `LibraryScope` / `LibrarySidebar` / `LibraryWindow` (the `.people` scope, its INSIGHTS row placed second, its center-column branch), `Coordinator+Automation` (`meetingpipe://library?scope=people`), `SpeakerLabelStore` (a `replace(overlay:)` single write, so the carry does not pay N read-modify-writes), `PreferencesView+Pipeline` (onto the shared path; its own `promptForName` deleted).

**UI affordance:** the rail itself (de-CLI rule satisfied; no new subcommand). Rows show "N meetings · N open actions · last seen 3d ago" collapsed, and expand to the two lists. The empty state names the only way in (name a speaker from a transcript), since Preferences and Facts are both dead ends when nobody is enrolled.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean, 1469 daemon tests green post-merge with UX25 / AI5 / LOCAL15 (27 new here: 20 over the derivation and the pure rename edit, 7 driving the carry against a real directory, plus a `.people` rail fence in `LibraryScopeTests`), CI5 truth fences green, and the 941 pipeline tests + ruff green across the merge. No Python change of its own. Docs: README (rail list, the People paragraph, the roster-management paragraph's rename-carries note, the automation URL row), ARCHITECTURE (INSIGHTS view list, Smart folders glossary, the `roster.json` and `speaker_labels.json` disk-table rows), CONVENTIONS (the `roster.json` contract's rename section rewritten around the carry), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a People rail row in the quick index), `design/ui_kits/macos_app/SummaryLibrary.jsx` (the INSIGHTS rows, which were also still missing Digests from AI4).

**Known limitation, stated rather than papered over:** membership is by name, so a meeting where a second person of the same first name spoke counts toward the enrolled one, and a carry would rename them there too. Both are reversible (drop the overlay entry), and no alternative exists without a per-meeting identity the sidecars do not carry. The rail is only as complete as the roster: a person the roster never matched in a meeting is invisible there, which is the enrollment volume the gate was always about.

**[DONE] CAL2 (P3): pre-meeting prep card.** Shipped 2026-07-24. Band origin: feature review 2026-07 (owner approved 2026-07-05). Original scope: before a recurring meeting, the relevant context (the matched workflow's last summary and its open actions) is a hunt through the Library. Add a user-invoked "Last time" affordance on the prompt panel (and `mp prep <workflow>` for the shell), quiet register, never a popup. The workflow-keyed version needs nothing new. The calendar-aware timing and title/attendee seeding that would have sequenced behind CAL1 are closed with it (CAL1 NO-GO 2026-07-17: the owner's calendar is not in macOS EventKit), and stayed closed here.

**On the P3.** The ToC row read P3, and the standing rule is to leave P3 alone. This band's own definition is the exception the rule names: "nice to P3 (P3 here means promote on demand, per the AI5/I8 precedent)", and the row's Status was `new`, not `deferred`. An explicit `/tech-task CAL2` is that promotion.

**As built.** Three pieces, one on each side of the language boundary plus the view.

`PrepCard.swift` is the pure projection: given a workflow name, a stem, a start time, and a decoded `MeetingSummary`, it returns the card or **nil**. Nil is the load-bearing case: a summary with no points and no open actions has nothing to recap, and returning nil is what keeps the affordance quiet rather than offering an empty card. Points come from `summary`, falling back to `decisions` when a run put its content there and left the recap empty (a local model does this often enough to matter); actions drop the resolved ones (both spellings, `resolved` and the legacy `done`) and the count past the cap is carried as `moreActions` so the card says it is truncating instead of silently dropping. `PrepCardStore` is the disk half: a directory listing, a newest-first walk restricted to stems that already have a summary, and a stop at the first meeting that yields a card. Matching prefers the sidecar's stable `workflow_id` and falls back to `workflow_name`, so a **renamed** workflow still finds its own history while a pre-`workflow_id` sidecar still matches by name.
