It also **falls through** a last meeting with an empty summary (a no-speech run, a failed run that still wrote a shell) rather than reporting an empty "last time".

`PrepCardView.swift` renders it inside the prompt panel. The whole layout is one arithmetic pass (`PrepCardView.layout(for:)`) shared by the row positions and by `height(for:)`, so the panel can animate to a frame the content then fills; a unit test asserts the height exceeds the top of the lowest row, which is the failure this shape exists to make impossible. Rows are single-line with tail truncation, deliberately: the card answers "what was this about" at a glance and the Library holds the full text one click away.

`MeetingPromptWindow` gained the affordance and the disclosure. The pill's subviews now hang off a fixed-height `NSLayoutGuide` rather than the background view's `centerYAnchor`, so growing the panel does not re-centre the action row over a taller panel; the card is pinned below that guide, and expanding moves the panel's origin down by the height delta so the **top edge stays put** under the pointer. The button is a new `PromptGhostButton` (hairline capsule, hover fill, `isOn` while the card is open) rather than `MPButton`, for the same reason `PromptRecordButton` is purpose-built: `MPButton` fills via its layer behind an `NSButtonCell` and does not composite on the panel's vibrant `hudWindow` material. It carries the `disclosureTriangle` accessibility role, not `button`, because it is a state.

**Deviations from the filed scope, each deliberate.** (1) The card is bounded to the **last** meeting, not to every open action the workflow has accumulated. Carrying older actions forward is a different feature with a different failure mode, and that failure mode is already filed: AI10 (the digest dumping 970 actions). "Last time" means last time. (2) The lookup is **eager, on `present`, off-main**, not lazy on click, so there is no click latency and, more importantly, the button can be absent when there is nothing to show. That is the quiet-register requirement: an affordance that appears and then says "nothing" is worse than no affordance. (3) `PrepCard.relativeDay` is not a second date formatter: `RelativeMeetingDateFormatter` gained an `Elapsed` bucket + `elapsedString` beside its existing calendar-position `Bucket`, because "3 days ago" and "Wed 09:00" answer different questions and both belong in one place. The rendering is localized through `RelativeDateTimeFormatter`; `mp prep` mirrors the same buckets in English. (4) `mp prep` is exempt from the SEC13 entry contract, added to `ENTRY_CONTRACT_EXEMPT` with a reason: it reads two local sidecars and reaches no sink, engine, or token, exactly like `mp actions` beside it. The tripwire caught it, which is the tripwire working.

**Overlap noted, not resolved by accident.** AI6 already injects the same workflow's prior decisions + open actions into the *summarizer*. CAL2 is the human-facing read of the same relationship, off the same sidecars; neither depends on the other.

**Verified:** 1447 `swift test` green (14 new), 956 pytest green (18 new), `swift build` clean, ruff + pyright clean, CI5 truth fences clean. No new config key, no new event category (the disclosure emits `prep_card_opened` under the existing `coordinator` category), no schema change, no new dependency. Docs synced: README (prompt-panel behaviour, the workflow "where it shows up" list, an `mp prep` section beside `mp actions`), ARCHITECTURE (subcommand table row, the prompt-panel entry under UI surfaces, a **Prep card** glossary entry and the prompt-panel one), `design/ui_kits/macos_app/MeetingPrompt.jsx` (the ghost capsule + the expanded card, so the recreation stays true for the part that changed).

**Owner-owed:** the standard rendered eyeball. The panel is a menu-bar-app `NSPanel` and is not screenshotable here, so the expand animation, the card's density on the vibrant material, and the truncation point on a real summary want one real meeting to confirm.

---

**[DONE] AI8 (P3): quiet meeting analytics.** Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Durations, workflow tags, and per-speaker talk time (the ADR 0009 stereo split gives you-vs-them ground truth) exist per meeting and nothing aggregates them. Hours-per-workflow and per-meeting talk ratio, quiet register (a Library view or a digest section, no streaks, no gamification), fully local. Acceptance: per-workflow hours and talk ratio for a date range, computed from existing sidecars.

**Shipped 2026-07-24** as the `Meeting time` rail under INSIGHTS, the fifth cross-library projection beside Facts, People, Ask and Digests. `MeetingStats.swift` is the data layer in the `Facts.swift` shape: a pure `derive` (bucket the range's meetings by workflow, total the hours, sum the speech), a pure `talk` (split one transcript into yours / theirs / unattributed), and `StatsLoader` for the one disk read. `StatsView.swift` is the chrome: a range segmented control over a table of workflow rows plus a Total line, in the PeopleView idiom.

**Chose the Library view over the digest section** (the spec offered either). The digest is a generated document with its own weekly cadence; hours-per-range is a thing you interrogate with a control, not something to freeze into a snapshot, and a rail view is also what satisfies the de-CLI rule with no CLI at all. No `mp` subcommand was added: everything the numbers need is already in the daemon's hands.

**Where the numbers come from.** The hours half is free: `Meeting.durationSec` and `Meeting.workflowName` are already on every row `MeetingStore` scanned, so bucketing costs no I/O. The talk half is not: the you-vs-them ground truth lives in the diarized `<stem>.json`, where the pipeline stamped `summarization.user_label` onto the owner's speaker (channel-assigned mic speaker first, per ADR 0009). That makes this the only projection that reads transcripts, which is precisely the ~80 MB read DV3 was built to avoid (measured on the owner's library: 215 transcripts, 80.4 MiB, 383 KiB average, against under 1 MB of summaries). It reads through the canonical `TranscriptLoader` rather than a second parser, so it counts exactly the lines the Transcript tab shows (in-app speaker naming included), and memoizes per stem for the view's lifetime, because a `MeetingStore` revision bumps on any sidecar write in the recordings directory and a finishing pipeline run would otherwise re-parse the whole library several times over.

**Two truthfulness rules, both tested, both the kind of thing that lies quietly.** A meeting whose owner voice was not identified reads `not measured`, never `you 0%`: `label_me_speaker` deliberately no-ops when no signal identifies the owner, so a silent meeting and a failed identification are indistinguishable in the transcript, and printing zero would assert the first. And `speaker_unknown` speech is kept in its own bucket rather than folded into "them", because speech the diarizer credited to nobody is not evidence that somebody else was talking; counting it as them would understate the owner by however badly diarization went that day. The footer names the uncovered meeting count, and when `summarization.user_label` is unset it names that instead, pointing at Preferences (nothing can be measured without it).

**Share is seconds-weighted** across the bucket, not a mean of per-meeting ratios: an hour-long call the owner barely spoke in and a two-minute one they filled must not count the same. A dedicated test pins the difference (3% versus a naive 50%).

**Deliberately not a dashboard.** No bars, no trend lines, no targets, no streaks, no per-person breakdown. `docs/research/2026-06-26-meeting-level-ux.md` put the coaching-shaped end of this family out of register for a single-user local tool and kept exactly one analytic, a self-directed talk-time view; that is what shipped. The visual vocabulary is deliberately the one already in `PeopleView` (List, section header, text rows, the rail's 8pt workflow dot with the transcript row's baseline guide), so no new visual primitive was invented blind.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean (no new warnings), 1578 `swift test` green (37 new in `MeetingStatsTests`), CI5 truth fences clean. No Python change, no new config key, no new event category, no schema change, no new dependency. One line beyond the spec: `?scope=time` on the existing `meetingpipe://library` deeplink, so the new rail is reachable the way the other four are. Docs synced: README (the rail list, a `Meeting time` paragraph, the deeplink scope row), ARCHITECTURE (the Library-window subsystem entry, a `MeetingStats.swift` note, the Smart-folders glossary entry), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a quick-index row), and `design/ui_kits/macos_app/` (the rail row plus a `clock` icon, so the kit recreation stays true; the kit mocks no projection body, for this or any of the other four). CONVENTIONS.md and PRODUCT.md checked, unaffected.

**Owner-owed:** the standard rendered eyeball. The Library is a menu-bar-app window and is not screenshotable here, so the row density, the two-column number alignment, and the footer's length on a real library want one look.
---

**[DONE] AI9 (P3): learned workflow routing.** Shipped 2026-07-24. Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12. Original scope in the ToC row above. WF8 reassignments quietly produce labelled correction pairs (detected source + title mapped to the corrected workflow) that nothing consumes, and manual recordings always fall to the default workflow. When enough pairs exist for a source, suggest (never auto-apply) the corrected workflow on the prompt chip. Gated on the AI5 owner verdict and real reassignment volume. Acceptance: after N same-source reassignments, the prompt pre-selects the corrected workflow with a visible, dismissible hint.

**The gate was self-blocking, which is why this shipped whole rather than waiting.** The spec's second gate ("real reassignment volume") could never clear on its own: the pairs the filing describes did not exist anywhere durable. `MeetingLibraryService.reassignWorkflow` emitted `workflow_reassigned` to `events.jsonl`, which size-rotates and drops its oldest generation (PERF7), and `MeetingMetaSidecar.reassigned` rewrites the workflow block in place, keeping the corrected side and forgetting that a correction happened at all. A count read back from either would have shrunk on its own over time, which is worse than no count. So the recording half had to be built before the volume could ever be observed, and once it is built the surfacing half is inert anyway (silence is the default), so shipping only the recorder would have left a partial for no benefit. The owner's log holds **one** reassignment ever; no backfill was attempted, because there is nothing to backfill from.

**What shipped, three pieces.** (1) `WorkflowCorrectionStore` persists `WorkflowCorrection` pairs to `~/.config/meeting-pipe/workflow_corrections.json` (the `ConsentStore` / `SavedSearchStore` single-file idiom, oldest dropped past 500), written from `reassignWorkflow` through an injected closure **after** the sidecar rewrite succeeds, so a failed rewrite never teaches the prompt something the library disagrees with. A meeting with no `source_bundle_id` is refused rather than stored: it could never be keyed back to a detection, and an unreadable row in an append-only file is exactly the dead weight HYG3 sweeps for. (2) `WorkflowRoutingHint.suggest` is the pure rule, every input passed in. Two key tiers, specific first: bundle + `normalizeTitle` (lowercased, letters only, single-spaced, capped at 120, so "Standup 07/23" and "Standup 07/30" are one key and a title carrying a genuinely changing word simply degrades to the broader tier instead of guessing at a title grammar), then bundle alone. A tier fires on a strict plurality at or above `minimumCorrections` = 3; the plurality half is what keeps three corrections spread across three workflows silent, and a suggestion the rules already make, or one naming a since-deleted workflow, is not a suggestion. (3) `MeetingSessionController` arms the pre-selection as `preselectedRoutingHint`, keyed to the source it was computed for, and `MeetingPromptWindow` renders the caption.

**The privacy clamp is the one real design decision beyond the spec.** The acceptance says "pre-selects", which on its face would let a Record click nobody read move a meeting the rules routed to an **NDA** workflow onto one that is not, and egress it. So `Suggestion.preselects` is false in exactly that direction: the hint is still shown (`Use <Workflow>?` instead of `Suggested · Undo`) and one explicit click still takes it, but the chip stays on the NDA workflow. Tightening the other way (suggesting an NDA workflow over a cloud one) pre-selects normally. This is the line WF8 itself draws when it confirms NDA transitions with an alert, so the two surfaces now agree about which direction is the sharp one.

**Precedence and lifetime.** Resolution is now override > AI9 hint > rules > default. The hint deliberately does **not** reuse `pendingWorkflowOverride`: that field is only cleared by a start, so a skipped prompt would leave an armed suggestion lying around for the next meeting. `preselectedRoutingHint` carries its source and `beginRecording` ignores it when the source differs, so an armed hint dies with its meeting. It is armed only where it is visible: never on auto-consent, a hotkey start, or a manual recording. The caption's Undo commits through `selectWorkflow`, the same path the chevron override menu uses, so it lands as an explicit pick that outranks the pre-selection rather than as a second way to choose a workflow.

**Deliberately not covered: the manual-recording half of the filing.** A manual start (`Ctrl+Opt+M`) raises no prompt, so there is no surface on which to show a visible, dismissible hint, and pre-selecting silently would be the auto-apply the spec forbids. The acceptance criterion is prompt-scoped and is met; giving manual recordings a learned default is a different feature needing a surface that does not exist. Named here rather than quietly dropped.

**No `meeting_type` dependency**, per the 2026-07-23 gate note: the pairs are real owner corrections and never touch AI5's refuted classifier. Nothing egresses (no engine call, no new dependency, no config key, no new event category: the four new actions ride the existing `workflow` category), and deleting the JSON file makes the prompt forget and costs nothing else.

**Verified:** 1612 `swift test` green (34 new across `WorkflowRoutingHintTests`, `WorkflowCorrectionStoreTests`, `MeetingLibraryServiceTests`, `MeetingSessionControllerBranchTests`), `swift build` clean, CI5 truth fences clean, no em-dash. No Python touched. Docs synced: README (learned-routing section under Matching, the precedence list, the prompt-panel bullet), ARCHITECTURE (the workflow-resolution diagram gains the hint step, the Workflows + Storage sections, the prompt-panel UI entry, the on-disk file table; also corrected a stale line that credited `WorkflowInspector.swift` with the prompt chip, which has lived in `MeetingPromptWindow` for some time), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (Workflows quick-index row), `design/ui_kits/macos_app/MeetingPrompt.jsx` + `index.html` (the caption, plus a second gallery instance showing the suggested state).

**Owner-owed: nothing blocking, one thing worth an eye.** The design-kit recreation was rendered and checked, which caught a real geometry bug before it shipped: the caption was first hung under the chip, where at any width past the chip's own it lands 2pt inside the vertically-centred waveform's box. It is now pinned to the pill band's bottom margin instead, clearing the waveform outright at any width. The real `NSPanel` still wants one glance on a live meeting for the caption's weight on the vibrant `hudWindow` material, but there is no known defect and nothing waits on it.

**[DONE] PIPE10 (P2): sleep-resilient summarize retry.** Shipped 2026-07-24. Band origin: filed 2026-07-17 (from the 2026-07-16 sleep incident). Original scope: filed from the 2026-07-16 incident (stem `20260716-163031`, a 110-min Teams meeting). The lid closed one minute after the meeting ended, so post-meeting processing froze under clamshell sleep; transcription finished across dark-wake slivers, and the Anthropic call left during a 21 s dark wake at 19:57:48. The Mac then slept through the request: the 120 s httpx read timeout (`summarize._REQUEST_TIMEOUT`) rides the monotonic clock, which freezes during sleep, so the budget burned away across dark-wake slivers (the run's 5 s progress beats reached `elapsed_s: 123` over 2 h 13 m of wall time) and fired under a minute after the 22:10 lid-open wake as `APITimeoutError`. That error is deliberately excluded from tenacity retries (`_should_retry_anthropic`: "A stall should drop straight to the backend fallback (local) instead"), but the fallback (`_AutoFallbackClient`) exists only for backend `auto`; this meeting's workflow pins `anthropic`, so the exclusion's own rationale does not hold there and the run hard-failed with `<stem>.error.json` stage=pipeline. The manual Library Retry then succeeded in 36 s, proving the request itself was fine.

**What shipped.** `_create_message` captures `time.time()` and `time.monotonic()` around the SDK call. On `APITimeoutError`, a wall-minus-monotonic gap of at least `_SLEEP_DIVERGENCE_SEC` (60 s) means the machine demonstrably slept mid-request, so the request never had its 120 s of awake time to answer in; the divergence is logged at WARNING naming both clocks, and the request is re-issued once, fresh. Everything else is unchanged: clocks that agree mean a genuine stall and raise on the first attempt, a second timeout is terminal whatever the clocks say, and non-timeout transients keep their existing tenacity classes and backoff. The wrapper is deliberately **inside** the tenacity-wrapped function rather than around it, which is what keeps `_should_retry_anthropic` a single-rule predicate; its docstring now points at where the one retryable timeout is handled, so the next reader is not surprised that a timeout can produce two requests.

**Scope held, and the promotion question answered rather than deferred.** `diarize_cleanup` imports this same `_create_message`, so it inherited the fix for free. The spec asked whether the helper is worth promoting to `engine.complete_text` and the `openai` provider: **not now.** Those paths reach cloud through `run_with_local_fallback`, which already absorbs `APITimeoutError` into a local fallback, so the failure class that made PIPE10 sharp (a pinned-cloud run with nowhere to fall) does not exist there today. Extracting a shared helper for a hole with no observed incident is the speculative abstraction the repo's own rules warn about; the memory note records where the seam would go if a pinned-cloud `engine` path ever appears.

**Verified:** 4 new tests in `test_summarize_backend.py` pin the acceptance's three paths plus the once-only bound (slept-through retries once and succeeds; slept-through twice still fails, 2 calls; genuine stall fails on attempt 1, 1 call; a 429/500 pair still rides tenacity to success, 3 calls), using a strict scripted clock that raises rather than inventing a value if the code samples more often than expected. The two sleep tests were confirmed to fail (calls == 1, the old behaviour) with the divergence threshold disabled, so they discriminate rather than pass vacuously; the stall and transient tests pass either way, which is the point. 986 pipeline tests, ruff, and pyright green. Docs: README gains a Troubleshooting entry for the lid-close case (user-visible: a class of Failed rows now self-heals, and the log line to grep is named); ARCHITECTURE, CONVENTIONS, and `pipeline/CLAUDE.md` were checked and needed nothing, since no subcommand, sidecar, schema, event category, or module responsibility moved. No new dependency, no config key, no UI surface (this is a resilience fix inside an existing path, not a new capability, so the de-CLI rule does not bite).

**[DONE] AI10 (P2): digest action list is unbounded.** Shipped 2026-07-24. Band origin: assessment review 2026-07-12.

**Original scope.** `mp digest` aggregated every still-open action across the whole library into one weekly digest: on the real 199-meeting library that is 970 action items in a single digest, past the point of review and, before the 2026-07-13 rendering fix, enough to freeze the reader. That fix was in the daemon (`SummaryRenderedView`'s action list became a `LazyVStack`, and the digest reader moved from the narrow, vertically-split center column to the wide detail pane, `DigestReaderView`), so the acute symptom was already gone; what remained was upstream. The digest should scope its action list to the review window, then cap, group and rank what it shows inline, with the long tail reachable but not dumped. Local-only, no new egress. Acceptance: a weekly digest over the full library renders a bounded, grouped action list scoped to its review window, with the raw count available but not the default inline dump.

**The window rule, and the one place the spec was softened.** An action is in the review period if it was raised in the window (its meeting is dated on or after the start) **or** it is already overdue, due today, or due inside the window ahead. The second leg is a deliberate widening of "scoped to the review window": a promise from February that is 159 days past due is precisely what a weekly review is for, and a digest that hid it to honour the window would be worse than the one that dumped it. What the window actually bounds is the *dormant* backlog, not the *old* one: the undated action from a meeting months ago that nobody closed and nothing will ever make due. That set is where the 970 comes from, and it is the only thing dropped. Both legs read through `OpenAction.age_days`, so no date parsing is duplicated (`age >= -since_days` is "overdue, due today, or due soon" in one comparison).

**Grouping, ranking, capping.** The group key is AI7's series key, the meeting's workflow, falling back to the meeting title when untagged, so no new computation and no new sidecar. Ranking is overdue first (most overdue first), then dated and coming (soonest first), then undated, with higher confidence breaking a tie and the newest meeting breaking that; recency rides as a stable pre-sort rather than an inverted string key. Group *order* falls out of the ranking rather than a second sort: buckets are created in ranked order, so the group holding the single most pressing action leads, and a dict keeps that insertion order. Caps are `MAX_ACTIONS = 25` total and `MAX_PER_GROUP = 5`, so one noisy series cannot crowd out the rest of the week. Deliberately module constants rather than config keys: a knob nothing changes is the dead-knob class CI5 exists to catch, and `mp actions --open` is already the escape hatch.

**Bounded output has to admit it is bounded.** Three surfaces carry the totals, because a number the reader sees quietly becomes the number they believe. (1) An `Action scope` extra section: `Showing 10 of 14 open action(s) from the last 7 day(s), across 3 of 3 group(s)`, the per-group residue (`Weekly standup: 5 of 9 shown`), and the library-wide open total with where the rest is. It is omitted entirely when nothing was hidden, so a small library gains no noise. (2) The narration facts header tells the model it is looking at a slice (`the 3 most pressing of 9 in this period, 9 of them overdue`), so a narrative written from a capped list never asserts the cap as the total; the deterministic fallback bullets count the window rather than the shown slice for the same reason. (3) `digest_generated` gains `actions_in_window` and `actions_open` beside `actions`, and the stdout/`--json` lines report all three, so a run that hid 900 items is visible in the event log.

**One additive schema field, and why that was the right cost.** Grouping had to be visible in the render, not just in the ordering, and `MeetingSummary.actions` is a flat list with no group concept. The alternatives were worse: duplicating every action into extra sections, or suffixing the group onto `task` (which mangles the Notion to-do text). So `ActionItem.group: str | None = None`, set only by the digest, absent from `SUMMARY_TOOL` so no model ever fills it, and null on every meeting summary, which renders exactly as before. Both markdown renderers gained the same `_action_runs` / `actionRuns` split into *consecutive runs* (not buckets, so a renderer never reorders a list it did not build), and the daemon's `SummaryRenderedView` renders those runs with a small-caps group header. The writer contract is all-or-none and is documented in CONVENTIONS, because a mixed list renders its untagged run under whatever heading preceded it. A new `action_groups_ai10` case in `summary-md-golden.json` pins the grouped render on both trees, so the two cannot drift on it.

**UI affordance (the de-CLI rule).** The capability ships with its surface: the Digests reader renders the grouped, bounded list with the scope section inline, and gains a `See every open action in Facts` link that switches the library scope to the Facts rail. That rail already holds the whole open set (clustered by AI7), so the tail is one click rather than a second copy of the data.

**Verified:** 996 pipeline tests (11 new in `test_digest.py`: the window rule keeping overdue and dropping dormant, the rank order, workflow-vs-title grouping, both caps with the hidden counts, the scope section's residue and library lines, its absence when nothing was hidden, the capped-facts header, window-counting fallback bullets, and a library-sized end-to-end that bounds 440 open actions to 25 across 5 groups), 1643 `swift test`, `swift build` clean, ruff, pyright, CI5 truth fences green, no em-dash. End-to-end sanity on a synthetic 254-open library: 10 shown across 3 groups, with the 159-day-overdue February commitment leading. Docs synced: README (a new `mp digest` paragraph plus the Digests rail bullet), ARCHITECTURE (the `digest.py` subcommand row, the `markdown.py` renderer note, the Library INSIGHTS rail entry, and the Prep-card line that still called AI10 an open problem), CONVENTIONS (a new `Action grouping (ActionItem.group, AI10)` section plus the golden-fixture table row). `daemon/CLAUDE.md`, `pipeline/CLAUDE.md` and `design/` were checked and needed nothing: no file moved, no event category or config key was added, and the reader reuses existing tokens.

**[DONE] UX26 (P2): resolve action items from the Summary tab.** Shipped 2026-07-24. Band origin: filed 2026-07-22 (owner feature ask, approved in the 2026-07-22 review session).

**Original scope.** The lifecycle already existed end to end except the affordance on the surface the owner actually reads. `ActionItem.resolved` is the AI1 state (`pipeline/src/mp/schemas.py`); the Facts rail's "Mark done" wrote it straight into `<stem>.summary.json` (optimistic removal, captured-index-with-text-match guard, atomic write, `action_resolved` library event, deliberately no correction record); the Notion publisher round-trips it as a checked to-do; DSN2's needs-republish badge lights when the summary is newer than the last publish, so republish needed zero new logic. The gap, and a truthful-states gap (PRODUCT.md principle 2) besides: the Summary tab rendered resolved and open actions identically, because the renderer dropped the flag entirely (it mapped only task/owner/due/confidence).

**One writer, and where it actually lived.** The spec pointed at `FactsView.markDone`; by the time this shipped that write had already been lifted into `FactsResolver.resolve` in `Facts.swift` (the DV1/AI7 data-layer split), where it had grown a per-file batching loop so two restatements in one summary both landed. The extraction went to `MeetingLibraryService.setActionResolved(summaryURL:actionIndex:taskText:resolved:)` as the spec asked, and the batching loop dissolved: each call re-reads the file, so a second instance in the same summary sees the first one's write. `FactsResolver.resolve` is now the cluster-shaped caller over it, one call per restatement, and the Summary tab's toggle is the other caller. The guard is the one the Facts rail already had, generalized to both directions: prefer the captured index while its task text still matches, else the first text match **in the opposite state**, so a reopen skips the rows that are already open. Blocking disk I/O, so both callers hop off-main; the helper is static and dependency-free precisely so a test can drive it against a temp file without the service's injected collaborators.

**Preserving what the toggle does not own.** The rewrite round-trips through `MeetingSummary.jsonObject()` rather than reconstructing the file from the fields the toggle knows about, which is the FEAT10 interlock: whichever of the two landed second had to keep the shared writer preserving the fields it does not itself edit. The test pins that as a general property (every top-level key except `actions` survives byte-identical, and inside the touched action only `resolved` moves), so it keeps meaning something as the model grows: FEAT10's `evidence` is covered the moment `MeetingSummary` carries it, without the test being touched. A field that Python writes and Swift does not model is still dropped by that round-trip, which is the standing constraint on this file and the reason the interlock is worded as "once Swift models it".

**The renderer's index is the load-bearing detail.** `ActionItemRow.Action` now carries its position in `summary.actions`, which is what the writer is called with. It cannot be the position in the rendered list: an action with an empty task is dropped from the render (it always was) and an AI10 digest splits the list into named runs, either of which shifts the two apart, and a row that pointed one place and wrote another would close somebody else's commitment. The runs partition the array in order, so a cursor over `MeetingSummary.actionRuns` recovers the original index without a second copy of the grouping rule; a test pins the mapping across both an empty row and a run boundary.

**UI + a11y.** The static circle glyph became an always-visible toggle: `circle` when open, `checkmark.circle.fill` when resolved (the same SF Symbol family, so the row never shifts on a click, and filled-as-selected is the sanctioned case in the design system's no-filled-icons rule). A resolved row stays in place, struck through, its task and glyph in `fgMuted` and its chips at 0.7 opacity, reversible with the same click: no removal, no layout jump. State never rides on colour alone, since the glyph shape and the strikethrough both carry it. VoiceOver reads "Mark done: <task>" / "Reopen: <task>", and the control is a real `Button`, so Full Keyboard Access reaches it. The callback is optional and nil by default, which keeps today's static render for the digest reader, the A16 candidate/compare pane, and any other surface with no writable summary behind the rows; `SummaryTab` passes it only for the live summary. The toggle updates optimistically first (the FactsView pattern), and because the write moves the file's mtime, `SummaryTab`'s mtime-guarded reload re-parses on the next store-revision bump instead of skipping.

**One deviation worth naming.** The `action_resolved` event moved down into the shared writer, so the Facts rail's cluster resolve now emits one row per summary it actually changed rather than a single row carrying `instances: N` and naming only the representative. That is strictly more truthful (for a 3-meeting cluster the old single row named two files it had not touched) and nothing reads the attribute, but it is a change to an existing event's shape that the spec did not ask for.

**Explicitly not:** no "remove as noise" affordance (deleting a wrong action already exists via Edit summary, and staying there keeps the LOCAL9 correction corpus fed); no correction record on a toggle (a state flip is not a text correction, the FactsView precedent); no auto-republish (the needs-republish badge is the nudge, DSN2's single-republish-path decision); no cross-meeting dedupe or overdue semantics (AI7 owns that).

**Verified:** 1661 daemon tests green, 15 of them new (`ActionResolveTests`: the index-guard fallback on a shifted summary in both directions, the captured index winning over an earlier text match, reopen, the preservation property, the no-op paths, both events read back out of an isolated `MEETINGPIPE_LOGS_DIR`, the row-index mapping across an empty row and a run boundary, the resolved presentation, and callback-nil staying static). 996 pipeline tests, ruff, pyright, and CI5 truth fences green (no Python changed; `action_reopened` is snake_case in the existing `library` category, which the fence confirms). Docs: README's Summary-tab paragraph, ARCHITECTURE's Library-window section, `daemon/CLAUDE.md`'s Library UI index row, and the design kit's `SLActionItem` recreation. Owner-owed: the eyeball on a real meeting.

**[DONE] HYG3 (P3): dead-code removal, 2026-07-23 sweep.** Shipped 2026-07-28. Band origin: filed 2026-07-23 (owner-commissioned dead-code sweep: two parallel very-thorough passes over daemon and pipeline, every actionable claim re-verified in the filing session by repo-wide word-boundary greps). Promoted on demand, per the band's "P3 here means promote on demand".

**Original scope.** Remove the seven verified dead or misplaced items the 2026-07-23 sweep found, and fix the one doc surface that claims infrastructure which never existed. Headline for calibration: both trees are genuinely clean at the type, knob, dependency, event, branch, and menu-action level; this is a short list, not an excavation.

Pipeline items (each verified: repo-wide `grep -rnw` over pipeline, daemon, scripts, .github, docs returns only the definition and, where noted, tests). (1) `_numbered` (publish_notion.py:560) and (2) `_todo` (publish_notion.py:568): zero callers; the live code builds `numbered_list_item` and `to_do` blocks inline (publish_notion.py:616, :667, the multi-run rich-text and checked-state paths that superseded these single-run helpers). Remove both. (3) `reap_orphan` (local_server.py): zero production callers, three test call sites; production reaping is Swift `LocalServerReaper.reapIfOrphaned()` (direct kill(2), no mp shell-out) and Python `doctor` only reports orphans via `orphaned_server` (alive); the module docstring already assigns reaping to the daemon. Remove the function and its tests. (4) `icloud_desktop_sync_enabled` (cloudsync.py:109): zero production callers, test-only; never wired into `detect_sync_provider`, so the "last-resort corroborator" its docstring describes does not exist in the detector. Remove the function, its tests, and the docstring's corroborator claim; wiring it into the detector instead would be SEC12-adjacent feature work, filed separately if ever wanted.

Daemon items. (5) `MuteLabelsValidator`: referenced only by its own tests; no CI job runs it. The sharp edge was documentation asserting fiction: `docs/architecture/signal-fusion-and-mic-gating.md` described "a CI job (`MuteLabelsValidator`) runs nightly on a build VM with Teams, Zoom, Webex, and Slack pre-installed in each locale" walking AX trees, and `MuteLabels.toml` repeated the claim; no such job has ever existed in this repo. Keep the validation logic only if it is made true (relocate into the test target as the explicit engine of `MuteLabelsValidatorTests`, having verified those tests validate the BUNDLED TOML) or delete it; rewrite the two doc surfaces either way. (6) `RecordingEventLog`: a deliberate shared test double shipped in the production target. Relocate into the test tree if it can serve its consumers there cheaply; otherwise keep and extend the comment so the next sweep does not re-flag it. (7) `MenuBarIcon.svg` + `MenuBarIconRecording.svg`: bundled but never loaded by any code path (the status-bar icon is drawn programmatically, `StatusBarController.makeIdleIcon` / `makeRecordingIcon`); the design source of truth is `design/assets/menubar-icon*.svg`. (8) `CaptureError.bufferConversionFailed`: declared and given an `errorDescription` arm but never thrown; the buffer-conversion failure path drops the frame and returns.

Kept-deliberately register, verified against their recorded decisions, so nobody re-files them: `ProcessAudioSignal` + `usesProcessAudio` + the `.processAudio` evidence class (DET2 NO-GO 2026-07-20, kept dormant by design, documented in the signal file and `NativeLifecycleAdapter`); `MeetingPipeAppIntents.swift` and its six intents (AUTO1, gated behind `MP_APP_INTENTS` pending a Developer ID); `services.NotionPublisher` alias (kept-importable comment at services.py:91-94); `summarize_local._largest_balanced_json_object` alias (documented test-compat comment, and the promoted `json_extract` original is production-live including diarize_cleanup.py:260).

Clean-sweep record, 2026-07-23, so the next sweep diffs instead of redoing: config knobs mechanically fenced (`scripts/truth_fences.py both` green, empty allowlists); no unused dependencies in pyproject main or dev extras (the DOC9 vcrpy precedent already resolved); no dead event categories; no provable dead branches (every pragma-no-cover is a defensive guard or main block); every @objc/menu action has a #selector wiring; all other bundled resources load and all 29 detection-corpus fixtures are INDEX.json-listed; no custom `Notification.Name` exists; all root and daemon scripts are referenced by CI, docs, or each other; single-use SwiftUI subviews and Core public probe/signal types are alive through their own files, the coordinator wiring, or the testability seam.

**Item 5 went the keep-and-relocate way, and the tests decided it.** The branch was conditional on whether `MuteLabelsValidatorTests` validates the shipped catalogue or only fixtures, and it already validates the shipped one: three of its four cases build their catalogue with `MuteLabelsLoader.loadDefault()`, which reads `MuteLabels.toml` out of the `MeetingPipeCore` resource bundle (the fourth passes `MuteLabels(entries: [:])` on purpose, to exercise the `noTomlEntry` finding). So the validator is a real guard on a real file, not a fixture toy, and deleting it would have removed the only thing that fails when that TOML is emptied or malformed. `MuteLabelsValidator.swift` moved to `daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeCoreTests/` unchanged apart from the import it now needs and a doc comment that says what it is; a header comment on the test file records why `loadDefault()` must not be swapped for a fixture.

**The two doc surfaces now name the real guard.** `MuteLabels.toml`'s header and the localization-strategy paragraph in `docs/architecture/signal-fusion-and-mic-gating.md` both said a nightly CI job scraped installed apps per locale. The replacement says what actually protects the file (the unit suites, on every CI push, against the bundled catalogue) and, more usefully, names the gap that no CI here can close: a vendor renaming a button needs Teams, Zoom, Webex, and Slack installed in every locale on a runner, which does not exist, so label drift surfaces from use and the fix is a label added to the TOML. The paragraph also dropped a second fiction found while rewriting it, an `signal:locale_unknown` event the daemon has never emitted; the truthful runtime story is `MuteLabels.recognize`'s cross-locale retry and then the HAL VAD plus RMS fallthrough.

**Item 6 stayed in Core, with the reason written down.** `RecordingEventLog` has consumers in *both* test targets (`MeetingPipeCoreTests` across the signal and gate suites, `MeetingPipeTests` in `MeetingAXWindowWatcherTests`), and two SPM test targets cannot share a source file. Relocating it would have meant a copy that drifts or a third target existing to hold one class, so the smallest truthful change was the doc comment: it ships in Core as a deliberate cross-target test seam.

**One deviation from the letter of the spec.** "Remove the function and its tests" would have taken `terminate_server`'s only unit coverage with it. Two of `reap_orphan`'s tests exercise the SIGTERM path and the SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL escalation, and `terminate_server` is production-live (`summarize_local` calls it on an identity mismatch, LOCAL11), with only the recycled-pid refusal covered elsewhere. Both tests were therefore retargeted at `terminate_server` directly, same bodies and fakes, renamed to match. No new test was written; the coverage simply stopped routing through a dead wrapper.

**Item 8's frame drop was read and left alone.** `SystemAudioCapture.pcmBuffer(from:)` returns nil on any conversion failure and the `SCStreamOutput` callback drops that frame. That is not a bug to fix in passing: the callback runs on the ScreenCaptureKit sample-handler queue, where the daemon's own rule is that logging or file I/O glitches the recording, and a total conversion failure surfaces downstream as an empty recording, which already has a terminal path. Filed nothing.

**Docs.** Beyond the two MuteLabels surfaces, one more had to move: `CONVENTIONS.md`'s cloud-sync section repeated the removed function's claim that `CLOUDDESKTOP` is "consulted only as a corroborator". Nothing consults it now (and the Swift `CloudSyncDetector` never did, so the two implementations that section pairs are back in parity), so that clause was corrected in place. `docs/backlog/q6-final.md`'s LOCAL11 entry still names `reap_orphan` as the function `terminate_server` was extracted from; that was left alone deliberately, since the archive is a dated record and this entry is the forward pointer, the same way LOCAL11's own row points at LOCAL15. README, ARCHITECTURE, SETUP, PRODUCT, `daemon/CLAUDE.md`, `pipeline/CLAUDE.md`, `MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md` and `design/` were checked and needed nothing: no module, subcommand, sidecar, event category, config key, or UI surface changed, and ARCHITECTURE's `local_server.py` paragraph was already written around `orphaned_server` reporting and Swift killing.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean, 1781 `swift test` green (full Xcode present here), 1086 pipeline tests, ruff and pyright clean, `scripts/truth_fences.py both` green. Those are the numbers after rebasing onto FEAT11, last of five siblings that landed while this ran; everything below was re-checked on that base, since the point of the acceptance bar is that nothing reintroduced a symbol this task removed. Acceptance re-checked by grep: `_numbered`, `_todo`, `reap_orphan`, `icloud_desktop_sync_enabled`, `bufferConversionFailed`, and both SVG filenames return zero hits across pipeline, daemon, scripts, .github, design, and the live doc set; `MuteLabelsValidator` no longer appears anywhere under `daemon/Sources`.

**[DONE] UX30 (P2): workflow delete flow (destructive affordance, dismissal, and the silent undo).** Shipped 2026-07-29. Band origin: filed 2026-07-23 (owner report of two defects in the workflow delete flow, plus three adjacent ones found while tracing it), re-verified 2026-07-29 when the owner re-reported both visible defects during the exploratory sweep. Original spec below, then what shipped.

**UX30 (P2, new): deleting a workflow does not read as destructive, does not close its sheet, and can be silently undone by the next keystroke.** The owner reported the first two on 2026-07-23; the third is the one that makes this P2 rather than P3, because a confirmed destructive action can be reversed without the user knowing.

Re-verified 2026-07-29 (owner re-reported both visible defects during the exploratory sweep; all five defects stand as written). The line cites below drifted with unrelated edits; current locations as of 2026-07-29: the actions row with the neutral ghost Delete is WorkflowsView.swift:597-615 (the stale "there is no `.mpDanger` capsule" comment at :603-605, Save's `.defaultAction` at :601, the disabled-for-default at :612); `deleteSelf` without `onCommit` is WorkflowsView.swift:680-686 against `save()`'s commit at :674; the host wiring is LibraryWindow.swift:1014 (edit sheet) and :1058 (new-workflow sheet). One premise sharpened: `MPDangerButtonStyle` lives at BatchActionsPane.swift:642 and is declared `private`, so the promotion into `Preferences/PreferencesControls.swift` this spec already calls for is a precondition of using it here, not an option. Cross-reference: UX32 (filed 2026-07-29) adds rail-row delete and edit entry points that must share this task's fixed flow, so land UX30 first or extract the shared flow in the same change.

Reported defect 1, the Delete button does not read as destructive. `actionsRow` renders it as a neutral ghost capsule, `.buttonStyle(.mpGhost)` with a trash glyph (WorkflowsView.swift:570-575), so it carries the same visual weight as Save beside it. The comment at WorkflowsView.swift:567-569 justifies this as DSN28's move off colored inline buttons and asserts "there is no `.mpDanger` capsule". That assertion is stale: `MPDangerButtonStyle` exists and ships (BatchActionsPane.swift:504), used by the Library's own Danger card for "Move to Trash" (BatchActionsPane.swift:256), and the `.mpDanger` colour token is live in this very file (WorkflowsView.swift:104). So the app already has a destructive-button precedent for exactly this class of action, and the workflow editor is the surface that does not use it. Decide and record: promote `MPDangerButtonStyle` out of its private scope into `Preferences/PreferencesControls.swift` (the documented home for shared primitives per daemon/CLAUDE.md) and use it here, matching BatchActionsPane, or keep the ghost capsule and carry the emphasis on the label and glyph via `.mpDanger`. Either resolution must also correct the stale comment, since the next reader will otherwise re-derive the same wrong premise.

Reported defect 2, the sheet stays open after a confirmed delete. `deleteSelf` calls `store.delete` and nothing else (WorkflowsView.swift:644-650), while `save()` ends with `onCommit?()` (WorkflowsView.swift:638). `onCommit` is documented at WorkflowsView.swift:49-51 as the hook "called after a successful Save so the host sheet can dismiss", and both hosts wire it to their close path: the edit sheet at LibraryWindow.swift:655 (`onCommit: onClose`) and the new-workflow sheet at LibraryWindow.swift:699 (`onCommit: { onClose(s.id) }`). Delete never reaches it, so after confirming, the user is left editing a workflow that no longer exists on disk, in both sheets.

Found defect 3, the confirmed delete is silently undone by Save. This follows directly from defect 2 and is the reason to treat the pair as correctness. The stranded sheet still holds the full form state, and Save carries `.keyboardShortcut(.defaultAction)` (WorkflowsView.swift:565), so one Return (the reflex after any dialog) calls `save()`, which calls `store.upsert(clone)`; `upsert` finds no matching id, takes its append branch, and persists (WorkflowStore.swift:63-68). The workflow the user just confirmed deleting is written back to disk, with no message either way. In the new-workflow sheet the same path resurrects a stub the editor's Delete had just removed.

Found defect 4, a refused delete is indistinguishable from a successful one. `deleteSelf` discards the return value (`_ = try store.delete(id: workflow.id)`), and `delete` returns `false` without throwing when the id is not found or the workflow is the default (WorkflowStore.swift:73-75). Both cases close the alert and leave the UI unchanged, so a no-op looks exactly like a delete. The default-workflow case is currently unreachable from the button (`.disabled(workflow.isDefault)`, WorkflowsView.swift:576), but that disabled control gives no reason for being disabled, which is its own small truthful-states gap.

Found defect 5, the confirmation copy understates the consequence. The alert says "Meetings already recorded keep their workflow metadata; only future meetings stop matching this workflow" (WorkflowsView.swift:119). The sidecar half is true, the rail half is not: `LibraryScope.resolveWorkflow` resolves a meeting to a workflow by NAME (LibraryScope.swift:112-116, the deliberate TECH-B4 bridge because the sidecar persists the name for cross-machine portability) while scopes are tagged by id (LibraryScope.swift:23, :107-108). After a delete, every past meeting whose sidecar names it resolves to nil, so it matches no workflow scope, and it is not swept into Untagged either, because `.untagged` tests for an absent or empty name (LibraryScope.swift:101-102) and the name is still present. Those meetings therefore vanish from the WORKFLOWS rail entirely while appearing nowhere that explains why. Either the copy says so, or the fall-through is fixed so a meeting naming a workflow that no longer exists reads as untagged; prefer the copy change, since altering filter semantics reaches further than this task.

WF9 interaction, cross-referenced rather than duplicated. The same name-based resolution means the owner's four same-named "Client work" workflows all resolve to whichever is first by name, so three of those four rail scopes necessarily count zero meetings regardless of which one recorded them, and deleting one can silently change which workflow every "Client work" meeting resolves to. That is context for WF9's cleanup, not a second cause-hunt here. One lead worth recording for WF9's pickup: the new-workflow sheet persists its stub on open and relies on Cancel to remove it (LibraryWindow.swift:686-692), so any dismissal that does not run Cancel orphans an "Untitled workflow", and the owner's config contains exactly one such row; consistent with that path, not proof of it.

Scope, smallest change per defect: `deleteSelf` invokes `onCommit?()` on a true return; a false or throwing delete surfaces the reason in the existing `saveError` slot instead of closing silently; a confirmed delete cannot be reversed by a later Save in the same sheet (dismissal is the primary fix, plus a belt at the editor seam, either a deleted latch that disables Save or an upsert that refuses an unknown id from an editor context; pick one and pin it); the destructive-affordance decision above, including the stale comment; a reason on the disabled-for-default Delete; the alert copy correction.

Explicitly not: WF9's duplication cause-hunt and the owner's manual cleanup; no undo or trash for workflows (a confirmation plus honest copy is the register, and the meta sidecar already preserves recorded meetings); no change to `WorkflowStore.delete`'s refusal to remove the default workflow (a correct invariant, only its silence is in scope); no re-opening DSN28's direction beyond this one destructive control; no change to name-based meeting resolution (TECH-B4's portability call).

Acceptance: Swift tests pin that a successful editor delete invokes the host's commit hook exactly once and a refused delete does not, and that a refused or failed delete leaves a visible reason; a test pins that a delete followed by a save cannot re-persist the deleted workflow (whichever belt is chosen); a presentation test pins the destructive style on the Delete control and the reason on the disabled-for-default state. If `MPDangerButtonStyle` is promoted, it lands in `Preferences/PreferencesControls.swift` and BatchActionsPane consumes the shared one, so no parallel copy exists. The stale comment at WorkflowsView.swift:567-569 and the alert copy both reflect what the code does after the change. Full daemon suite green, `swift build` clean. Owner eyeball: the Delete control reads as destructive, confirming closes the sheet, the workflow is gone from the rail, and pressing Return afterwards does not bring it back.

**What shipped, and the two decisions the spec left open.**

*One seam, not a fixed call site.* `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/WorkflowDeletion.swift` is the whole flow: the confirmation title / message / button, the two refusal reasons (the workflow is the default; the workflow is already gone), the disabled-for-default explanation, the save-after-delete reason, a three-case `Outcome` (`deleted` / `refused` / `failed`) whose `message` is nil only on success, a pure `refusalReason(id:in:)` so a control can pre-flight without touching disk, and `run(id:store:dismiss:report:)` which calls `dismiss` exactly once on success and never otherwise. This is the `LibraryDialogs` move (ARCH5) applied to workflows, and it is what makes UX32's rail-row Delete a call rather than a fork. `WorkflowEditor.deleteSelf` is now three lines with no branching left in the view.

*Decision 1, the destructive affordance: promote.* `MPDangerButtonStyle` was `private` inside `BatchActionsPane`, which is exactly why the editor's code comment asserted the app had no destructive button style and rendered Delete as a neutral ghost capsule carrying the same weight as Save. It now lives in `Preferences/PreferencesControls.swift` with a `.mpDanger` accessor beside `.mpGhost` / `.mpIcon`, and BatchActionsPane's "Move to Trash" consumes it, so there is one declaration. The geometry is unchanged (6px rounded rect, pulse tint), deliberately: re-shaping it into a capsule would change a shipped Library surface and re-open DSN28's direction, which this task's own scope forbids. What did change is the state honesty: the promoted style gained `@Environment(\.isEnabled)` dimming plus the press scale and reduce-motion the other two MP button styles already had. That is not polish here. Both consumers disable themselves (the default workflow; a running batch), and until now a disabled "Move to Trash" rendered at full destructive weight.

*Decision 2, the belt: refuse an unknown id at the editor seam.* Dismissal is the primary fix, so the belt only has to catch the residue, and the cheaper-looking option (a `deleted` latch disabling Save) is invisible to any other surface and needs a synthetic helper to be testable at all. `WorkflowStore.update(_:)` is `upsert` without the append branch, returning `false` on an id the store does not hold, and the editor's Save routes through it. The invariant it states is true and worth stating: an editor only ever edits something already persisted, including the New workflow sheet whose stub is written on open, so `upsert`'s append branch was reachable only by accident. It also covers a case the latch would miss and UX32 makes real: a rail-row delete landing while an editor sheet is open on the same workflow.

*Beyond the spec.* The editor grew an `onDelete` host hook defaulting to `onCommit`. Wiring delete straight into `onCommit` would have been correct for the edit sheet and wrong for the New workflow sheet, whose commit hook routes the rail to `.workflow(stub.id)`: after a delete that id names nothing, which is the same stranding moved one surface along. `NewWorkflowSheet` passes `onDelete: { onClose(nil) }`.

*Copy.* The alert used to say only that recorded meetings "keep their workflow metadata", which is true and misleading. `LibraryScope.resolveWorkflow` matches a meeting to a workflow by **name** (TECH-B4's cross-machine portability call) while a rail scope is keyed by id, and `.untagged` tests for an *absent* name. So after a delete those meetings resolve to nothing, match no workflow scope, and are not swept into Untagged either: they leave the rail with nothing anywhere explaining it. The copy now says that, and a test fails if the superseded sentence comes back. The fall-through itself is untouched, per the spec's preference: changing filter semantics reaches further than this task.

*The disabled Delete.* On the default workflow it now sits beside a muted caption naming the reason and the way past it ("Make another workflow the default first"), carried to the tooltip and to VoiceOver as well, since a caption alone does not reach a screen reader in the button's reading order.

**Tests (12 new).** `WorkflowDeletionTests` pins: a successful delete dismisses exactly once and reports nothing; a refused default delete and an unknown-id delete each dismiss zero times and surface their own distinct reason; `refusalReason` separates the two cases purely; a delete followed by an editor Save cannot re-persist the workflow, verified through a fresh store reload rather than the in-memory array; the confirmation copy names the rail consequence and no longer carries the superseded half-truth; the disabled reason says what to do about it; and the editor builds for both the default and a deletable workflow. Two source fences carry the parts a SwiftUI button's style makes unobservable at runtime: that `MPDangerButtonStyle` is declared once, in the shared primitives file, and consumed by BatchActionsPane rather than re-declared; and that the editor's Delete wears `.mpDanger` while the stale "there is no `.mpDanger` capsule" premise is gone. `WorkflowStoreTests` gained the `update` pair (replaces and keeps the single-default invariant; refuses an unknown id and writes no file).

**Docs.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the Workflows section gained `WorkflowDeletion.swift` and `WorkflowStore.update`; the editor bullet and the Preferences-primitives bullet updated), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (the Workflows quick-index row, the shared-primitives "Don't" which now also says don't declare a shared-looking primitive `private`, and the `WorkflowStore.delete` gotcha which now points at the one flow), `README.md` (a "Deleting a workflow" paragraph under Workflows), and `design/README.md`, where the drift predated this task: the Pulse reservation read "exclusively for the recording dot and the destructive confirm in dialogs" while the app has shipped a pulse-tinted destructive *button* since BatchActionsPane, and the "buttons are capsules" rule had an unrecorded exception in the same place. Both now describe what ships. `CONVENTIONS.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, and `docs/SETUP.md` were checked and are unaffected (no schema, event category, pattern, or install-path change).

**Verified.** `cd daemon && swift build` clean; `swift test` 1793 tests, 0 failures (full Xcode present on this machine); `python3 scripts/truth_fences.py both` green. No pipeline change, so the Python suites were not in scope.

**[DONE] UX31 (P2): one name per summarization engine (the label drift, and the two backends with no name at all).** Shipped 2026-07-29. Band origin: filed 2026-07-28 (owner report: the detail header says "Claude (cloud)" while the Re-summarize menu one click away says "Anthropic (cloud)", for the same backend on the same meeting).

**What was wrong.** Every summarization backend was named differently depending on which surface you looked at, and two of the six were not named anywhere. `provenanceLabel` rendered `anthropic` as "Claude (cloud)" while `reSummarizeMenu`, in the same file and on screen at the same time, rendered the same id as "Anthropic (cloud)"; the owner hit it on `20260727-140126`, whose `run.json` records `"backend": "anthropic"`. The sweep found the same drift on the other backends: `local` carried three names and `apple_intelligence` three, across seven label sites (Preferences picker, workflow picker, workflow inspector, provenance line, Re-summarize menu, busy line, Ask footer), with no site agreeing with all the others.

The worse half was coverage rather than drift. `claude_cli` and `openai` were selectable in Preferences and implemented end to end in the pipeline (PROV1), but appeared on neither the provenance line (whose `default` returned nil, and the caller drops the whole row on nil, so a meeting summarized by OpenAI displayed **no engine at all**), nor the workflow picker, nor the inspector; `LibraryRun.progressLabel` fell through to a bare "Summarizing", and `AskView.backendFooter` fell through to interpolation and printed the raw config id: "Answered via claude_cli".

Root cause, and the reason it recurred: no single home. Seven independent hardcoded switch tables each restated the vocabulary, so adding a backend meant six edits nobody was prompted to make. The same failure mode `daemon/CLAUDE.md` already names for config defaults under the `ConfigDefaults` / ARCH5 rule.

**Vocabulary, owner decision recorded 2026-07-28:** name the vendor for API backends, name the tool for the CLI backend. `anthropic` -> Anthropic (cloud), `openai` -> OpenAI (cloud), `claude_cli` -> Claude CLI (cloud, the one exception to the vendor rule), `apple_intelligence` -> Apple Intelligence (on-device), `local` -> Local MLX (on-device), `auto` -> Auto (cloud, falls back to local).

**What shipped.** `SummarizationEngine.swift`: one pure value type keyed off the raw backend id (a string, since the surfaces reporting what actually ran read theirs off `<stem>.run.json`), with a `Locality` of cloud / on-device / cloud-then-local and three registers. `name` is the bare name, for pickers. `qualified` is "Name (where it runs)", for the provenance line, the Re-summarize menu, and the inspector, the three surfaces where the question behind the label is "did this leave my Mac". `runPhrase` is the tail of a present-tense sentence, so `"Summarizing \(runPhrase)"` and `"Answered \(runPhrase)"` come from one register: an on-device engine says *where* ("on-device"), a cloud engine says *which* ("with Anthropic"). A fourth accessor, `pickerLabel`, is the bare name for everything except `auto`, which keeps its behaviour gloss, since "cloud, fall back to local" is explanation rather than identity and a picker is the register with room for it. `WorkflowBackend.engine` is the typed accessor, an exhaustive switch, so a new case cannot compile until it names itself.

**Eight sites read it, not seven.** The spec listed seven; the eighth is `SummaryTab`'s failed-summary recovery button, which said "Re-summarize with Local" while the header menu said "Local (on-device)" for the identical run, so leaving it would have been new drift of exactly the kind the task exists to kill. Nothing keeps a literal: grepping the daemon sources for every string in the drift table returns the one home plus four comments that quote the old strings as history.

**The workflow-picker question, resolved by checking rather than by assuming.** The spec left it conditional ("a labelling consequence, not a new capability, and if pinning them needs pipeline work then the picker entry waits"). Pinning `claude_cli` or `openai` works end to end with **no** pipeline change, verified along the whole chain: the picker writes `Workflow.backend`, `WorkflowStore` round-trips the raw value through TOML, `MeetingMetaSidecar.build` stamps `workflow_backend`, `mp.workflow.apply_overrides` accepts any id in `config.BACKENDS` (which has held both since PROV1), and `summarize._build_client` / `engine.complete_text` dispatch both. The privacy clamp still holds: `config.effective_backend` forces local for any non-`local` backend under `zero_egress`, which is precisely why `CLI_BACKENDS` classes `claude_cli` as cloud. So both went into the picker, and `WorkflowBackend` gained the two matching cases.

**Decisions that were not in the spec.** (1) Both pickers read `pickerLabel`, so Preferences' Auto row gains the gloss the workflow picker already had and the two agree on every row; Preferences' picker widened 200pt -> 260pt to fit it, the same width and the same reason as the workflow editor's. (2) Ask's preposition moved from "via" to "with", because one present-tense register cannot have two prepositions and "Summarizing with Anthropic" is the half with pinned tests and a README mention. (3) An unknown id hides the Ask footer instead of interpolating it, matching what the provenance row always did for the same case. (4) The provenance glyph now keys off `locality` rather than `backend == "anthropic"`, so the two cloud providers get the cloud glyph instead of being drawn as if they ran on this Mac. (5) Per the spec, Preferences' "Apple (experimental)" loses its parenthetical to the shared name; the experimental warning stays in the backend footer, which means it is now read after selecting rather than in the list. Small and deliberate, and named here because it is a real (if minor) loss.

**Explicitly not:** no backend id, config value, or sidecar key changed (`summarization.backend` is a written contract with the pipeline); no change to which engines the Re-summarize menu offers, since that is UX29's question and not a labelling one; no Python change; `WorkflowInspector` was left otherwise untouched, since UX33 rewrites that pane.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean, `swift test` 1816 green (18 new, full Xcode present here), `scripts/truth_fences.py both` green, no pipeline change so no Python run needed. Re-run on the rebased base after UX35 and UX30 landed mid-flight; UX30 is the one that overlapped, in `WorkflowsView.swift` and four doc files, and nothing here assumed the `upsert` semantics it changed (this task adds no store write at all). `SummarizationEngineTests` (16) is the fence the acceptance asked for: every engine non-empty in every register, no register containing its own id or any snake_case token, ids unique and round-tripping, nil for absent / unknown / `"unknown"`, every id the Preferences picker offers fully named, the workflow picker offering the inherit row plus every pinnable backend, and `WorkflowBackend.allCases` in lockstep with `SummarizationEngine.all`, so a seventh backend named in one and absent from the other fails here. Two presentation tests in `RenderedViewSmokeTests` pin the reported defect (the provenance line and the menu render one string for `anthropic`) and the coverage half (an `openai` or `claude_cli` meeting renders an engine row). Docs in the same commit: README (the provenance-label list, the `...` menu, the failed-row recovery button, a new workflow **Backend** paragraph, and the `summarization.backend` value list, which had been missing the two PROV1 backends), `docs/SETUP.md` (its step-7 option names now match the picker), ARCHITECTURE (a UI-surfaces bullet), CONVENTIONS (the `workflow_backend` sidecar row), and `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (a quick-index row plus a "Don't" line beside the `ConfigDefaults` one). `PRODUCT.md` and `pipeline/CLAUDE.md` were checked and needed nothing. `design/ui_kits/macos_app/SummaryLibrary.jsx` was checked and deliberately left: its `SLProvenance` renders "Summarized by Claude Opus 4.8, cloud", a model id in a sentence shape the shipped line never used, and ship already deviates from that mockup by a documented FEAT6 a11y decision. **Owner eyeball owed:** open a meeting summarized in the cloud and confirm the header and the `...` menu agree on the engine's name.

**[DONE] UX34 (P2): fixed-size detection prompt, cap the Last time prep card and scroll inside.** Shipped 2026-07-29. Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 2: "clicking on it expands significantly the recorder prompt... the size of the prompt window should be fixed size").

**Original scope.** The detection prompt panel is 600x64, positioned top-centre with an 80pt top inset. The CAL2 "Last time" disclosure grew it: `toggleLastTime` computed `newHeight = panelHeight + PrepCardView.height(for: card)` and animated `setFrame` keeping the top edge fixed. There was no maximum, no clamp, and no scrolling anywhere in the panel; the only bound was on content count (`PrepCard.maxPoints = 3`, `maxActions = 3`), not on height. A full card works out to ~196pt from `PrepCardView.layout`'s row metrics, so the panel jumped 64 to ~260pt, the 4x expansion the owner reported, and at 260pt the panel bottom landed ~340pt from the screen top, over a meaningful slice of the meeting window it is supposed to sit above. Scope as filed: add a `maxCardHeight` constant and clamp the growth to `min(PrepCardView.height(for: card), maxCardHeight)`; wrap `PrepCardView` in an `NSScrollView` at the build site; lift the height decision into a pure function so the clamp is unit-testable without AppKit; add a one-line comment on `prep_card_opened`'s first-build semantics.

**The cap is derived, not eyeballed.** The spec said to pick `maxCardHeight` visually, hinting at "something near 120pt". A headless session cannot eyeball it, so it is computed from the row metrics instead and lands at **123pt**: `separator + padTop + titleRow + titleGap + PrepCard.maxPoints * row + sectionGap + eyebrow + eyebrowGap + row / 2`. In words, the card's head (the title row plus a full three-point recap), then the OPEN ACTIONS eyebrow, then **half** an action row. The half row is the load-bearing part, not a rounding artifact: macOS overlay scrollers are invisible at rest, so a viewport that ends on a row boundary reads as the end of the card, while one that slices a row through the middle is what tells the eye there is more below. It lives on `PrepCardView` beside the private `Metric` enum it is built from, so the arithmetic sits next to the numbers it depends on and a chrome change moves the cap automatically. Result: an expanded panel is 64 + 123 = **187pt** against a full card's 196pt of content, inside the spec's "~190pt total" budget, and the owner's eyeball has a number to check rather than a literal nobody derived.

**The pure decision.** `PrepDisclosure.decide(card:expanded:pillHeight:maxCardHeight:)` in `MeetingPromptWindow.swift` returns the panel height, the card's viewport height, and the content height, with `scrolls` derived from the last two. Both call sites use it: the build site sizes the scroll view and its document, the toggle sizes the panel frame, so the two cannot drift into two copies of the clamp. Nil card and collapsed both return the bare pill height, which is what makes the existing collapse-to-zero behaviour a property of the decision rather than a separate branch.

**Two AppKit details the spec did not name.** The card is now an `NSScrollView` document view, and an **unflipped** document view opens showing its *last* row, so `PrepCardView` gained `isFlipped`. Its rows are placed with Auto Layout anchors, which are orientation-independent, so nothing about the existing layout moved. And the document view is frame-sized rather than constrained: its exact height is known before the view exists (that is the whole point of `PrepCardView.layout`), so there is nothing for a clip view to solve for. The scroll view sets `drawsBackground = false` on both itself and its clip view, or it would paint an opaque rectangle over the panel's vibrant `hudWindow` material.

**Knowingly accepted.** The card's 1pt top separator is inside the document, so it scrolls away with the content and there is no divider under the pill while scrolled. Lifting it out would change `PrepCardView.height(for:)`'s contract and ripple into the existing geometry tests for a hairline; left alone deliberately.

**Explicitly not:** the prep card's content and its 3+3 caps are untouched (CAL2's call, and CAL3 owns whether the card's content is even the right one); the panel is not user-resizable; the prompt pill's own 600x64 geometry, the collapsed state, the top-edge anchoring, and the reduce-motion branch are all exactly as they were.

**Verified:** `swift build` clean, **1821** `swift test` green (full Xcode present here), 5 of them new in `PrepCardTests`: no card leaves the panel at the pill, collapsing returns to the pill however tall the card is, a partial card under the cap gets exactly what it needs and does not scroll, a full 3+3 card is clamped and scrolls with the document keeping its full height, and a tripwire pinning the derivation (the cap at 123, the panel under 190, every recap point and the eyebrow inside the viewport, the first action row starting inside it and the second outside, so the cut lands mid-row). The suite baseline had moved well past the 1770 the spec's acceptance named: 1781 when this started, 1816 by the time it rebased onto UX35, UX30 and UX31, all three of which landed while it ran. Nothing here shares a Swift file with any of them, and the suite was re-run green on that base before landing. `scripts/truth_fences.py both` green (no config key or event category changed; `prep_card_opened` is an existing `coordinator` action). Em-dash guard clean. Docs synced: README's "Last time" paragraph (the cap and the scroll are user-visible), ARCHITECTURE's prompt-panel section plus the Prep-card glossary entry, CONVENTIONS' pure-`decide` list, and the design kit's `MeetingPrompt.jsx`, which now recreates the clamped viewport and carries the derivation in its header comment. `daemon/CLAUDE.md`, `pipeline/CLAUDE.md`, `MeetingPipeCore/CLAUDE.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, `docs/SETUP.md` and `design/README.md` were checked and needed nothing: no module moved, no sidecar or schema changed, and the quick indexes do not name the prompt panel. Owner-owed: the eyeball on a workflow with a full prep card, which is where 123pt gets confirmed or retuned.
**[DONE] UX32 (P2): workflow row quick actions (swipe + context menu).** Shipped 2026-07-29, immediately after UX30. Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 1: "assess the possibility to have swipe actions on workflow rows, for removing, to make it simple"). Original spec below, then what shipped.

**Context.** Deleting a workflow was a five-step gauntlet: select the rail row, have no meeting selected so the inspector appears (see UX33 on that reachability catch), click "Edit workflow", find Delete in the sheet, confirm. WF9's owner-owed cleanup (four duplicate "Client work" rows, a stub, a duplicate "Personal") pays that path once per row, which is exactly the friction that prompted this filing. The feasibility question the owner asked was settled by the code: the WORKFLOWS rail is a real SwiftUI `List` (`LibrarySidebar.swift:21`, `.listStyle(.sidebar)` at :124), not a custom stack, so `.swipeActions` works on macOS without custom gestures. Workflow rows were the `ForEach` at `LibrarySidebar.swift:73-83` rendering `WorkflowScopeRow` (:409-474), and they carried no row-level affordances at all: no `.contextMenu`, no `.swipeActions`, only a `.help` tooltip (:467). The precedent is one section up in the same file: saved-search rows attach a context menu with Rename, Update, and a `role: .destructive` Delete (`LibrarySidebar.swift:56-61`). One AppKit escape hatch shares that List: `.noNativeListSelection()` (:127) reaches into the backing `NSTableView` to set `selectionHighlightStyle = .none` (`NoNativeListSelection.swift:39-48`); it touches only the highlight style and should not block swipe actions, but it was the one thing worth a smoke test before committing to the swipe half. UX30 (filed 2026-07-23, re-confirmed by the owner 2026-07-29) owns the delete flow's defects, so this task must not fork a second delete path around those fixes.

**Scope.** Two entry points on each workflow row, both routed through one shared delete flow: a context menu mirroring the saved-search precedent ("Edit workflow..." opening the existing editor sheet, "Delete workflow..." with `role: .destructive`), and trailing `.swipeActions` with the same two actions (Delete full-swipe disabled; destructive actions should not fire on momentum alone). The delete goes through the same confirmation alert and the same store call the editor sheet uses, extracted to one host-level helper if needed so UX30's fixes apply to both surfaces by construction. The default workflow's Delete is disabled with a visible reason, matching UX30's truthful-disabled requirement. Sequencing note: UX30 should land first or together; if this lands first, it must not duplicate the broken flow, it must extract-and-share it.

**Explicitly not in scope.** Drag-to-reorder workflows (order is a store field edited elsewhere; separate concern). Multi-select of workflow rows. Duplicating a workflow from the menu (nothing asked for it; YAGNI). UX30's editor-sheet fixes themselves. Any change to `WorkflowStore.delete` semantics.

**Acceptance.** Tests pin: the context menu offers Edit and Delete for a non-default workflow and a disabled-with-reason Delete for the default; the row delete path and the editor delete path invoke the same underlying flow (one implementation, asserted by both call sites hitting the same seam); a refused delete surfaces a reason (inherited from UX30's fix, pinned here for the row entry point). Smoke-verified: swipe actions render and fire on a row in the real List despite `.noNativeListSelection()` (owner eyeball, since swipe rendering is not unit-testable). `cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770 tests; the real base was 1798 when this was written and 1829 after the rebase onto UX31 / UX34 / UX36). Repo dash guard green. Owner eyeball: right-click a duplicate "Client work" row, Delete, confirm; the row disappears without opening the editor. Swipe left on another; same result.

**What shipped, and the decisions the spec left open.**

*The sequencing resolved in the easy direction.* UX30 landed first, so there was nothing to extract: `WorkflowDeletion.run(id:store:dismiss:report:)` already existed and this task is a second caller of it. `LibrarySidebar` gained two host hooks beside `onCreateWorkflow` (`onEditWorkflow`, `onDeleteWorkflow`) and routes both surfaces through them, which keeps the store mutation in `LibraryRootView` where the saved-folder rows already put theirs. `LibraryRootView.deleteWorkflowFromRail` is the whole row path: confirm, then `WorkflowDeletion.run`. A source fence pins the claim the acceptance actually cares about, that the *set* of files calling `WorkflowDeletion.run(` is exactly `{WorkflowsView.swift, LibraryWindow.swift}`; a third surface forking its own delete path fails the suite rather than being noticed in review.

*Decision 1, where the row's confirmation lives: an `NSAlert`, not a SwiftUI alert.* A rail row is not a sheet and owns no state, so a SwiftUI `.alert` would have meant a pending-delete slot plus an error slot on `LibraryRootView` and a second alert presented in the first one's dismissal frame, which is exactly the shape that is flaky on macOS. `LibraryDialogs` is already the Library's home for a modal destructive confirm (`confirmSoftDelete`, called the same way from `MeetingRow`'s context menu), so the confirm and the refusal report landed there as `confirmWorkflowDelete()` / `reportWorkflowDeleteProblem(_:)`. Both render `WorkflowDeletion`'s strings unmodified, including the confirmation title, so the rail asks the identical question the sheet asks; a test pins that the dialog reads the constants rather than restating them. The confirm button also gets `hasDestructiveAction`, which is UX30's "the control reads as destructive" carried into the one place the row's Delete is still reversible.

*Decision 2, what `dismiss` means for a row.* The sheet's `dismiss` closes the sheet; the row has no sheet, but it does have a rail selection, and a scope left pointing at a deleted workflow renders an empty list that explains nothing. So `dismiss` clears the scope back to All meetings when it was pointed at the deleted workflow, which keeps the seam's contract meaningful rather than passing an empty closure. The stranded-sheet case needs nothing here: the editor is a window-modal sheet, so an in-window rail delete cannot race it, and the cross-window case is already covered by UX30's `update`-refuses-an-unknown-id belt plus the `alreadyGoneReason` pre-flight.

*Decision 3, one pure type for two surfaces.* `WorkflowRowActions` (new, `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/WorkflowRowActions.swift`) holds the two titles, the disabled reason, and the accessibility label, resolved by `WorkflowDeletion.refusalReason` verbatim.
