The duplication it exists to prevent is specific: the same pair of actions is now rendered twice per row, and a Delete live in the context menu while greyed in the swipe (or captioned differently) is the drift `WorkflowDeletion` and `LibraryDialogs` were both extracted to stop. Pure, like `ReSummarizeMenu`, so what the menu offers is pinned without rendering a `List`.

*One judgement call worth flagging.* The context menu can carry the disabled-Delete reason as a caption row under the item, the shape UX29 uses for its clamped cloud engine. A swipe action has nowhere to put one, so on the default workflow the disabled swipe Delete carries its reason in the accessibility label only. The alternative considered and rejected was hiding Delete from the swipe entirely on the default row, which is honest by absence but silent, and inconsistent with the menu one gesture away.

*The swipe half was committed on analysis, not observation, and that is the one soft spot in this entry.* The spec singled out `.noNativeListSelection()` as worth a smoke test before committing to swipes. It was read rather than smoke-tested: the modifier walks to the backing `NSTableView` and sets `selectionHighlightStyle = .none`, which is a highlight-*drawing* property with nothing to do with the row-action layer, so there is no mechanism by which it would suppress a swipe. That is a reading of the code, not a swipe anybody performed, and rendering cannot be reached from `swift build` or `swift test`. So the affordance ships unobserved: if it turns out not to fire, the fix is in `LibrarySidebar.workflowRowSwipeActions` and its `.swipeActions` attachment, and nothing else in this task depends on it (the context menu is a separate attachment over the same `WorkflowRowActions` and needs no such caveat). Archived `done` on the owner's 2026-07-29 call, with a fresh session to pick it up if the swipe misbehaves.

**Docs.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the Workflows section gained `WorkflowRowActions.swift`, naming the two call sites and the full-swipe rule), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (the Workflows quick-index row gained the new file; the Library UI row's `LibraryDialogs` note now names the workflow-delete confirm), `README.md` (the rail's WORKFLOWS paragraph, the list of places the editor opens from, and the "Deleting a workflow" paragraph, which now leads with two ways in and one flow behind both). Checked and unchanged: `CONVENTIONS.md` (no pattern, schema, or event category changed), `PRODUCT.md`, `docs/SETUP.md` (no install, permission, or first-run change), `design/` (the row's resting chrome is identical, the JSX kit recreates no menus, and `trash` / `pencil` are already in the app's SF Symbols vocabulary). One incidental fix: the README line about the seeded General workflow carried a historical em-dash, and editing that line brought it under the added-lines dash guard, so it was rewritten.

**Verified.** `cd daemon && swift build` clean (no new warnings; the one SPM "unhandled file" warning pre-dates this change and reproduces with the change stashed); `swift test` 1839 tests, 0 failures (full Xcode present on this machine), 10 of them new in `WorkflowRowActionsTests`, re-run after rebasing onto UX31 / UX34 / UX36 (1829 on that base). The `WorkflowDeletion.run(` fence was re-run against the rebased tree, since UX31 edited `WorkflowsView.swift`, one of the two files it pins: the calling set is still exactly `{LibraryWindow.swift, WorkflowsView.swift}`; `python3 scripts/truth_fences.py both` green; dash guard green over the added lines, the two new files, and the whole `daemon/Sources` + `daemon/Resources` tree. No pipeline change, so the Python suites were not in scope.


**[DONE] UX47 (P2): prep card wraps its lines, admits its scroll, and stays inside its panel.** Shipped 2026-07-30, filed and closed the same day. Owner confirmed the eyeball after the mask fix ("okay, looks good").

Band origin: filed 2026-07-30 (owner, from a live prompt screenshot during a real Teams call: bullets sliced mid-glyph at the panel edge, "I was thinking text will be line breaked or what... and it is not visible that it is scrollable"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).
## Context
Three defects wearing one outfit, separated by a layout probe before any design was argued.
**Defect 1 is a bug with a probe-proven mechanism, a UX34 regression.** UX34 rehosted the card as an `NSScrollView` document, deliberately "frame-sized, not constrained". Under the layout engine a document view with `translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints` true and constraint-based subviews takes its width from its *content*, not from the frame it was handed, so in a 600pt panel the card laid itself out **879pt wide**. A throwaway XCTest built the card exactly as the panel does and measured where each label ends: the long bullets sat at maxX 773, 867, 823 against a 588 inset, every trailing constraint holding against a right edge 279pt past the glass, and the right-aligned relative-day label sat at maxX 867, meaning **the date has rendered off-panel since UX34 landed** (the owner's screenshot shows nothing to the right of the title). The configured `byTruncatingTail` never fired because the labels were never compressed. Before UX34 the card was constraint-hosted and the same labels ellipsized correctly.
**Defect 2 is a design decision the owner has overruled by eyeball.** `PrepCardView`'s header comment records CAL2's trade: single-line truncation, "the card answers what was this about, and the Library holds the full text one click away". The owner's expectation on seeing it live: lines should wrap. A recap whose every sentence dies mid-thought answers "what was this about" worse than the trade assumed.
**Defect 3 is UX34's scroll cue, falsified.** The 123pt viewport deliberately ends mid-row on the theory that a sliced row reads as "more below" (overlay scrollers are invisible at rest, so a clean row boundary reads as the end). The owner's read of the slice: "this is not okay", broken rather than inviting. The half-row bet is settled against.
A design run on 2026-07-30 put three options to the owner at 1:1 (wrap fully + fade cue; two-line clamp; honest single-line ellipsis), all three sharing the width-anchor bug fix. **Owner picked A: wrap fully, fade the bottom edge when more content is below.**
## Scope
- **Anchor the document width** (the bug, fix shared by any option): the card becomes a constraint-hosted document, width pinned to the clip view's, height pinned to the measured content height. The date comes back on-panel by construction.
- **Wrap points and actions**: `maximumNumberOfLines = 0`, word-wrapping, `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` at the real text column width. The title stays one line with a working tail ellipsis beside its date; the eyebrow and "N more in the Library" line stay one line.
- **Measure instead of counting**: `PrepCardView.layout(for:)` gains a `width:` parameter and measures each wrapped string, keeping the one-arithmetic-pass invariant (layout and `height(for:)` agree, the panel never animates to a frame the content does not fill). The row gap self-calibrates so a single-line row advances exactly the old `Metric.row`, keeping every short-card height and the existing `PrepDisclosure` arithmetic byte-identical.
- **Admit the scroll**: a bottom fade (gradient mask on the clip, so the vibrant panel material shows through rather than a wrong-colored overlay) whenever content continues below the viewport, gone when scrolled to the bottom; the overlay scroller flashes once on expand. The visibility rule is a pure function beside `PrepDisclosure.decide`, table-tested.
- **The 123pt viewport stays** as the panel's budget (UX34's cap was the right call; only its cue was wrong). Its derivation comment updates: it was derived from single-line metrics and is now simply the fixed viewport the fade reads against.
## Explicitly not in scope
The card's content and its 3+3 caps (CAL2's call, and CAL3's series-keying decides what this card shows at all; this task is the card's form, the two compose). The panel pill's 600x64 geometry. Option B's two-line clamp, recorded here as the pre-agreed retreat if wrapped scrolling annoys in practice: it is a `maximumNumberOfLines` constant away, not a rebuild.
## Acceptance
The promoted probe is the regression test: a card with real-length bullets, laid out at 600pt, has every label end inside the right inset, the date label visible and right-aligned, and at least one wrapped label taller than a single line. Pure layout tests pin: a long point grows the card while short cards keep their exact pre-wrap heights; row tops stay ordered under wrap; the fade rule's table (not scrollable, scrolled to top, at bottom, within slop of bottom). `PrepDisclosure` tests thread the width and keep their existing clamp semantics.
`cd daemon && swift build` clean and `swift test` green, N new tests stated at ship. Truth fences green. Repo dash guard green.
Owner eyeball (the remainder that keeps this row active): on the next real prompt, expand Last time and confirm the bullets read as complete sentences, the fade reads as "continues" rather than a defect, scrolling reveals the rest, and the date sits at the card's top right.
## Progress (shipped 2026-07-30, owner-owed on the eyeball)
Shipped the same day as filed, option A as picked. 1886 daemon tests green (4 new in `PrepCardTests`), truth fences green, no pipeline change.
**The bug fix.** The card is a constraint-hosted document now: `translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints` off, leading and top pinned to `scroll.contentView`, width pinned to the clip's, height pinned to the measured content height. The promoted probe is the permanent regression fence, and its first draft could not pass for an instructive reason: constraints position a label's *alignment rect* while the test measured its *frame*, which sits about 2pt wider per side, so every correctly-placed label read as 590 against the 588 bound. The fence now measures alignment rects, which is what the layout actually controls, and its failure message carries the original 773/867/823 numbers so the next reader knows what the regression looks like at full size.
**The wrap.** Points and actions render via `NSTextField(wrappingLabelWithString:)` with `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` at the measured text width and an equality trailing constraint (a wrapping label needs the exact edge; the old `<=` was for truncation). `layout(for:width:)` measures each string with the same font the label renders in, and the row gap self-calibrates against a one-line probe so a single-line row advances exactly the old fixed `Metric.row`: every pre-wrap card height is byte-identical, which is why the UX34 clamp arithmetic and its tests stood untouched. The action ring pins to the text's first baseline rather than its centre, so a wrapped action does not float its ring between lines. Title, date, eyebrow, and the "N more" line stay single-line chrome.
**The cue.** `PrepDisclosure.fadeVisible(contentHeight:viewport:offsetY:)` is the pure rule (visible only while content remains below, lifting within 2pt of the bottom), table-tested. The fade itself is a `CAGradientLayer` mask on the clip view, because the panel is vibrant `.hudWindow` material and an overlay would have no flat colour to paint; the mask tracks scrolling with implicit animation disabled, since a trailing fade-chase reads as lag. The clip posts bounds changes, the observer is torn down at all three `prepCardScroll` teardown sites, and the overlay scroller flashes once on expand.
**Docs updated in the same commit:** README's Last time paragraph (wrap + fade), ARCHITECTURE's prompt-panel bullet and prep-card glossary entry, CONVENTIONS' decide list (width-threaded `decide`, new `fadeVisible` sibling), daemon/CLAUDE.md (new Don't: frame-sizing a TAMIC-true scroll document that hosts Auto Layout content), and `design/ui_kits/macos_app/MeetingPrompt.jsx`'s header comment (records the retired half-row bet; the mock itself is not re-drawn and says so).
**Observed, not fixed here:** the backlog ToC's MIC8 row has parsed as 7 columns since before this task (a stray pipe in its comment); table hygiene for a later pass.
**Owner-owed:** the acceptance's eyeball on the next real prompt: complete sentences, a fade that reads as "continues", scroll revealing the rest, the date at the card's top right. Needs one `./scripts/rebuild.sh`.
## Follow-up, 2026-07-30: the fade mask did not scroll with the card
The owner rebuilt and the wrap landed, but the card looked empty below its second bullet and the eyebrow and actions were unreachable however far they scrolled. A second throwaway probe built the real scroll-plus-document arrangement and dumped the geometry, which cleared the arithmetic first: for a live 3-point card, `height(for:width:)` returned 205, the lowest label ended at 191, and the slack was exactly the 12pt bottom pad plus rounding. Rows measured 30pt where a two-line advance predicted 33. So the wrap accounting was right, and the defect was elsewhere.
**The bug: `grad.frame = CGRect(origin: .zero, size: clip.bounds.size)`.** A `CALayer` mask is positioned in its layer's coordinate space, and an `NSClipView`'s bounds *origin* is precisely what moves when its document scrolls. Anchored at zero, the mask kept covering the document's first screenful while the viewport moved down, so every point past bounds y = viewport height had no mask coverage at all and composited fully transparent. The numbers predict the owner's screenshot exactly: scrolled 82pt into a 205pt document with a 123pt viewport, content was visible only in bounds 82 to 123 (the tail of point 2 and point 3), while the eyebrow at 142, the action at 159, and the "2 more" line at 177 were masked to nothing; the fade band, bounds 97 to 123, is why point 3's second line looked dissolved. One-line fix: the mask takes `clip.bounds` whole, origin included.
Also lifted the gradient stops into pure `PrepDisclosure.fadeLocations(viewport:fadeHeight:)`, so the band stays a fixed 26pt rather than a proportion that would grow with the viewport, and clamps into `[0, 1]` for degenerate inputs that Core Animation would otherwise reject.
**A fence that tripped on its own prose.** The source fence pinning `grad.frame = clip.bounds` initially failed against the correct code, because the explanatory comment beside it quotes the `origin: .zero` literal being banned. It strips `//` comments before scanning now. That is the second time in this task's history a source fence was wrong about its own subject (the first measured frames where constraints position alignment rects), so the standing habit holds: mutate a new fence both ways before trusting it. This one was verified failing on the reintroduced bug and passing on the fix.
Verified: `swift build` clean, **1888 daemon tests, 0 failures** (2 more than the first pass), truth fences green.
**Owner-owed, unchanged:** the eyeball on the next real prompt, now that scrolling actually reveals the eyebrow and the actions.

**[DONE] UX36 (P2): summary reading measure: centered column, honest padding, wrapping metadata.** **Closed 2026-07-30: owner confirmed the eyeball ("UX36 - good to close") after the fourth pass put both reading tabs on the shared 720 measure.**

Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 4: "the summary content is cut in width at some level... make size of the text area dynamic to the size of the panel, or make max width of the right panel fixed, or any other options"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).
## Context
What the owner sees as "cut at some width" is the reading-measure cap, not a clip. `SummaryLayout.readingMeasure = 640` (`SummaryRenderedView.swift:8-10`) is a deliberate typography decision (TECH-DSN8 / UX13: ~70 characters at body size, "reads like paper"), applied to the rendered summary at :91-93:
- `.padding(MPSpace.s5)` (20pt) is applied INSIDE the cap, so the real text column is 640 - 40 = 600pt.
- `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` then parks the capped block hard left, so on a wide window all remaining width is dead space on the right. The detail column itself has a min of 450 and no max (`LibraryWindow.swift:782`, `LibraryLayout.swift:25-26`), so the asymmetry grows with the window.
The same cap-then-left-park pattern repeats in `SummaryTab.swift:242/:245` and :287/:288 and in `CorrectionEditor.swift:41`. Two stray literals shadow the constant: `.frame(maxWidth: 460, ...)` at `SummaryTab.swift:421` and :435 (the BYO/preview area), which will not track any change to the shared measure.
A real horizontal-space defect hides underneath: `ActionItemRow`'s metadata band is a non-wrapping `HStack` inside the cap (`SummaryRenderedView.swift:325-338`: a 16pt spacer, owner chip, due chip, confidence chip, FEAT10 `EvidenceChips`), with no `ViewThatFits` and no wrap, so a long owner name plus several evidence chips compresses and truncates. The one hard `lineLimit(1)` in the reader is the attendee chip (:432). Prose itself wraps correctly (`.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)`, e.g. :197, :323).
## Scope
Decision, taken as delegated: keep a reading measure. Unbounded text width is rejected on readability grounds (70-90 characters per line is the defensible measure; a 1200pt line is objectively worse to read), and a fixed max on the detail panel is rejected because it would punish the Transcript and Audio tabs that use the width. The fix is to make the measure honest and the layout symmetric:
- Apply `.padding` outside the cap (or widen the cap by the padding) so 640 is the true text measure.
- Replace the left-park with a centered placement (`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .top)` centering the measured block), so a wide window frames the column like a page rather than abandoning the right half. Apply the same change at the `SummaryTab` and `CorrectionEditor` sites so reader and editor stay aligned (the stated reason the shared constant exists).
- Fold the two 460 literals into a named constant in `SummaryLayout` (a second, narrower measure for preview panes) so they track future changes.
- Make the action metadata band wrap: `ViewThatFits` with a two-line fallback, or a trailing-truncation policy that keeps evidence chips visible; pick whichever reads better with real data, record the pick.
- Optional, owner-eyeball-gated: nudge the measure from 640 toward ~680 if the centered layout reads too narrow on the owner's screen. Not a spec requirement.
## Explicitly not in scope
A user-facing width setting. Changing the detail column's min/ideal (`LibraryLayout`). Transcript tab layout. The red scroll indicator in the owner's screenshot (stock scroller styling, not a defect).
## Acceptance
The measure constant is applied at every summary surface through `SummaryLayout` with no shadowing literals (grep for `maxWidth: 4` and `maxWidth: 6` literals in the summary surfaces comes back empty).
A layout-level test or presentation seam pins that the metadata band renders all chips at `detailMinWidth` (450pt column) without truncating the evidence chips (if not testable headlessly, the acceptance is the owner eyeball case below plus a code-review assertion that `ViewThatFits`/wrap is in place).
`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770). Repo dash guard green.
Owner eyeball: open the meeting from the 2026-07-27 screenshot at full-screen width; the summary column sits centered with even margins, nothing is parked against the left edge, and an action row with owner + due + confidence + evidence chips shows all of them at the narrowest detail width.
## Progress (shipped 2026-07-29, owner-owed on the eyeball)
Implemented across the three summary surfaces the spec names: `SummaryRenderedView.swift`, `SummaryTab.swift`, `CorrectionEditor.swift`. 1829 daemon tests green, 8 of them new (`SummaryLayoutTests.swift`), no pipeline change, dash guard and CI5 truth fences green. `LibraryWindow.swift` and `LibraryLayout.swift` are untouched, as scoped.
**The measure is honest now, and it moved.** The gutter was applied inside the 640 cap, so the real text column was 640 - 2 * 20 = 600pt. All four cap sites now read `.frame(maxWidth: readingMeasure).padding(contentPadding)` instead of the reverse, which widens the actual text column from 600 to 640 with no change to the constant. Worth naming because 600 was outside the 640-720 reading column `design/README.md` asks for, so the app had quietly drifted below its own design system, and closing that gap is a visible width change on its own before any centering.
**Centered, per the spec's own modifier.** The outer frame is `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .top)` at all four sites. `.top` is `Alignment(horizontal: .center, vertical: .top)`, which is exactly the pairing wanted: centered across, pinned to the top of the scroller. The inner cap keeps `alignment: .leading`, so the prose is still left-aligned inside a centered column rather than becoming centered text.
**`SummaryLayout` grew two named constants** so nothing shadows the measure: `contentPadding` (the gutter, `MPSpace.s5`, now meaningful because it sits outside the cap) and `noticeMeasure = 460`, which absorbs the two stray literals in the failed state. The spec called those the "BYO/preview area"; they are actually the failure-reason dump and the model-download notice, both centered state panels, which is what the constant's name and doc comment say. The acceptance grep for `maxWidth: 4` and `maxWidth: 6` in the three summary surfaces comes back empty.
**The band pick: `ViewThatFits` with a two-line fallback, not trailing truncation.** Truncation has to choose something to sacrifice and both candidates are bad here: the owner is the most load-bearing fact on an action item (who owes it) and a clipped "Alex Nov..." can name two people, while the anchors are the row's only route back to the recording. In a pane that already scrolls vertically a second line costs nothing, so paying width with height is the cheaper trade. The split is at the semantic boundary (owner / due / confidence on line one, FEAT10 anchors on line two), which converges on the shape the bullet rows already use, where anchors sit on their own line under the point. Residual risk is bounded and named in the code: the fallback is the last `ViewThatFits` child, so it renders as-is rather than being re-checked, and line one can still compress for an owner name of roughly 50 characters at the 450pt detail floor. At that floor the column is 410pt and a realistic owner + due + confidence needs about 255pt, so the case is far outside real data. `MeetingRow` dropped `ViewThatFits` deliberately, but that was a `List` problem (NSTableView caches row heights, so the branch picked at first measure outlived the width that picked it); these rows are in a `LazyVStack` inside a `ScrollView`, which caches no height.
**Decisions not in the spec.** (1) Status is `owner-owed`, not `done`: the backlog's rule is that eyeball work stays active, and the geometric half of the acceptance ("all chips visible at `detailMinWidth`") is precisely what a headless session cannot verify, so the spec's own escape clause applies. (2) A latent bug closed on the way: the band's guard read `action.evidence` directly, but `EvidenceChips` draws nothing without an `onCite` handler, so a static surface (the digest reader, the A16 compare pane) with anchors and no other metadata opened an empty indented band. A new `shownEvidence` gates on the handler and both the guard and the two wrap variants read it. (3) The inline editor's `contentPadding: 16` became the shared `SummaryLayout.contentPadding`. Its rationale ("avoid squeezing the fields further") was about the old geometry where padding ate into the measure; with the gutter outside, the fields get the full measure either way, and matching the reader is the whole reason the shared constant exists, since 16 against the reader's 20 would shift the column by 4pt a side across the edit crossfade. (4) `chipRow` went from `private` to internal, joining `glyphName` / `isInteractive` / `toggleLabel` as a test seam, and the inline `Spacer().frame(width: 16)` became a named `metadataIndent` leading pad so both wrap variants state the indent once. (5) The A16 compare pane's "Current" / "Candidate" headings had to move with the columns they label. They sat flush at the pane's leading edge, which was only 20pt off the summary text below them; centering the summaries would have left a heading a whole margin away from its own column on a wide window, so a `compareHeading` helper puts them on the same measure. The `Divider()` between the two is deliberately left full-bleed: a rule across the pane separates the two candidates more clearly than a measure-width one.
**Not done, and deliberately.** The optional 640 to 680 nudge is untouched. It is owner-eyeball-gated by the spec's own wording and a headless session cannot judge whether the centered column reads too narrow; `SummaryLayoutTests` pins the measure inside the design system's 640-720 band so the nudge stays safe to apply later. The `design/ui_kits/macos_app/SummaryLibrary.jsx` mockup was not re-authored: it models neither a reading-measure cap nor FEAT10's evidence chips, and restructuring a pane's layout blind is the visual churn the backlog forbids without eyes, so it is recorded as a Known lag in that kit's README beside the UX22 and FEAT11 ones.
## Second pass, 2026-07-29 (the owner's eyeball, and what it found)
The eyeball happened the same day and came back negative: "still looks pretty much ugly, this time less readable". A layout review was mocked at true scale and approved before any further code was written, which is the process change this task's first pass argued for by failing without it.
**What the eyeball actually caught, and it was not the measure.** The body was centred while the header sat at the pane's left edge and the tab strip spread across the full pane, so the first 200pt of vertical travel offered three different left edges and none of them was the one the prose used. Readability is a property of the relationship, not of the column; the first pass fixed the column and orphaned it.
**A real bug underneath, not a layout choice.** `tabButton` drew the active-tab underline as a bare `Rectangle` sibling in a `VStack`. A `Rectangle` is a greedy shape: with no width constraint it consumes every point offered, the `VStack` inherits that, each tab button becomes greedy, and the three of them divide the whole pane while the trailing `Spacer(minLength: 0)` gets nothing. The strip read as left-aligned in source and shipped as three tabs spread across 1230pt with an underline several times wider than its word. The underline is now an `.overlay(alignment: .bottom)` on the label, so it sizes to the text. This is a `daemon/CLAUDE.md` "Don't" now, since the source looks correct and only the render is wrong.
**Decision taken and recorded: the header and tab strip are chrome, not content.** The obvious fix, putting them on the summary's reading column, was traced and rejected: `TranscriptTab` and `AudioTab` are full-width and uncapped, so a header indented to the summary measure is aligned on the Summary tab and misaligned against the other two the moment the tab changes. Three options were put to the owner (chrome band; one shared ~1000pt column for the whole pane, which would have narrowed this spec's own "no max on the detail panel" rejection; left-park everything, which returns the dead right-hand space this task was filed for). The owner picked the chrome band on 2026-07-29. Header, metadata, provenance and the tab strip now sit together on `bgRaised` spanning the pane, with the pre-existing `Divider` as the band's bottom edge. Chrome spans, content centres beneath it, which is the mac convention and stops the eye expecting a shared left edge at all.
**The nudge was taken.** `readingMeasure` 640 to 680, the value this spec pre-registered and reserved for the owner ("720 is fine" for the inspector card; 680 chosen here after arguing the two surfaces are different content types). 680 sits inside the 640-720 band `design/README.md` asks for and lets the action metadata band hold one line more often before `ViewThatFits` drops it to two. 720 was rejected for prose: at the body size it puts a line near 100 characters, past where the eye reliably finds the start of the next one, which is the entire reason a measure exists. The inspector's card is 720 because a label/value grid is not prose, and matching the numbers would be a consistency that is not real.
Verified: `swift build` clean, **1877 daemon tests, 0 failures** (2 new fences in `SummaryLayoutTests`, one pinning the underline overlay against the greedy-shape regression, one pinning that the chrome band exists and does not adopt the reading measure). The band-range test the first pass wrote is what let 680 land without an edit.
**Owner-owed, still.** Rebuild (`./scripts/rebuild.sh`) and confirm three things on a wide window: the chrome band reads as chrome rather than as a tinted stripe, the tab strip is tight and left-packed with the underline hugging its word, and the 680 column reads right centred beneath it. Then narrow to the detail floor and confirm an action row with owner + due + confidence + anchors wraps to two readable lines. The `SummaryLibrary.jsx` mockup remains a Known lag in that kit's README, now including the chrome band.
## Third pass, 2026-07-29 (cap the column, then left-align)
The second pass's chrome band shipped and the owner looked again: better, but the summary still sat between two visible gutters, and they marked both on a screenshot. Two findings came out of that look, one of them mine to own.
**The band was invisible.** `bgRaised` over `bg` in dark mode is an 11-of-255 step. It could not be found in the owner's screenshot even knowing where to look, and a band nobody can see licenses nothing, so the header's offset from the body read as a bug exactly as before. It now recesses to `bgSunk` with a `borderStrong` hairline. Recessing is the direction the mac's own toolbars take, and it frees `bgRaised` to mean content rather than competing with it.
**The axis jumped between tabs, and that was my error in proposing the band.** I traced whether a measured *header* would fight the transcript and concluded it would, which is true; I never checked whether the two tab *bodies* would fight each other. They did: the summary centred its 680 measure and started at 275, the transcript is full-width and started at 16, so changing tab slid the content 259pt sideways under a header that never moved. Looking at one tab in isolation is what hid it, which is why the second review mocked all three.
**The owner's fix, and it is better than the one I proposed.** I had drawn an inner page card (`bgRaised`, hairline, 14pt radius, 940 wide) so the gutters would read as page margins. The owner's counter was that a 940 page inside a 1372 pane just relocates the gutters, and that the real move is to stop the pane growing: cap the detail column instead. That is right, and it is the option this spec had itself rejected in its first pass ("a fixed max on the detail panel is rejected because it would punish the Transcript and Audio tabs that use the width"). That rejection assumed the other tabs used the width well. They do not: the transcript was running past 1300pt unmeasured, which is not using width, and capping it at ~1008 is an improvement on its own terms.
`LibraryLayout.detailMaxWidth = 1040`, applied as the `max:` on the detail column's `navigationSplitViewColumnWidth`. Split-view columns always fill, so the slack goes to the meeting list, which has no max and where titles truncate today; on the owner's ~2000pt window the list goes from ~460 to ~740 and the detail pane carries **zero** gutters. With the pane bounded, the measured column can left-align at no cost, so the centring is gone from all five cap sites across the three summary surfaces and every tab's content shares one left edge. The card in the workflow inspector is now nearly redundant at 720 inside a 1040 pane (140pt of gutter each side rather than the 812 UX33 was filed against).
Checked before answering the owner's "will that work everywhere" question: there is exactly **one** three-panel UI, a single `NavigationSplitView` at `LibraryWindow.swift`, so the cap has one home and cannot drift. Preferences is a separate two-panel split fixed at 780x660 and is unaffected. The detail column hosts five contents and a cap suits all five: `MeetingDetailView`, `WorkflowInspector`, `BatchActionsPane`, the empty-state nudge, and `DigestReaderView`, whose own comment claimed it takes the detail pane "so a large digest reads at full width" and which is prose, so it improves for the same reason the transcript does.
Verified: `swift build` clean, **1882 daemon tests, 0 failures**, truth fences green. Three new: two arithmetic invariants in `LibraryLayoutTests` (detail ideal within its max; the ceiling clears both measured blocks plus their gutters, so a future cap cannot re-open the squeeze the floor exists to prevent) and one source fence that no cap site centres its block.
**A fence that could not fail, caught by mutation.** The left-align fence was first written as a 320-character window after each cap site. The explanatory comment between the cap and its placement in `SummaryRenderedView` is longer than that, so the assertion ran zero times and the test passed against a deliberately mutated file. It now anchors on the next placement frame and asserts a per-file `checked > 0`. Worth remembering as a habit: a new source fence is worth one mutation before it is trusted.
**Owner-owed, still.** Rebuild (`./scripts/rebuild.sh`) and judge the two things a mockup could not show: whether a ~740pt meeting list reads generous or sparse, and whether left-aligned prose under a capped pane reads right. The transcript may want its own tighter measure now that it is bounded at ~1008; that is an eyeball call deliberately left open rather than guessed.
## Fourth pass, 2026-07-29 (option A: the transcript joins the measure, shared at 720)
The third pass closed the left seam and the owner's next look found the remaining one: the right edge. Summary text ended at 700 while transcript text ran to ~1024, so the pane taught a reading boundary on one tab and ignored it on the next ("readable zone is good for eyes... and bad, cause there's an inconsistency with transcript tab"). A three-option design run was mocked at the shipped 1040 cap (A: one measure for both reading tabs; B: screenplay transcript with a speaker gutter, which re-breaks the just-fixed left axis; C: prose plus a reference rail, which fills the pane at the cost of drifting the register toward the dashboard PRODUCT.md rejects). The owner picked **A at 720**.
What shipped: `TranscriptTab`'s reading zone rides the identical `SummaryLayout.readingMeasure` stack, cap then gutter then `.topLeading`. Two cap sites, the cast strip (`VoiceResolutionBar`, whose internal horizontal 16 moved out to the caller so the chips sit on the rows' text edge) and the `LazyVStack` of lines (captions, marker chips, and rows all dropped their own horizontal 16s so the stack's gutter is the only inset). The playback transport, the empty state, and the Audio tab stay full-bleed: instruments and states, not reading content. The transcript's monologue turns were the longest lines in the app at ~1024 and are the surface a measure most obviously helps, so A is not a consistency tax; it is the original fix applied to the tab that needed it most.
The measure moved 680 to 720, the top of the design band, by owner pick after seeing 680 running. The 680 was my argument (~100 characters a line reads past comfortable); the owner judged 720 on their own screen, and inside the band the eye decides. 720 now equals `WorkflowInspectorLayout.cardMaxWidth`, which the old inspector test forbade with a strictly-greater assertion whose message said "if these converge, say why": the why is recorded in that test (deliberate convergence at the band top, 2026-07-29) and the assertion relaxed to greater-or-equal, keeping the half that matters, that a label/value grid is never narrower than prose.
Verified: `swift build` clean, **1882 daemon tests, 0 failures**, truth fences green. The left-align source fence gained `TranscriptTab.swift` as a fourth scanned file (its `checked > 0` guard confirms both new cap sites are really scanned), and the `LibraryLayoutTests` ceiling arithmetic still clears at 720 + 40 = 760 against 1040.
**Owner-owed, still.** Rebuild and read a real meeting across both tabs: the right edge should sit at 740 on Summary and Transcript alike, the cast chips should start exactly where the transcript text does, and 720-wide prose should read comfortable rather than wide. The remaining single knob if the trailing margin bothers the eye is `detailMaxWidth`, which trades Audio's waveform width.

**[DONE] UX35 (P2): hUD width parity between recording and off-the-record states.** **Closed 2026-07-30: owner confirmed the eyeball ("UX35 good to close"); the 96pt pill reads right in both states.**

Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 3: "Off the record mode looks too wide, while when it is disabled it is too narrow... make it same size"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).
## Context
The recording HUD has three hard-coded widths: `panelWidth = 76` for normal recording (`RecordingHUDWindow.swift:51`, with the sizing arithmetic in the comment at :48-50: dot 8 + gap 5 + "Recording" ~50 = 63pt plus margin), `offRecordPanelWidth = 150` for off-the-record (:59), and `degradedPanelWidth = 232` for the system-audio-degraded card (:56).
`setOffTheRecord(_:)` (:238-250) swaps the label text ("Recording" to "Off the record", :239), retints, flips the mic-slash glyph, and resizes the window per state at :248 via `resizePanelAnchoringTopRight`. Nothing measures the text: 150 is a literal, and "Off the record" at the 10pt label size needs ~76pt of text, so the off-record pill carries ~60pt of dead width while the normal pill is snug at 76.
Convergence is layout-safe: every element in `makeContentView` is either centred on the pill's centerX (glyph :379, dot row :387, meter :394, stop key :409) or pinned to both edges (elapsed :390-391, workflow label :399-400). Nothing anchors to an absolute x. The one width-sensitive element, the truncating workflow name (`HUDWorkflowLabel`, :617-624), only gains room.
## Scope
One shared width for the normal and off-the-record states, sized for the longer "Off the record" content (roughly 100-110pt; pick by eyeball against the real label), applied as a single constant. Delete the per-state resize between those two states at :248 (the degraded card's 232pt resize stays). The label swap, tint, glyph, and meter behavior are untouched.
Coordinate with UX46 (filed the same day), which adds a flag-moment button to the same pill: the shared width must fit the control row with three controls, so if both tasks are picked up, size once for the final control count rather than twice.
## Explicitly not in scope
The degraded-mode card width. Rewording the "Off the record" label to something shorter (it is MIC14's discoverability surface and the wording is load-bearing; the off-record feature also splits on workflow NDA mode, so the label must stay unambiguous). Any HUD redesign beyond the width.
## Acceptance
The two states share one width constant; no `resizePanelAnchoringTopRight` call fires on the off-record toggle (pin by unit test if the resize decision is lifted into a testable seam, else by code inspection at review plus the owner eyeball).
`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770). Repo dash guard green.
Owner eyeball: start a recording, toggle off the record and back; the pill does not change size in either direction, the label fits in both states, and the workflow name still truncates gracefully.
## Progress (shipped 2026-07-29, owner-owed on the eyeball)
Implemented in `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/RecordingHUDWindow.swift`. 1786 daemon tests green, 5 of them new, no pipeline change.
**The width is measured, not eyeballed, and the spec's estimate was high.** The spec asked for "roughly 100-110pt; pick by eyeball against the real label", which a headless session cannot do, so the number came off the layout instead. The pill's widest content is the dot row (`dot 8 + NSStackView spacing 5 + the state label`), and reproducing that row's constraints and asking for its `fittingSize` gives **64.0pt for "Recording" and 83.0pt for "Off the record"** at 10pt medium. The label alone measures 50.5 and 70.0pt intrinsic, so the spec's "~76pt of text" and the ToC row's "~89pt of content" were both about 6pt high. `pillWidth = 96` is 83 plus the same 12pt of side margin the old 76 gave the 64pt "Recording" row, rounded onto the 4pt grid. That lands just under the spec's 100-110 band, on a smaller true content width; the owner eyeball is the check on whether the resulting margin reads right.
**The resize decision was lifted into a seam, per the acceptance's preferred branch.** New `HUDPanelGeometry` in the same file, in the house pure-logic style (`daemon/CLAUDE.md`: explicit inputs, nothing read off AppKit inside), holding the four geometry constants and one `size(offRecord:degraded:) -> CGSize`. `offRecord` is a deliberate input that changes nothing, and `HUDPanelGeometryTests` asserts exactly that, so a reintroduced per-state width fails a test rather than needing to be caught at review. `setOffTheRecord` no longer calls `resizePanelAnchoringTopRight` at all; the degraded card is the only resize the HUD performs, and its 232pt width is untouched.
**A latent bug closed on the way.** `clearSystemAudioDegraded` resized to the recording-only 76 unconditionally, with no off-record branch, while `setOffTheRecord` skipped its resize whenever the banner was up. So going off the record, then having system audio degrade, then a successful retry, left the pill at 76 with "Off the record" (83pt of content) in it, clipped by the rounded mask. Both degraded call sites now route through `HUDPanelGeometry.size`, which returns the shared pill for either off-record value.
**Decisions not in the spec.** (1) Status is `owner-owed`, not `done`: the backlog's own rule is that eyeball work stays in the active backlog, and the eyeball is load-bearing here because the width came off a measurement rather than the eye the spec asked for. (2) `design/ui_kits/macos_app/RecordingHUD.jsx` had drifted to `width: 60` (the pre-DSN25 value) while calling itself a faithful recreation; its width is now 96 and its header names the shared-width rule. Its other drift is named there but not fixed: it models neither the off-the-record state nor the mic-slash toggle, and its `minHeight` is still 162 against the shipped 192. Those predate UX35 and are worth their own hygiene task.
**Not done, and deliberately.** UX46's flag button is not built. The shared width is sized for the current two-control row, and both `HUDPanelGeometry.pillWidth` and the `stop.centerXAnchor` offset carry comments naming UX46 as the place to resize.

**[DONE] WF9 (P3, owner-promoted 2026-07-28): workflow store duplicates (cause, diagnostic, name-collision cue).** **Closed 2026-07-30: the owner-owed remainder is verified done and owner-confirmed. The workflows dir holds exactly Client work / General / Personal, no duplicates and no stub, and Personal carries `is_default = true`, so the NDA "Client work" is no longer the silent default for unmatched meetings.** Filed 2026-07-23 (observed during the 2026-07-23 assessment session while reading the owner's live config). Original spec and cause hunt follow.

**WF9 (P3, new): the owner's workflow store holds duplicate rows nothing explains, and name-keyed surfaces mis-scope on collisions.** On 2026-07-23, `~/.config/meeting-pipe/workflows/` held 8 TOMLs: four named "Client work" (distinct ids, orders 3, 5, 7, 8; one `is_default = true`; all `nda_mode = true`, filesystem-only sinks), two named "Personal", one "Untitled workflow", one "General". Every one has `matching_rules = []`, so the duplicates change nothing about routing (precedence falls through to the default), but they clutter the Workflows tab and the prompt chevron, and the Library WORKFLOWS rail resolves scope by workflow NAME (the WF8 ship note records "scope resolves by workflow name"), so four same-named workflows collapse into one scope whose counts and membership are not explainable from the UI. Two adjacent facts recorded for the owner, decisions not code: the effective default workflow is an NDA "Client work", meaning every unmatched meeting summarizes local with filesystem-only sinks (if unintended, flip the default in the Workflows tab); and the "Untitled workflow" row is presumably abandoned scaffolding to delete by hand.

Cause is unknown; that is the first deliverable. Candidate mechanisms to test, in order: the WorkflowsView editor's save path (it clones state on save, WorkflowsView.swift:588, 623; does an edit-save loop ever create instead of update?), `WorkflowMigrator` re-seeding (does the first-run shim re-run and re-create under some condition, e.g. a missing marker or a config reset?), and plain repeated manual creation (the duplicates' `order` values 3, 5, 7, 8 interleave with other rows, which weakly suggests creation events over time rather than one bug burst). Reproduce or rule out each with evidence; if a bug is found, fix it at the store/editor seam with a regression test.

Scope beyond the cause: (a) a load-time diagnostic in `WorkflowStore` that logs one event when duplicate names are present (existing daemon event category, no new category); (b) a quiet name-collision cue in the Workflows tab (a small badge or hint row, PRODUCT.md register, no modal); (c) nothing auto-deletes: cleanup of the existing debris is the owner's, by hand, in the UI, because deleting a workflow is destructive and the duplicates may differ in fields the owner wants to compare first.

Explicitly not: no unique-name constraint (renaming a workflow is common and a hard block on save is hostile; the cue in (b) is the register); no change to name-keyed scope resolution itself (moving scopes to id-keyed is a bigger WF change; note it in the ship if the collision cue proves insufficient); no migration that rewrites the owner's files.

Acceptance: the duplication cause is named with a repro test or each candidate mechanism is ruled out with evidence recorded in this file; the duplicate-name event and the collision cue are pinned by tests (store fixture with two same-named workflows); `WorkflowStore` round-trip suite stays green; owner-owed: manual cleanup of the four "Client work" rows and the default-workflow decision, after the cause is known (cleaning up first would destroy the repro).

## Cause hunt, 2026-07-28

**Found. It is the onboarding workflow step, not the editor.** Two independent leaks in `OnboardingStepWorkflow.choose`, both closed by reusing the created row's id so a preset swap is an update:

1. **Re-picking a preset within the step.** `choose` deleted the workflow it had created before creating the replacement. Every preset is created `isDefault: true`, and `WorkflowStore.delete` refuses to remove the default and returns `false`, which `_ = try? …` threw away. So the delete silently no-opped and the replacement was appended beside its predecessor, once per re-pick.
2. **Leaving and re-entering the step.** `OnboardingRootView` is a `switch step`, so Back / Continue destroys and rebuilds the step. `createdID` lived in the step's own `@State`, so it died on each round trip and the next pick did not even attempt a delete.

Evidence that this is what happened, from the owner's live store (read, never modified):

| order | name | color | backend | default | reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | General | `#3478F6` | anthropic | no | migrator seed: notion sink carrying the real `notion.database_id`, and the blue is the pre-DSN11 `defaultWorkflowHex` |
| 1 | Untitled workflow | `#3478F6` | anthropic | no | the `NewWorkflowSheet` stub, field for field: name, colour, `backend: .anthropic`, and a notion sink with an **empty** `database_id`. Opened, never named, never cancelled |
| 2 | Personal | `#FF6074` | anthropic | no | off-palette, so hand-picked through the old free-hex `ColorPicker` DSN11 removed. Manual creation |
| 3, 5, 7, 8 | Client work | `#B27300` | local | order 8 only | onboarding's Client preset, post-DSN11 (amber is `workflowSwatches[5]`) |
| 6 | Personal | `#0E9488` | anthropic | no | onboarding's Personal preset, post-DSN11 (teal is `workflowSwatches[0]`) |

Order 4 is absent, which is the one delete that worked: a non-default row, so the guard let it go. The 3 / 5 / 6 / 7 / 8 interleave is alternating picks, and the newest being the default is `upsert`'s single-default invariant.

The negative check is the proof: reverting `presetWorkflow` to mint a fresh id and re-running `OnboardingWorkflowPresetTests` produces `["Client work", "Client work", "General", "Personal", "Personal"]` from four alternating picks, the owner's shape in miniature.

Candidates as the task filed them:

- **WorkflowsView editor save path (`:588`, `:623`) - ruled out.** `save()` clones `workflow`, whose `id` is a `let`, and `upsert` matches on `id`, so an edit-save loop is an update by construction. It cannot create. `test_upsert_enforces_single_default` and the round-trip suite already pin it.
- **`WorkflowMigrator` re-seeding - ruled out.** `runIfNeeded` returns early on `guard store.workflows.isEmpty`, and the store has held rows continuously since order 0. The live store holds exactly one "General", which is one seed. `WorkflowMigratorTests` pins the idempotence.
- **Plain repeated manual creation - partly true, and it is a third bug.** Order 1 is `NewWorkflowSheet`'s stub, which is persisted on open so the rail can route to the new scope, and removed only by Cancel or kept by Save. Its Cancel calls the same `delete` that refuses the default, so on an empty store (`isDefault: store.workflows.isEmpty`) a cancelled New workflow cannot be removed at all. Order 2 is genuine manual creation, later hand-recoloured.

**Shipped in this task:** the two onboarding leaks fixed with `OnboardingWorkflowPresetTests` as the regression; a `workflow.duplicate_names` event on `WorkflowStore.load()` (existing category); the quiet cue in both places a collision is visible (the rail row's `· duplicate name` caption with an explaining tooltip, and a hint row under the editor's Name field). No unique-name constraint, no change to name-keyed scope resolution, no migration, nothing auto-deleted.

**Still owner-owed:** the four "Client work" rows, the "Untitled workflow" stub, and the duplicate "Personal" are all still on disk, by design. Deleting a workflow is destructive and the duplicates differ in fields worth comparing first. The default is still an NDA "Client work", so every unmatched meeting summarizes on-device with filesystem-only sinks; flip it in the workflow editor's Default toggle if that is not intended. The rail now says which rows collide, which is the surface to do the cleanup from.

**Not done, and worth naming:** `NewWorkflowSheet`'s stub-on-open is the same "delete may refuse" shape as leak 1 and can still orphan a row on an empty store. It is out of WF9's scope (its own surface, its own decision about whether a stub should be persisted before the first save at all) and is unreachable while the migrator guarantees a default exists. If the collision cue proves insufficient, the bigger move behind it is switching scope resolution from name-keyed to id-keyed, which is a WF-band change touching the sidecar contract, not this task.

**[DONE] LOCAL9 (P2): corrections Phase 3, local LoRA fine-tune.** **Closed 2026-07-30 as the spec's pre-registered honest negative: adapter-not-worth-it, measured three independent ways in the first real run of the loop; `summarization.local_adapter_path` stays unset (owner decision 2026-07-30, formal scorecard grading waived by the owner).** The run: `mp train-adapter --source runs` on the M1 Pro under caffeinate + the <10% free-RAM watchdog. Memory forced `--max-seq-length 8192 --batch-size 1`: a 12288 backward drove 32 GB to 4-5% free at any batch size across the instrumented attempts (two early attempts were additionally contaminated by a v1 watchdog wrapper that killed only `caffeinate` and orphaned the live trainer, so runs briefly overlapped; the v2 wrapper kills the process group), and the `--batch-size` passthrough this surfaced shipped as commit c0d5209. The ceiling cut the corpus from 176 candidate pairs to 42 (train=33, all short meetings; the long information-rich ones are exactly what dropped). 300 iters, ~3 h, checkpoints at 100/200/300. Measurements: train loss memorized to 0.001 while valid-split loss rose monotonically from the untrained 1.173 baseline (1.294 at iter 200, 1.532 at 300); the held-out test triple (base evaluated via a zero-weight adapter, since `mlx_lm.lora --test` unconditionally loads one) read base 1.659 < ckpt-100 1.792 < final-300 2.052; and the `mp dogfood --adapter` A/B on the untrained LOCAL13 anchor `20260723-103118` showed the distillation mechanism itself working (register shifted to cloud-style specificity, 13 actions against 3, the spoken "second three one zero" correctly normalized to ticket 2310) while disqualifying the artifact: zero evidence anchors anywhere (all 42 training pairs are April-June meetings summarized before FEAT10 existed, so the adapter faithfully learned evidence-free output), the one real decision demoted to a question, blanket high confidence, at least one unverifiable ticket number, and ASR garbles promoted to entities (the "Venusaur" ticket; "Irish" as an action owner). Disposition per the spec's own terminal clause: the corpus stays a prompt-context asset, README's Improving-local-quality section records the measured outcome, and the revisit is filed as LOCAL17 (bottom of the backlog, P3) keyed on a post-FEAT10 evidence-bearing corpus large enough to clear the ceiling that gutted this one. Artifacts kept for the trail: `~/Documents/Meetings/adapters/2026-07-30-runs-7b/` (all three checkpoints, `train.log`, `eval.log`, `dogfood-ab.log`, `split.json`) and the side-by-side in `pipeline/runs/20260723-103118.dogfood.md` (gitignored). One more specimen worth remembering: a first A/B attempt crashed on the BASE side with an 11-bullet summary against the 10-item schema cap, live confirmation of the unconstrained-decoding flakiness LOCAL13 documented. Original spec and progress trail follow.

**LOCAL9 (P2): corrections Phase 3, local LoRA fine-tune.** `mp corrections-stats` advertises a Phase 3 readiness check (~20 corrections over ~200 minutes) that no backlog task owns; make it real or stop advertising it. Scope: an owner-invoked `mp train-adapter` that fine-tunes a LoRA on the standard 7B via the MLX LoRA path, building prompt/completion pairs from the corrections corpus (original vs corrected summaries); evaluation is `mp dogfood` A/B (base vs adapter) on held-out meetings, hand-graded like the engine comparison; the adapter ships only through an explicit config opt-in (`summarization.local_adapter_path`), never silently applied. Fully on-device, no egress; training is a command the owner runs, no background training. An honest terminal outcome is allowed: if the A/B shows no capture win, close as adapter-not-worth-it, keep the corpus as a prompt-context asset (few-shot exemplars are the cheaper fallback lever), and fix the README wording. Gated on the corpus bar, which is owner-owed grading volume. Classification note (2026-07-10): `mp train-adapter` is owner-dev tooling under the de-CLI standing rule, CLI-only by design, like dogfood. Acceptance: with the bar met, a training run produces a loadable adapter; the dogfood scorecard decides adoption; README's corrections section describes the real flow either way.

**Progress (2026-07-23): the blocker was the corpus source, not the tooling.** Measured first: the corrections dir has held **0 records** since it was created on 2026-05-18, while the recordings dir holds **169 run sidecars, all with transcript and summary on disk, 2,422,719 transcript chars** (12x the 200k bar), 167 of them summarized by `claude-sonnet-4-6`. So the spec's only training source was empty for two months next to a ready-made distillation corpus. This pass added `--source {corrections,runs,both}` (default unchanged): `runs` builds pairs from the run sidecars (transcript -> the summary that run published), skipping local-backend runs since a model learns nothing from its own output; `both` puts corrections first and withholds their stems from the runs fill. Measured end to end on the real corpus: 167 pairs, train=135 / valid=16 / test=16.

Three defects surfaced while validating against real data, each of which would have made a training run worthless without failing loudly. (1) **Train/inference prompt mismatch.** `_base_instruction()` hand-substituted two of the master prompt's four placeholders, so every training prompt carried a literal `{extra_sections_directive}`, said "(no team context configured)" where the server sends the real team context, wrapped the transcript in `<transcript>` tags the server never uses, and omitted the schema block entirely. Fixed by building the pair from the shipping helpers: `summarize._load_system_prompt` plus `summarize_local.augment_with_schema` / `.compose_user_message`, the last two promoted from `LocalSummaryClient` methods to module functions so there is one copy. (2) **Wrong dataset format.** `{prompt, completion}` has no system role, so the system turn was being folded into the user turn; now emits `{"messages": [...]}` (mlx-lm's `ChatDataset`), the same three turns the server exchanges. (3) **Silent truncation.** `mlx_lm.lora` defaults to `--max-seq-length 2048` and truncates the tail, which is where the summary lives; 106 of 135 real pairs exceeded it, so most examples would have taught the model nothing at all. Now an explicit `--max-seq-length` (default 4096) with over-long meetings dropped and counted in the log, never truncated, plus `--mask-prompt` (loss on the summary, not the transcript) and `--grad-checkpoint`.

The A/B is now actually held-out: training writes `split.json` beside the adapter recording which stems went to train/valid/test, and `mp dogfood --adapter` refuses a transcript the adapter trained on, naming a held-out one instead (`--allow-trained` overrides; an adapter with no manifest warns rather than blocks, since it cannot be verified either way). Verified: 913 pipeline tests green, ruff and pyright clean, CI5 fences pass, and `mp train-adapter --source runs --dry-run` exercised against the owner's real 169 meetings. **Owner-owed remainder** (unchanged in kind, now actually runnable): run `mp train-adapter --source runs --adapter-path <dir>` on the M1 Pro, then `mp dogfood --adapter <dir> <held-out transcript>`, hand-grade the scorecard, and set `summarization.local_adapter_path` only if it wins. An honest negative result stays a valid terminal outcome. Note that LOCAL11 (warm-server identity) is a real precondition for trusting the A/B: a warm base-model server would otherwise serve base weights on both sides.

**Both preconditions are now resolved; the A/B is cleared to run (2026-07-23).** LOCAL11 shipped (commit 65ba629) and LOCAL15 shipped in the same session that raised it, so a configured adapter genuinely serves and a warm base-model server no longer silently answers both sides of the comparison. Verified end to end against a real `mlx_lm.server`: a summarize run carrying the adapter returns a summary, and a `local_adapter_path` that does not exist stops the run naming the path instead of quietly producing a base-model summary. The remaining work is the owner's training run, hand-grading, and the adoption call. The original blocker, kept for the trail:

~~**Do not run the A/B until LOCAL15 lands (filed 2026-07-23).**~~ A second, more fundamental precondition surfaced while shipping LOCAL11: mlx-lm 0.31.3 silently drops the `--adapter-path` flag this task's serving seam passes (`ModelProvider.load` keys its `_adapter_map` lookup by the already-resolved model path while the map is keyed `"default_model"`), so `summarization.local_adapter_path` is a no-op and the base model serves regardless. Verified by code read, empirically against a stubbed `ModelProvider`, and upstream (ml-explore/mlx-lm#1248 open since 2026-05-06, fix PR #1249 unmerged). Running the A/B before that is fixed compares the base model against itself, and the false negative would read as an honest "the adapter did not help" and wrongly close this task after real training and grading time was spent. Full evidence and scope in [LOCAL15](./LOCAL15.md).

**Progress (2026-07-11): tooling shipped, adoption owner-owed.** The full tooling is in: `train_adapter.py` (`mp train-adapter`) reads the summary-grading corpus, builds `{prompt, completion}` pairs (the base summarization prompt + transcript, from `mp.prompts/meeting_summary.md`, mapped to the owner's corrected summary JSON), splits train/valid/test, and shells out to `mlx_lm.lora --train` (subprocess, matching `build_server_command`'s precedent, so the module stays importable on Linux CI); it refuses below the `corrections-stats` readiness bar unless `--force`, and runs local-only via `entry.prepare(secrets=False)`. `summarization.local_adapter_path` (config, empty default) is the opt-in, threaded through `summarize_local.build_server_command` (+`--adapter-path`) and both `LocalSummaryClient` construction sites plus the `mp serve-local` warm path, so a configured adapter is actually served and never applied when unset. `mp dogfood --adapter <dir>` adds the base-vs-adapter A/B (local-only, no cloud baseline, no zero-egress refusal). README's "Improving local quality" section, `config.example.toml`, and the ARCHITECTURE subcommand table describe the real flow. Verified: pipeline 673 green (ruff + pyright clean), incl. pair-building / split / command-construction / `--adapter-path`-when-set / dogfood-adapter-mode-skips-cloud tests; `mp train-adapter --help` and the readiness gate (0/20 on this Mac) confirmed by hand. The two owner-owed remainders keep this partial: the **corpus is empty on this Mac** (grading volume is owner-owed), and the **A/B adoption decision** (run `mp train-adapter` then `mp dogfood --adapter`, hand-grade, set `local_adapter_path` only if it wins) needs a real corpus + Apple-Silicon Mac, not exercisable here. An honest negative result stays a valid terminal outcome.

**[DONE] DET7 (P2): record the suppressed meeting, attach off the live discovery candidate.** Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 7: "if the meeting got suppressed cause I didnt click record, after this I am not able to start the meeting recorder connected to a teams, only 'generic' one"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md). P2; the owner hits this in live dogfood, so re-band to P1 on the next recurrence that costs a meeting's attribution.

**Context.**

After a prompt dismissal (Skip, the close button, force-stop while prompting, or timeout with the skip default), `abandonPrompt` arms the cooldown and the skip latch and returns the state machine to `.idle` (`DetectionStateMachine.swift:104-109`). The latch is refreshed by every discovery sighting (~3 s cadence) for the meeting's whole lifetime (`MeetingSessionController.swift:1058-1061`), lapsing ~15 s after the app stops being seen. That suppression design is correct and stays.

The gap is the way back in. `toggleManual` has a correct attach branch: for `.prompting` and `.suppressed` it dismisses the prompt, clears suppression, and begins recording with the source (`MeetingSessionController.swift:171-187`, comment included). But `.suppressed` is legacy: nothing in production ever sets it (`DetectionStateMachine.setSuppressed` is documented as legacy, :79-85), and after `abandonPrompt` the machine is `.idle`, so every manual start takes the `.idle` branch: `beginRecording(source: nil)`. The status-bar menu deliberately keeps offering "Start Recording" during suppression, with a comment saying a skipped meeting must keep a menu route to record (`StatusBarController.swift:509-515`), but the route it offers is the generic one. The strong hint is that this feature existed and was lost to a refactor, not never designed: the dead `.suppressed` branch is exactly the wanted behavior.

What a generic (`source: nil`) recording costs: workflow resolution falls to the default (`MeetingSessionController.swift:345-349`), the meta sidecar carries no source bundle, display name, kind, or meeting title (`MeetingMetaSidecar.swift:33-37`) so the Library row has no app chip, and there is no lifecycle end-detection anchor (`armLifecycleLeaveButton` early-returns on nil, `MeetingSessionController.swift:1111`), leaving the stop to the silence backstop, which in this dogfood has historically fired approximately never (real ends are AX-detected), so a forgotten generic recording can trail many minutes of silence.

The one attached re-entry that exists is narrow: the timeout-skip path posts a notification whose "Start recording" action rebuilds the source, clears suppression, and records attached (`Notifier.notifySkippedMeeting` fired only from the timeout branch, `MeetingSessionController.swift:728`; handler `didRequestStartLate` at `Coordinator+Delegates.swift:121-133`). An explicit Skip or close posts nothing, and the notification route dies with Notification Center.

Where the live candidate is available: `handleDiscovery` receives a fresh `AppSource` every ~3 s while latched and discards it (`MeetingSessionController.swift:1058-1061`). Caveat for the affordance's label: discovery-path sources carry no `meetingTitle` (`MeetingSourceScanner` constructs without it, :93-96, :126-129, :175); only the lifecycle `.starting` path resolves titles. So the affordance names the app ("Record this Teams meeting"), not the meeting, unless it calls `MeetingTitleResolver.resolve` itself.

Adjacent fact, folded here rather than filed separately: `meetingpipe://record` / `toggle` are unconditionally `source: nil` too (`Coordinator+Automation.swift:44-45`), so a Raycast/Stream Deck start mid-meeting is also unattributed. The same cached-candidate preference can serve both.

**Scope.**

- Cache the last latched sighting where it is currently discarded (`MeetingSessionController.swift:1058-1061`) as `lastSuppressedCandidate: (source: AppSource, seenAt: Date)`, exposed through `SessionHost` (per daemon/CLAUDE.md: session logic reaches the host, not the Coordinator), cleared when the latch lapses.
- Add an explicit `recordAttached(to:)` entry that runs the `didRequestStartLate` sequence: clear suppression for the bundle, dismiss any prompt remnant, `beginRecording(source:)`.
- Surface it in the status-bar menu's suppressed state: the existing "Start Recording" row, when a live latched candidate exists, becomes (or is joined by) "Record this <App> meeting", built through `StatusBarModel` so the row logic is table-tested; plain "Start Recording" stays for the truly-idle case.
- Prefer the cached candidate in `toggleManual`'s `.idle` branch and in the `meetingpipe://record`/`toggle` router when the latch is live, so the hotkey and automation paths attach too. Record the decision if the hotkey is deliberately left generic.
- Decide-and-record: whether `notifySkippedMeeting` should also fire on explicit Skip/close (the plumbing is end-to-end already); recommendation is yes with a quiet banner, since the explicit-skip regret case is exactly the one the owner reported.

**Explicitly not in scope.**

Reviving the `.suppressed` machine state (the latch + `.idle` design is deliberate; add the candidate cache beside it rather than re-litigating the state machine). Changing latch/cooldown semantics or durations. Prompt re-display (this is record-without-reprompting). Title resolution improvements (CAL3's territory; the affordance may call the existing resolver, nothing more).

**Acceptance.**

Tests against `FakeSessionHost` (`MeetingSessionControllerBranchTests` idiom): a latched discovery sighting populates the cached candidate and refreshes it; the cache clears when the latch lapses; `recordAttached(to:)` clears suppression and begins recording with the source (meta sidecar inputs carry bundle id and display name); `toggleManual` from `.idle` with a live cached candidate records attached, and without one records generic; the automation `record` verb with a live candidate attaches.

`StatusBarModel` tests pin the menu row: suppressed-with-candidate shows the named attach row, idle shows plain Start Recording.

`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770). Repo dash guard green.

Owner eyeball: let a Teams prompt time out (or skip it), open the menu bar: "Record this Microsoft Teams meeting" appears; click it; the HUD shows the workflow chip, the Library row carries the Teams chip, and the recording stops on its own when the call ends (AX end detection armed).

**Shipped 2026-07-30.** 1906 daemon tests green (18 new: 4 on the state machine's candidate facade, 9 on the controller's attach paths, 5 on the menu row), `swift build` green, `python3 scripts/truth_fences.py both` green, no em-dash in any added line. What landed, against the scope above:

- **The cache** lives on `DetectionStateMachine` beside the skip latch (`noteSuppressedCandidate` / `liveSuppressedCandidate`), not on the controller. That is the spec's "exposed through `SessionHost`" satisfied by the shortest route: the state machine is already a `SessionHost` member, so the session logic still reaches it through the host, and the two *other* readers (the status-bar menu and the `meetingpipe://` router, both Coordinator-side) need no second seam. Its lifetime is the latch's by construction, so `liveSuppressedCandidate` is gated on `isSkipLatched` and drops a lapsed candidate on read; nothing has to run a timer to expire it, and `clearSuppression` drops it for that bundle so a consumed candidate cannot be re-attached minutes later.
- **`recordAttached(to:summaryMode:trigger:)`** on `MeetingSessionController` is the sequence `didRequestStartLate` already ran, lifted so all four entry points share it. `didRequestStartLate` now delegates to it rather than keeping a fourth copy, and the `start_late` event became `record_attached` with a `trigger` attribute naming who asked (`manual`, `automation`, `notification`), so the log still distinguishes them.
- **The menu row** is `StatusBarModel.Row.recordSuppressedMeeting`, chosen over joining a second row beside Start Recording: after this task the plain row *also* attaches, so two rows would be two labels for one action and one of them would be lying. `StatusBarController.populateMenu` builds `NSMenuItem`s by hand rather than from `rows`, so the copy lives in one `StatusBarModel.startRecordingTitle` helper both read, and the controller reads the candidate at `menuNeedsUpdate` time rather than caching it (the menu is repopulated in place on open, so the row is right at the instant it is looked at, and nothing has to rebuild on the 3 s discovery cadence).
- **Hotkey and automation**, the first decide-and-record item: **not** left generic. Both take one `startManual(summaryMode:trigger:)` rule on the controller. The reasoning is asymmetric cost, not preference: attaching when the user meant a fresh generic recording loses nothing (the workflow is re-routable from the Library, and the meeting really is the one running), while recording generic when they meant this meeting loses the workflow, the sidecar identity, the Library chip, and the AX end anchor, which in this Teams-only dogfood is what actually stops a recording. Automation keeps its own `.idle` guard, so a URL-scheme start still cannot stack onto a live recording. `startManual` exists as a named rule mostly so the automation branch is reachable from a test: `Coordinator` is unconstructible (ARCH4), so its three-line guard is the only part left uncovered.
- **`notifySkippedMeeting` on explicit Skip / close**, the second decide-and-record item: **yes**, as the spec recommended, and quiet in a literal sense. A new `SkipOrigin` splits the two causes: `.timedOut` keeps the sounded "Meeting prompt timed out. Start recording?" (the user may never have seen the prompt), `.dismissed` posts silently and says the meeting is still running (the user chose this, so it is an undo affordance, not an alert). The explicit skip also now gets the `Suppressed (<app>)` menu-bar label the timeout has had since UX10; without it the two surfaces disagreed, and the label is what makes the named row legible in context. Both reuse the existing per-bundle notification id, so repeat skips of one app still coalesce.

Not done, deliberately: the `.suppressed` machine state stays legacy and unrevived, latch and cooldown semantics are untouched, no prompt is re-displayed, and no title is resolved (the row names the app, since discovery-path sources carry no `meetingTitle`, exactly as the spec's caveat says).

Docs synced in the same commit: `README.md`'s `detection.default_prompt_action` entry (both skip paths, the renamed row, what attaching buys, and that it lapses with the call), a new **Suppressed candidate** glossary entry in `ARCHITECTURE.md` beside **Skip latch**, and `design/ui_kits/macos_app/MenuBarDropdown.jsx` gained the `suppressed` state so the mockup shows the row it now ships. Checked and unchanged: `CONVENTIONS.md` (no new event category, no schema change), `daemon/CLAUDE.md` (nothing renamed or moved, no new trap), `docs/SETUP.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, `design/README.md`.

One copy call the owner may want to overrule: the row reads **"Record this Microsoft Teams meeting"**, sentence case after the verb. That is the string this spec's own eyeball step names, and it reads against `design/README.md`'s "menu items use Title Case" rule; "Record This Microsoft Teams Meeting" shouts and the app name is a proper noun inside a sentence rather than a command word. The deviation is recorded at `StatusBarModel.startRecordingTitle`, so flipping it is one line plus two test strings.

**[DONE] UX37 (P2): Ask is safe to leave (root-owned model, cancellable run, preserved state).** **Shipped 2026-07-30: `AskModel` owns the run, the launcher retains `mp ask` in its own cancel slot, and the deeplink is consumed on completion. History cap 20 and refuse-the-second-ask are the two delegated decisions, recorded. One deliberate deviation from the acceptance text: `retainForCancel` did not become a `PipelineDriver` parameter a fake could record, because every ask should be cancellable and a knob with one possible value is noise. Retention is a `RetainSlot` inside `PipelineLauncher` with a second slot for ask, and the observable half is `cancelAsk()` on the driver protocol, which `MeetingLibraryServiceTests` pins reaching the fake. Owner eyeball still to do; everything else is pinned by 14 `AskModelTests` plus that forwarding test.** Original spec follows.

Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 6: "Ask should be safe to leave. Currently if I ask anything, and then leave tab and get back, process got interrupted"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

## Context

What the owner experiences as "interrupted" is state loss, not process interruption, and the truth is worse than a cancel: the run keeps going, invisibly, and its answer is thrown away.

The run's entire state is `@State` on the view struct: `question`, `asking`, `answer`, `errorText` (`AskView.swift:15-18`). `LibraryRootView` mounts `AskView` inside an `if/else` scope chain (`LibraryWindow.swift:747-751`), so switching to Facts or People tears the view down and destroys that state. The `Task {}` started by `ask()` (:224-248) is unstructured, so it is NOT cancelled on teardown; it completes into destroyed state.

The subprocess chain is `LibraryWindowModel.askMeetings` (`LibraryWindow.swift:358-360`) to `MeetingLibraryService.askMeetings` (`Library/MeetingLibraryService.swift:458-482`) to `PipelineLauncher.ask` (`PipelineLauncher.swift:437-461`): `runMP(["ask", question, "--out", tmp], timeout: 10 * 60)`. `retainForCancel` is not passed, so `cancelActiveRun()` cannot reach it (the UX28 machinery exists and is unused here); an abandoned ask holds a pipeline process for up to 10 minutes with no surface and no stop. The `--out` JSON is deleted in a `defer` (:441-442), so the returning answer is lost with the view.

Two adjacent losses. The `meetingpipe://ask?q=` deeplink prefill is consumed and cleared before the run (`AskView.swift:46` clears `model.pendingAskQuestion`), so navigating away loses even the question. And nothing anywhere persists an answer or history; every visit starts blank.

The in-repo fix precedent is two scopes over: `DigestListModel` is an `ObservableObject` held as `@StateObject` by the Library root (`DigestsView.swift:14-26`, `LibraryWindow.swift:450`, `:760`), so a scope switch destroys only the view and an in-flight generate lands on surviving state (`DigestsView.swift:164-178`).

## Scope

- Introduce `AskModel: ObservableObject` (question, asking, answer, errorText, and a session-scoped list of past question/answer pairs) held as `@StateObject` on the Library root beside `digestList`; `AskView` becomes a renderer over it. An in-flight ask completes into the model whether or not the view exists.
- Thread `retainForCancel: true` through `PipelineLauncher.ask` and give the running state a visible Cancel (the UX28 idiom: the run is cancellable, the cancel surfaces as a named outcome, not a silent blank).
- Preserve the deeplink question until the run it started completes (consume-on-completion, not consume-on-render).
- Session history stays in memory (the model), newest first, so returning to Ask shows the previous answers this session. Durable on-disk history is UX38's call, not this task's.

## Explicitly not in scope

Engine performance, indexing, and memory behavior (AI11 owns that; this task makes the UI honest around whatever the engine costs). The Ask pane redesign (UX38, which depends on this model extraction). Durable cross-restart history. Parallel asks (one at a time stays; a second ask while one runs queues or replaces per the model's single `asking` slot, record which).

## Acceptance

Model-level tests: start an ask, simulate view teardown (drop the view, keep the model), deliver the completion; the answer is present when the view remounts. Cancel invokes the launcher's cancel path (fake launcher records `retainForCancel: true` and the cancel call). The deeplink question survives a mount-unmount-mount cycle until its run completes. History accumulates newest-first and caps sanely (pick a cap, record it).

`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770). Repo dash guard green.

Owner eyeball: ask a question, immediately switch to Facts, come back; the spinner is still there and the answer arrives in place. Cancel a slow ask; the pane says it was cancelled instead of going blank.
