**[DONE] FEAT10 (P2): Evidence-grounded summaries (citations with transcript jump).**
Band origin: filed 2026-07-22 (owner feature ask, designed and approved in the 2026-07-22 review session; owner calls from that session: published notes carry timestamps only, and the decisions section is included). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

**FEAT10 (P2, new): every summary point carries verified evidence (verbatim phrase + timestamp) and a one-click jump to the transcript row.** The ask: a summary bullet, decision, action item, or open question is currently uncheckable against the meeting; the reader either trusts it or replays audio. Give each point up to two evidence anchors, each a short verbatim phrase plus a timestamp, rendered as quiet [mm:ss] chips in the Summary tab (hover shows the phrase) that switch to the Transcript tab, scroll to the source row, and briefly highlight it. Published Markdown gets plain ` [12:34]` suffixes, timestamps only.

Why the codebase is ready (evidence, line numbers as of 2026-07-22): the transcript sidecar carries absolute per-segment times (`TranscriptSegment` start/end, `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/TranscriptTab.swift:10`); time-to-row mapping plus scroll already exist for FEAT8 markers (`TranscriptSegment.index(at:in:)` TranscriptTab.swift:28, `ScrollViewReader` + `.id(seg.index)` + `proxy.scrollTo` at TranscriptTab.swift:535/601/615); tab switching is one write to `MeetingDetailView.selectedTab` (MeetingDetailView.swift:15); and `mp ask` is the verification precedent (model cites, `rag.verify_citations` checks against ground truth, graceful fallback when a weak model cites badly, ask.py:103-110). The one real gap: the model input has no timestamps. `summarize.summarize` reads the overlaid speaker-merged Markdown (summarize.py:396) built by `transcript_corrections.overlaid_markdown` (applies the speaker + text overlays to the `<stem>.json` segments, then `render_markdown`, transcript_corrections.py:82-106), and `render_markdown` emits no times (markdown.py:26). Second hazard, named so it is not rediscovered the hard way: Swift rewrites `summary.json` by re-serializing only the fields it models (`MeetingSummary.jsonObject()`, `daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/Library/MeetingSummary.swift:143`), from both `FactsView.markDone` (FactsView.swift:145) and `SummaryTab.persistEdit` (SummaryTab.swift:632), so an unmodeled evidence field would be silently dropped on the first mark-done or edit. Swift must model the field.

Scope, pipeline: (1) additive schema, ADR 0014 stays intact: `Evidence {quote: verbatim <=15 words, t: float seconds into the recording}`; `ActionItem.evidence: list[Evidence]` inline, and `MeetingSummary.evidence: {"summary"|"decisions"|"questions": list[list[Evidence]]}` index-aligned for the string sections; at most 2 anchors per item; legacy files (field absent) round-trip unchanged and every existing consumer ignores the field. (2) model-facing shape: `SUMMARY_TOOL`'s string sections become `{text, evidence[]}` objects so anchors attach at generation and can never misalign (schemas.py:77); one shared normalizer flattens the model shape to the stored shape and is used by every backend (the anthropic tool path in summarize.py; the JSON recovery paths in summarize_local.py, provider_claude_cli.py, provider_openai.py via `largest_balanced_json_object`). (3) timestamped model input: a timestamps variant of the overlaid render prefixing each merged turn with [mm:ss]; the stored `<stem>.md` is unchanged, and a meeting with only plain md (BYO paste) simply gets no evidence, no error. (4) verification pass in `summarize` after the language check: t must land inside or within 45 s of a real segment (snap t to that segment's start); the quote must be a normalized substring (case/whitespace/punctuation tolerant) of transcript text in that window; drop what fails and emit a pipeline event with kept/dropped counts. A wrong anchor never reaches disk. (5) LANG1 interplay: quotes are verbatim transcript language, so `summary_language.divergent_sections` must exclude evidence or a translated summary (summary_language=en over a uk transcript) gets falsely repaired. (6) published render: `render_summary_md` appends ` [12:34]` (multiple: ` [12:34, 18:22]`) per item (markdown.py:96); the Swift mirror `AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.renderMarkdown` matches, and the CI3 golden `summary-md-golden.json` is regenerated with an evidence-carrying case (`cd pipeline && uv run python ../scripts/gen_contract_fixtures.py`).

Scope, daemon: (7) `MeetingSummary` models Evidence with tolerant decode and `jsonObject()` serialization; the corrections editor remaps on save: an item whose text is unchanged keeps its anchors (text-match, the FactsView.markDone guard pattern at FactsView.swift:150-157), an edited or new item drops them, and index alignment for the string sections is rebuilt (the editor edits and deletes rows in all four sections, CorrectionEditor.swift:17-137). (8) `SummaryRenderedView` renders the chips after each bullet/decision/question and inside the ActionItemRow chip row (the existing chip idiom, SummaryRenderedView.swift:211): monospaced digits, muted tint, no sparkle per PRODUCT.md; hover shows the quote; VoiceOver reads "Source at 12:34: <quote>"; chips are keyboard-focusable; the target-row flash honors reduced motion. (9) a chip click sets `selectedTab` to transcript and hands TranscriptTab a pending seek time, consumed via the existing time-to-index mapping + scrollTo. This pending-seek seam is also what AUTO2 rides.

Backends and degradation: full treatment on anthropic, openai, claude_cli, and the local single-pass path. The local map-reduce path (`summarize_local._batched` / `_render_for_reduce`, summarize_local.py:110-124) and the Apple Intelligence backend (`Summarization/AppleIntelligenceSummarizer.swift`) emit no evidence in v1: absence over wrong anchors, and the verifier enforces the same rule everywhere. Old meetings gain evidence per-meeting via the existing Regenerate flows; no bulk migration. Expect a modest output-token bump (capped quote length and 2 anchors per item keep it small on cloud; on local it is the price of the feature).

Explicitly not: no quotes in published notes (owner call 2026-07-22; the phrase is hover detail in-app). No meetingpipe:// links in notes (AUTO2, filed separately at P3). No audio-seek on chip click in v1 (cheap follow-on once the pending-seek seam exists; `PlaybackBar` already lives in the Transcript tab, note for the pickup session). No evidence on `mp digest` / DigestsView (cross-meeting synthetic summaries; the renderer tolerates absence) or on WF7 extra_sections. No new config knob; the caps are constants. Rejected during design: inline [mm:ss] markers inside the bullet strings (pollutes Facts rows, `mp actions`, digest, the embedding index, and the corrections corpus) and a `summary: list[str] -> list[object]` migration (breaks the ADR 0014 lock and every publisher for no gain over the additive field).

Acceptance: Python tests pin the normalizer (all four sections), the verifier (valid anchor kept with t snapped; fabricated quote dropped; out-of-range t dropped; tolerant quote matching), the `render_summary_md` suffix, the LANG1 exclusion, per-backend evidence survival through JSON recovery, and that a legacy no-evidence summary round-trips unchanged. Swift tests pin decode/jsonObject round-trip preserving evidence through a mark-done and a correction save, the remap-on-edit rule, the chip presentation model, and the pending-seek time-to-row mapping. CI3 fixture regenerated and both suites green on it. End to end: a regenerated meeting's `summary.json` carries verified anchors and every rendered chip resolves to a real transcript row; ruff, pyright, pytest, swift test, and truth fences all green. Owner-owed eyeball: a chip click lands the Transcript tab on the cited row on a live meeting.

## Progress

**2026-07-24, shipped to `owner-owed`.** Every scope item (1) to (9) is built and the whole acceptance list except the two items that need a real model and a real meeting. Both suites green: 1665 Swift, 1018 Python, plus ruff, pyright, and truth fences.

Decisions taken that the spec left open:

- **The normalizer is a pydantic `mode="before"` model validator**, not a function each backend calls. The spec asked for "one shared normalizer used by every backend"; wiring it into `MeetingSummary` validation makes that true by construction, so the Anthropic tool path and the three JSON-recovery paths inherit it and a fifth backend cannot forget it. `normalize_model_summary` stays public and pure for direct testing.
- **`Evidence.t` decodes from `"mm:ss"` as well as seconds.** The model reads `[12:34]` markers in the transcript, and asking it to emit `754.0` adds an arithmetic step whose only failure mode is a silently dropped anchor. It can now copy what it saw. The stored shape is still float seconds, so this is tolerance on the read edge, not a schema change.
- **An anchor snaps to the segment that actually carries the quote**, when one does, rather than to whichever segment the timestamp landed nearest. Both satisfy the spec's "snap t to that segment's start"; this one makes the chip land *on* the cited row instead of near it, which is what the acceptance criterion asks for. A quote spanning a turn boundary falls back to the nearest segment in the window.
- **A quote over 25 words is dropped** even if it matches. The schema asks for <=15; this is the ceiling at which a "verbatim phrase" is really a pasted paragraph, and it keeps the hover card readable.
- **`SummaryRenderedView` bullets now carry their pre-filter index.** The view drops empty strings before rendering, which would have shifted every anchor after a gap onto the wrong bullet. Display numbering still counts rendered position.
- **The LANG1 exclusion needed no code change**, only a test and a comment: quotes live in their own field, so `_summary_sections` never saw them. That is worth keeping structural rather than defending with a filter.

Owner-owed remainder:

1. The eyeball the spec reserves: click a chip on a live meeting and confirm the Transcript tab lands on the cited row.
2. Confirm real yield. Every path here is exercised by fakes, so what is unmeasured is how often a real cloud model produces anchors that survive verification. The prompt's rule 10 is the tuning surface if the answer is "rarely"; the verifier's thresholds are deliberately not the first thing to loosen, since letting a wrong anchor through is the one outcome the feature cannot afford.

## Owner walkthrough (for the session that picks this up)

1. [owner] Open a meeting summarized after 2026-07-24 (or Re-summarize any meeting first) and click a [mm:ss] chip in the Summary tab. Pass: the Transcript tab opens, scrolls to the cited row, briefly highlights it, and the row's text matches the phrase shown when hovering the chip.
2. [session] Measure real yield, the second reserved leg: count anchors surviving verification across recent real-model runs (the `evidence_verified` event and `summary.json` contents). If real cloud runs rarely produce surviving anchors, the tuning surface is the prompt's rule 10; the verifier's thresholds are deliberately not the first thing to loosen.
3. Both good: [session] moves FEAT10 to done per the archive process.


**[DONE] UX29 (P2): NDA-aware re-summarize affordance.**
Band origin: filed 2026-07-23 (owner finding, 2026-07-23 assessment session). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

**UX29 (P3, new): the UI offers a cloud action that can never do what it says on an NDA meeting, then stays silent about the substitution.** On 2026-07-23 the owner clicked "Re-summarize with Anthropic" twice on an NDA-workflow meeting (stem `20260723-103118`, toolbar events 07:46:26Z and 07:48:43Z), got local Qwen output both times, and read it as a bug. It is not: the daemon passes `--backend anthropic` and the pipeline's `effective_backend` clamps to local (the PIPE6 one-shot override deliberately "still flows through the regulated/NDA clamp"; the daemon additionally strips the API key from the child env, `PipelineLauncher.cloudSecretPolicy`, PipelineLauncher.swift:827-857). The clamp is the product's core privacy promise and stays untouched. The defect is purely communicative: the menu item is emitted unconditionally (`MeetingDetailView+Header.swift:205-208`, the comment at :203-204 even names the reliance on downstream clamping), and no surface says "this ran locally because NDA".

Scope. In the detail `...` menu (and any twin affordances), when the meeting's meta carries `workflow_nda_mode = true` or global regulated mode is on: render the cloud option disabled with an inline caption in the quiet register ("NDA workflow: summaries stay on this Mac"), and add a "Change workflow..." row beneath it that opens the existing reassign flow (`beginReassign`, MeetingDetailView+Header.swift:416-449), whose explicit NDA-transition confirmation is already the designed escape hatch the owner ended up using by hand. Derive the flag from the same meta the row already loads (MeetingStore carries the workflow block); no new state. VoiceOver reads the disabled reason.

Explicitly not: no per-meeting cloud override under NDA (it would weaken the promise the clamp exists to keep; the reassign flow with its confirm IS the override); no pipeline changes; the post-run provenance label ("Summarized locally") is PIPE11's run.json fix rendered by the existing badge, not new UI here; regulated-mode handling beyond reusing the same disabled state.

Acceptance. A presentation-model test pins: cloud item disabled with the caption when meta says NDA (and when regulated is on), enabled otherwise; the Change-workflow row routes to the reassign entry point; VoiceOver label carries the reason. Full daemon suite green. Owner eyeball: on an NDA meeting the menu explains itself and the path to Anthropic is one click away, with the existing confirmation intact.

Pass: "Re-summarize with Anthropic" is disabled with a quiet caption naming the NDA clamp, and "Change workflow..." sits beneath it, opening the existing reassign flow with its confirmation intact. VoiceOver on the disabled item reads the reason.
3. Known deliberate behaviour, not a bug: with global regulated mode ON, the caption names regulated instead and no Change-workflow row appears, because reassigning cannot lift a global clamp.
4. Pass: [session] moves UX29 to done.


**[DONE] UX33 (P2): Workflow inspector rework.**
Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 1: the workflow details panel "looks quite strange to me, I think it requires rework", decision delegated and taken: rework is warranted). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

## Context

`WorkflowInspector.swift` (187 lines) renders the right-column pane for a selected workflow scope. Traced 2026-07-29, the "strange" read decomposes into six concrete defects:

1. **The Edit button floats mid-pane.** The layout is `ScrollView > VStack` (:11-12) with a `Spacer(minLength: 0)` at :18 meant to pin the "Edit workflow" button (:19-23) to the bottom. A `Spacer` inside a `ScrollView` is inert (the scroll view sizes to content), so a workflow with little content shows a short card and a large empty region below it. This is the biggest single contributor to the broken feel.
2. **A 320pt ribbon in an unbounded pane.** The inspector caps itself at `minWidth: 240, idealWidth: 260, maxWidth: 320` (:27) while the detail column it sits in has a min of 450 and no max (`LibraryWindow.swift:782`, `LibraryLayout.swift:25-26`). On a wide window the pane is mostly background.
3. **"Modes" usually says "-".** `modesLine` (:131-133) returns "-" whenever NDA is off, which is most workflows. A permanent row whose value is a dash is noise.
4. **Recent chips look interactive and are not.** The RECENT section (:87-111) renders up to 5 meeting titles as chip-backgrounded `Text` (:95-108) with no button, tap gesture, or navigation. Clicking a recent meeting does nothing.
5. **Trigger leaks implementation detail.** `triggerLine` (:115-129) prints raw bundle ids and `/regex/` strings joined by commas; "Output" truncates a Notion DB id to an 8-char slug (:140-143). Neither is written for the product's register (PRODUCT.md: quiet, deliberate, non-technical owner).
6. **The pane is unreachable whenever a meeting is selected.** The detail-pane branch order at `LibraryWindow.swift:950-954` is multi-select, then single meeting, then workflow scope, so selecting a workflow in the rail while a meeting selection exists shows the meeting, not the inspector. The pane is effectively an empty state, not an inspector.

Also missing entirely: retention policy (a real `Workflow` field, visible only in the editor sheet), meeting count, last-meeting date. The pane has exactly one affordance (Edit).

## Scope

Redesign `WorkflowInspector` into a pane that fills its column and earns each row:

- Identity header: emoji/colour, name, DEFAULT badge, the FEAT11 context state line (keep; it is good), and the context prompt behind a disclosure rather than a 3-line dump ahead of the facts.
- Facts that earn their row: drop "Modes" when it would read "-" (show it only when NDA or redaction is on); humanize Trigger ("Matches Microsoft Teams" plus a quiet secondary line for title rules); show Output as the sink names with the Notion target's display name where the picker already knows it.
- Add the two cheap truths the pane is missing: meeting count for the scope and the last meeting's date (both derivable from the already-loaded scope counts and meeting list; no new disk reads).
- Recents become buttons: clicking one selects that meeting in the list (the selection state lives on `LibraryRootView`; hand the tap up rather than resolving stems in the inspector).
- Actions: keep Edit primary; visually pin it (give the pane a non-scrolling footer or accept content-flow placement, but kill the dead region); the row-level Delete stays UX32's.
- Layout: widen to fill the detail column with a centered max around 400-480pt so it reads as a card, not a ribbon.
- Reachability: decide-and-record between (a) selecting a workflow rail scope clears the meeting selection so the inspector always shows, or (b) the inspector is also reachable from a compact header chip when a meeting is open. Recommendation: (a); it matches how scope switches already feel and is one line where the scope changes.
- Extract the row derivation into a pure `InspectorPresentation`-style type per the repo's decide-pattern (daemon/CLAUDE.md "Pure logic on its own type"), so trigger humanization and row visibility are table-tested.

## Reachability decision, taken at pickup 2026-07-29

**(a): selecting a workflow rail scope clears the meeting selection.** Taken as recommended, and implemented as `LibraryScope.clearsMeetingSelection` (true only for `.workflow`), read by one `onChange(of: scope)` in `LibraryRootView`.

Why not (b), the compact header chip: it adds a second entry point to a pane whose problem is that it is unreachable, which leaves the original branch-order bug in place and pays for it with new chrome in a header PRODUCT.md's register wants quiet. (a) removes the condition instead of routing around it.

Why the rule is scoped to `.workflow` and not "any scope change": `.allMeetings` is what Facts, People, Ask, Quick Find, and the `meetingpipe://open?stem=` deeplink all switch to *before* setting a selection, in the same closure. A blanket clear would fire on that scope write and undo the navigation those five surfaces had just performed. Pinned by `test_no_other_scope_clears_the_meeting_selection`.

Residual, accepted: with a meeting open under a workflow scope, getting back to the inspector still means deselecting. That is one click, the toolbar keeps its own `Edit workflow` button in that state, and it is the same shape every other scope already has.

## Explicitly not in scope

Inline editing in the inspector (the sheet remains the single editor). UX32's row actions. Any new analytics beyond count and last-date. Changing `LibraryScope` resolution semantics (TECH-B4 name-based resolution stays; UX30 owns the alert-copy honesty about it).

## Acceptance

Unit tests on the extracted presentation type: trigger humanization cases (bundle-only rule, bundle+title rule, no rules), Modes row hidden when NDA and redaction are both off and shown otherwise, Output naming with and without a known Notion display name, recents capped at 5.

A UI-level test (or the presentation seam) pins that tapping a recent invokes the selection callback with the right stem.

The reachability decision is recorded in this file at pickup and the chosen behavior is pinned by a test on the scope-change path.

`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green (baseline at filing: 1770). Repo dash guard green.

Owner eyeball: select the Client work workflow on a wide window; the pane fills sensibly, no dead half-screen, "Modes: -" is gone, clicking a recent meeting opens it, and the count/last-date line reads true.

## Second pass, 2026-07-29 (the owner's eyeball, and the ribbon that survived it)

The eyeball happened the same day: "workflow view looks better, yet still ugly, also 'edit workflow' button looks like something from another world". A layout review was mocked at true scale and approved before any further code was written.

**The card was still a ribbon.** `cardMeasure = 416` was derived as `LibraryLayout.detailMinWidth (450) - 2 * padding`, which is a correct derivation of the wrong quantity: it sizes the card for the narrowest window the app allows and then freezes it at every width. On the owner's 1230pt pane that left 812pt of background. This task was filed to kill "a 320pt ribbon in an unbounded pane" and shipped a 416pt ribbon in an unbounded pane. The spec is what failed, and it failed by self-contradiction: "widen to fill the detail column with a centered max around 400-480pt" asks for two things that cannot both be true at 1230pt, and the first pass followed the number rather than the intent. `.frame(maxWidth:)` already shrinks a wider cap back at the floor, so the floor never needed deriving at all.

`cardMeasure` is now `cardMaxWidth = 720`, owner-picked, a ceiling rather than a width. It is the top of the 640-720 reading column `design/README.md` asks for. The summary's own measure moved to 680 in the same session and deliberately stops short of 720: prose has a measure ceiling near 90 characters a line, a label/value grid does not, so the two numbers differ because the content does. A test pins the card inside the band and pins that it exceeds the floor-derived figure, which is the relation that actually matters now.

**Widening alone would have bought whitespace, not information.** The rows were a stacked eyebrow-over-value column, so extra width just moved the right edge. `InspectorRow` now renders a label/value grid above a `gridFloor` and falls back to the stacked form below it via `ViewThatFits`, so a narrow pane is unchanged and a wide one reads as a spec sheet.

**The footer was the "another world" control.** It was a full-pane `bgRaised` plinth: a filled band running the whole 1230pt to hold one ~140pt button, three times wider than the card above it, which is why it read as a separate surface rather than as this card's footer. It is now the card's width, carries a hairline instead of a fill (`design/README.md` is explicit that resting surfaces get a hairline and no fill), and puts Edit at the card's trailing edge so content-left and action-right bound the card. The capsule and `.mpGhost` were never the problem and are unchanged; the first pass was right about the style and wrong about the surface.

Verified: `swift build` clean, **1879 daemon tests, 0 failures**. The old floor-derivation test was replaced rather than edited, since it pinned exactly the relation being rejected; three tests stand in its place (grows past the floor-derived width, stays inside the design band and above the prose measure, and a source fence that the footer carries no `bgRaised` and aligns trailing).

**Owner-owed, still.** Rebuild (`./scripts/rebuild.sh`) and select Client work on a wide window: the card should fill sensibly with the facts reading as a grid rather than a list, and the Edit button should look like it belongs to the card under it. Then narrow to the detail floor and confirm the rows fall back to the stacked form without squeezing.

## Third pass, 2026-07-30: Edit moves to the top right

Owner, on seeing the shipped footer: "can we move edit button to the top right? it looks lonely bottom left". One correction for the record, since it does not change the answer: the second pass had already moved Edit to the card's *trailing* edge (`dd22cc9`), and the owner had rebuilt past that commit, so the running build showed it bottom-right rather than bottom-left; the "bottom left" recollection is of the screenshot from before that pass. The lonely part was accurate either way, and it was the band, not the side.

Edit now rides the identity row's trailing edge, pushed there by a `Spacer` rather than a frame of its own, and the footer band is deleted outright: the pane is a single `ScrollView` again. This is the second route this task's own spec offered ("give the pane a non-scrolling footer **or** accept content-flow placement"), so it needs no re-argument. Top right beside the name it edits is the mac inspector convention, and the action rides the first row in the pane, so "does not scroll away" survives in practice without a band to enforce it. The workflow name gained `lineLimit(1)` with tail truncation so a long name truncates rather than shoving the button off the card's edge.

The footer fence was replaced rather than edited, since it pinned a footer that no longer exists. The new one asserts no `private var footer` and no `bgRaised` survive, that `editButton` is its own named piece, and that inside the identity row the `Spacer` *precedes* `editButton` (without that ordering the button would sit beside the name instead of at the edge). It strips `///` and `//` comments before scanning, because the code's own history note names the plinth the fence bans, and it was mutation-verified two ways: removing the `Spacer`, and moving `editButton` out of the row. Both turn it red.

Verified: `swift build` clean, **1888 daemon tests, 0 failures** (no count change; one fence replaced another), truth fences green.

**Owner-owed, unchanged:** the eyeball on a wide window, now with Edit at the top right.

1. Precondition: any rebuild after 2026-07-29 (the second pass).
2. [owner] Open the Library at full width and select Client work in the rail. Pass: the facts read as a label/value grid inside a card capped near 720pt with visible margins, Edit sits at the identity row's top right and visually belongs to the card, there is no dead region, no "Modes: -", a recent-meeting click opens that meeting, and the count / last-date line reads true.
3. [owner] Now narrow the window to its floor. Pass: the grid rows fall back to stacked labels without squeezing or truncation.

**[DONE] UX38 (P2): Ask surface redesign: invitation empty state, docked input, history.**
Band origin: filed 2026-07-29 (owner exploratory sweep, item 6: "build a nice animated search bar at center of the page, inviting user to ask anything, and show the results of the search in a list form below... and move the search to the top left/top right once search completed. what do you think?"). Status and priority live in this task's ToC row in [meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md](../meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md).

## Context

The owner's instinct is right about the core thing: Ask has a different job from every other rail scope. Facts, People, Stats, and Digests are projections you read; Ask is a conversation you start. Rendering it as another sidebar-plus-content page makes an empty pane with a lonely text field, which invites nothing.

The design opinion the owner asked for, recorded: yes to the centered invitation, with one amendment. After the first answer, the input should dock to the TOP of the pane (full-width or centered), not shrink into a top-left/right corner. A corner placement hides the primary affordance of the surface behind a small target, and the center-then-top pattern is the one the muscle memory already exists for (Spotlight, Raycast, every command palette). Top-docked keeps ask-again one click and zero hunting.

Register constraints from PRODUCT.md: quiet, deliberate, local-first; anti-flashy. "Animated" therefore means a restrained focus/transition treatment honoring `MPMotion.reduceMotion` (the prompt panel at `MeetingPromptWindow.swift:566-573` is the in-repo precedent for motion gating), not a pulsing hero. QuickFind (Cmd+K) already owns find-a-meeting; Ask's copy must frame answering ("Ask about your meetings: what did we decide about X?"), not searching, or the two surfaces blur (the QuickFind Ask handoff row, UX25, already routes questions here).

Dependencies and material: UX37 extracts the root-owned `AskModel` with a session history list this redesign renders; AI11 gives the running state something honest to show (index-rebuild progress instead of a bare spinner). Citations in answers already resolve to real meetings (`rag.verify_citations`) and the answer view already deep-links stems; keep that affordance prominent.

## Scope

- Empty state: centered input with a one-line invitation, 2-3 suggested question chips derived cheaply (recent workflow names, a recent meeting title), and the session history beneath when it exists.
- Ask flow: on submit, the input docks to the top with the quiet transition; the running state shows the AI11 progress line when the index is rebuilding, else the thinking state; Cancel per UX37.
- Results: newest-first history list of question/answer cards (question, answer, citation chips that open the meeting), rendered from `AskModel.history`.
- Decide-and-record with the owner at pickup: whether history persists across app restarts (a small JSON in the config dir, the `SavedSearchStore` single-file idiom) or stays session-only. Default to session-only until asked; durable history is one store away if wanted.
- Reduce-motion path renders the same states without the transition.

## Explicitly not in scope

Engine behavior (AI11). Model ownership and cancellation (UX37; this task builds on it and must not land first). Multi-turn conversation threading (an answer is a card, not a chat; revisit only if the owner asks). Replacing QuickFind or merging the two surfaces.

## Decided at pickup (2026-07-31)

The four calls this spec left to the implementer, recorded here so the walkthrough below is read against what actually shipped.

- **History stays session-only.** The spec's own default, taken rather than escalated. A question is asked about today's library and a restart is a reasonable place for it to end; a durable store is one `SavedSearchStore`-shaped file away if the owner asks. UX37's cap of 20 is what the list renders.
- **The pane docks on "there is anything to show", not on "an answer landed".** Layout is docked whenever a run is in flight or the history is non-empty, so the first question docks the input immediately (the move reads as a response to asking) and returning to the rail with history finds the same docked pane rather than an invitation sitting over a list.
- **The refused second ask is made legible before the click, not after it.** UX37 refuses a second ask while one runs and leaves the field untouched, which on its own is indistinguishable from a dead button. The running card carries the rule and names the way out ("One question at a time. Cancel to ask another."), and the field and the Ask button are visibly inert while the run is up. No transient toast: a state that explains itself is quieter than a message that appears and leaves.
- **The input clears when a run ends, not when it starts.** Through the run the field is the only place the running question is shown (and UX37 keeps the text on purpose so a rail switch cannot blank it); once the answer or the stop is a card, the same text left in the input is a leftover to delete before asking again, which is the opposite of what docking is for. Done in the renderer, so `AskModel`'s contract and UX37's tests are untouched.
- **Both chip kinds stack rather than wrap.** A suggestion is a whole question and a citation is a meeting title, so a flow row would break them mid-sentence at any width the list column actually gets (400 floor, 460 ideal). No wrapping layout was introduced, so the `private` `FlowLayout` in `PreferencesView+Recording.swift` stayed where it is rather than being promoted for one speculative caller.

The AI11 leg needed daemon-side plumbing that did not exist: `mp ask` was printing the heartbeat, but `PipelineLauncher.ask` never passed `runMP`'s `onProgress`, and `PipelineProgress` dropped everything past `stage` + `elapsed_s`. That is presentation plumbing rather than engine behaviour (AI11's own ship note names UX38 as the renderer for those counters), so it landed here: the counters ride `PipelineProgress`, `PipelineLauncher.askPhase` decodes the two `mp ask` stages, and `AskInFlight` gained the field UX37 left room for.

## Acceptance

Presentation-level tests over the view's state derivation: empty model renders invitation, non-empty history renders the list with the input docked, asking state renders progress when the engine reports index work. Citation chips invoke the meeting-open callback with the right stem.

`cd daemon && swift build` and `swift test` green. Repo dash guard green.

Owner eyeball: fresh Ask scope shows the centered invitation; asking docks the input up with a quiet motion (and no motion under Reduce Motion); the answer arrives as the first card with clickable citations; asking again stacks a second card; leaving and returning shows the same history (UX37's guarantee, visible here).
